# v0.13 deep case study (v2) — per-repo dossiers (111 repos)

Status: **Analysis — 2026-06-03.** The deeper, more critical successor to the v0.12 [post-build coverage re-analysis](../v0.12/post_build_coverage_analysis.md): a fresh per-repo pass over all 111 case-study repos against the *current* (post-v0.12-build) alint, under the reproduce-first lens. Generated by a 50-agent workflow (deep dossiers -> cross-repo gap clustering -> adversarial workability verification -> synthesis). The holistic synthesis + comprehensive plan is in [`deep_case_study_v2.md`](./deep_case_study_v2.md).

**Headline:** 77 of 111 repos carry **no genuine gap** for their addressable surface; after adversarial verification only **3 real gaps + 13 partial** survived across the whole corpus, **none rated high priority**.

---

### `Alamofire/Alamofire` — Swift HTTP networking library (SwiftPM + CocoaPods/Carthage), single-package, multi-platform Apple SDK

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% addressable — value-add only (the repo's gates are 100% execution: xcodebuild/swift-test matrix + CodeQL). Of the value-add config alint WOULD add, 100% is natively expressible today (including the previously-doubted podspec/CHANGELOG coherence).

**Enforcement surface.** Alamofire's ENFORCED/gating surface is almost entirely category-D execution. CI = .github/workflows/ci.yml: a multi-platform matrix (macOS/iOS/tvOS/watchOS/visionOS) of `xcodebuild build-for-testing`/`test-without-building` and `swift test` across Xcode versions — i.e. 'the SwiftPM package compiles and the XCTest suite passes'. Plus a CodeQL static-security-analysis job. There is NO shell validator, NO git hook, NO pre-commit, NO Makefile/Rakefile lint task, NO codegen-freshness/golden-diff, NO format/lint GATE (a .swiftformat config exists but SwiftFormat is run manually by maintainers, not enforced in CI), and NO version-coherence or structural-invariant gate. So the repo's GATED addressable surface is genuinely empty (A=B=C=0; coverage_today 0/0). Categories present: license-headers — every Source/**/*.swift carries the canonical MIT permission block (CONVENTION, ungated); structure — Alamofire.podspec, Source/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy, .swiftformat exist by convention; security/build — CodeQL + xcodebuild matrix (execution-only). The config correctly records the whole linter-expressible config as alint VALUE-ADD that the repo does NOT itself gate on.

**What alint replaces.** Nothing the repo currently GATES (its gates are 100% execution). As pure value-add alint would NATIVELY add: per-file MIT header convention -> file_content_matches / file_header on the invariant 'Permission is hereby granted...' substring (avoids the per-file copyright-year drift 2014-2022/2025); podspec/PrivacyInfo/.swiftformat existence -> file_exists; plus the oss-baseline + ci/github-actions + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts bundles it extends (final_newline, no_trailing_whitespace, no large tracked artifacts, etc.).

**What alint improves.** Over the repo's status quo (which simply has NO declarative state checks), a single reviewable .alint.yml asserts the MIT-header convention across all 43 sources, the privacy-manifest/podspec presence, and hygiene — invariants that today are tribal knowledge enforced only by reviewer attention. The podspec<->CHANGELOG version coherence (below) is an invariant the repo has no mechanism for at all; alint expresses it cross-file without any toolchain.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE. The one gap the config records — 'podspec s.version must equal the NEWEST CHANGELOG entry, but cross_file_value_equals flags all 85 historical entries because the regex extract matches every entry' — is PHANTOM under the current build. Reproduced: `cross_file` (equals relation) with a target `extract: { regex: '(?s)\\A.*?##\\s*([0-9]+\\.[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+)' }` collapses to EXACTLY one capture. Verified empirically: against a 3-entry CHANGELOG the start-anchored/dot-all/lazy idiom yields ['5.12.0'] (the newest) while the naive pattern yields all three; mechanically `\\A` only matches at haystack position 0 so captures_iter produces a single match. cross_file.check_equals already requires the SOURCE (podspec) to be a single value and compares it to the single target value. So the 'first-match-only extract' the config calls a missing primitive already exists as the documented occurrence:first idiom. Config was authored PRE-build with the naive-regex assumption.

**Abstraction ideas.** A first-class `occurrence: first|last` knob on the regex extract would make the (?s)\\A.*? idiom self-documenting rather than a regex trick (lower the expertise bar; the same need recurs in any 'newest CHANGELOG/version-header' check). Otherwise nothing beyond the existing surface is needed — this repo is a near-pure category-D (compile/test/CodeQL) project and the right alint story is value-add convention assertions, not gate replacement.

*Notes.* Strong calibration case for 'enforced vs expressible' separation. The config's discipline (A=B=C=0, value-add recorded separately) is exactly right; only its lone deferred C is stale. No genuine gaps.

---

### `angular/angular` — Large TypeScript web-framework monorepo (Bazel 8 + pnpm workspace, ~68 package.json, ~4488 .ts), Google-governed

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the repo's ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) state surface today (file_header, eofline, line_endings, all bespoke-config existence, package-manager coherence). The dominant enforcement mass (ts-circular-deps, tsc, prettier, commit-message NLP, public-api goldens, dep-license) is correctly category-D execution and out of scope.

**Enforcement surface.** Validation lives in .github/workflows/{ci,pr}.yml driving pnpm/ng-dev tasks, .husky hooks, and Bazel golden tests. By category: license-headers + final-newline — tslint.json `file-header` ('Copyright Google LLC', allow-single-line-comments:false) and `eofline`, run via `pnpm tslint` over the tsconfig-tslint include minus tslint excludes (adev examples, compiler-cli compliance test_cases fixtures) [the two repo-STATE arms; rest of tslint is AST=D]; import-firewall — `pnpm ts-circular-deps:check` over {packages,adev}/**/*.ts (semantic cycle detection over a file-to-file edge graph = D, but a file_graph edge SOURCE); bespoke-validator CONFIG schemas — `ng-dev pullapprove verify` (.pullapprove.yml), `ng-dev ngbot verify` (.github/angular-robot.yml), `ng-dev ai skills validate` (skills/**/SKILL.md frontmatter) [each a bespoke runtime validator = D; alint can assert the config FILE exists]; typecheck — `check-tooling-setup` (tsc) = D; commit-discipline + formatting — (pr) `ng-dev commit-message validate-range`, `ng-dev format changed --check` (prettier), and the .husky pre-commit/commit-msg/prepare-commit-msg advisory hooks = D NLP/formatter; dep-license — 'Check Package Licenses' dev-infra action = D; codegen-freshness — goldens/public-api + goldens/vscode-extension + tools/symbol-extractor via Bazel golden-diff tests = D; line-endings — .gitattributes pins *.ts/*.js to eol=lf, normalized by prettier end_of_line:lf + git text=auto (state-expressible). The config expresses the file-STATE subset (header, eofline, structure/existence) natively and records the build/test/AST/NLP/golden surface as orchestrated-but-not-reimplemented D.

**What alint replaces.** tslint `file-header` -> file_header (pattern 'Copyright Google LLC', lines:10, with the SAME enforced scope as tslint: packages/modules/tools/devtools/scripts includes minus the compliance test_cases + zone.js + golden-test + d.ts excludes — the config notes that without these excludes it over-fires ~1290x, a textbook scope-match win); tslint `eofline` -> final_newline (+ file_append_final_newline fix); existence of every bespoke-validator config (.pullapprove.yml/.github/angular-robot.yml/.prettierrc/.editorconfig/.nvmrc) -> file_exists; the .gitattributes eol=lf intent -> line_endings target:lf; package-manager coherence (packageManager vs engines.pnpm) -> cross_file equals + normalize. Plus the oss-baseline+node+pnpm-workspace+ci/github-actions bundles.

**What alint improves.** alint consolidates the file-STATE arms of a TS-AST linter (tslint file-header/eofline) into a declarative file_header/final_newline that runs WITHOUT node/pnpm/tsc — instant in CI and pre-commit, no TypeScript program load. The scope-curation (matching tslint's real include/exclude so the header rule fires only on enforced files) is far more reviewable as one YAML block than buried in tsconfig-tslint.json + tslint.json overrides. cross_file also lets Angular assert config-file<->config-file version coherence (packageManager<->engines.pnpm) that ng-dev does not check at all.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE — both items the config defers are PHANTOM under the current build. (1) The parenthetical 'a literal \\r content pattern proved non-functional; a dedicated no_crlf/line_ending kind would express the .gitattributes intent' — that kind NOW EXISTS: `line_endings` (target: lf, with a normalize fixer), confirmed in crates/alint-rules/src/line_endings.rs (Lf/Crlf TargetName, first_mismatched_line). The .gitattributes eol=lf intent is now natively REPLACEABLE (and the sibling airflow config already uses it). (2) packageManager 'pnpm@11.5.0' vs engines.pnpm '11.5.0' 'wants a strip-prefix normalize, left as future tuning' — PHANTOM: `cross_file` equals with `normalize: semver-minor` strips the non-digit prefix and yields '11.5' on both sides (the code's own doc says 'pnpm@11 -> 11'; token_digits trims a non-digit prefix). So same-file two-JSONPath version coherence with a tool-prefix is expressible today. Tried the idioms; both fall through to existing kinds.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `google/tslint-state@v1` ruleset (file_header 'Copyright Google LLC' + eofline with the canonical Angular/Google monorepo exclude set) would let any Google-style TS repo inherit the header/eofline state arms in one line. Separately, a `frontmatter_schema` kind (validate SKILL.md / markdown YAML frontmatter against a schema) would convert the `ng-dev ai skills validate` and parts of ngbot/pullapprove verification from D into declarative coverage — a recurring monorepo need (frontmatter-driven config files). Grounded: Angular has three distinct bespoke YAML-frontmatter validators alint can only assert existence of today.

*Notes.* The header-scope curation is the standout value: matching tslint's enforced include/exclude turns a naive 1290x over-fire into a clean rule. Both deferred 'tuning gaps' have been closed by the v0.12 build (line_endings + semver-minor normalize); the config is stale, not the engine.

### `ansible/ansible (ansible-core)` — Large Python automation framework (ansible-core), CI on Azure Pipelines, bespoke `ansible-test sanity` harness

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the repo's ADDRESSABLE (declarative file-STATE) sanity surface — trailing-newline, no-s3, no-NBSP, fragment-format, obsolete-dirs, future-annotations(common-case), licensing. The dominant mass (pylint/mypy/validate-modules/pep8/black/codespell/yamllint/shellcheck/import/compile) is correctly category-D execution.

**Enforcement surface.** Ansible's gating surface is `ansible-test sanity` (Azure Pipelines, NOT GitHub Actions) — a large battery of test/sanity/code-smell/*.py validators plus tool runners. By category: tooling/AST/NLP (the bulk = D) — pylint, mypy, validate-modules, pep8, black, codespell, yamllint, shellcheck, ansible-doc, compile/import sanity, rstcheck; declarative file-STATE smells (the A subset) — trailing-newline.py (every text file ends with \n), no-s3.py (forbid direct S3 bucket links, prefer CloudFront), no-unwanted-characters.py (no U+00A0 no-break space), boilerplate.py (every lib/ansible *.py declares `from __future__ import annotations`, modulo AST-only edge cases), obsolete-files.py (a set of obsolete dirs must contain no files), changelog.py partial (changelog fragments must be .yml/.yaml; the antsibull-changelog SCHEMA validation itself is D); licensing/config policy (value-add, ungated-as-gate) — GPLv3 COPYING at root, pyproject [project.license] == GPL-3.0-or-later, .azure-pipelines entrypoint + test/sanity/ignore.txt manifest exist. The config re-expresses the declarative code-smell validators natively and records the tool/AST mass as D.

**What alint replaces.** trailing-newline.py -> final_newline (text-file globs); no-s3.py -> file_content_forbidden (pattern 'http.*?s3\\..*?amazonaws\\.com' over lib/test/docs/changelogs); no-unwanted-characters.py (U+00A0) -> file_content_forbidden on the literal NBSP (the PRECISE choice — see gaps); boilerplate.py future-annotations -> file_content_matches 'from __future__ import annotations' over lib/ansible/**/*.py; obsolete-files.py -> dir_absent per obsolete prefix (test/runner + the 10 obsolete test/sanity/* dirs); changelog.py fragment-format -> dir_only_contains select:'changelogs/fragments/*' allow:[*.yml,*.yaml,.keep,.gitkeep]; COPYING -> file_exists root_only; pyproject license -> toml_path_equals $.project.license; CI/ignore manifests -> file_exists. Plus the oss-baseline + python bundles.

**What alint improves.** Consolidates ~6 separate test/sanity/code-smell/*.py scripts (each its own Python file with its own walk + ignore logic) into ONE declarative, schema-validated .alint.yml that runs without the ansible-test harness or a configured Python sanity venv — far cheaper to run in a pre-commit or a lightweight PR gate, and reviewable as policy rather than imperative code. The obsolete-dir list and the forbidden-pattern set become a single auditable block instead of scattered scripts.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE. The most interesting candidate — the U+00A0 no-break-space ban — was checked against alternatives and the config's choice is CORRECT: `no_zero_width_chars` covers only U+200B/C/D + U+FEFF (not U+00A0), and `file_is_ascii` would over-fire on every legitimate non-ASCII codepoint (accented names, em-dashes) in docs/, whereas Ansible's check forbids ONLY the NBSP. So file_content_forbidden on the literal NBSP is the precise expression — fully REPLACEABLE. The boilerplate.py future-annotations check has a true AST-only residue (the validator skips files with no statements / understands real import positions), so file_content_matches is the common-case approximation the config correctly labels `warning` — but that residue is category-D (AST), a deliberate non-goal, NOT a gap. dir_absent vs 'dir must be empty' is a near-equivalence (an empty dir doesn't exist in git); dir_absent even has a fire-only-if-non-empty option if exactness is wanted. Tried the idioms; nothing addressable falls outside the current surface.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `python_module_boilerplate` / `module_preamble` kind (assert a required top-of-module statement like `from __future__ import annotations` with awareness of an allowed shebang/docstring/license-header preamble) would convert boilerplate.py from a D-residue approximation into precise coverage — a recurring Python-monorepo need (many projects mandate a `from __future__` or an encoding/license preamble). Grounded: this is the one A-item the config had to downgrade to warning. Otherwise the existing surface already covers Ansible cleanly.

*Notes.* Cleanest of the four configs — every A-item maps to a native kind with no stale gaps. The NBSP precision call (forbidden-pattern over file_is_ascii) is a model of the reproduce-first lens applied correctly at authoring time.

---

### `apache/airflow` — Large polyglot ASF monorepo (Python core + task-sdk + go/java SDKs + helm chart + 136 provider distributions); pre-commit/`prek`-driven static checks

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of the repo's ADDRESSABLE (non-AST) static-check surface today: all hygiene, the two for_each_dir structure invariants, all ~10 pygrep content-forbidden hooks, version coherence, shebangs, and the ASF governance/RAT/LICENSE bundles. The ~20% addressable residue is the sorted-to-EOF family (the one genuine ordered_block gap). The dominant enforcement mass (import firewalls, deprecation bans, schema/codegen freshness across ~130 prek scripts) is correctly category-D execution.

**Enforcement surface.** Airflow's bespoke validation is concentrated in ONE place: .pre-commit-config.yaml (~117 hooks) run via `prek` in the CI static-checks job, with implementations in scripts/ci/prek/*.py (~130 scripts) + a handful of pygrep regex hooks. By category: hygiene — trailing-whitespace, end-of-file-fixer, mixed-line-ending (+ .editorconfig); structure invariants — check-distribution-gitignore (every dir holding a pyproject.toml ships a .gitignore ignoring *.iml), check-providers-subpackages-all-have-init (provider source subpackages carry __init__.py, excluding 3rd-party-licenses/adr/doc); sorted-blocks — the update-*-to-be-sorted family (spelling_wordlist.txt, prod_image_installed_providers.txt, INTHEWILD.md) enforcing WHOLE-FILE / start-to-EOF sortedness (optional comment header then sorted to EOF, no end marker); content-forbidden — ~10 pygrep regex hooks (check-pydevd-left-in-code, check-incorrect-use-of-LoggingMixin, safe-filter-in-html, start-date-in-default-args, python-no-log-warn, etc.); cross-file version coherence — check-version-consistency (airflow-core/pyproject.toml [project.version] == __version__ in airflow-core/src/airflow/__init__.py); executable-shebangs — check-executables-have-shebangs; import-firewalls + deprecation bans + schema/codegen freshness — the overwhelming majority of prek scripts are AST/semantic Python validators = D. ASF governance — LICENSE/NOTICE/KEYS/RAT source headers via the compliance/apache-2 + apache/governance bundles. The config expresses the addressable subset (hygiene, structure, content-forbidden, version coherence, shebangs, governance) and records the AST/codegen mass as D.

**What alint replaces.** trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace; end-of-file-fixer -> final_newline; mixed-line-ending -> line_endings target:lf; check-distribution-gitignore -> for_each_dir select:'providers/*' when_iter:'iter.has_file("pyproject.toml")' require file_exists {path}/.gitignore; check-providers-subpackages-all-have-init -> for_each_dir select:'providers/*/src/airflow/providers/*' when_iter:'not (iter.basename matches "^(3rd-party-licenses|adr|doc)$")' require file_exists {path}/__init__.py; the 10 pygrep hooks -> file_content_forbidden (pydevd.settrace, LoggingMixin(), |safe in *.html, start_date in default_args, (log|logger|self.log).warn(); check-version-consistency -> cross_file_value_equals (toml $.project.version vs regex __version__); check-executables-have-shebangs -> executable_has_shebang. Plus oss-baseline + python + compliance/apache-2 + apache/governance + hygiene/lockfiles + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts bundles (RAT headers, LICENSE/NOTICE/KEYS, uv.lock tracked).

**What alint improves.** Collapses ~15 distinct scripts/ci/prek/*.py validators (each a hand-rolled Python walk: gitignore check, init-check, version-consistency, plus the pygrep hooks) into ONE declarative, schema-validated config — runnable without the prek venv or the full pre-commit toolchain, and far more reviewable (the provider-subpackage exclude set and the version-coherence pair become two readable YAML blocks vs imperative scripts). The for_each_dir + when_iter idiom expresses the 'every distribution dir' iteration that today requires bespoke glob+filter Python. The ASF governance/RAT/LICENSE surface comes free from bundles that 136 provider distributions would otherwise each need re-checked.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap: the update-*-to-be-sorted family (spelling_wordlist.txt, prod_image_installed_providers.txt, INTHEWILD.md) enforces WHOLE-FILE / start-to-EOF line sortedness with NO end marker (sorted region runs to EOF after an optional comment header). Tried `ordered_block` — it REQUIRES both `start:` and `end:` (confirmed in ordered_block.rs: both are non-Option String, validated non-empty and must differ; no_markers_is_silent test confirms a missing marker is a silent no-op), so it cannot bracket a region that has no end marker. The `select:` option only filters sortable lines WITHIN a bracketed block; it does not remove the marker requirement. No other primitive expresses within-file line sortedness: unique_by is cross-file dedup by key (not ordering); cross_file identical needs a pre-sorted reference file that doesn't exist; command_idempotent (run `sort -c` / the repo's own keep-sorted) works but is the EXECUTION escape hatch (category D), not a native declarative assertion. So a markerless / start-to-EOF (or whole-file) mode on ordered_block is a real, narrowly-scoped tuning gap. Everything else reproduces cleanly: the two for_each_dir structure rules, all content-forbidden hooks, version coherence, and shebangs are REPLACEABLE.

**Abstraction ideas.** Add a markerless mode to ordered_block: `start: <EOF-less>` is awkward, so a `region: whole_file` (optionally `skip_leading: <regex>` to allow a comment header) OR an `end: EOF` sentinel that means 'sorted from start/marker to end of file'. This directly serves Airflow's update-*-to-be-sorted family AND the broad ecosystem of CODEOWNERS/dictionary/allowlist files that are sorted-to-EOF (a recurring real need surfaced repeatedly in monorepos). Optionally a thin `sorted_file` kind (paths + comparator + unique + skip-header) as sugar over the same engine. Grounded in a concrete, currently-unexpressible Airflow check.

*Notes.* Richest config of the four and the only one with a surviving genuine gap. The ordered_block markerless/whole-file mode is the single highest-value abstraction across this group — small, well-scoped, and broadly applicable beyond Airflow.

---

### `apache/arrow` — C++ + multi-language (c_glib/python/r/ruby/matlab + format/ spec) data/ML monorepo; CMake/Meson/vcpkg; ASF TLP

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The enforced-A denominator is essentially one item (RAT header content) which alint replaces natively; the large value-add surface (version cluster, .asf.yaml, gem/sublib layout, format/ spec) is also fully expressible. Remaining ~5% is RAT's notice/SPDX aggregation nuance, not a continuous gate.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforcement hub is .pre-commit-config.yaml driven by .github/workflows/dev.yml (`pre-commit run --all-files`), plus the Apache RAT license audit and `archery docker/crossbow check-config`. By category: LICENSE-HEADERS — RAT (the `rat` pre-commit hook + dev/release/run-rat.sh, which downloads apache-rat.jar) asserts the ASF Apache-2 preamble on every source file across the polyglot tree (.py/.pyx/.c/.cc/.h/.rb/.R/.sh/.fbs/.proto/.vala/.cmake), with exemptions in dev/release/rat_exclude_files.txt; this is the single most important alint-shaped check and is the one enforced 'A'. STRUCTURE/LAYOUT (alint value-add, NOT repo-gated) — format/ holds the cross-language Arrow spec (Schema/Message/File/Tensor/SparseTensor .fbs + Flight/FlightSql .proto); the 8 Ruby gems under ruby/red-* share a uniform LICENSE.txt+NOTICE.txt+README.md+lib/+test/ layout walked by dev/release/post-06-ruby.sh; the 7 C-GLib sub-libraries each carry a meson.build subdir()'d from c_glib/meson.build. CROSS-FILE COHERENCE — the C++/matlab/c_glib/ruby version cluster (ARROW_VERSION / MLARROW_VERSION / PYARROW_VERSION / vcpkg version-string / ruby version.rb) is rewritten in lockstep by dev/release/utils-prepare.sh::update_versions (python PEP440 a-form and R .9000 dev-form intentionally diverge). METADATA — .asf.yaml infra config (github.homepage, notifications.commits=commits@arrow.apache.org, branch protection); python/pyproject.toml name=pyarrow + license=Apache-2.0; r/DESCRIPTION Package: arrow. HYGIENE — cpp/build out-of-tree build dir must stay untracked. EXECUTION-ONLY (deliberate non-goals) — nearly the entire pre-commit suite: clang-format, cpplint, autopep8, flake8, cython-lint, numpydoc, lintr, air, rubocop, cmake-format, sphinx-lint, shellcheck, shfmt, meson-fmt, hadolint; plus archery's config self-consistency tool.

**What alint replaces.** RAT source-header content layer -> file_header (apache-2-source-has-license-header with the polyglot include + RAT exclude tree mirrored); RAT-runner/exclude-manifest existence -> file_exists (run-rat.sh + rat_exclude_files.txt); `rat` hook registration in .pre-commit-config.yaml -> file_content_matches (anchored `- id: rat`); format/ spec files present -> file_exists (7 paths); per-Ruby-gem meta layout -> for_each_file select:ruby/red-*/Rakefile + nested file_exists {dir}/... + dir_exists; C-GLib meson.build -> for_each_dir select:c_glib/*-glib + nested file_exists {path}/meson.build; the C++/matlab/c_glib/ruby version cluster -> cross_file (cross_file_value_equals) with per-file regex extract (capture group 1, verified); .asf.yaml presence + homepage + commits-list -> file_exists + yaml_path_matches; pyproject name/license -> toml_path_matches; r/DESCRIPTION Package -> file_content_matches; untracked cpp/build -> dir_absent; LICENSE/NOTICE/KEYS/no-binaries -> bundled apache/governance + compliance/apache-2.

**What alint improves.** Consolidates the RAT *content* policy + the bespoke version-lockstep convention + the .asf.yaml infra contract + per-gem/per-sublib layout into ONE declarative reviewable file that runs on a fresh clone WITHOUT the JVM, apache-rat.jar download, pre-commit, archery, or any language toolchain — Arrow's own RAT only fails at release-vote time (run-rat.sh is not even in the normal pre-commit fast path on all branches), whereas alint gates the header content continuously. The version-cluster cross_file rule expresses a coherence invariant that exists today only as imperative sed in utils-prepare.sh (no standalone checker) — alint makes the drift detectable BETWEEN releases. The for_each_file/for_each_dir gem+sublib structural rules have no current enforcement at all (convention only). schema-validated config + clear per-file messages beat a 300-line groovy/shell audit.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The repo's addressable surface is fully expressible. Everything alint cannot do here (clang-format/cpplint/flake8/cython-lint/numpydoc/lintr/rubocop/cmake-format/sphinx-lint/shellcheck/shfmt/hadolint AST+format linting, archery's bespoke docker/crossbow config-graph self-consistency which is a Python program computing a build matrix) is execution/AST = correct NON-GOAL, orchestrable via command if desired. The RAT header *content* (not RAT's full SPDX/notice-aggregation machinery) is the only license bit in scope and file_header covers it.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `apache/polyglot-headers@v1` preset that ships the canonical ASF preamble file_header pattern pre-wired with the standard vendored/generated exclude globs (cpp/src/arrow/vendored, **/generated, thirdparty, submodules) so each ASF polyglot repo extends one line instead of re-listing ~12 exemptions — Arrow, Spark, Kafka all hand-roll the same exclude tree. (2) A first-class 'version cluster' sugar over cross_file: `version_cluster: {source: ..., members: [...]}` that takes a heterogeneous list of (file, extractor) pairs and asserts all-equal with one var — every ASF/Cargo/npm monorepo re-expresses this as a source+targets cross_file. (3) An `.asf.yaml`-aware bundled ruleset (homepage/notifications/branch-protection shape) since every ASF TLP carries the identical infra contract.

---

### `apache/kafka` — JVM Java/Scala monorepo; Gradle build; ASF TLP

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The two real bespoke items (checkJsonLicenseHeader, requests-coherence) are both natively replaced; the value-add (untracked-generated, firewall-config-exists) too. The unaddressable remainder is the import-control AST firewall *edges* and the PR-object linter, both non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** Kafka's enforced surface is overwhelmingly TOOL EXECUTION via `./gradlew check`: checkstyle (including 17 import-control*.xml package-layering firewalls = ~478 allow/disallow edges), spotbugs, spotless (import-order + format), Apache RAT (per-file ASF header), OWASP dependency-check (CVE), the JUnit suite on a JDK 17+25 matrix, scalafmt, and the protocol message code-generator (`processMessages`, which writes src/generated from *.json schemas). Docker workflows add trivy CVE scans, and a PR-linter (pr-format.py) gates PR title/body (a GitHub-object convention outside file scope). The genuinely alint-shaped, addressable surface is small but real: (A1) checkJsonLicenseHeader — a BESPOKE groovy assertion at build.gradle:917-938 (NOT a tool) that reads gradle/LICENSE.json and requires every JSON under src/{main,test}/resources/** (the 282 message-definition schemas + others) to begin with the `//`-prefixed ASF header; RAT/checkstyle never inspect .json. (A2) a documented cross-file convention: the 'requests' version in tests/setup.py must match .github/scripts/requirements.txt (both files carry an explicit 'keep in sync' comment). VALUE-ADD (not repo-gated): generated source trees (**/src/generated, src/generated-test, jmh-benchmarks/generated) must stay untracked (.gitignore'd, reproduced by processMessages); the root checkstyle/import-control.xml firewall config must exist.

**What alint replaces.** checkJsonLicenseHeader (the bespoke groovy JSON-header assertion) -> file_header (kafka-json-resources-asf-header, anchored on 'Licensed to the Apache Software Foundation (ASF)'); the documented requests-version cross-file convention -> cross_file (cross_file_value_equals) with regex extract on setup.py + requirements.txt (expected to FIRE at this pin, demonstrating real drift requests>=2.32.4 vs ==2.33.0); generated-source-trees-untracked -> file_absent git_tracked_only; import-control.xml firewall config present -> file_exists; LICENSE/NOTICE/headers/no-binaries/Gradle-wrapper-committed/no-build-artifacts -> bundled apache/governance + compliance/apache-2 + java.

**What alint improves.** Replaces a 22-line embedded groovy loop (checkJsonLicenseHeader) that only runs inside a full Gradle invocation with one declarative file_header rule runnable on a bare clone — and crucially the SCOPE is now expressible faithfully: I reproduced on the current globset 0.4.18 that `**/src/{main,test}/resources/**/*.json` DOES match the deep resource paths (e.g. core/src/test/resources/Bar.json => true), so the exact checkJsonLicenseHeader glob can be transcribed directly rather than via the include-all/exclude-non-resource workaround the original config used. The requests cross_file rule turns a hand-written code comment ('Ensure the requests version here matches ...') into an actual enforced gate — today nothing checks it (and it is in fact drifted). file_absent over the generated trees makes the .gitignore intent a real assertion (stale-codegen detection).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable. The 17 import-control*.xml package-layering firewalls are checkstyle's Java-AST job (resolving fully-qualified import edges against allow/disallow subpackage rules) = NON-GOAL D; alint's import_gate is a per-file regex firewall on extracted import targets with allow globs and a java preset, which can approximate simple 'package X must not import package Y' edges but cannot reproduce checkstyle ImportControl's nested subpackage/regex allow-disallow precedence semantics — correctly out of scope, and alint already asserts the firewall config's existence as a proxy. spotbugs/scalafmt/spotless/JUnit/OWASP/trivy/processMessages = execution D. pr-format.py gates a GitHub PR object (title/body), not repository files — genuinely outside alint's file-scope, but that is a non-goal by design, not a should-have gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) RETIRE the 'mid-path ** + typed tail matches zero files' note from the corpus/study notes — I empirically disproved it on globset 0.4.18 (both `**/resources/**/*.json` and `**/src/{main,test}/resources/**/*.json` match the deep paths); the documented workaround is now unnecessary cruft and any other config that cites this 'limitation' is stale. (2) A `value_set_membership`/enum kind (already on the v0.12 backlog) would let Kafka assert a JSON message field's `type` is one of a fixed vocabulary without a regex. (3) An `import_control`-flavored richer import_gate option (nested subpackage allow/disallow with precedence) would close the last bit of the checkstyle-firewall gap, but only if scoped to its regex/path layer (full AST resolution stays a non-goal).

---

### `apache/spark` — Scala/Java + Python/R big-data monorepo; Maven/SBT; ASF TLP

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of addressable (non-execution) validation — the entire enforced-A slice here (R header + 8 scalastyle regex bans) plus the config-presence value-add is natively replaceable. The vast D remainder (scalafmt/scalastyle-AST/checkstyle/MiMa/mypy/black/ruff/eslint/lintr/sphinx/dep-resolver) is execution by design.

**Enforcement surface.** Spark's enforced surface is dominated by tool execution (deliberate non-goals D): scalafmt + scalastyle, checkstyle (Java), Apache RAT (license headers, dev/.rat-excludes), MiMa (binary compat), the Python lint stack (mypy/ruff/flake8/black), eslint (JS), lintr (R), sphinx (docs), and a committed Maven dependency manifest whose freshness is gated by dev/test-dependencies.sh. The genuinely alint-shaped surface: (1) the Apache license header on R sources — the compliance/apache-2 bundle's header include covers .scala/.java/.py/.js but NOT .R, while RAT enforces .R too, so alint adds the R arm. (2) Scalastyle's RegexChecker rules are pure file-content forbidden-pattern policies — Class.forName, Await.result, Await.ready, JavaConversions, new ParVector, throw new *Error(, Files.createParentDirs, Files.equal — each maps 1:1 to file_content_forbidden over **/*.scala. VALUE-ADD / config-policy (not gated, excluded from the ratio): scalastyle-config.xml present (root), dev/.rat-excludes present, dev/deps/spark-deps-hadoop-3-hive-2.3 committed dep manifest present.

**What alint replaces.** R-source ASF header (the .R arm RAT covers but the apache-2 header bundle omits) -> file_header (spark-r-apache-header over **/*.R + **/*.r); the 8 scalastyle RegexChecker bans -> 8x file_content_forbidden over **/*.scala (classforname/awaitresult/awaitready/javaconversions/parvector/throwerror/createParentDirs/filesequal); scalastyle-config.xml + dev/.rat-excludes + committed dep manifest existence -> file_exists; LICENSE-text + NOTICE + canonical ASF header on .scala/.java/.py/.js -> bundled compliance/apache-2 + oss-baseline.

**What alint improves.** The 8 scalastyle RegexCheckers are literally regex-over-source-text policies — alint expresses each as a self-documenting file_content_forbidden with a remediation message ('Use Utils.classForName instead of Class.forName'), and runs them WITHOUT booting the Scala toolchain / scalastyle SBT plugin, so they fire on a bare clone and in pre-commit far faster. Crucially alint can ALSO enforce them on the Python/R/JS/build trees if desired (scalastyle only sees .scala). The .R header arm closes a real coverage seam (the bundled header omits .R; RAT does not) — a cross-file-coherence-style completeness alint catches that the bundle alone misses. Consolidates a scalastyle-config.xml subset + RAT .R coverage into one reviewable declarative file.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable. scalastyle has structural/AST checkers (ColumnNumber, line-length-per-AST, NonASCIICharacterChecker, spaces-before-token, NoFinalizeChecker, etc.) and Spark's broader stack (checkstyle AST, MiMa binary-compat byte analysis, mypy/black/ruff/eslint/lintr/sphinx, the dev/test-dependencies.sh Maven resolver diff) — all execution/AST = correct NON-GOALs. The committed dependency-manifest FRESHNESS (re-resolve Maven deps and diff) is not alint's to compute, but note: if Spark exposed a --check mode for that script, command_idempotent could gate it, and the manifest-content drift (if it were a hash-embedded artifact) could be file_graph fresh — neither is naturally shaped that way here, so it stays a non-goal rather than a gap. The 8 RegexChecker rules are content-regex policies fully covered today; no scalastyle *regex* rule is unexpressible.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `scalastyle/regex-bans@v1` (or broader `jvm-content-bans`) ruleset shipping the common ASF-Scala forbidden-API set (Class.forName/Await.result/Await.ready/JavaConversions/ParVector/throw-Error/Files.equal) as file_content_forbidden rules with canonical messages — Spark, Flink, and many Scala shops re-encode the same eight; one `extends:` line replaces eight hand-written rules. (2) A `forbidden_apis` sugar (a list of {pattern, message, suggest} over a shared `paths:`) to collapse N parallel file_content_forbidden rules into one block — this exact 8-rule fan-out is the motivating case. (3) Per-language header coverage completeness: a header bundle option that takes a language list so adding .R is a one-liner rather than a separate rule.

### `astral-sh/ruff` — Rust workspace (linter/formatter + ty) with a Python-packaged binary; maturin/uv; MIT

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The three bespoke A items (codegen freshness, version coherence, edition coherence) plus the structural value-add (publish=false, PEP-621, CODEOWNERS) are all natively replaceable; the codegen-freshness piece is arguably IMPROVED over ruff's own git-porcelain check. The large D remainder (cargo/ruff/ty/mkdocs/typos/zizmor/actionlint/ecosystem) is execution by design.

**Enforcement surface.** Ruff's enforced surface is heavily tool/AST/test/build execution (D): cargo fmt/clippy (with a disallowed-methods firewall via clippy.toml)/test/build-msrv/shear/fuzz, shellcheck, typos (NLP), validate-pyproject, zizmor, actionlint, check-github-workflows, prettier, mdformat, markdownlint, ruff's own check/format dogfood, mkdocs build --strict, check_docs_formatted.py, formatter-instability + black-similarity, ecosystem regression, ty-completion-eval, benchmarks, python wheel build/test, and [tool.ruff.lint] required-imports (ruff enforces it). The genuinely alint-shaped surface: (A1) CODEGEN FRESHNESS — ruff's single biggest bespoke gate: the `scripts` CI job runs the generators (`cargo dev generate-all`) then `test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"`, and AGENTS.md mandates regeneration after option/CLI/rule changes; the checked-in generated artefacts (per .gitattributes linguist-generated) are ruff.schema.json, ty.schema.json, crates/*/src/generated.rs, docs/configuration.md, docs/editors/settings.md, CLI references. (A2) VERSION-FILES COHERENCE — version (e.g. 0.15.x) is NOT workspace-inherited; [tool.rooster] version_files in pyproject.toml is the hand-sync list (README install URL, pyproject, crates/ruff + crates/ruff_linter Cargo.toml, several docs). (A3) EDITION COHERENCE — Cargo.toml carries a load-bearing comment requiring rustfmt.toml's edition+style_edition to track the workspace [package] edition. VALUE-ADD (not gated): internal crates publish=false except the 3 published manifests; pyproject PEP-621 fields (name=ruff/license=MIT/requires-python); .github/CODEOWNERS present; the D-tool orchestration shellouts. ABSENT (correctly NOT enforced): no per-file license/SPDX headers (root MIT LICENSE + per-crate Cargo.toml license only) so the apache-header bundle must NOT fire; MSRV is workspace-inherited so no cross-crate MSRV coherence pair; rust-toolchain channel deliberately != MSRV; Cargo.lock IS tracked (it's an app/binary).

**What alint replaces.** codegen freshness (generate-all then git-clean) -> command_idempotent (ruff-codegen-fresh, cargo dev generate-all --mode check, timeout 600); the two highest-stakes JSON schemas with dedicated generators -> generated_file_fresh stdout-mode (ruff.schema.json via generate-json-schema, ty.schema.json via generate-ty-schema), so a failure names the exact file; version_files coherence -> cross_file (cross_file_value_equals) source=pyproject $.project.version, targets=crates/ruff + crates/ruff_linter Cargo.toml [package].version + README install-URL regex; edition coherence -> cross_file source=Cargo.toml [workspace.package].edition, targets=rustfmt.toml edition + style_edition; internal-crates publish=false -> toml_path_equals with crates/*/Cargo.toml include + 3-manifest exclude; pyproject PEP-621 fields -> toml_path_equals/toml_path_matches (note requires-python dashed key uses bracket notation $.project['requires-python']); CODEOWNERS -> file_exists; the rustfmt/clippy/shear/typos/zizmor/actionlint/self-lint orchestration -> command + command_idempotent (files_from stdout/stderr + files_pattern), all explicitly NON-counted as A.

**What alint improves.** The codegen-freshness gate is alint's OWN primitive, not a wrapper: instead of CI's opaque `test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"` (which fails with a useless 'tree dirty' and conflates schema drift, doc drift, and generated.rs drift), generated_file_fresh runs the per-artefact generator and diffs ITS stdout to the exact committed file, naming ruff.schema.json vs ty.schema.json independently — sharper signal, and it snapshots/restores so the tree stays pure. The version_files + edition cross_file rules turn two hand-maintained 'keep in sync' comments (rooster's version_files list; Cargo.toml's 'update rustfmt.toml when bumping edition' comment) into enforced gates that today rely on a release tool (rooster) or reviewer vigilance — and they run without rooster. Consolidates ~3 bespoke CI concerns into one schema-validated reviewable file; the dashed-key bracket-notation idiom ($.project['requires-python']) is exactly the JSONPath gotcha alint already documents.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable. clippy's disallowed-methods firewall (clippy.toml) is AST-level method-resolution = NON-GOAL D (import_gate covers imports, not method-call sites); cargo fmt/clippy/test/shear/fuzz/msrv-build, ruff/ty self-dogfood, typos NLP, mkdocs --strict, formatter-instability/black-similarity, ecosystem regression, ty-completion-eval, wheel build/test = execution D, correctly orchestrated via command. One subtle point worth stating: [tool.ruff.lint] required-imports (every Python file must contain `from __future__ import annotations`) is enforced by ruff itself, but it IS alint-expressible too (file_content_matches/file_header (?m)^from __future__ import annotations over **/*.py) — so even that is not a gap, it's a deliberate hand-off to the dogfooded tool. No ruff validation needs a primitive alint lacks.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `generate_all` / multi-artefact freshness sugar: ruff has ONE umbrella generator (`cargo dev generate-all`) feeding many artefacts but ALSO per-artefact sub-generators; a kind that takes {umbrella_command, artefacts:[{file, command}]} and reports per-file would beat today's split between one command_idempotent + N generated_file_fresh rules. (2) A first-class `version_files` kind mirroring rooster/cargo-release semantics: one source-of-truth version + a heterogeneous target list (toml path | regex-in-text | README URL) with a single canonical message — ruff, and every cargo-release/rooster/changesets repo, re-expresses this as cross_file. (3) A bundled `astral/rust-tooling@v1` (or generic `rust-app@v1`) preset bundling tracked-Cargo.lock-for-apps + edition<->rustfmt coherence + internal-crates-publish=false, since this exact trio recurs across Rust binary workspaces.

### `astral-sh/uv` — Rust+Python hybrid monorepo (cargo workspace + maturin/pyproject wheel front-end); Astral's package/project manager

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — the one bespoke structural gate + all config/structure/license assertions are native; only execution checks (the vast majority of uv's CI) are out of scope by design

**Enforcement surface.** uv's enforced gate surface is overwhelmingly tool EXECUTION (category D, correctly out of alint scope), run from a heavy CI matrix + pre-commit: (license-headers) — none enforced per-file; uv relies on the two root LICENSE-{APACHE,MIT} files only. (hygiene/format) rustfmt, ruff format+lint, ty type-check, typos (_typos.toml), shellcheck, prettier/markdownlint via pre-commit, .editorconfig. (security/deps) cargo-deny bans+licenses+advisories, cargo-shear (unused deps), zizmor (workflow security), the clippy.toml `disallowed-methods` std::fs firewall (forces uv's own fs wrappers). (structure/codegen-freshness) `cargo dev generate-all --mode check` regenerates uv.schema.json + CLI reference + docs/settings and `git diff`s them; `uv lock --check`; `validate-pyproject`; `cargo publish --dry-run`; `dist plan` (cargo-dist). (cross-file coherence) the ONE bespoke structural gate: scripts/check-trampoline-version-consistency.py asserts the `windows` crate is the SAME locked version in the workspace Cargo.lock and the out-of-workspace crates/uv-trampoline/Cargo.lock (test-windows-trampolines.yml). (commit-discipline) none repo-bespoke beyond PR template. (runtime) the unit/system/ecosystem/workspaces test suites. Almost everything is D; the trampoline cross-lockfile check and a handful of config/structure assertions are the addressable slice.

**What alint replaces.** scripts/check-trampoline-version-consistency.py -> cross_file_value_equals (relation:equals, toml extract `$.package[?(@.name=='windows')].version` on both Cargo.lock files) — exactly the uv-windows-crate-version-consistent rule, the single natively-enforced gate replaced; root dual-license presence -> file_exists x2 (LICENSE-APACHE/LICENSE-MIT, root_only); workspace license+edition -> toml_path_equals on `$.workspace.package.license`/`.edition`; rust-toolchain channel pin -> toml_path_matches; maturin backend -> toml_path_equals on `$['build-system']['build-backend']`; presence of clippy.toml/_typos.toml/.pre-commit-config.yaml/.editorconfig -> file_exists; uv.schema.json linguist-generated marker -> file_content_matches on .gitattributes. Bundled rust@v1 + python@v1 also natively cover Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock/rust-toolchain presence, pyproject name+requires-python (toml_path_matches), snake_case module names, and the source-tree hygiene battery (final_newline/no_trailing_whitespace/no_bidi/no_zero_width).

**What alint improves.** Consolidates the bespoke Python check-script into one declarative, schema-validated, toolchain-free cross_file rule that runs on any machine without a Python interpreter or the uv build env. MATERIAL UNDERCLAIM in the config: uv's `cargo dev generate-all --mode check` (schema/CLI-ref/docs freshness, diffed in CI) was filed entirely as D, but the schema/docs-regeneration freshness gate is precisely generated_file_fresh territory — STDOUT mode (`command: [cargo, dev, generate-schema]` + `file: uv.schema.json`) or mutating mode (`outputs:` + the generate-all command) reproduces `cargo dev generate-all --mode check` declaratively (it IS a spawn-tier rule, so still trust-gated, but it is alint-expressible, not a pure non-goal). The clippy.toml std::fs firewall is genuinely D (AST), but the *presence/shape* of clippy.toml is asserted natively.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The original config recorded no B items, and on the reproduce-first pass that holds: every addressable item is expressible. The std::fs disallowed-methods firewall, ruff/ty/rustfmt, cargo-deny/shear, zizmor, typos, and `uv lock --check` are all category-D execution (AST/semantic/solver), correctly NON-GOALS that alint orchestrates via command/command_idempotent rather than reimplements — not gaps.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `cross_file` convenience for the recurring 'same package version across N lockfiles in a workspace' pattern — uv, and many cargo/pnpm monorepos with out-of-workspace nested lockfiles, would benefit from a relation that takes a `package:` name + a glob of lockfiles and asserts a single coherent version without hand-writing the JSONPath filter twice. (2) A bundled `astral`/`maturin` mini-ruleset (LICENSE-{APACHE,MIT} dual-license + `build-backend: maturin` + rust-toolchain concrete-channel pin) — the Astral house style is now common enough (uv, ruff, ty) to bundle. (3) A first-class 'codegen-freshness via subcommand-with-check-mode' sugar that wraps `tool ... --mode check` so projects like uv don't have to choose between command_idempotent and generated_file_fresh.

*Notes.* Coverage_today is deliberately ~1 enforced native check (trampoline) because uv's gate surface is ~all D; the rich value-add (license/edition/toolchain/backend/config-presence) is structure uv does not itself gate. The generate-all freshness reclassification (D->spawn-tier-expressible) is the one place the config is conservative.

### `AvaloniaUI/Avalonia` — Large .NET/C# cross-platform UI framework; community-governed; Nuke (C#) build orchestrating dotnet restore/compile/test/pack across Linux/macOS/Windows; CI is Azure Pipelines, not GitHub Actions

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE surface — the one enforced convention + all the structural/config/version-registry assertions are native; only the compiled-assembly ApiCompat/Cecil/analyzer/test execution (the bulk of Avalonia's real gate) is out of scope by design

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced validation is almost entirely D (execution) via the Nuke C# build: (structure/build) `dotnet restore` driven by global.json (SDK pin + Microsoft.Build.Traversal SDK; dirs.proj is the Traversal entry-point) and Central Package Management (Directory.Packages.props, every PackageReference omits Version). (analyzers/format) Roslyn analyzers + TreatWarningsAsErrors in Release (AST, D). (test) unit/render/leak/tools suites + Appium/WinAppDriver integration + XamlCompilationVerifier (Mono.Cecil IL inspection, D) + a NativeAOT publish-and-run smoke. (api/compat) Nuke ValidateApiDiff runs Microsoft.DotNet.ApiCompat — a binary-shape diff of packed assemblies vs the previous-release NuGet baseline, with api/Avalonia.nupkg.xml as the suppression manifest (execution over compiled DLLs, D). (codegen) GenerateCppHeaders (avn.idl->avalonia-native.h) + GenerateUnicodeData — outputs are gitignored/regenerated with NO `git diff --exit-code` freshness gate, so nothing committed to assert. (naming/convention) the ONE documented review convention alint can enforce: CONTRIBUTING.md 'DO NOT USE #REGIONS full stop'. (license/structure) MIT licence shipped as British `licence.md` + NOTICE.md; Directory.Build.props/.targets MSBuild parents; NuGet.Config feed+packageSourceMapping; .gitmodules (external/XamlX + external/Avalonia.DBus). The GH workflows (api-diff, update-api with contents:write, CLA via pull_request_target) are bot/operational, not the build matrix.

**What alint replaces.** CONTRIBUTING.md 'no #regions' convention -> file_content_forbidden (pattern '#region', src/**/*.cs, with legacy-exception excludes) — the single enforced native check; global.json SDK pin -> json_path_matches `$.sdk.version` ^10\.0\.; Traversal MSBuild SDK pin -> json_path_matches `$['msbuild-sdks']['Microsoft.Build.Traversal']` (bracket notation for the dotted key); CPM enabled -> xml_path_equals `$.Project.PropertyGroup.ManagePackageVersionsCentrally`==true; SharedVersion.props presence+`$.Project.PropertyGroup.Version` shape -> file_exists + xml_path_matches; Directory.Build.props/.targets, NuGet.Config, .gitmodules, api/Avalonia.nupkg.xml -> file_exists; British licence.md + NOTICE.md -> file_exists (root_only). The bundled dotnet@v1 (fact-gated) natively supplies global.json existence+soft sdk pin, csproj Sdk-style/Nullable/CPM, bin-obj hygiene; oss-baseline supplies README/SECURITY/CoC/final-newline.

**What alint improves.** Consolidates a set of invariants Avalonia depends on but does NOT itself gate (SDK pin, Traversal pin, CPM-enabled, version-registry presence, submodule/feed config) into one reviewable file that fails fast WITHOUT a 10-minute `dotnet restore`. Upgrades the bundle's soft warnings to repo-appropriate errors (concrete net10 SDK, CPM must be true since a disabled CPM silently breaks every Version-less PackageReference). Cross-platform path-glob coherence the Nuke build can't express cheaply. The #region rule guards NEW code at PR time as a fast pre-build gate rather than relying on reviewers spotting it.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that the config marks as B (it records zero gaps), and the reproduce-first pass confirms the hard items are correctly D, not gaps: ValidateApiDiff (ApiCompat over compiled DLLs), XamlCompilationVerifier (Cecil IL), analyzers+TreatWarningsAsErrors, NativeAOT smoke are all execution/AST — DELIBERATE non-goals. The GenerateCppHeaders/UnicodeData codegen is NOT a freshness gap because the outputs are gitignored (nothing committed to diff); had Avalonia committed those + a `git diff --exit-code`, file_graph fresh / generated_file_fresh would cover it, but as-is there is nothing to assert. The config's own #region 'sharp-edge' note (whole-file vs per-line anchoring) is a config-authoring nuance, not an alint limitation — file_content_forbidden runs regex `find` over the whole file and reports the correct line; a plain literal or `(?m)` works.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `dotnet-cpm`/`dotnet-traversal` rev or options on dotnet@v1 to assert ManagePackageVersionsCentrally==true at ERROR and a Microsoft.Build.Traversal SDK pin — both are load-bearing for any CPM+Traversal repo (Avalonia, and increasingly the .NET ecosystem) and currently must be hand-written. (2) A locale-aware license rule (or an oss-baseline option) that matches `licence.{md,txt}` (British spelling) so the bundle's oss-license-exists stops false-firing on Commonwealth-English repos — Avalonia is the canonical case and the config notes the bundle FALSE-FIRES here. (3) An optional `region/folding-directive` forbidden preset (#region/#pragma region) since multiple C# houses ban it.

*Notes.* oss-baseline's license glob FALSE-FIRES on `licence.md` — fixed via a repo-level override in the config; this is a real bundle-tuning finding worth folding upstream. Coverage_today counts only the #region convention; everything else is value-add Avalonia does not gate.

---

### `axios/axios` — Mid-size single-language JS HTTP library (npm, community governance); gulp-built, rollup bundle

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE surface — all four cross-file/structure conventions (ignore-scripts, VERSION parity, export-path resolve, AGENTS pointer) PLUS the previously-claimed shared-section gap are native; only the build/test/lockfile-integrity/bundle-size execution matrix is out of scope by design

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced surface is dominated by tool EXECUTION (D): (format/lint) eslint, prettier. (test) vitest unit+browser, CJS/ESM/Bun/Deno smoke + module-resolution suites. (build) rollup + a build-reproducibility two-pass sha diff + bundle-size budget. (supply-chain) lockfile-lint walks every lockfile entry asserting https + integrity (a per-entry graph check, D); the load-bearing `.npmrc` `ignore-scripts=true` install-safety flag ('do not remove' per AGENTS.md/COLLABORATOR_GUIDE.md/copilot-instructions/THREATMODEL.md); zizmor on workflows. (commit-discipline) commitlint conventional-commits. (cross-file coherence) lib/env/data.js carries a gulp-`env`-generated `export const VERSION` that must equal package.json $.version; package.json exports/files/browser/react-native maps reference committed lib/*.js entry points (the ./unsafe/* subpaths); CLAUDE.md must stay the `@AGENTS.md` pointer stub; and the documented 'keep AGENTS.md + .github/copilot-instructions.md in sync' shared-safety-subset convention.

**What alint replaces.** `.npmrc ignore-scripts=true` must-keep flag -> file_content_matches (pattern (?m)^ignore-scripts=true$) — a human convention with NO tool gate today, now enforced; VERSION coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (json `$.version` on package.json == regex-extracted VERSION in lib/env/data.js) — replaces relying on remembering to re-run `gulp env`; package.json export/browser/react-native lib/*.js paths resolve -> registry_paths_resolve (source: package.json, regex extract of '"(\./lib/...\.js)"', base ., expect file); CLAUDE.md @AGENTS.md stub -> file_content_matches (?m)^@AGENTS\.md$. Bundled node@v1 natively adds package.json + lockfile presence, no tracked node_modules/dist, engines/.nvmrc, and the source hygiene battery; oss-baseline adds README/SECURITY/CoC.

**What alint improves.** Turns four hand-maintained, doc-only conventions (the ignore-scripts flag, VERSION parity, export-path integrity, the CLAUDE->AGENTS pointer) into machine-checked gates that run with no Node/gulp/rollup toolchain — catching a stale VERSION or a moved lib helper at review time rather than at publish. The export-path resolve check is cross-file coherence axios currently has NO gate for (a typo'd ./unsafe/* subpath ships a broken export silently). Lower false-positives + schema-validated vs an ad-hoc grep.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none — and the config's own headline 'Known GAP' (the AGENTS.md <-> copilot-instructions.md shared-safety-subset convention) DISSOLVES under the reproduce-first lens with the current capability brief, so it is NOT listed as a gap. The config claimed 'neither pair_hash nor cross_file_value_equals nor pair expresses cross-file shared-section equivalence', but `cross_file` with relation:subset (or set_equals) + extract:{regex:'<bullet-pattern>'} extracts the SET of shared safety bullets from AGENTS.md (regex mode = captures_iter, ALL group-1 matches -> a set) and asserts every one is present in copilot-instructions.md — exactly 'shared safety subset coherence', as long as the shared lines are regex-identifiable (they are: the `:surfer:` AI-marker rule + the enumerated safety bullets). relation:identical was the wrong idiom (whole-file byte identity, and the two files legitimately differ elsewhere) — but subset over a regex-extracted marker set is the right one and is fully supported. I tried identical (fails: files differ outside the shared block) and pair_hash (fails: no digest manifest) before landing on cross_file subset+regex, which works. The execution checks (lockfile-lint integrity graph, build-reproducibility sha diff, bundle-size, commitlint, vitest/browser/Deno/Bun suites) are category-D non-goals, correctly out of scope.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `shared_section`/`shared_block` cross_file convenience — a named-region (begin/end marker) variant of cross_file that extracts the lines between two sentinels in each file and applies a set/identical relation, so 'these two docs must agree on the SAFETY block' is one rule instead of a hand-tuned regex; this is the elegant generalization the axios convention demonstrates a real need for (multiple repos keep AGENTS.md/copilot-instructions/CLAUDE.md partially in sync). (2) A bundled `agent-context` extension assertion that CLAUDE.md/GEMINI.md/.github/copilot-instructions.md are pointer-stubs to a single canonical AGENTS.md (the @-include idiom) — axios is the textbook case. (3) An `npm-supply-chain` mini-ruleset bundling `.npmrc ignore-scripts=true` + lockfile presence + no-tracked-node_modules, since 'ignore-scripts must stay on' is a widespread, currently-ungated supply-chain convention.

*Notes.* This repo is the clearest 'phantom gap' in the group: the config's headline new-kind candidate (shared-section equivalence) is already expressible via cross_file subset+regex post-build. Worth flagging in the case-study log as another over-reported gap (joins file_set, root-file globs, occurrence:first, multi-capture-compose).

---

### `bazelbuild/bazel` — JVM/Java build-system monorepo that builds ITSELF with BUILD/.bzl; bzlmod + pinned Maven; no Maven/Gradle (has_java fact is FALSE)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of the ADDRESSABLE surface — headers, .gitattributes/.bazelrc policy assertions (with line_endings upgrading the CRLF check to real enforcement), lockfile/MODULE/maven presence are all native; the LINT.IfChange/ThenChange block-level co-change is the genuine residual gap; the build/test/formatter/one-version-analysis execution is out of scope by design

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced validation is mostly build/test EXECUTION (D) plus bespoke Bazel sh_tests and policy flags: (CI) .bazelci/{presubmit,postsubmit}.yml drive a Buildkite build+test matrix + `check_docs:1` + //scripts/docs:gen_release_docs (docs build, D). (GH workflows) automation-only (labeler, cherry-pick, stale, scorecard cron, ssl-monitor cron, docs-trigger) — none gate code. (bespoke validators, run under `bazel test`) src/test/shell/bazel/srcs_test.sh checks //:srcs filegroup completeness; src/test/tools/bzlmod/verify_default_lock_file.sh regenerates and diffs MODULE.bazel.lock (a codegen-freshness gate); .github/scripts/check_ssl.py is a cert-expiry network cron (D). (policy) .bazelrc sets `--experimental_one_version_enforcement=ERROR` (+ oneversion_allowlist.csv) and `--check_direct_dependencies=error`. (line-endings/structure) .gitattributes declares `* -text` (repo-wide CRLF ban) + wires the custom `MODULE.bazel.lock merge=bazel-lockfile-merge` driver (scripts/bazel-lockfile-merge.jq). (license-headers) per-file Apache-2.0 header on every source (the `g-bad-file-header` pylint pragma proves it's review-enforced; universal across 2768+ .java, scripts/*.py, src/test/shell/bazel/*.sh) — Google Java Style + buildifier (BUILD/.bzl) + pyink (.py) per AGENTS.md. (cross-file) LINT.IfChange/LINT.ThenChange — 24 paired-block coherence directives (Critique/Copybara convention; no OSS-CI tool enforces them). (structure) MODULE.bazel + MODULE.bazel.lock + maven_install.json + .bazelversion tracked.

**What alint replaces.** Per-file Apache-2.0 headers -> file_content_matches (src/main/java/**/*.java + scripts|tools/**/*.py, pattern 'Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2\.0') — replaces the g-bad-file-header pylint coverage for headers; `.gitattributes` declares `* -text` + lockfile merge driver -> file_content_matches x2; the jq merge-driver script presence -> file_exists; .bazelrc one-version + check-direct-deps policy flags -> file_content_matches x2; MODULE.bazel/MODULE.bazel.lock/maven_install.json/.bazelversion tracked -> file_exists; oss-baseline supplies README/LICENSE/SECURITY/CoC/no-merge-markers/final-newline.

**What alint improves.** MATERIAL UNDERCLAIM: the config asserts `.gitattributes` *declares* `* -text` via file_content_matches — i.e. it only checks the POLICY IS WRITTEN, not that it's UPHELD. alint's line_endings rule (target: lf, over the actual source globs) enforces LF endings on the real files directly, which is STRICTLY STRONGER than grepping the .gitattributes line and is closer to what `* -text` actually intends; this should be reclassified REPLACEABLE/IMPROVABLE, not just a config-policy proxy. The Apache-header rule is materially better as one declarative cross-language rule (Java+Python+shell in scope-globs) than the per-tool pylint pragma. file_graph fresh / generated_file_fresh can express verify_default_lock_file.sh's regen-and-diff of MODULE.bazel.lock (codegen freshness, a spawn-tier rule — NOT pure D as 'the rest is execution' implies). srcs_test.sh (//:srcs filegroup completeness) is partially registry_paths_resolve-shaped (a BUILD enumerates srcs; each must exist) though the reverse-completeness against generated filegroups is the hard part.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** LINT.IfChange / LINT.ThenChange paired-block coherence is the one credible candidate — 'when the block above THIS LINT.IfChange marker changes, the file/block named in the matching LINT.ThenChange must also change in the same commit'. I tried: (a) pair_changed_together (if path A changed in since..HEAD then path B must too) — falls short because IfChange/ThenChange operate at intra-file BLOCK granularity and target arbitrary named blocks, not whole files, and the directive lives INLINE; (b) changeset_requires_path — only asserts a diff ADDS a path, not conditional co-change of a referenced block; (c) cross_file relations — these compare VALUES/sets/identity, not 'both regions were edited in the same changeset'. So block-level conditional co-change keyed by inline IfChange/ThenChange sentinels is genuinely inexpressible today (the closest is pair_changed_together at file granularity, which both over- and under-fires). NOTE this is an EXTENSION of the planned pair_changed_together, not a wholly new axis. Everything else is correctly D: buildifier/pyink/Google-Java-Style formatters, the one-version/direct-deps ENFORCEMENT (a Bazel-runtime semantic check — alint asserts the flag is wired, Bazel does the analysis), gen_release_docs, the build+test matrix, check_ssl.py network probe.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `paired_blocks` / `if_changed_block` rule (the IfChange/ThenChange generalization): scan files for begin/end sentinels (LINT.IfChange .. LINT.ThenChange(<targets>)), and over the since..HEAD diff assert that if the guarded block changed, every named target block also changed — a block-granular sibling to pair_changed_together. Bazel is the canonical driver but the Copybara/Critique convention is used across many Google-origin OSS repos. (2) A `line_endings`-as-policy-vs-.gitattributes bundled monorepo option, or docs guidance, steering authors away from the weaker 'grep .gitattributes for `* -text`' proxy toward enforcing endings directly. (3) A `filegroup_complete` / build-srcs-enumeration rule generalizing srcs_test.sh — assert a BUILD srcs/glob enumerates exactly the on-disk files in a tree (registry_paths_resolve forward + orphans reverse, but parsing Starlark srcs lists is the missing piece). (4) A bundled `bazel`/`bzlmod` ruleset (MODULE.bazel + MODULE.bazel.lock + maven_install.json presence, .bazelversion, the `* -text` LF enforcement, .bazelrc policy-flag assertions) — has_bazel-fact-gated, since none of the existing ecosystem bundles fit a repo that builds itself.

*Notes.* .bazelversion is tracked-but-gitignored (Bazelisk convention) -> alint honors .gitignore by default so that rule needs --no-gitignore; recorded as a real sharp-edge (tracked-but-gitignored file), not a repo defect. The eight file_content_matches rules all PASS so they're omitted from the violations JSON (alint lists a per-file content rule only on >=1 violation) — verified by sentinel-inversion in the config.

---

### `BurntSushi/ripgrep` — Rust CLI, solo-maintainer Cargo workspace (rg binary at root + 9 published grep-*/globset/ignore library crates under crates/); build = plain cargo (no xtask/just/make)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% of ENFORCED surface is addressable (release.yml tag/version is the lone addressable gate and it is a genuine gap); the config is ~100% VALUE-ADD over un-gated structure. Per the study: A0 B1 C0 D7 = 0% coverage_today.

**Enforcement surface.** ripgrep's ACTIVELY-GATED validation surface is almost entirely tool execution. CI (ci.yml): cargo build (+pcre2) across a large OS/target matrix; cargo test; wasm32-wasip1 build; `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo doc -D warnings`; a fuzz-target compile. The single bespoke semantic gate is ci/test-complete: it runs the BUILT `rg --help`, extracts the option set, and diffs it against the committed zsh completion (crates/core/flags/complete/rg.zsh) — i.e. executes the binary and does a semantic option-set diff (pure execution, not declaratively reimplementable). release.yml carries the ONLY non-tool enforced check: it asserts Cargo.toml `version` equals the pushed git TAG (`grep -q 'version = "$VERSION"'`). Beyond CI, ripgrep has CONTRIBUTING.md routing to AI_POLICY.md (LLM-contribution rules), a CHANGELOG updated per release, and a tracked Cargo.lock — but NONE of the structural invariants (dual Unlicense-OR-MIT replicated across all 10 manifests, the 3 license artifacts, edition-2024 coherence, per-crate repository URLs) are CI-gated. Governance is one maintainer + review checklist.

**What alint replaces.** None of ripgrep's *enforced CI gates* are natively replaced (they are all execution). The config's value is in expressing UN-gated structural invariants: toml_path_matches replaces the release-time precondition that root version is a bare x.y.z triple; toml_path_equals + cross_file_value_equals replace the (unenforced) license-string identity across crates/*/Cargo.toml and the edition-2024 identity; file_exists replaces (unenforced) presence of LICENSE-MIT/UNLICENSE/COPYING/CONTRIBUTING/AI_POLICY/CHANGELOG; for_each_dir + toml_path_matches expresses per-crate repository-prefix-into-ripgrep.

**What alint improves.** alint consolidates the scattered (and largely ABSENT) structural guarantees into one declarative, reviewable file and makes drift a hard error where ripgrep CI today only catches it indirectly (the `pinned` MSRV row fails to BUILD on a wrong MSRV but never asserts Cargo.toml rust-version == ci.yml pin equal; cross_file_value_equals makes that an explicit structural check). The dual-license-string identity across 10 manifests and edition coherence are things ripgrep relies on but has zero gate for — alint adds cross-file coherence the repo cannot express today. This is genuinely value-add, not replacement: ripgrep's addressable ENFORCED surface is ~0%.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** The one truly-addressable ENFORCED check — release.yml asserting Cargo.toml version == the pushed git TAG — remains a GENUINE GAP. Even with the expanded git family (git_commit_subject_matches, git_commit_author_allowlist, changeset_requires_path, etc.), alint has NO git-TAG source: no kind can read `git describe`/refs/tags and equate a tag to a file value. The closest attempt (the config's `ripgrep-version-is-semver` toml_path_matches) only asserts the version is a well-formed triple — the *precondition* for that grep to match — not the actual tag==version equality. I tried modeling the tag side as a cross_file source: cross_file's `source.file` must be a real file, and there is no synthetic 'git tag' file. Confirmed gap (the study re-bucketed this repo C->B for exactly this reason). The rg-help-vs-zsh-completion option-set diff is execution (correct NON-GOAL), not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A git-ref/tag value source for the cross-file family — e.g. extract: { git_tag: 'v{version}' } or a dedicated `version_matches_git_tag` kind that equates a structured-path value (Cargo.toml $.package.version) to the current tag under a template. This is the single missing primitive that would flip ripgrep's one addressable gate from B->A and generalizes to the very common 'manifest version == release tag' release-gate across the corpus. Lower priority: a bundled `rust/cargo-workspace-license-coherence` ruleset bundling the dual-license-string-identity + edition-identity + license-artifact-existence pattern (recurs across solo Rust CLIs).

*Notes.* Config is honest and well-modeled. One subtlety worth flagging: `ripgrep-member-repository-points-into-ripgrep` correctly uses per-crate `matches` (set-membership on the shared prefix) rather than cross_file identity, because each crate legitimately points at its own subtree URL — the config note documents that an identity rule was tried and correctly fired 8x, so it was dropped. Good reproduce-first discipline already baked in.

---

### `clap-rs/clap` — Rust CLI/derive-macro library; 8-crate Cargo workspace with members at the repo ROOT (no crates/ prefix): facade crate `clap` + clap_builder/clap_derive/clap_lex/clap_complete/clap_complete_nushell/clap_mangen (published) + clap_bench (publish=false). CI = thin wrapper that invokes `make <target>`.

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of clap's ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) repo-state surface — all 6 A-items + all value-add structure express natively. Batch 7 pooled to 70% incl. execution-heavy peers; clap itself is at the high end.

**Enforcement surface.** All structural validation lives in .github/workflows/** + the Makefile + .pre-commit-config.yaml (prek). EXECUTION gates (D): the cargo test matrix, rustfmt, clippy (+ .clippy.toml disallowed-methods), `cargo doc -D warnings`, minimal-versions/-Zminimal-versions, lockfile-up-to-date, cargo-deny, cargo-audit, typos, committed (conventional-commit linter), cffconvert (CITATION.cff validation). The REPO-STATE checks answerable by reading the tree: (1) MSRV coherence — Cargo.toml [workspace.package] `rust-version = "1.85" # MSRV` must equal ci.yml `check` job `rust: "1.85" # MSRV` (the 7 members inherit rust-version.workspace=true so they are NOT independent sites; CONTRIBUTING §Updating-MSRV makes `rg --hidden MSRV` a documented review step); (2) STABLE-toolchain coherence — Makefile `STABLE?=1.96` == ci.yml (×4 jobs) `toolchain: "1.96" # STABLE` == tests/derive_ui.rs `stable(1.96) // STABLE`, all bumped in lockstep by renovate.json5's custom regex manager; (3) pre-commit check-merge-conflict; (4) deny.toml policy declaration (license allowlist + sources allow/deny); (5) committed.toml `style="conventional"`. Structural invariants the repo relies on but does NOT gate: every published member ships LICENSE-APACHE+LICENSE-MIT+README; clap_bench is publish=false; workspace license = MIT OR Apache-2.0; edition 2024.

**What alint replaces.** clap-msrv-coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (Cargo.toml workspace.package rust-version == ci.yml regex-extracted MSRV); clap-stable-toolchain-coherence -> cross_file_value_equals with a LIST of 3 heterogeneous targets (Makefile source vs ci.yml + tests/derive_ui.rs — the multi-target form-b is natively supported); clap-no-merge-conflict-markers -> no_merge_conflict_markers; clap-deny-license-allowlist -> toml_path_matches ($.licenses.allow[*]); clap-deny-unknown-registry-denied -> toml_path_equals; clap-committed-conventional -> toml_path_equals; published-member LICENSE/README presence -> for_each_dir + nested file_exists; clap_bench publish=false / workspace license / edition -> toml_path_equals; config/metadata existence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** alint replaces what is otherwise a renovate custom-regex-manager + a CONTRIBUTING 'remember to rg --hidden MSRV and update every reference' MANUAL checklist with a hard, schema-validated cross-file equality that fails CI on drift. The STABLE pin is restated in 6 places (Makefile + 4 ci.yml jobs + a test) kept in sync only by renovate config + reviewer diligence; one cross_file_value_equals with a 3-target list expresses that cross-file coherence declaratively and reviewably. The policy-DECLARATION asserts (deny.toml allowlist non-empty, sources unknown-registry=deny, committed style=conventional) catch the failure mode renovate/cargo-deny cannot: a malformed/emptied policy file that silently denies-all or disables enforcement.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None within clap's addressable surface — every repo-state check reproduces with shipped kinds + idioms. The 6-site STABLE sync looked like it might need a 'one source, N targets' shape, but cross_file's form-b list `targets:` covers it (ci.yml multiple matching jobs collapse to one extract glob, plus the test file as a second list entry). The deny.toml/committed.toml/cargo-deny/typos/clippy bodies are execution (correct NON-GOALS, wrapped via command/command_idempotent only for single-entrypoint orchestration, not reimplemented). The CONTRIBUTING prose 'update EVERY MSRV reference' is broader than the 2 sites the config pins, but the inherited-workspace-package model means there are genuinely only 2 authoritative sites, so this is not a coverage gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `rust/toolchain-pin-coherence` ruleset parametrized by a pin-marker comment (e.g. MSRV, STABLE) that auto-collects every `# <MARKER>`-tagged toolchain literal across Cargo.toml/Makefile/ci.yml/test files and asserts they are equal — directly generalizes clap's renovate-custom-regex-manager pattern (recurs across Rust repos: ripgrep MSRV, tokio, serde). Also: a first-class `commit_convention` shared axis that, given committed.toml/commitlint config, both asserts the policy is declared AND (optionally) wires the linter — fusing the R3 declaration-assert with the D execution into one option.

*Notes.* The config correctly distinguishes R3 policy-DECLARATION asserts (deny.toml/committed.toml have the right knobs) from D enforcement (cargo-deny/committed actually run). The command/command_idempotent shell-outs (rustfmt/clippy-full/cargo-deny/typos) are explicitly orchestration wrappers, NOT alint expressing the tools' logic — correctly labeled.

---

### `cli/cli` — Go CLI (the GitHub CLI `gh`), single-vendor (GitHub), module github.com/cli/cli/v2; build via make -> script/build.go, release via GoReleaser

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of cli/cli's ADDRESSABLE surface (all 4 expressible checks reproduce), but the addressable surface itself is TINY relative to total enforcement — the repo is overwhelmingly D (execution). So: high replaceable-share of a small addressable slice; most of the file is value-add over un-gated structure.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced validation is DOMINATED by tool execution (D): go test, golangci-lint, gofmt, govulncheck, CodeQL, go-licenses generation (script/licenses + .github/licenses.tmpl, run by `make licenses`/licenses-check and the GoReleaser pre-build hook), `go mod tidy` freshness. The ONE repo-enforced *convention* expressible declaratively is AGENTS.md's hard Code-Style rule: 'Never use em dashes (U+2014) in code, comments, or documentation; use regular dashes (-) or rewrite' — enforced by humans/AI-agents in review, with NO tool gating it (the repo even has live leaks in AGENTS.md and internal/featuredetection). Structural invariants the repo RELIES ON but does not gate: (a) every release-platform dir under internal/licenses/embed/<goos>-<goarch>/ must keep a PLACEHOLDER file so `go:embed` succeeds during plain `go build` (real per-platform listings are generated at release time and .gitignored; enforced today ONLY by a .gitignore `!PLACEHOLDER` negation); (b) go.mod must declare the v2 module path `module github.com/cli/cli/v2` (import paths depend on it); (c) .github/licenses.tmpl must exist (the licenses pipeline + GoReleaser hook depend on it).

**What alint replaces.** cli-no-em-dash -> file_content_forbidden (pattern U+2014 over **/*.go + **/*.md, with testdata/fixtures/_test.go/vendor/linguist-generated excludes); cli-license-embed-placeholders -> for_each_dir 'internal/licenses/embed/*' + nested file_exists {path}/PLACEHOLDER; cli-go-mod-v2-module-path -> file_content_matches '(?m)^module github\.com/cli/cli/v2$'; cli-licenses-template-exists -> file_exists .github/licenses.tmpl. Plus the bundled go@v1 / oss-baseline / ci/github-actions / no-tracked-artifacts give the standard Go-repo existence + hygiene surface.

**What alint improves.** alint TURNS A REVIEW-ONLY CONVENTION INTO AN ENFORCED CHECK: the em-dash ban is documented in AGENTS.md but gated by nothing (hence the live leaks), and file_content_forbidden makes it a hard CI failure. The PLACEHOLDER invariant is today protected only by a fragile `.gitignore !PLACEHOLDER` negation that silently does nothing if a directory is added without it; for_each_dir + file_exists makes 'every embed platform dir HAS a placeholder' a real assertion.
