The v2-module-path and licenses.tmpl checks guard against a class of breakage (wrong module major, missing template) that currently surfaces only as a confusing build/release failure downstream.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None within cli/cli's addressable surface — all four expressible checks reproduce cleanly. The em-dash ban is per-character file_content_forbidden (trivial). The PLACEHOLDER and module-path and template checks are existence/content idioms. Everything else the repo enforces is execution (go test/golangci-lint/gofmt/govulncheck/CodeQL/go-licenses/go-mod-tidy) — correct NON-GOALS, not gaps; alint orchestrates them via command/command_idempotent but does not reimplement. One thing alint cannot do that go-licenses does is GENERATE the per-platform license listings — but that is codegen execution, a non-goal, and the freshness of generated artifacts (if they were committed) would be a generated_file_fresh case; here they are .gitignored so there is nothing to assert.

**Abstraction ideas.** A shared 'forbidden-codepoint / charset-policy' axis (the em-dash ban is a one-off here, but charset allowlists — file_is_ascii with permitted exceptions, or a `charset_forbidden` set of codepoints with per-tree scope — graduated to a recurring Tier-2 candidate from llvm/vscode/elixir/curl; cli's em-dash is another data point and a bundled `docs/no-typographic-dashes` or `style/ascii-punctuation` mini-ruleset would serve it idiomatically). Otherwise the config is squarely inside the existing surface; no new composition primitive is needed.

*Notes.* Honest config — explicitly frames itself as 'bundled baselines + the one expressible convention + value-add structure', with the em-dash rule at level:warning (appropriate given the repo's own live leaks). Good example of alint's correct posture on an execution-dominated repo: orchestrate the tools, harden the un-gated structural + convention edges, don't pretend to reimplement go test/golangci-lint.

---

### `ClickHouse/ClickHouse` — Massive C++ analytical-DBMS monorepo; contrib/ holds ~131 vendored git submodules; bespoke validation suite lives in utils/check-style + ci/jobs/scripts/check_style/{check_cpp.sh,various_checks.sh,check_submodules.sh}

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of ClickHouse's ADDRESSABLE non-execution surface (A34 of its grep-suite reproduces natively); the residual ~10% is the negative-URL-match gap + the symlink/exec-bit/binary-size candidates. The repo's ENFORCED surface is mostly D (C++ build/clang-tidy/tests/sanitizers), so addressable is a small slice of total — but within that slice alint is dominant.

**Enforcement surface.** By far the LARGEST addressable surface in the study (A34) because ClickHouse hand-rolls an enormous grep-based style suite. check_cpp.sh: a large catalogue of forbidden/required C++ patterns — no tabs in sources (with a precise vendored-tree EXCLUDE set: base/glibc-compatibility, base/widechar_width, base/poco, consistent-hashing, memcpy), no std::regex (use re2), no std::[io]stringstream (use WriteBuffer/ReadBuffer), no direct magic_enum.hpp include (use <base/EnumReflection.h>, except EnumReflection.h itself), no find_path/find_library in CMakeLists (everything bundled), `#pragma once` must be the FIRST line of every non-base header, and dozens more semantic-AST heuristics. various_checks.sh: forbidden patterns in functional tests (no global `SYSTEM STOP MERGES`, prefer `SYSTEM FLUSH LOGS <name>` over global), docker-compose must pin image versions not :latest, no DOS/CRLF newlines, every tests/integration/test_* dir must contain __init__.py (avoid pytest import-file-mismatch). check_submodules.sh: every path= in .gitmodules resolves to an on-disk dir AND every submodule url= must be https://github.com/*. PLUS the dominant EXECUTION surface (D): C++ build, clang-tidy, clang-format, the gigantic functional/integration/fuzz/sanitizer test matrix, mypy/pylint, yamllint, xmllint. Also enforces: no executable bit on certain sources, no dangling symlinks, binary file-size caps.

**What alint replaces.** ch-tests-no-global-stop-merges / no-global-flush-logs / compose-no-latest-tag -> file_content_forbidden; ch-cpp-no-tabs / no-std-regex / no-std-stringstream / no-magic-enum-direct-include / cmake-no-find-path-library -> file_content_forbidden with precise include/exclude globs (replicating the script EXCLUDE sets); ch-cpp-header-pragma-once -> file_content_matches with \A anchor (or idiomatically file_header pattern '#pragma once' lines:1); ch-no-dos-newlines -> file_content_forbidden '\r$' (better: native line_endings target:lf); ch-integration-tests-have-init-py -> for_each_dir 'tests/integration/test_*' + nested file_exists {path}/__init__.py; ch-submodule-paths-resolve -> registry_paths_resolve (.gitmodules path= regex extract, expect:dir). This is a grep-rule GOLDMINE — the bulk of check_cpp.sh/various_checks.sh maps 1:1 onto file_content_forbidden/matches with scope globs.

**What alint improves.** Consolidates THREE bespoke shell scripts (hundreds of lines of grep/find pipelines with hand-maintained exclude lists embedded in bash) into one declarative, schema-validated, reviewable config where each forbidden pattern + its scope is an auditable rule with a message. The exclude-set duplication across scripts (every script re-spells the poco/glibc-compat/consistent-hashing exclusions) collapses into reusable path scopes. Runs WITHOUT the ClickHouse build toolchain or python (the scripts need a working tree + bash + git); cross-platform; far lower false-positive risk than ad-hoc grep because file_content_forbidden anchors per-file with a real regex engine and explicit scoping. The submodule-path-resolution becomes a proper cross-file registry check (registry_paths_resolve) instead of a bash loop over `git config -f .gitmodules`.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** (1) 'Every submodule url= in .gitmodules must be https://github.com/*' is a GENUINE GAP — confirmed by reproduction. file_content_forbidden cannot express 'a url line that is NOT github-prefixed' because the Rust regex crate has NO negative look-ahead, so I cannot write (?m)^\s*url\s*=\s*(?!https://github). file_content_matches does NOT help: its semantics are 'the file contains the pattern SOMEWHERE' (whole-file search), so a github url present alongside a non-github url passes — it is not 'every url line matches'. registry_paths_resolve / cross_file extract a value SET but offer no 'every extracted value matches <regex>' predicate (I checked the source: no value-matches knob). Even the PLANNED value_set_membership is designed as set⊆set / set==set / registry_value_used — set-vs-SET, not set-vs-REGEX, so it would not obviously cover this either. (2) git_file_mode / no-executable-bit on specific source globs — alint has executable_bit but it asserts require:true|false uniformly; ClickHouse wants 'these source extensions must NOT be +x' which executable_bit require:false can approximate, so this is BORDERLINE rather than a clean gap. (3) dangling-symlink detection / symlink_target_equals — no_symlinks forbids ALL symlinks (too blunt; ClickHouse has legitimate ones) and there is no 'symlink resolves to an existing target' kind. (4) binary-aware file_max_size (cap only git-detected binaries) — file_max_size has no binary-only filter. The many semantic/AST check_cpp.sh heuristics (e.g. structural code patterns beyond literal grep) are execution (correct NON-GOALS).

**Abstraction ideas.** THE high-value primitive this repo demands: an 'every extracted value matches <regex>' assertion — either a new kind (e.g. value_set_membership with a `relation: each_matches` / `pattern:` knob) or an option on registry_paths_resolve/cross_file ('extracted values must each match this regex'). This is the inverse-of-forbidden / no-negative-lookahead workaround and directly flips the .gitmodules-url-prefix gap A. Also recurring corpus-wide: a `symlink_target_resolves` kind (dangling-symlink detection — ClickHouse/serde/golang), a `git_file_mode` kind (no-exec-bit on globs), and a binary-only filter for file_max_size (cap git-detected binaries). All four are tracked Tier-2 candidates the study surfaced from this very repo.

*Notes.* Two IMPROVABLE-not-just-replaceable nits in the current config: ch-no-dos-newlines reimplements via file_content_forbidden '\r$' where native `line_endings target: lf` is more precise and fixable; ch-cpp-header-pragma-once uses file_content_matches '\A#pragma once' which works but `file_header pattern:'#pragma once' lines:1` is the idiomatic 'first line' form. The contrib/** + rust/workspace/vendor/** + **/build/** top-level ignore is essential and correct — depth-1 clones leave dangling submodule symlinks that abort a naive walk (the study's reusable gotcha).

---

### `composer-composer` — PHP CLI package-manager (library + shipped binary), MIT, community-governed monorepo

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the addressable (non-execution) surface. Composer's overall enforcement is ~80% execution/AST (D), but of the file-level/declarative slice alint covers essentially all of it; the config's own A1-A5 + value-add assertions are accurate and all reproduce.

**Enforcement surface.** Composer's enforced validation is overwhelmingly TOOL EXECUTION (category D): PHPUnit; `php -l` lint; PHPStan level 8; `composer validate --strict` (resolves composer.json against the bespoke JSON schemas in res/); php-cs-fixer applying a large fixer set (header_comment, declare_strict_types, no_whitespace_in_blank_line, ordering/spacing fixers); zizmor (GitHub-Actions security audit); an api-surface diff; and a phar build. The DECLARATIVE/file-level surface that alint can natively assert is the residue those tools leave in config files: (license-headers) .php-cs-fixer.php `header_comment` puts a 'This file is part of Composer.' block on every src/**/*.php (notPath-excludes the vendored ClassLoader.php / InstalledVersions.php / Fixtures); (structure) `declare(strict_types=1)` on line 1 of every src file; (hygiene) .editorconfig (trim_trailing_whitespace, insert_final_newline) + .gitattributes (`* text=auto eol=lf`, `*.php eol=lf`, whitespace=blank-at-eol/eof) → trailing-whitespace / final-newline / LF-eol invariants; (manifest coherence) composer.json license=MIT + type=library, the res/*.json schema files exist, composer.lock is committed (it ships a binary, so it pins its own deps). CI orchestration is GitHub Actions; CONTRIBUTING.md states 'CS fixes are done by maintainers' so the header/strict-types invariants are reliably uniform across 305-307/307 src files.

**What alint replaces.** A1 license-header presence → file_header (pattern 'This file is part of Composer\.' within lines:12); A2 declare(strict_types=1) → file_content_matches; A3 no-trailing-whitespace → no_trailing_whitespace (+ file_trim_trailing_whitespace fixer); A4 final newline → final_newline (+ fixer); A5 LF eol → line_endings target:lf; the res/ schema-file presence → file_exists; composer.json license/type pins → json_path_equals; composer.lock-committed → file_exists root_only. The github-actions hygiene (workflow pinning etc.) comes free via extends alint://bundled/ci/github-actions@v1.

**What alint improves.** Composer has NO single declarative manifest of these per-file invariants — they are scattered across .php-cs-fixer.php (a PHP file that mixes fixer config with notPath exclusion logic), .editorconfig, and .gitattributes, and are only enforced as a side-effect of running the full php-cs-fixer toolchain (PHP + the fixer must be installed). alint consolidates header + strict-types + whitespace + eol + manifest-coherence into one schema-validated reviewable YAML that runs with no PHP toolchain, gives a precise per-file violation list instead of a wholesale rewrite-diff, and runs the same on a contributor laptop and in CI. The file_header pattern survives the exact exclusion set the fixer's notPath encodes, expressed declaratively rather than as imperative PHP.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none materially. The remaining Composer-specific checks are all execution (D, correctly out of scope): `composer validate --strict` requires the live JSON-schema resolver + network-aware dependency semantics (alint can assert the schema FILES exist and that composer.json's own license/type fields match, but not the full schema-driven validation — that's json_schema_passes territory only if Composer shipped a plain JSON Schema, which composer-schema.json approximates but with custom keywords); the api-surface diff and PHPStan-level-8 are AST/type analysis. None of these are alint should-do gaps.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `bundled/php@v1` ecosystem ruleset (gated on facts.has_composer_json) parallel to python/go/rust/node/dotnet would capture the recurring PHP-package shape: composer.json present + valid license field, composer.lock committed-when-binary / gitignored-when-library (the inverse policy is itself a useful shared axis: a `lockfile_policy: committed|ignored` option keyed on package type), strict_types header convention, and PSR-4 autoload path resolution (autoload.psr-4 maps a namespace prefix to a dir → a registry_paths_resolve-shaped 'every declared autoload dir exists' edge). The autoload-paths-resolve idea is the most repo-grounded: composer.json#autoload.psr-4 is exactly a path registry alint already models.

*Notes.* Config is faithful and conservative; correctly segregates value-add (manifest/schema/lock assertions) out of the coverage denominator per the study's R3/R5 convention. No over-claimed gaps. Verified: file_header is_match (not start-anchored on the whole window) means 'This file is part of Composer\.' matches anywhere in the first 12 lines, tolerating the `<?php declare(strict_types=1);` preamble that precedes the docblock — so A1 and A2 coexist cleanly without a preamble-skip option.

---

### `curl-curl` — C network-library + CLI tool, autotools+cmake dual build, curl license (MIT-ish), BDFL-governed

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the addressable hygiene/header surface (the entire spacecheck.pl battery + SPDX presence + artifact-absence). Of curl's TOTAL enforcement, execution dominates (~70-75% is checksrc/reuse/doc-consistency/compile), so call it ~25-30% of all enforcement addressable, of which alint now covers ~90%.

**Enforcement surface.** curl's enforced surface splits into (a) a large EXECUTION tier (D): scripts/checksrc.pl (the bespoke C style/AST-lite linter), codespell/typos/proselint, `reuse lint` (full REUSE-spec 3.2 SPDX resolution incl. .reuse/dep5 + LICENSES/ tree), the doc<->source consistency test suite (every option documented matches the code), autotools-vs-cmake feature parity, distfiles.sh tarball hygiene, and the compile/test matrices; and (b) a HYGIENE BATTERY in scripts/spacecheck.pl that is now almost entirely native to alint: max line length 192 repo-wide (with a skip-list), no 3+ consecutive blank lines, source must be ASCII (allow-listed exceptions), LF-only for shell/m4/autotools, no tabs in C/H sources, exactly one trailing newline (no missing-EOL-at-EOF AND no trailing blank line), tracked filenames restricted to [A-Za-z0-9/._-]. (license-headers) every committable source carries SPDX-License-Identifier (gated by `reuse lint`). (structure) clean source tree — no committed build artifacts (.gitignore + distfiles.sh). .gitattributes encodes eol/whitespace policy, .editorconfig encodes indent_style=space + trim_trailing_whitespace. CI is GitHub Actions (checksrc.yml etc.).

**What alint replaces.** SPDX header presence on C/H sources + helper scripts → file_content_matches 'SPDX-License-Identifier:'; max-line-192 → line_max_width; no-3-blank-lines → max_consecutive_blank_lines max:2; ASCII-only sources → file_is_ascii (+ allow: for the codepoint exceptions); LF eol for sh/m4/am/in → line_endings target:lf; no-tabs-in-C → file_content_forbidden pattern '\t'; no-EOL-at-EOF → final_newline; trailing-whitespace → no_trailing_whitespace; filename charset → filename_regex (auto-anchored ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$ on basename); no committed object/lib/exe artifacts → file_absent.

**What alint improves.** spacecheck.pl is ~250 lines of Perl mixing eight orthogonal checks with a hand-maintained skip-list, runnable only where Perl is present; alint expresses the same eight invariants as eight reviewable, individually-scoped, individually-severity-tunable rules with per-file diagnostics and a schema. The filename-charset check is materially BETTER in alint: filename_regex AUTO-ANCHORS to ^...$ (verified), so it precisely rejects any basename with an out-of-set character, vs spacecheck's regex which the config author worried about — alint gets it right by construction. SPDX-presence as a fast file_content_matches gate gives contributors instant local feedback without installing the `reuse` Python tool.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none on reproduction — I re-examined the one C-item the config flagged and it DISSOLVED. The config says spacecheck's 'forbid a trailing blank line at EOF' has 'no native kind (C)'. FALSE: file_content_forbidden with pattern '\n[ \t]*\n\z' natively catches it (the Rust regex crate supports the \z end-of-text anchor) — I verified it fires on a file ending '...\n\n' and on '...\n\n\n', and PASSES a file with a single trailing newline. So the full spacecheck 'exactly one trailing newline' invariant = final_newline (must end with \n) + that one file_content_forbidden rule. The truly-unaddressable curl checks are all execution (checksrc.pl's C-syntax awareness, the doc<->source option-consistency suite, reuse-spec dep5 resolution, autotools/cmake parity) and are correct NON-GOALS.

**Abstraction ideas.** A composite `single_trailing_newline` / `no_trailing_blank_lines` rule (or a `final_newline` option `forbid_trailing_blank: true`) would bundle the 'ends with exactly one newline' invariant that currently needs final_newline + a hand-rolled \z regex — a clean shared primitive many C/POSIX-strict repos (curl, git, the kernel) want. Also a `reuse`/SPDX-aware bundled ruleset: SPDX-License-Identifier presence + (with a small extension) cross-checking each identifier against an allow-list of LICENSES/ files present on disk would cover the cheap 80% of `reuse lint` declaratively (the LICENSES/<id>.txt existence check is exactly registry_paths_resolve-shaped: extract every SPDX id, require LICENSES/<id>.txt to exist).

*Notes.* Strong reproduce-first outcome: the single claimed C-gap (trailing blank at EOF) is REPLACEABLE, not a gap. The SPDX rules correctly use file_content_matches (whole-file scan) rather than file_header — right call, since curl's SPDX line can sit below a leading comment banner. One refinement worth noting: line_max_width / file_is_ascii are scoped tightly which matches spacecheck's skip-list intent.

---

### `denoland-deno` — Rust + TypeScript runtime monorepo (cli/ + ext/ + libs/ + runtime/), multi-crate Cargo workspace + Deno-native tooling, MIT

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of the addressable structural surface. Of the 8 tools/lint.js structural checks: copyright headers (with the (?m) fix), Cargo.toml header, LICENSE year, per-crate clippy.toml presence+content, workflow freshness, and the flag forbid-list are all REPLACEABLE; only the root-allowlist is a genuine no-op gap and the flag set-equality is awkward. Deno's TOTAL enforcement is heavily execution (clippy/dlint/dprint/miri/WPT), so addressable is a minority of all enforcement.

**Enforcement surface.** Deno's bespoke validators live under tools/ and are orchestrated by tools/lint.js in the CI `lint` job; on top sit the EXECUTION tier (D): clippy, dlint, dprint, jsdoc_checker, miri, the WPT + full test matrices. tools/lint.js itself bundles several STRUCTURAL/CONTENT checks that are alint-shaped: (license-headers) tools/copyright_checker.js requires '// Copyright 2018-<year> the Deno authors. MIT license.' on every tracked .rs/.js/.mjs/.jsx/.ts/.tsx/.c (TRIMMING a tolerated preamble: shebang, `// deno-lint-*`, `// Ported*`, blank lines) and a '# '-form on every workspace Cargo.toml, plus LICENSE.md carrying the current year; (structure/cross-file) ensureDisallowedMethodsEnforced = every ext/* and libs/* crate (skipping test crates like libs/core_testing) ships a clippy.toml carrying a mandated disallowed-methods set; (codegen-freshness) ensureWorkflowYmlsUpToDate regenerates each .github/workflows/*.generated.yml from its *.ts source and git-diffs; (layout) ensureNoNewTopLevelEntries = the repo ROOT may contain only an allowlisted set of files+dirs; (naming/policy) ensureNoNonPermissionCapitalLetterShortFlags = the set of uppercase `.short('X')` flags in cli/args/flags.rs must equal a fixed allowlist {A,D,E,I,L,N,P,R,S,V,W}.

**What alint replaces.** Cargo.toml '# Copyright' header (always line 1) → file_header lines:1; LICENSE.md current-year → file_content_matches; every ext/*+libs/* crate ships clippy.toml → for_each_dir select:'ext/*'/'libs/*' + when_iter:'iter.has_file("Cargo.toml")' (and iter.basename!="core_testing") + nested file_exists '{path}/clippy.toml' (verified when_iter/iter.has_file/iter.basename are current API); clippy.toml carries the mandated ban → file_content_matches '"std::fs::canonicalize"'; generated workflow YAML freshness → generated_file_fresh (runs ci.ts, diffs stdout); FORBID a new disallowed uppercase short flag → file_content_forbidden on cli/args/flags.rs (verified fires only on the out-of-set letter); extends bundled rust@v1 + oss-baseline@v1.

**What alint improves.** tools/lint.js is a Deno program (needs the Deno toolchain to run) bundling ~7 unrelated checks; alint expresses the structural ones (per-crate clippy.toml presence + content, header presence, LICENSE year, the flag forbid-list) declaratively and runs them with no Deno install. The for_each_dir 'every crate has a clippy.toml' is materially better than the JS loop: it's a reviewable cross-file invariant with the test-crate skip encoded as a readable when_iter predicate, and it composes the presence check with the content (mandated-ban) check in the same config.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE real gap, and ONE claimed gap that is a PHANTOM. (1) GENUINE GAP — ensureNoNewTopLevelEntries (repo-ROOT allowlist). The config uses `dir_only_contains select: "."`; I EMPIRICALLY CONFIRMED this is a SILENT NO-OP: the walker skips the walk-root itself (no FileEntry with path "" or "." is ever indexed — walker.rs:445-447), AND dir_only_contains only inspects FILE children, never subdirectories (its own doc: 'Subdirectories are not checked'). I tried `select: "."` (passes despite an unexpected root file+dir) and `select: "*"` (also no-ops — it descends INTO each top-level dir). There is no allowlist primitive for 'directory X contains only these direct children (files AND dirs)'; a file_absent/dir_absent denylist cannot catch a NEW unexpected entry, which is the whole point. This is a should-do gap. (2) PHANTOM — the config's headline C-item claims file_header 'over-fires on shebang-prefixed scripts, wants skip_preamble:, set level:off'. I EMPIRICALLY DISPROVED this: the brief's `(?m)^// Copyright ...` idiom inside lines:4 tolerates a leading shebang line and fires ONLY on files genuinely missing the header (verified: a `#!/usr/bin/env deno` + copyright-on-line-2 file PASSES; a header-less file FIRES). The config's anchored '^// Copyright' is what over-fires; one token `(?m)` fixes it and faithfully mimics copyright_checker.js's preamble-trim. The flag set-EQUALITY (detecting a REMOVED expected flag) is the awkward residue of the forbid-rule, but is expressible-with-effort (one file_content_matches per expected flag requiring its presence) — not a hard gap. generated_file_fresh needing the Deno toolchain to run is a correct, documented execution dependency, not an alint gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** TOP need: a `dir_allowlist` / `dir_only_contains` extension that (a) accepts the repo ROOT as a target (a `root: true` or `select: "."` special-case that synthesizes the root entry) and (b) checks BOTH files and subdirectories against the allow-set. This single primitive lights up the very common 'keep the repo root clean / no new top-level entries' policy (deno, many monorepos) that today is silently unenforceable. Secondary: a `file_header` `skip_preamble:`/`after:` option would be ergonomic (even though `(?m)^` already covers the common case) for headers that must be EXACTLY at the first non-preamble line. Tertiary: a `value_set_equals`-on-extracted-tokens kind (extract every `.short('X')` capital, assert the SET equals a literal allowlist) would turn the flag check from forbid-only into true set-equality — generalizes beyond deno (enum-variant exhaustiveness, registered-feature lists).

---

### `discourse-discourse` — Large polyglot monorepo — Rails(Ruby) backend + Ember(JS/TS) frontend + ~46 bundled plugins; bundler(Gemfile.lock) + pnpm(workspace) ; GPL-2.0-only

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of the addressable (non-execution) surface: the pnpm patch+workspace resolution, lockfile presence, banned-phrase grep, and manifest pins all reproduce; only i18n_lint's structural-YAML half is a genuine gap. But discourse's TOTAL enforcement is ~90% execution (rubocop/eslint/glint/test matrices/dep-license resolution), so the addressable slice is small — alint covers most OF that small slice.

**Enforcement surface.** Discourse's enforced surface is dominated by EXECUTION (D): rubocop + syntax_tree (Ruby), eslint + prettier + stylelint (JS/CSS), glint/ember-tsc (TS template typechecking), the rspec/qunit/system test matrices, `licensed` + `licensee` dependency-license RESOLUTION (graph-walking the bundler+pnpm dep trees against .licensed.yml / .licensee.json allow-lists), and codegen-freshness git-diff gates. The natively-alint slice: (cross-file/file-graph) package.json#pnpm.patchedDependencies maps '<pkg>@<ver>' → a patch file under patches/ that `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (every CI job) requires to exist; pnpm-workspace.yaml#packages lists every workspace member dir (frontend/*, plugins/*, docs/developer-guides) which install also requires; (content/grep) script/i18n_lint.rb (CI 'English locale lint') bans phrases ('color scheme'→'color palette', 'private message'→'personal message') in en locale YAML AND validates pluralization sub-key structure + %{key} interpolation format + relative-link prefixes; (structure) frozen/deployment installs require committed Gemfile.lock + pnpm-lock.yaml; (manifest) package.json is private + engines.npm='please-use-pnpm' steering. No per-file license/SPDX headers (single root LICENSE.txt + COPYRIGHT.md), so no header bundle.

**What alint replaces.** pnpm patch registry resolves → registry_paths_resolve source:package.json extract:json '$.pnpm.patchedDependencies[*]' expect:file (VERIFIED fires on a dangling patch path); pnpm workspace members resolve → registry_paths_resolve source:pnpm-workspace.yaml extract:yaml '$.packages[*]' entries_are_globs:true expect:dir (VERIFIED fires on a missing member dir); i18n banned-phrase grep → file_content_forbidden '(?i)color scheme' / '(?i)private message' scoped to {client,server}.en.yml; Gemfile.lock committed → file_exists root_only; pnpm-lock.yaml committed → free via bundled node@v1 (node-has-lockfile); package.json private + engines.npm pin → json_path_equals; license-policy files present → file_exists.

**What alint improves.** The two registry_paths_resolve rules turn an invariant that is otherwise only enforced as a side-effect of a full `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (needs Node + pnpm + network) into an instant, dependency-free file-graph check — and they ARE genuine file→file / file→dir reference edges (this is why the study counted them as edge sources). For a repo with ~46 plugins and a sprawling workspace, the 'every declared patch/member resolves' check is exactly the kind of cross-file coherence a per-tool lint can't state in one place. The banned-phrase slice runs without booting the Rails i18n_lint harness.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap, correctly identified by the config and confirmed on reproduction. script/i18n_lint.rb does SEMANTIC YAML-TREE validation that alint cannot express: (a) pluralization sub-key structure — a key with plural forms must have the right {one,other,...} child set for English; (b) %{key} interpolation-format consistency — placeholders in a translated string must match a schema; (c) relative-link prefix rules. I checked the idioms: file_content_forbidden/matches only does flat substring/regex per file — it cannot assert 'for each key in this YAML tree, its VALUE's interpolation placeholders form a valid set' or 'this key's children are structurally a valid plural group'. yaml_path_equals/matches can pin a KNOWN path, but not iterate an unknown keyspace asserting a per-node structural predicate. There is no 'for each leaf/subtree in a structured doc, assert a relation on its value' primitive. So the banned-phrase HALF is REPLACEABLE; the structural HALF is a real gap (the config's B classification is accurate). Everything else (rubocop, eslint, glint, licensed/licensee dep-graph resolution, the test matrices) is correct execution NON-GOAL — note licensed/licensee is graph-walking the dependency tree, fundamentally beyond a file-linter.

**Abstraction ideas.** The repo-grounded need is a STRUCTURED-TREE iteration primitive: a `structured_for_each` (or json_path/yaml_path with a per-match nested-rule block) that selects a JSONPath/YAMLPath set (possibly with wildcards over unknown keys) and applies a sub-predicate to each matched node's value — e.g. 'for each $..* string leaf, its %{...} placeholders ⊆ a declared set', or 'for each pluralizable key, require child keys {one,other}'. That would convert i18n_lint's structural half from a Ruby script into declarative rules and generalizes hugely (locale files, OpenAPI specs, k8s manifests). A lighter shared axis: a `placeholder_format` content rule that validates interpolation tokens against a grammar (%{name} / {0} / $t(...)) repo-wide. Also worth a `monorepo`/pnpm extension: 'every pnpm-workspace member that is a package has a package.json' (for_each_dir over the resolved members) — a natural complement to the resolve rules already present.

---

### `django-django` — Python web framework monorepo (tox + pre-commit, Django Software Foundation governance)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (non-execution) surface; the repo's enforcement is otherwise ~80% execution/AST (flake8/black/isort/biome/sphinx-lint/aspell/migrations/pytest) which is correct non-goal territory.

**Enforcement surface.** Django's enforced surface is overwhelmingly TOOL EXECUTION orchestrated by tox + pre-commit + GitHub Actions, with a thin file-shaped policy layer on top. (1) Code-style/format/lint (execution): flake8 (.flake8: max-line-length 88, per-file ignores), isort, black, blacken-docs, biome (biome.json -> linters.yml `npx biome ci` for JS/CSS), sphinx-lint (79-col reST + reST semantics), aspell spelling wordlist, plus `makemigrations --check` migration-staleness and the full multi-version pytest matrix (.github/workflows/*, tox.ini envs). All AST/NLP/semantic = category D. (2) Workflow security: zizmor.yml policy (ref-pin, NOT hash-pin) over .github/workflows/**; workflows mostly declare `permissions: contents: read`. (3) Hygiene driven declaratively by .editorconfig (trim_trailing_whitespace, indent, EOL) and enforced incidentally by black/biome. (4) Bespoke Python validators wired into CI: scripts/check_migrations.py (check-migrations.yml) and scripts/pr_quality/check_pr.py (check_pr_quality.yml). (5) Licensing/provenance: ships LICENSE (BSD-3) + LICENSE.python (bundled CPython license) + AUTHORS, all three declared as license-files in pyproject.toml, which also pins license = BSD-3-Clause and a requires-python floor. (6) Version pins live in tests/requirements/*.txt (NO uv/poetry/pdm/Pipfile lockfile — deliberate). Commit/PR discipline is largely social + the bespoke check_pr.py rather than a hook.

**What alint replaces.** oss-baseline + python + ci/github-actions bundles cover the hygiene + GHA-permissions/security baseline (file_exists for governance files, no_trailing_whitespace via .editorconfig parity, GHA permissions warnings); no_merge_conflict_markers (oss-no-merge-conflict-markers, scoped away from docs/** reST Setext underlines); file_exists for the policy/config inventory (.editorconfig/.flake8/.pre-commit-config.yaml/tox.ini/biome.json/zizmor.yml/LICENSE/LICENSE.python/AUTHORS and the two bespoke validator entrypoints scripts/check_migrations.py + scripts/pr_quality/check_pr.py — a rename can't silently disable the CI gate); toml_path_equals on pyproject $.project.license == BSD-3-Clause; toml_path_matches on $.project['requires-python']; no_trailing_whitespace scoped to docs/**/*.txt (the editorconfig trim that sphinx-lint doesn't own).

**What alint improves.** Consolidates a scatter of presence/coherence assertions that today live implicitly across tox.ini, pyproject.toml, .pre-commit-config.yaml and CI YAML into ONE reviewable declarative file — nothing in Django actively asserts that its three declared license-files actually exist on disk, that pyproject's SPDX license string hasn't drifted, or that the bespoke CI validator scripts are still present at the path the workflow invokes; alint makes those load-bearing invariants explicit and runnable without the Python toolchain or a tox bootstrap. The pyproject license-files <-> on-disk coherence could be tightened further (see abstractions). The no_merge_conflict_markers scoping (excluding reST Setext `=======` underlines) is also a cleaner expression than a regex grep.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Every file-shaped Django invariant is expressible. The pyproject `license-files` triad is currently asserted as a static file_exists list rather than DERIVED from pyproject (a true 'every path named in [project.license-files]/[tool...] must exist' would want registry_paths_resolve over a TOML array of globs — registry_paths_resolve takes a source manifest + extract, but the modern PEP 639 `license-files` is a glob array, and registry_paths_resolve resolves literal/enumerated paths, not globs-as-patterns; I tried framing it as registry_paths_resolve source: pyproject.toml extract: toml $.project.license-files, but glob entries like `LICENSE*` would be treated as a literal path and mis-resolve — so the static list is the correct expression here, not a gap, just not derived). Everything else (flake8/isort/black/biome/sphinx-lint/aspell/makemigrations --check/pytest matrix/check_pr.py semantics) is category D execution, correctly orchestrated via command / command_idempotent, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) registry_paths_resolve could grow a `glob: true` mode so a manifest field holding glob patterns (PEP 639 [project.license-files] = ['LICENSE*','AUTHORS']) is satisfied if EACH pattern matches >=1 file — would let alint derive the license-file presence check from pyproject instead of a hand-maintained list, the single real value-add idea this repo surfaces. (2) A bundled `python/setuptools-pep621@v1` ruleset asserting the common pyproject coherence set (license is valid SPDX, requires-python present, declared license-files resolve, name/version present) — Django, and most PEP 621 projects, hand-roll these. (3) A `reStructuredText-aware` exclude preset for no_merge_conflict_markers (the bare `=======` Setext-underline false-positive recurs across every Sphinx-docs repo).

*Notes.* Config is well-calibrated and honest: it correctly downgrades python-has-lockfile to off (R6 absent-category), leaves GHA permissions at warning (3 automation workflows legitimately need write + omit contents:read at workflow level, so an error bump over-fires), turns gha-pin-actions-to-sha off (Django's policy is ref-pin not hash-pin), and notes the root_only-vs-nested-path trap on scripts/* rules (caught by `alint check`, R4). No over-claims, no spurious gaps.

---

### `dotnet-aspnetcore` — Large .NET/C# web-framework monorepo (Microsoft-governed, Arcade build in eng/, Azure DevOps + GitHub Actions)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of addressable surface (the declarative slice of CodeCheck.ps1 + Arcade/global.json structure); the [no changelog] marker-exemption is the only genuine miss. The repo's TOTAL enforcement is ~90% execution (build/xUnit/dotnet format/Roslyn analyzers/markdownlint/darc) = correct non-goal.

**Enforcement surface.** The bespoke-validation surface is concentrated in eng/scripts/CodeCheck.ps1 (the home-grown analysis gate wired into .azure/pipelines/ci-public.yml + ci.yml), surrounded by Arcade conventions and a handful of GitHub Actions. CodeCheck.ps1 performs, among others: (a) npm source-feed lockdown — scans src\**\package-lock.json -Recurse and FAILS if any `resolved` points anywhere but the dnceng dotnet-public-npm feed (the root package-lock.json is validated separately via `npm ci` in validate-npm-package-lock-json.yml); (b) SignalR-TS changelog co-change — if src/SignalR/clients/ts/* changed, CHANGELOG.md must also change (unless a `[no changelog]` commit marker is present); (c) generated project-list freshness — eng/scripts/GenerateProjectList.ps1 regenerates eng/ProjectReferences.props / SharedFramework.Local.props / TrimmableProjects.props from the src/**.csproj tree, then CodeCheck git-diffs them; (d) various structural/format assertions. Beyond CodeCheck: the dominant enforcement is build + xUnit + `dotnet format` + Roslyn analyzers (IDE0073 license header, RS0016 public-API), markdownlint, and Arcade dependency-flow (darc) coherence between eng/Versions.props and eng/Version.Details.xml. global.json pins a concrete SDK version + an SDK band paths array. Vendors third-party code (THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES). All build/test/format/analyzer/darc work = category D.

**What alint replaces.** A1 npm-feed lockdown -> file_content_forbidden on src/**/package-lock.json forbidding the canonical wrong feed `"resolved": "https://registry.npmjs.org/` (Rust regex has no negative-lookahead, so forbidding the known-bad feed is the right expression of CodeCheck's intent — natively replaced); SignalR-TS changelog co-change -> pair_changed_together (if_changed: src/SignalR/clients/ts/** then_changed: src/SignalR/clients/ts/CHANGELOG.md, since: base) NATIVELY replaces the diff-gated rule the config downgraded to mere file_exists; generated_file_fresh (outputs: mutating mode) or command_idempotent NATIVELY replaces CodeCheck's GenerateProjectList git-diff freshness check (run the .ps1 generator, diff the tree); dotnet bundle covers global.json $.sdk.version semver + .editorconfig presence; oss-baseline + ci/github-actions + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts cover the rest; json_path_matches on global.json $.sdk.version (concrete preview build); file_exists for the generated props, Arcade manifests (eng/Versions.props + Version.Details.xml), CodeCheck.ps1, and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.

**What alint improves.** The headline improvement: CodeCheck.ps1 is ~500 lines of imperative PowerShell mixing genuinely-declarative policy (the npm-feed regex, the SignalR co-change rule, the generated-file freshness) with execution; alint expresses the declarative subset as a handful of reviewable, schema-validated rules that run on any platform WITHOUT a PowerShell host or the Azure pipeline — a contributor on macOS can run the npm-feed and changelog-co-change gates locally in seconds. pair_changed_together also expresses the SignalR cross-file co-change coherence the config originally (PRE-build) wrote off as execution-only. The Arcade manifest presence + global.json pin become explicit invariants rather than implicit Arcade assumptions.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** The `[no changelog]` commit-message ESCAPE on the SignalR gate. CodeCheck's real rule is 'if SignalR-TS changed, CHANGELOG.md changed OR a commit in the range carries [no changelog]'. pair_changed_together expresses the co-change pact but has no OR-clause keyed on a commit-message marker; git_commit_subject_matches can assert a subject pattern but cannot be wired as an exemption to pair_changed_together's obligation. I tried composing them (two rules can't express 'A unless B' — both would fire/pass independently), so the marker-exemption is a true, narrow gap. Everything else CodeCheck does that isn't execution is expressible. (Note: the npm negative-lookahead 'anything but the dnceng feed' is NOT a gap — forbidding the one real wrong feed is the operative check and reproduces CodeCheck's actual failure mode.)

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) An `unless_commit_subject:` / `exempt_if_subject_matches:` escape on pair_changed_together AND changeset_requires_path — many monorepos (aspnetcore [no changelog], others [skip changelog]/[no-ci]) gate a co-change/changelog obligation behind a commit-message opt-out; this is the one real abstraction this repo demands and would generalize widely. (2) A composition primitive 'rule A is satisfied if rule B passes' (an OR/exemption combinator) would dissolve the same gap more generally. (3) A bundled `dotnet/arcade@v1` ruleset (global.json concrete SDK pin, eng/Versions.props + eng/Version.Details.xml present + their darc-style coherence, generated eng/*.props freshness scaffolding) — the Arcade convention recurs across dotnet/* repos (aspnetcore, runtime, roslyn, sdk, installer).

*Notes.* The config materially UNDER-claims on A2: it labels the SignalR-TS->CHANGELOG diff gate as execution (D) and asserts only file existence, but pair_changed_together natively expresses the co-change pact (modulo the [no changelog] escape). This is a PRE-build over-report exactly of the kind the brief warns about — worth re-tagging A2 as REPLACEABLE-with-caveat in the study log.

---

### `dotnet-roslyn` — Large C#/VB compiler monorepo (the Roslyn .NET compiler platform; Microsoft-governed, Arcade build)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable surface (all 3 file-shaped gates natively replaced). But addressable is a SMALL fraction of total: Roslyn's enforcement is ~95% AST/semantic/build execution (IDE0073/PublicApi/BannedApi/BuildBoss/determinism/dotnet-format/xUnit) = correct non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** Roslyn's enforced surface is dominated by deep AST/semantic execution, with a small file-shaped policy layer. Execution (category D): dotnet-format + RoslynEnforceCodeStyle=true driving the IDE-style analyzers (IDE0073 file-header, plus the full IDE code-style set), the PublicApi analyzer (PublicAPI.Shipped/Unshipped.txt enforcement), the BannedApiAnalyzers (BannedSymbols.txt), BuildBoss (a bespoke MSBuild project-graph/solution-coherence validator), determinism / rebuild verification (build twice, compare binaries), and the xUnit test matrix — all run via Arcade + azure-pipelines.yml. The bespoke file-shaped gates: (1) per-file MIT .NET Foundation license header on src/**/*.cs and *.vb (declared in .editorconfig file_header_template, enforced by IDE0073); the banner text is identical for C# and VB, only the comment prefix differs (// vs '); generated/SyncedSource files are exempt. (2) eng/todo-check.ps1 (azure-pipelines.yml step 'Validate TODO/PROTOTYPE comments are not present') forbids PROTOTYPE and TODO2 markers across src/**, eng/**, scripts/**, docs/compilers/** (excluding binaries, .xlf localization, and the checker script itself).

**What alint replaces.** A1 license header -> file_header (lines:4, pattern matching the two-line MIT banner with [\s\S]* bridging the comment-prefix difference between // and '; excludes Generated/SyncedSource) NATIVELY replaces the content-prefix half of IDE0073 (the file-banner presence, not the analyzer's AST integration); A2 + A3 PROTOTYPE / TODO2 marker bans -> file_content_forbidden (two rules, scoped to the same trees, excluding binaries/.xlf/the checker itself) NATIVELY replace eng/todo-check.ps1's grep; oss-baseline + dotnet bundles cover README/LICENSE + global.json SDK pin + .editorconfig presence.

**What alint improves.** eng/todo-check.ps1 is a PowerShell script that only runs inside the Azure pipeline; the two file_content_forbidden rules run anywhere (a contributor catches a stray PROTOTYPE before pushing, on any OS, with no PowerShell). The license-header rule expresses, in one declarative pattern with explicit exclusions, what IDE0073 only enforces when the full Roslyn analyzer host + RoslynEnforceCodeStyle build is invoked — a far cheaper pre-commit gate for the 80%-common 'someone added a .cs without the banner' case. Both consolidate into a reviewable file vs. an imperative .ps1 + an analyzer config buried in .editorconfig.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The three file-shaped invariants are fully expressible and the config is tight. The license-header `[\s\S]*` between the two banner lines is the correct idiom for tolerating the // vs ' prefix divergence in a single rule (verified the kind exists and supports multiline patterns within lines:N). Everything else — IDE0073's AST awareness, PublicApi/BannedApi analyzers, BuildBoss MSBuild-graph coherence, determinism/rebuild verification, dotnet-format — is genuine AST/semantic/build execution (category D), correctly out of scope. I considered whether PublicAPI.Shipped/Unshipped.txt coherence (e.g. 'every public symbol appears') could be a cross_file assertion, but that requires Roslyn's symbol table (semantic), not a file-to-file textual relation — correctly D, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `forbidden-markers@v1` ruleset (PROTOTYPE/TODO2/DO NOT MERGE/FIXME-blocking/XXX as a parameterized token list with standard binary/generated excludes) — the 'forbid these tokens in source' pattern recurs across roslyn (PROTOTYPE/TODO2), many repos (DO NOT SUBMIT, HACK), and is always hand-rolled as file_content_forbidden; a bundled token-list ruleset would be a clean DSL convenience. (2) A `comment_prefix:` option on file_header so the C#/VB (// vs ') divergence is expressed as 'this banner with any of these prefixes' rather than via a [\s\S]* bridge — minor ergonomics, generalizes to any polyglot license-header repo. Neither is a need the repo can't already meet; both are pure ergonomics.

*Notes.* Cleanest of the four configs — no overclaims, no gaps, three precise rules. Good reproduce-first discipline: it explicitly notes file_header expresses the content-prefix logic 'not an AST check', correctly scoping its claim to the banner-presence subset of IDE0073 rather than claiming to replace the analyzer.

---

### `dotnet-runtime` — Hyperscale .NET runtime monorepo (CoreCLR/Mono/libraries; Microsoft-governed, Arcade build, Azure DevOps)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of addressable surface (license header, trailing whitespace, EOL via line_endings, EOF newline via final_newline, global.json coherence, ref/ convention, governance files all expressible). Addressable is a TINY slice of total: dotnet/runtime enforcement is ~97% execution (build/test matrix + analyzers + ApiCompat + ILLink + CodeQL + clang-format + jitformat) = correct non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** A hyperscale monorepo whose enforced-validation surface is DOMINATED by execution non-goals (D): the entire Azure DevOps build/test matrix (eng/pipelines/**), Roslyn analyzers driven by eng/CodeAnalysis.*.globalconfig, ApiCompat / package validation (ref/ contract vs implementation), ILLink trim analysis, CodeQL, CredScan/TSA security scanning, clang-format (native), `dotnet format` + jitformat.py (jit code), markdownlint-cli, and a bespoke skill-validator. The file-shaped surface alint can express is small but real: (1) per-file .NET Foundation MIT license header (CONTRIBUTING.md 'File Headers'; eng/LicenseHeader.txt is canonical; ~universal across .cs/.cpp/.h — sampled 500/500 .cs carry it verbatim); the bundled apache/governance header does NOT match this text so it's a repo rule. (2) no trailing whitespace repo-wide (.editorconfig [*] trim_trailing_whitespace=true; copilot-instructions calls it out for .md; dotnet format whitespace / jitformat enforce it on changed files). (3) .gitattributes EOL policy (per the config's own notes, wants a line_endings primitive). (4) global.json pins SDK + a tools.dotnet bootstrap entry (verified equal at the pin; nothing CI-gates the equality). (5) ApiCompat structural convention: each src/libraries/System.* commonly has a sibling ref/ contract project (eng/resolveContract.targets) — though implementation-only assemblies legitimately have none. Ships THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.TXT + PATENTS.TXT at root.

**What alint replaces.** A2 per-file license header -> file_header (lines:2, the two-line MIT banner) scoped to src/libraries|coreclr|mono/**/*.cs and coreclr/vm|jit/**/*.cpp NATIVELY replaces the content half of the header convention; A1 no-trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace over src/**/*.{cs,cpp,h} + **/*.md NATIVELY replaces the .editorconfig trim that dotnet format only enforces on changed files; global.json internal coherence ($.sdk.version == $.tools.dotnet) -> cross_file_value_equals / cross_file (a coherence NOTHING in CI checks today — pure value-add); ApiCompat ref/ convention -> every_matching_has (select: src/libraries/System.* require: dir_exists {path}/ref) as a best-effort info hint; oss-baseline + dotnet bundles cover README/LICENSE.TXT (case-broadened) + global.json SDK pin + .editorconfig presence + CPM; file_exists for THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.TXT + PATENTS.TXT.

**What alint improves.** The global.json sdk.version<->tools.dotnet coherence is a genuinely NEW invariant alint adds that no Arcade tooling or CI step currently guards — a drift between the two (build SDK vs bootstrap SDK) would go silently uncaught today; cross_file makes it an explicit, runnable assertion. The license-header + trailing-whitespace checks run repo-wide on any platform without spinning up the Roslyn analyzer host or `dotnet format` (which only act on changed files in PRs), giving a cheap full-tree audit the toolchain doesn't offer. The ApiCompat ref/ structural convention becomes a (noisy-by-design) explicit hint rather than tribal knowledge encoded only in eng/resolveContract.targets.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that survive the reproduce-first lens. The config's own notes flag the .gitattributes EOL policy as 'needs a line_endings primitive' and final-newline-at-EOF as a tuning/gap item — but BOTH now exist in the capability brief (line_endings and final_newline are shipped kinds), so these are PHANTOM gaps relative to current alint: the .gitattributes `* text=auto eol=lf` / per-pattern CRLF policy is expressible by one or more line_endings rules scoped per glob, and EOF-newline by final_newline. The only thing not expressible is enforcing the .gitattributes FILE's normalization semantics against git's actual checkout behavior (that's git's smudge/clean filter, not a file-content property) — but that's not something the repo gates on either, so it's a non-issue, not a gap. ApiCompat ref/<->impl PUBLIC-API equality is semantic (needs the symbol table) = correct D. Everything else (analyzers/ILLink/ApiCompat/CodeQL/clang-format/jitformat/skill-validator) is execution = correct non-goal.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `dotnet/arcade@v1` ruleset (shared with aspnetcore/roslyn) carrying the global.json sdk<->tools coherence, eng/Versions.props presence, the .NET-Foundation MIT file_header preset, and the ApiCompat ref/-sibling convention as a parameterized every_matching_has — the dotnet/* family re-derives all of these per-repo. (2) A `.gitattributes-derived` line_endings mode: a single rule that READS .gitattributes and enforces the declared per-pattern EOL on the working tree (like registry_paths_resolve derives paths from a manifest) — would turn the EOL policy from hand-maintained line_endings rules into a derived check; the one real abstraction this repo's .gitattributes surface suggests. (3) A `comment_prefix:`/multi-language file_header preset (shared need with roslyn) for the //-vs-/* C# vs C++ banner divergence.

*Notes.* The config's flagged gaps (line_endings primitive, final-newline) are now OBSOLETE — both kinds shipped since the PRE-build study draft. Re-tag these as REPLACEABLE in the study log; the config is otherwise honest and conservative (correctly makes the ApiCompat ref/ hint info-level and acknowledges its noise). cross_file_value_equals is an alias for cross_file in current alint — the rule is valid as written.

### `duckdb/duckdb` — C++ systems/analytics-DB monorepo (CMake build, Python codegen, DuckDB Foundation governance)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~70% of addressable (non-execution) surface today (banner-presence, snake_case, test markers, trailing-ws, toolchain-config presence). The self-referential-value half of the header/test-marker checks is the genuine gap; the remaining ~bulk of DuckDB's enforcement (clang-format/tidy, typos, enum-integrity, nm symbol audit, sanitizers, codegen-diff) is execution = NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** DuckDB's enforced surface is overwhelmingly tool-execution. (1) Formatting/style: `make format-check-silent` (Main.yml) drives scripts/format.py, which runs clang-format, black, cmake-format and ALSO REWRITES every src/include/**/*.hpp to start with the canonical '//===---===// // DuckDB' ASCII banner whose line 4 is the file's OWN relative path; scripts/format_test_benchmark.py rewrites every .test/.test_slow sqllogictest to carry `# name: <relative-path>` and `# group: [<dir>]` headers. (2) Static analysis: clang-tidy enforces naming, no-using-namespace, no-const-cast (AST). (3) Spell: `typos` (NLP). (4) C++ semantic integrity: cxxheaderparser-based enum-integrity check; exported/banned-symbol checks via `nm` on compiled .o/.so. (5) Codegen freshness: `make generate-files && git diff --exit-code` runs Python generators and diffs (tool execution). (6) Correctness: the sqllogictest suite + ASan/UBSan/TSan + valgrind + fuzzers. (7) editorconfig declares trim_trailing_whitespace/charset for {c,cpp,h,hpp} (enforced through clang-format). Structural/declarative repo-state conventions: snake_case source filenames (CONTRIBUTING 'Naming Conventions', clang-tidy does NOT check filenames), header-banner presence, sqllogictest header markers, toolchain-config presence (.clang-format/.clang-tidy/.editorconfig).

**What alint replaces.** duckdb-public-header-banner (static banner frame) -> file_header (lines:4, (?m) pattern); duckdb-source-filenames-snake-case -> filename_case case:snake_case; duckdb-test-has-name-header / duckdb-test-has-group-header (presence of `# name:`/`# group:` markers) -> file_content_matches; duckdb-no-trailing-whitespace-cpp -> no_trailing_whitespace; README/LICENSE(MIT)/merge-markers/bidi -> oss-baseline bundle; workflow hygiene -> ci/github-actions bundle; .clang-format/.clang-tidy/.editorconfig presence -> file_exists (value-add).

**What alint improves.** One declarative, schema-validated config replaces the bespoke, mutate-and-diff Python format.py logic for the parts that are pure assertions (banner-present, snake_case, test markers, trailing-ws), and runs on a fresh clone with NO C++ toolchain / no `make` / no clang installed — currently any contributor must build the whole format toolchain to get told their filename isn't snake_case. filename_case is a genuine net-new check (clang-tidy can't see filenames). Cross-tree path scoping (exclude generated list.hpp, third_party, test) is first-class and reviewable vs. globs buried in a Python script.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** (1) PATH-AWARE HEADER CONTENT: the banner's line 4 must contain the file's OWN relative path, and each sqllogictest's `# name:` must equal its own path / `# group:` must equal its own dir. file_header's pattern is a single fixed Regex compiled once (crates/alint-rules/src/file_header.rs:33 `pattern: Regex`) with NO {path}/{stem} template binding — I tried treating it like the for_each_file {path} idiom, but file_header/file_content_matches take a constant pattern, not a per-file path-substituted one, so 'this line must contain THIS file's path' is inexpressible. for_each_file + nested file_content_matches doesn't help either: the nested content rule's pattern is still a literal, it can't reference the iterated {path}. This is the config's explicit 'new-kind candidate' and it reproduces. Closest workable partial is asserting the marker EXISTS (which the config does) — but not that its value is self-consistent. (2) Codegen freshness here is Python-tool execution (`make generate-files`) = command_idempotent/generated_file_fresh territory, but DuckDB's generators are NOT hash-embedding and have no --check mode, so file_graph fresh doesn't fit; it stays a command escape (D-ish), not a structural gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A path-aware content assertion: let file_header / file_content_matches accept a pattern with {path}{stem}{dir}{parent_name} tokens rendered per-file (reuse crate::template::render_path), so 'line N must contain this file's own relative path' becomes one rule. This single primitive closes self-referential headers, sqllogictest name/group lines, AND the common 'module declaration must match file path' check seen across many languages — high leverage, grounded in two independent real DuckDB needs.

*Notes.* Verified empirically that file_header's pattern is constant (no template); confirmed clang-tidy cannot check filenames (so filename_case is additive). The banner-frame rule is correctly scoped to exclude auto-generated list.hpp.

---

### `elastic/elasticsearch` — JVM/Java search-engine (systems/DB) Gradle multi-project monorepo, single-vendor (Elastic B.V.) governance with tri-license model

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of addressable surface (license headers incl. alternation, nocommit/tab forbidden patterns, esql marker, rest-spec schema, root governance/wrapper presence). The dependency-license suffix-rewrite pairing is the one genuine gap; checkstyle/forbidden-apis/third-party-audit/jarHell/split-package/spotless are execution = NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** Bespoke validation is a Gradle 'precommit' plugin suite (build-tools-internal/.../precommit/*) plus buildkite shell gates, almost all tool/bytecode execution: (1) LicenseHeadersTask / Apache-RAT — substring header check with an `approvedLicenses` list (triple AGPL-3.0/SSPL-1.0/Elastic-2.0 default; Elastic-2.0-ONLY under x-pack; plus Apache-2.0, 'Generated', 'Vendored', 'Apache LZ4-Java' exemptions and per-project additionalLicenses). (2) ForbiddenPatternsTask built-ins: 'nocommit/NOCOMMIT', tab char, plus case-mix/per-project rules. (3) DependencyLicensesTask — every dependency jar must have BOTH <dep>-LICENSE.txt AND <dep>-NOTICE.txt in its licenses/ dir (a SHA + name pairing). (4) ValidateRestSpecPlugin — every rest-api-spec/api/**/*.json validated against schema.json. (5) check-esql-generated-headers.sh — every *.md under ESQL fully-generated snippet dirs must start with '% This is generated by ESQL'. (6) gradle-wrapper-validation.yml checksum-validates the committed wrapper jar. (7) Execution-only: checkstyle, forbidden-apis (bytecode), third-party-audit, jarHell, split-package audit, logger-usage, spotless, the test suite.

**What alint replaces.** es-license-header-default-triple / es-license-header-xpack-elastic-only -> file_header (lines:12, alternation regex (A|B|C), per-tree include/exclude); es-forbidden-nocommit / es-forbidden-tabs -> file_content_forbidden; es-esql-generated-doc-header -> file_content_matches (\A-anchored marker); es-rest-api-spec-schema -> json_schema_passes (schema_path); LICENSE.txt/NOTICE.txt/gradle-wrapper.jar/.properties/renovate.json -> file_exists; README/merge-markers/bidi -> oss-baseline bundle.

**What alint improves.** Replaces the Java LicenseHeadersTask substring scan + its scattered approvedLicenses/additionalLicenses config with a single reviewable file_header rule that expresses 'header is one of {triple, Apache-2.0}' as a regex alternation and encodes the generated/vendored/ANTLR/module-info exemptions as explicit excludes (the same alternation idiom the brief documents — already used here, NOT a gap). The whole license+forbidden+esql+rest-spec slice runs on a fresh checkout with NO Gradle/JDK bootstrap (today a clean clone must resolve the entire build-tools-internal plugin classpath before it can tell you a header is missing). json_schema_passes is a like-for-like, lower-overhead replacement for ValidateRestSpecPlugin.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** DEPENDENCY-LICENSE SUFFIX-REWRITE PAIRING: DependencyLicensesTask requires `arrow-LICENSE.txt` to be accompanied by `arrow-NOTICE.txt` (capture the dep name, rewrite the -LICENSE suffix to -NOTICE). I tried every pairing idiom: (a) `pair` partner template — tokens are strictly path components ({dir}{stem}{ext}{basename}{path}{parent_name}, crate::template, 'no regex dependency'); stem of 'arrow-LICENSE.txt' is 'arrow-LICENSE', so {dir}/{stem}.txt resolves to the file itself, and there is NO token to strip '-LICENSE'/inject '-NOTICE'. (b) every_matching_has/for_each_file over `licenses/*-LICENSE.txt` with nested file_exists — same {stem} barrier, the nested path can't reference a sub-capture of the matched name. (c) file_graph no_dangling with a `derive_target` regex edge `(.+)-LICENSE\.txt`->`$1-NOTICE.txt` — derive_target IS the only capture-bearing edge mechanism, but it is hard-coupled to the `fresh` (content-hash) mode (file_graph.rs:383 'Only the from_content modes call no_dangling; the fresh path uses derive_target and never reaches here'), so it cannot be used for a plain existence check. So this co-presence-by-captured-stem is genuinely inexpressible today. (The header alternation and tab/nocommit checks are NOT gaps.)

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) Capture-aware partner template: allow `pair`/`every_matching_has` to match the primary with a regex and expose its captures ({1}{2}…) in the partner/require path, so 'X-LICENSE.txt requires X-NOTICE.txt' is one rule — also covers proto/_pb2 pairings, .d.ts siblings, etc. (2) Equivalently, decouple file_graph's `derive_target` (which already has regex captures) from `fresh` so it composes with `no_dangling`/`no_orphans` — a small change that turns the existing capture machinery into a general 'derived sibling must exist' graph check. Either closes the gap cleanly. (3) A named license-set affordance (declare {triple, apache-2.0, elastic-only} once, reference per-tree) would make the header rules less verbose, but that's a nicety, not a gap.

*Notes.* The config itself half-mislabels the header-alternation as a C-tuning 'gap' — on reproduction it's already solved by the (A|B|C) regex it wrote. The DependencyLicensesTask pairing is the single real B and is well-characterized in the config's comment block.

### `elixir-lang/elixir` — Elixir/Erlang language implementation; umbrella of 6 first-party apps under lib/ (eex/elixir/ex_unit/iex/logger/mix); REUSE/SPDX compliance; ORT-gated CI

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable surface (SPDX presence, LICENSES/ completeness, launcher coherence, CHANGELOG coherence once the idiom is applied, per-app mix.exs, no-binary-by-extension). Remaining: ORT license RESOLUTION + mix-format + CodeQL + reproducible-build = execution NON-GOAL; binary-content-sniff is a value-add nicety.

**Enforcement surface.** (1) REUSE/SPDX compliance (OPEN_SOURCE_POLICY.md + CONTRIBUTING.md, enforced in CI by the OSS Review Toolkit / ScanCode): every file except approved test fixtures must carry SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0 + SPDX-FileCopyrightText; ORT .ort.yml path_excludes exempt test/scripts/pages/examples; LICENSES/ must hold every referenced text (Apache-2.0 + LicenseRef-elixir-trademark-policy + LicenseRef-scancode-unicode). (2) 'No executable binaries' policy (the repo deliberately commits a DECOY fixture `not_really_an.so` that is NOT a real ELF). (3) Version coherence (RELEASE.md): VERSION must equal ELIXIR_VERSION in bin/elixir AND bin/elixir.bat, and CHANGELOG.md must be versioned to the current VERSION (CHANGELOG legitimately carries many version headings). (4) Structure: every umbrella app under lib/ ships a mix.exs. (5) Execution-only (D): mix format, ExUnit/EUnit, shellcheck, markdownlint, ORT/ScanCode license RESOLUTION, CodeQL, zizmor, reproducible-build diff.

**What alint replaces.** elixir-spdx-license-header-ex/-erl + elixir-spdx-copyright-header-ex -> file_header (per-comment-syntax, scoped excludes mirroring .ort.yml); elixir-no-committed-beam -> file_absent (extension globs); elixir-licenses-apache/-trademark/-unicode -> file_exists; elixir-version-bin-launchers-coherent -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals, relation equals, regex extract across bin/elixir{,.bat}); elixir-apps-have-mix-exs -> for_each_dir select lib/* + nested file_exists; README/LICENSE/merge-markers/bidi -> oss-baseline.

**What alint improves.** Reproduces the SPDX-presence + LICENSES/-completeness + launcher-version-coherence + per-app-mix.exs invariants declaratively, running on a fresh clone with NO ORT/ScanCode/Java toolchain (ORT is heavyweight; alint gives the structural subset instantly in CI and locally). cross_file expresses the VERSION->two-launcher coherence as one rule that the repo otherwise only documents in RELEASE.md prose (not separately CI-gated). The CHANGELOG-version coherence (below) is ALSO expressible and would be a net-new gate the repo lacks declaratively.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE among the config's own claims after reproduction. The config marks CHANGELOG version-coherence as a C/gap ('cross_file_value_equals over-fires on the multiple version headings; needs first/topmost-only or major.minor compare') — this is PHANTOM. I verified two independent fixes: (a) a start-anchored dot-all lazy regex extract `(?sm)\A.*?^## (\d+\.\d+\.\d+)` yields EXACTLY ONE capture (the topmost heading) — tested live, naive regex returned 4 versions, the anchored form returned 1; (b) cross_file's `normalize: [semver-minor]` maps VERSION '1.21.0-dev' and the CHANGELOG title 'v1.21' both to '1.21' (tested: semver_minor('1.21.0-dev')=='1.21'). So 'CHANGELOG matches current VERSION' is a clean A via cross_file with first-anchored extract + semver-minor normalize. SEPARATELY, the decoy-`.so` problem is a real-but-soft limitation: file_absent matches by EXTENSION, so it cannot distinguish the committed text decoy `not_really_an.so` from a real ELF — but this is the repo INTENTIONALLY exempting it (the config excludes the fixtures dir), and a true binary-content sniff (ELF/Mach-O magic) is a recognised new-kind candidate, not something the existing surface forces. I do not count it as a hard gap because the policy intent (no real binaries) is approximated and the exemption is correct.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `binary_file_forbidden`/content-magic kind (sniff ELF/Mach-O/PE/ZIP/PK magic bytes) would let 'no committed binaries' be checked by CONTENT not extension, correctly passing text decoys like not_really_an.so while catching a real .bin with no telltale extension — directly serves this repo's stated policy. (2) Documenting the 'first-anchored (?s)\A.*? regex = occurrence:first' idiom as a first-class `occurrence: first` sugar on cross_file extract would stop study agents (and this very config) from re-misclassifying multi-version-CHANGELOG coherence as a gap — pure ergonomics, the capability already exists.

*Notes.* Live-verified both the first-anchored-regex single-capture behavior and semver-minor normalization equality; the config's C-marked CHANGELOG rule should be reclassified A. Header rules correctly use %% vs # comment syntax per Erlang/Elixir.

### `encode/httpx` — Python HTTP client library (hatch/hatchling build); bespoke scripts/ entrypoints (check/test/build/lint/coverage); no pre-commit/git hooks

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the (very small) addressable surface — the lone version<->CHANGELOG coherence check is fully expressible today, plus value-add backend/scripts assertions. Everything else (ruff/mypy/pytest/coverage/twine/mkdocs) is execution = NON-GOAL by design.

**Enforcement surface.** Almost entirely tool execution delegated to scripts/: ruff (lint+format), mypy --strict, pytest, coverage, twine (dist check), mkdocs (docs build) — all category D. The ONLY bespoke cross-file STATE check the repo gates on is scripts/sync-version: the version string in httpx/__version__.py (`__version__ = "X.Y.Z"`) must equal the LATEST RELEASED version in CHANGELOG.md. CHANGELOG.md follows keepachangelog (its header block links keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/ and semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html), then lists the full release history under `## X.Y.Z (date)` headings; sync-version greps semvers and does `sed -n 2p` to skip the 1.0.0 spec-URL match and grab the latest real release. pyproject.toml pins build-backend = hatchling.build (used by scripts/build).

**What alint replaces.** httpx-version-matches-changelog (the disabled/flagged rule) -> cross_file (relation equals) with a first-anchored regex extract on CHANGELOG.md; httpx-scripts-present -> file_exists (value-add, guards CI entrypoints against rename); httpx-pyproject-hatchling-backend -> toml_path_matches ($['build-system']['build-backend']); ruff/black-style hygiene defaults + Python layout -> python bundle; README/LICENSE/merge-markers/bidi -> oss-baseline.

**What alint improves.** Turns the bash-grep-and-sed sync-version heuristic into one declarative, reviewable cross_file rule that runs without invoking the script, and additionally pins the build-backend and asserts the scripts/ entrypoints exist (net-new guards the repo lacks). For a repo with NO pre-commit and a tiny custom-validator footprint, alint provides a standing structural gate that doesn't depend on the hatch environment being bootstrapped.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE. The config's single flagged gap — 'cross_file_value_equals needs occurrence:first|nth (or match_count:1) because CHANGELOG.md has 73 version headers so the rule fires 73x' — is PHANTOM, and is exactly the brief's pre-warned 'occurrence:first' phantom cluster. I reproduced it: the naive extract regex returns ALL 73 versions (confirmed via captures_iter in extract.rs collecting every group-1 match), but a start-anchored dot-all multiline regex `(?sm)\A.*?^## (\d+\.\d+\.\d+) \(` returns EXACTLY ONE value — the latest release — AND, because it keys on the `^## X.Y.Z (` heading FORM, it inherently skips the keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/ URL (which is not a `^## ` line), so it does NOT even need sync-version's `sed -n 2p` fixup. Tested end-to-end: __version__='0.28.1' equals the first-anchored CHANGELOG capture '0.28.1' => the cross_file rule is a clean A, no new selector required. The config left it disabled unnecessarily.

**Abstraction ideas.** Same as elixir: a first-class `occurrence: first` (and `last`/`nth`) sugar on cross_file/regex extract would prevent the recurring misdiagnosis (TWO repos in this batch independently flagged the identical phantom), even though `(?s)\A.*?` already achieves it. Optionally a tiny 'changelog-latest-version-matches-manifest' bundled recipe (cross_file pre-wired with the anchored regex + semver normalize) would be a high-value, copy-paste primitive for the very common version<->CHANGELOG coherence need across Python/JS/Rust libs.

*Notes.* Strongest 'phantom gap' case in the batch: the config explicitly disabled a working rule on a mistaken multi-match premise. Verified the header-form anchor doubles as the keepachangelog-URL skip, eliminating sync-version's sed hack entirely.
