### `eslint/eslint` — JS/Node monorepo — the reference linter; a rule-ecosystem PARITY GRAPH (rule <-> doc <-> test <-> index) + internal-module import firewall + codegen freshness, all orchestrated by a bespoke Makefile.js + flat eslint.config.js.

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of the addressable (non-execution) surface. The parity-graph existence edges, the rule-type JSDoc convention, the import firewall, and the hygiene/policy assertions are all natively expressible; the residual ~20% is the doc-frontmatter<->JS-meta consistency and the index.js set-completeness, both genuine gaps. The huge self-lint + test + doc-example-lint + codegen-diff surface is correctly NON-GOAL (execution, category D).

**Enforcement surface.** eslint's repo-level enforcement is unusually rich and almost all of it is bespoke Node code in Makefile.js plus a self-applied flat ESLint config. By category: (1) STRUCTURE / PARITY GRAPH — checkRuleFiles (Makefile.js) asserts that for every lib/rules/<x>.js there exists docs/src/rules/<x>.md AND tests/lib/rules/<x>.js, that each rule is wired into lib/rules/index.js, and that deprecated/removed rules are handled consistently; checkRuleExamples / checkRuleDocs parse each doc's frontmatter (title, rule_type, the `recommended`/`fixable`/`hasSuggestions` flags) and cross-check them against the rule's exported `meta` object; the docs build also validates that every fenced ```js code sample in a rule doc itself lints clean under that rule. (2) RULE-SOURCE CONTENT CONVENTION — checkRuleFiles requires each rule file carry the `/** @type {import('../types').Rule.RuleModule} */` JSDoc tag (hasRuleTypeJSDocComment) and a leading title-block comment. (3) IMPORT FIREWALL — eslint.config.js wires n/no-restricted-require so that lib/rules/** may not deep-require lib/linter or lib/source-code internals (only each layer's index.js is importable), enforcing the layered architecture. (4) HYGIENE / FORMATTING — self-applied ESLint flat config (eol-last, no-trailing-spaces, the eslint-plugin-* internal rules, jsdoc completeness, unicorn rules) plus Prettier; .editorconfig + .npmrc(`package-lock=false`, lockfiles gitignored). (5) CODEGEN FRESHNESS — generated artefacts (the conf/rule-type-list, the docs rules index, versions data) are regenerated and the working tree must stay clean (a `Makefile.js` target diffs them in CI). (6) LICENSING — Apache-2.0 LICENSE at root; eslint does NOT put per-file SPDX headers, so there is no per-file header gate. (7) COMMIT DISCIPLINE — a commit-message lint (the eslint convention `type(scope): subject` checked by a bot/Action) and CLA. Execution-only: the entire ESLint self-lint, the Mocha+nyc test suite, the doc-example linting (each runs the JS engine), the TypeScript type-build, and the codegen diffs.

**What alint replaces.** checkRuleFiles rule->doc existence edge -> every_matching_has select:'lib/rules/*.js' nested file_exists 'docs/src/rules/{stem}.md'; checkRuleFiles rule->test existence edge -> every_matching_has + file_exists 'tests/lib/rules/{stem}.js'; the RuleModule @type JSDoc requirement -> file_content_matches over lib/rules/*.js (exclude index.js); the n/no-restricted-require architecture firewall (lib/rules must not deep-require lib/linter or lib/source-code) -> import_gate with a custom import_pattern matching require('../linter/...') / require('../source-code/...') + forbid; the package-lock=false install policy assertion -> file_content_matches on .npmrc; node_modules-not-committed (with the tests/fixtures/** carve-out eslint genuinely needs) -> dir_absent with include/exclude; CODEOWNERS / .editorconfig presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** The parity graph is alint's flagship win here: eslint hand-rolls checkRuleFiles as imperative Node that must boot the rules index, glob three trees, and reconcile them; alint expresses the same rule<->doc and rule<->test edges as two declarative every_matching_has rules whose intent is reviewable at a glance and which run with zero Node toolchain (a fork or a downstream rule-pack repo gets the invariant for free). The import firewall is materially better as a custom import_pattern: it targets only runtime `require()` (eslint is CommonJS), so unlike the n/ plugin it needs no eslint runtime and no plugin config, and unlike alint's own js preset (which is comment-aware and would blank a JSDoc `@typedef {import(...)}` anyway) the explicit pattern is self-documenting about exactly which edges are forbidden. dir_absent with a first-class exclude is cleaner than eslint's ad-hoc fixtures ignore. All of these consolidate scattered Makefile.js logic + flat-config plugin wiring into one schema-validated file.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** Two real ones, both narrow. (1) doc-frontmatter <-> rule-meta CONSISTENCY: checkRuleDocs asserts that a rule doc's YAML frontmatter `rule_type`/`recommended`/`fixable` flags AGREE with the value in the rule's exported `meta` object — this is a cross-file equality between a structured value in a .md frontmatter block and a value embedded in arbitrary JS (`module.exports = {meta:{type:'problem',...}}`). cross_file can read the frontmatter side (yaml/regex extract), but the JS-meta side is a JS object literal, not a parseable structured file; you cannot reliably regex `meta.type` across all rule files, and there is no JS-AST extractor. I tried framing it as cross_file with two regex extracts — it falls short because the JS side has no stable textual anchor (meta can be spread, computed, or imported). This is genuinely beyond alint and arguably a NON-GOAL (it needs the JS AST). (2) checkRuleFiles also enforces that lib/rules/index.js ENUMERATES exactly the set of rule files (no missing, no stray) — i.e. set-equality between the basenames under lib/rules/*.js and the keys/requires listed in index.js. The closest idiom is cross_file relation:set_equals with a lines/regex extract on index.js vs ... but the source side would have to be 'the set of filenames matching a glob', and cross_file's source is a single file's extracted values, not a directory listing. registry_paths_resolve handles manifest->disk (and would catch index.js entries with NO file), but NOT the reverse stray-file direction in the same rule, and it resolves PATHS, not a basename set membership against a glob population. So the 'index.js lists exactly the rules present on disk' set-equality is a genuine gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `glob_set` source for cross_file: let the source side of a set relation be 'the set of {stem}s matching select: lib/rules/*.js', so set_equals can compare a directory population to an enumerating manifest (index.js requires). This single primitive would close eslint's index-completeness gap AND generalizes to any 'the barrel file must re-export exactly the modules present' pattern (extremely common in TS/JS monorepos). (2) A bundled `js-rule-ecosystem` or more generally a `barrel-parity` ruleset capturing the rule/doc/test triad + index enumeration as one extends:-able unit — eslint-like plugin repos (eslint plugins, stylelint, prettier ecosystems) all share this exact shape. (3) A lightweight structured extractor for `module.exports = { ... }` / `export default { ... }` object literals (a 'js-object' Format alongside json/yaml/toml/xml) would unlock the frontmatter<->meta consistency check without a full JS AST — high value across the JS ecosystem.

*Notes.* Config is high quality. One correction to its own commentary: the note 'the js preset over-matches JSDoc type-imports' is now partly stale — alint's js import_gate preset IS comment-aware (strip_comments blanks /** */ blocks), so it would NOT flag a JSDoc @typedef import; the custom require()-only import_pattern is still the better choice (more precise, runtime-only), so the rule is correct even though the rationale is dated. eslint moved docs to docs/src/rules/ and tests to tests/lib/rules/ — the {stem} templates in the config match the current layout.

### `etcd-io/etcd` — Go CNCF multi-module workspace (13 go.mod modules under one go.work) built with make + a large hack/scripts/verify_* suite run on Prow.

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of the addressable surface (toolchain coherence x2, shell-tab hygiene, Apache headers, generated-banner, plus all the structure/value-add presence assertions). The remaining enforcement is dominated by execution gates (golangci-lint, gomodguard, shellcheck/yamllint/markdownlint, go mod tidy, govulncheck, codegen diffs) which are correctly NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** etcd's enforcement is a Makefile `verify` umbrella driving a big set of bespoke scripts/verify_*.sh / scripts/test.sh passes, plus golangci-lint config, gomodguard, and codegen. By category: (1) CROSS-FILE VERSION COHERENCE — verify_go_versions.sh asserts the `toolchain go<X.Y.Z>` directive in every go.mod AND in go.work equals the single source of truth .go-version (go.work is special-cased because `go work edit -json` omits toolchain); related: the Go version is also pinned in CI workflow YAML. (2) SHELL HYGIENE — scripts/test.sh shellws_pass forbids tab indentation in all *.sh (mandates two-space), and shellcheck runs repo-wide. (3) LICENSE HEADERS — golangci-lint `goheader` enforces the canonical 'Copyright <yr> The etcd Authors / Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0' header on every .go file against the .header template; the repo ships a full Apache LICENSE but DELIBERATELY no NOTICE. (4) IMPORT FIREWALL — per-module .gomodguard.yaml files declare cross-module dependency bans (e.g. api/ must not import server/); gomodguard enforces on Prow. (5) CODEGEN FRESHNESS — generated protobuf/gRPC, mocks, the go.work file (scripts/update_go_workspace.sh, carries a 'do not edit' banner), and bill-of-materials.json (SBOM, verify-bom regenerates+diffs) must all be clean. (6) DEP HYGIENE — go mod tidy cleanliness, gomodguard, govulncheck, dependency-license bill-of-materials. (7) MARKDOWN/YAML LINT — markdownlint + yamllint. (8) COMMIT DISCIPLINE — DCO Signed-off-by mandated by CONTRIBUTING; CNCF OWNERS-based (Prow) review, not GitHub CODEOWNERS. CI runs on Prow (pull-etcd-verify), NOT GitHub Actions. Execution-only: golangci-lint (the dominant gate), gomodguard, shellcheck, yamllint, markdownlint, go mod tidy diff, govulncheck, license-bill-of-materials, and every codegen-freshness diff.

**What alint replaces.** verify_go_versions.sh toolchain-vs-.go-version coherence (per go.mod) -> cross_file_value_equals source .go-version regex, targets **/go.mod regex on `^toolchain go(...)`; the same for go.work -> a second cross_file_value_equals; shellws_pass no-tab-indentation -> file_content_forbidden pattern '(?m)^[ ]*\t' over **/*.sh; the Apache per-file .go header -> compliance/apache-2 bundle's file_header; the go.work 'do not edit' generated-banner presence -> file_content_matches; .header / .gomodguard.yaml / bill-of-materials.json / DCO / OWNERS presence -> file_exists (value-add structure assertions).

**What alint improves.** The two version-coherence scripts are the clean win: verify_go_versions.sh is a ~bash/jq script that special-cases go.work; alint replaces both with two declarative cross_file rules that need no Go, no jq, and read in any CI (including the GitHub-Actions side etcd doesn't currently use). file_content_forbidden over **/*.sh is more robust than the repo's `find | xargs grep` (handles the empty-match and no-files cases deterministically, integrates with the same report). The Apache header comes free from the bundle rather than golangci-lint goheader config. The whole bundle stack (oss-baseline + go + no-tracked-artifacts + apache-2) gives etcd a baseline it currently assembles from scattered golangci/Makefile pieces.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** Effectively none that are addressable — and one item that LOOKS replaceable but is correctly NON-GOAL: gomodguard's cross-module import firewall. At first glance import_gate covers 'module X must not import module Y', but etcd's gomodguard bans are MODULE-PATH bans (e.g. go.etcd.io/etcd/server/v3 forbidden from go.etcd.io/etcd/api/v3) declared per-module in .gomodguard.yaml; import_gate's go preset extracts the import string and could regex-forbid a module path prefix, so a SUBSET is expressible — but the full gomodguard semantics (allowed-vs-blocked lists per source module, version constraints, recommendation messages) are a tool with its own config, so reimplementing it is a category-D non-goal, not a gap. The toolchain coherence, shell hygiene, and headers are all covered. I also checked whether the SBOM/bill-of-materials freshness could be a generated_file_fresh: it could (a generator + diff), so that is IMPROVABLE rather than a gap, though the config left it as value-add file_exists.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `cncf/governance` or `go/workspace` ruleset: toolchain<->.go-version coherence across go.mod+go.work, the generated-banner-present check, OWNERS/DCO presence, and the apache-2 header — etcd, kubernetes, and most CNCF Go monorepos share this exact shape, so it would replace a lot of per-repo verify_* scripting. (2) A go.mod-aware module extractor (treat go.mod's `module`/`toolchain`/`go` directives as structured fields, like a mod_path_equals) so the toolchain coherence doesn't rely on a hand-written regex and the go.work special-case is handled by the kind, not the user. (3) A first-class generated_file_fresh recipe in the go bundle for `go work edit`/`go mod tidy`/SBOM so codegen-freshness is declarative rather than a Makefile diff.

*Notes.* CONFIG BUG (C-class sharp edge, not an alint gap): both etcd-go-toolchain-matches-go-version and etcd-go-work-toolchain-matches-go-version use message placeholders {{ctx.target}} / {{ctx.target_value}} / {{ctx.source_value}}, but cross_file (verified in crates/alint-rules/src/cross_file.rs — mismatch()/set_violation() clone self.message verbatim and never call template::render_message) does NOT substitute ctx placeholders in a user message. These tokens will render LITERALLY. The fix is to DROP the custom message and let the default fire ('<target> value "X" != .go-version value "Y"', which already embeds path+both values) — i.e. ctx-templated cross_file messages are an authoring footgun the corpus author assumed worked. The 'no NOTICE file' apache-2-notice finding is a real recorded finding, not a config error, as the config notes.

---

### `expressjs/express` — JS web-framework library (npm, OpenJS Foundation governance). Minimal bespoke enforcement — its entire ENFORCED gate set is execution (eslint/mocha/codeql/scorecard).

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% of the ENFORCED surface is addressable — express's enforced denominator is entirely execution (A=B=C=0 over what it actually gates). alint's role here is value-add only: it covers ~100% of the structure/coherence drift-guards a maintainer would want (publish-allowlist integrity, manifest canonicality, header convention, doc-floor coherence), none of which express currently enforces.

**Enforcement surface.** express has an unusually THIN repo-level enforcement surface — deliberately so for a mature library. By category: (1) CI GATES (.github/workflows) — ci.yml runs `npm run lint` (= eslint . over the flat .eslintrc.yml, which itself enforces eol-last, no-trailing-spaces, eqeqeq, indent, no-unused-vars, no-restricted-globals(Buffer)) and `npm run test-ci` (nyc + mocha) across a {ubuntu,windows} x node{18..26} matrix; legacy.yml runs the same tests on older node; coverage uploads to coveralls; codeql.yml runs the CodeQL JS+actions semantic scan; scorecard.yml runs OpenSSF Scorecard. (2) There is NO bespoke shell/script gate, NO husky/pre-commit/lefthook, NO Makefile, NO scripts/tools/ci/hack dir, NO codegen-freshness step, NO version-sync script, and NO per-file header check. (3) The per-file `/*! express ... MIT Licensed */` header exists on every lib/*.js + index.js by convention but is UN-ENFORCED (eslint has no header rule configured). (4) package.json carries a `files:` publish allowlist (LICENSE, Readme.md, index.js, lib/, History.md) that npm pack relies on but nothing validates on disk. (5) The Readme states a 'Node.js N or higher is required' floor that mirrors engines.node but is not gated. So express's ENFORCED ADDRESSABLE surface is essentially EMPTY — every gate it runs is a category-D execution non-goal (lint/test/coverage/codeql/scorecard). Everything alint adds here is value-add drift-guard the maintainer would want but hasn't hand-rolled.

**What alint replaces.** There is almost nothing bespoke to NATIVELY replace because express runs no bespoke checks — alint is purely additive value-add here. What alint ADDS (not 'replaces'): package.json name/license/repository canonicality -> json_path_matches; engines.node floor -> json_path_matches; Readme floor <-> engines.node drift -> cross_file_value_equals; `files:` publish-allowlist on-disk integrity -> per-path file_exists (LICENSE/Readme.md/index.js); core lib module presence -> for_each_dir select:'lib' + nested file_exists; the un-enforced MIT header -> file_header over index.js+lib/**/*.js; eslint config presence smoke check -> file_exists. The eslint hygiene rules (eol-last, no-trailing-spaces) COULD be lifted to alint's final_newline + no_trailing_whitespace if the maintainer wanted them enforced outside the eslint run, but express deliberately keeps them inside eslint.

**What alint improves.** Because express's bespoke surface is empty, the 'improvement' is entirely about adding the cross-file/structure coherence eslint-the-linter structurally cannot express: (1) the `files:` publish-allowlist integrity is the standout — npm silently ships a broken tarball if a listed path is renamed/deleted, and no existing express gate catches it; alint's per-path file_exists makes the publish manifest self-checking. (2) the Readme node-floor <-> engines.node coherence catches doc drift no test covers. (3) the per-file MIT header (file_header) locks a convention eslint isn't configured to enforce. These are net-new guarantees, each one a single reviewable rule, with no toolchain. The config also correctly demonstrates two sharp-edge idioms (file_exists multi-path is OR -> split into per-path AND; root_only would break lib/ subpaths -> omitted).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None addressable. The only thing alint can't do here is what it shouldn't: run eslint/mocha/codeql/scorecard (category-D execution). I checked the `files:` allowlist for a deeper expression — ideally one would assert 'every glob in package.json `files:` resolves on disk' directly FROM the manifest (so adding a new entry is auto-covered) rather than hand-mirroring three paths; registry_paths_resolve does exactly manifest->disk path resolution, and `files:` is a JSON array of path-ish globs, so this is largely EXPRESSIBLE via registry_paths_resolve with a json extract on $.files[*] — meaning the config's hand-split per-path rules are actually a missed idiom, not evidence of a gap. (The one caveat: `files:` entries can be globs like 'lib/' and registry_paths_resolve resolves literal paths / directories, so glob entries would need the dir to exist; for express's concrete entries this works.) So: no genuine gap; one IMPROVABLE-on-the-config note.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `npm-publish-integrity` ruleset: package.json `files:`/`main`/`exports`/`bin` targets must all resolve on disk (registry_paths_resolve on each), `private` consistency, license<->LICENSE coherence — every published npm library needs this and none hand-roll it. (2) A package.json-aware convenience that treats `files:`/`main`/`exports` as registry sources out of the box (a node-bundle option) so the maintainer writes one line, not three file_exists rules. (3) A generic 'doc-prose <-> manifest value' coherence helper (the Readme-floor pattern) bundled for node/python, since 'the README states a version that drifts from the manifest' is near-universal.

*Notes.* Config is correct and a good demonstration of the OR-vs-AND and root_only sharp edges. The cross_file_value_equals for the Readme floor uses NO custom-message ctx placeholders, so it (unlike etcd) will render its default message correctly. Improvement opportunity for the config itself: collapse the three express-publish-*-exists rules into one registry_paths_resolve over package.json $.files[*] (and $.main) so the rule auto-tracks allowlist edits.

### `facebook-react` — JS/Flow Yarn-classic-v1 multi-package monorepo (39 packages, 22 published) + self-contained compiler/ workspace; React core

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of addressable (non-execution) surface; the remainder is the codes.json append-only/membership pair (1 genuine gap + 1 AST-bound non-goal). The large eslint/flow/jest/danger surface is category-D execution, correctly orchestrated not reimplemented.

**Enforcement surface.** react's structural/CI enforcement is broad and tool-heavy. (license-headers) scripts/ci/check_license.sh = `git grep PATENTS` asserting the 2017 Meta relicense removed all PATENTS references; the standard 6-line Meta copyright + MIT block prepended to every published bundle by scripts/rollup/wrappers.js and expected on hand-edited packages/*/src/**.js. (hygiene) prettier-check (yarn prettier-check), trailing/format via prettier; no tracked build/coverage dirs (every CI workflow `rm -rf build` before install). (structure/layout) per-package package.json must have repository.directory==packages/<own-name> (packages/react-refresh has a known copy-paste regression pointing at packages/react), homepage==https://react.dev, semver-only version; root MAINTAINERS file read by shared_check_maintainer.yml; .nvmrc single vX.Y.Z; .gitattributes `* text=auto`. (cross-file coherence) scripts/tasks/version-check.js asserts ReactVersion.js's `export default '<semver>'` equals $.version in react/react-dom/react-test-renderer package.json; ReactVersions.js `const ReactVersion` must equal ReactVersion.js; packageManager pinned yarn@1. (codegen-freshness) yarn extract-errors regenerates scripts/error-codes/codes.json (flat {<digits>:<msg>}) by crawling BUILD artefacts; codes.json is a documented APPEND-ONLY numeric registry (~600 entries, only human git-diff review enforces append-only). (import/config-firewall) .eslintrc.js must register the in-tree 'react-internal' plugin and parser:'hermes-eslint'; root package.json keeps the `link:./scripts/eslint-rules` dep; .eslintignore lists **/node_modules + build/; canonical script names (lint/prettier-check/flow-ci/version-check) referenced by workflows by name. (security) every .github/workflows/*.yml declares top-scope `permissions: {}`; workflow name: carries a (Runtime)/(Compiler)/(DevTools)/(Shared) category prefix matching the filename. (commit-discipline) react squash-merges; no fixup!/squash! on main (only implicit maintainer review + scripts/git/pre-commit eslint-on-staged). (execution-only, D) the 5 in-tree TSESTree eslint rules (no-primitive-constructors, no-production-logging, prod-error-codes, safe-string-coercion, warning-args); flow/flow-ci type checker; jest test*; scripts/print-warnings hermes-AST walk + test_print_warnings.sh; dangerfile.js (PR-diff bundle-size regression vs base build); lint-build (second eslint pass over built bundles with per-channel eslintrcs).

**What alint replaces.** version-check.js cross-source equality -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals, source regex extract vs three json_path targets); ReactVersions.js<->ReactVersion.js constant parity -> cross_file regex/regex; codes.json shape (flat numeric->string map) -> json_path_matches $.* + file_content_forbidden for JSONC comments; react/package.json#files resolve on disk -> registry_paths_resolve (entries_are_globs, base, expect:file); per-package repository.directory==own subdir -> for_each_dir select packages/* + nested json_path_matches matches:'^packages/{basename}$' (the value-predicate parity idiom, done correctly); homepage/version per package -> same for_each_dir + json_path_matches; PATENTS firewall -> file_content_forbidden (with check_license.sh + lockfiles + CHANGELOG excluded); Meta copyright header -> file_header (20-line default window, ^/\*\* anchored); .nvmrc/.gitattributes/.eslintrc/.eslintignore/.prettierrc discipline -> file_content_matches + file_exists; canonical script-name guards -> json_path_matches on $.scripts.*; MAINTAINERS present+nonempty -> file_exists + file_min_lines; no tracked build/coverage -> dir_absent; workflow category prefix + permissions:{} -> yaml_path_matches $.name + file_content_matches; no fixup!/squash! in PR range -> git_commit_no_fixup (scope_filter.changed_since); eslint/prettier/flow/print-warnings orchestration -> command

**What alint improves.** One declarative `alint check` consolidates yarn lint + prettier-check + flow-ci + version-check + check_license.sh + the structural floor that today is scattered across 23 workflow YAMLs, scripts/ci/*.sh, scripts/git/pre-commit, and scripts/tasks/version-check.js. version-check.js (a bespoke node script the v0.9.17 example had to shell out to) becomes a 12-line static cross_file rule with no node runtime. The repository.directory parity check is cross-FILE coherence no single eslint rule expresses today — and it would have caught the live packages/react-refresh regression. The `permissions: {}` defence-in-depth convention and the (Category) workflow-name prefix are conventions react enforces only by reviewer habit; alint turns them into schema-validated gates. PATENTS as file_content_forbidden is reviewable in one place vs a grep buried in a shell script only check_license.sh self-excludes.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** Two, both narrow and both correctly flagged in the config's footer. (1) codes.json APPEND-ONLY-ACROSS-GIT-REVISIONS: the invariant is 'this revision's key set is a strict superset of the parent commit's, with no key reassigned.' I tried cross_file set relations (only compares files in the working tree, no prior-git-rev side), file_graph fresh (content-hash freshness, not key-set monotonicity), and pair_hash (digest, not set delta) — none reaches across git history to a prior blob, so this is a true gap (a `registry_append_only`/git-blob-set-superset kind). (2) prod-error-codes' codes.json half: 'every `new Error(<string-literal>)` in packages/** is a key in codes.json.' cross_file subset/superset CAN do N-in-1 set membership, BUT only when BOTH sides are statically extractable; here the source set is AST-extracted JS-string-literal arguments (which arm of which call), which requires a JS AST — that extraction is a category-D NON-GOAL, so the membership rule as a whole is not alint-expressible. The reverse (every codes.json value is referenced) is also AST-bound. lint-build/dangerfile/print-warnings are pure execution/PR-diff = NON-GOAL, not gaps.

**Abstraction ideas.** 1) `git_registry_monotonic` / append-only kind: a JSON/line registry's key (or line) set must be a superset of the same path at `since:` (a base git ref), optionally forbidding value-reassignment of existing keys — reuses the since..HEAD machinery already in changeset_requires_path/commit_range; directly serves codes.json and any append-only ID ledger (Misc/NEWS.d-style, error-code tables). 2) A `prev_git_rev` extract source for cross_file (extract the SAME path at a base ref) would generalize (1) and let cross_file subset express 'no key removed since main.' 3) A bundled `meta/react-internal` or generic `monorepo/version-single-source` ruleset capturing the ReactVersion single-source-of-truth + per-package parity pattern (it recurs in every JS monorepo). All grounded; nothing here needs an AST.

*Notes.* Config is mature and mostly accurate. The for_each_dir + json_path_matches value-predicate parity usage is exemplary. One nuance: react-error-codes-fresh (generated_file_fresh) is honestly marked info/advisory because extract-errors crawls BUILD artefacts absent in a clean checkout — correct call; could equally be generated_file_fresh outputs-mutating mode, but stdout mode with --stdout is fine. cross_file_value_equals is now an ALIAS of unified cross_file (per the capability brief) — the config's kind name still works.

### `facebook/docusaurus` — TS docs-site framework monorepo (yarn + lerna; packages/* workspaces). Enforces bespoke STRUCTURE + per-package manifest invariants via __tests__/validate-*.test.ts plus eslint/stylelint header plugins.

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the addressable (non-execution) surface — the per-package repository/license/tsconfig manifest invariants, the name<->dir parity, the MIT headers (JS/TS + CSS), the editorconfig hygiene trio, the monorepo shape, and the dictionary header are all natively expressible. The remainder is execution (eslint/stylelint/tsc/Jest/cspell run + docs build + visual e2e), correctly NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** docusaurus's repo-level enforcement is split between Jest validation tests, eslint/stylelint plugins, and editorconfig/gitattributes. By category: (1) PER-PACKAGE MANIFEST INVARIANTS (Jest, __tests__/validate-package-json.test.ts) — every packages/* package.json must declare repository.type=git, repository.url = the canonical https://github.com/facebook/docusaurus.git, and repository.directory == that package's own path; the test also checks naming (@docusaurus/* scope), `publishConfig.access`, `license: MIT`, the `engines.node` floor, that `main`/`types`/`exports` fields are coherent, and (for published packages) presence of a README. (2) TSCONFIG INVARIANTS (validate-tsconfig.test.ts) — every packages/*/tsconfig.json must extend one of the two shared bases (../../tsconfig.base.json or ../../tsconfig.base.client.json). (3) MIT HEADER — CONTRIBUTING.md §Licensing mandates the 4-line MIT header on every new source file; eslint-plugin-header enforces it on JS/TS and stylelint docusaurus/copyright-header enforces it on CSS. (4) TEXT HYGIENE — .editorconfig (trim_trailing_whitespace, insert_final_newline) + .gitattributes (LF) + the formatter (oxfmt/prettier). (5) MONOREPO STRUCTURE — every packages/* has package.json + README.md (the monorepo bundle's shape); lerna/yarn workspaces. (6) SPELLING — a CI-managed project-words.txt dictionary (cspell) with a mandatory 'DO NOT TOUCH' header line. (7) The big execution gates: eslint (with the header plugin + TS rules), stylelint, tsc type-check, the full Jest suite, prettier/oxfmt, the docs build, and the e2e/argos visual tests. CI is GitHub Actions.

**What alint replaces.** validate-package-json repository.type/url/directory invariants -> three for_each_dir select:'packages/*' + nested json_path_equals (with equals:'{path}' for the directory == own-path case — the value-predicate-parity idiom); per-package license MIT -> json_path_equals; validate-tsconfig extends-allowlist -> json_path_matches with if_present + a (base|base.client) alternation regex; the MIT header on TS+CSS -> file_header (pattern 'This source code is licensed under the MIT license', lines:8) with __tests__/__fixtures__/*.d.ts excludes; trailing-whitespace/LF/final-newline -> no_trailing_whitespace + line_endings + final_newline over packages/*/src; project-words.txt 'DO NOT TOUCH' header -> file_header lines:1; the monorepo per-package package.json+README shape -> the monorepo bundle.

**What alint improves.** The per-package manifest invariants are the headline win and a textbook value-predicate-parity case: docusaurus hand-writes a Jest test that loads every package.json and reconciles repository.directory against the package's path; alint expresses 'repository.directory == {path}' as one for_each_dir + json_path_equals equals:'{path}' rule that needs no Jest, no ts-node, and reads in any CI — and the canonical-url / type=git / license invariants collapse to three more one-liners. The tsconfig extends-allowlist (json_path_matches + if_present) replaces another bespoke validate-*.test.ts. The header enforcement comes free of the eslint-plugin-header + stylelint-copyright DUAL plugin setup — one file_header rule covers JS/TS and one covers CSS, no plugin install. The hygiene rules lift the .editorconfig intent into an enforceable gate that doesn't depend on contributors' editors honoring editorconfig. Net: three Jest validation files + two header plugins consolidate into ~10 declarative, reviewable rules.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None addressable — and notably several apparent gaps dissolve under the idioms. (1) 'repository.directory == its own path' looks relational but is exactly value-predicate-parity (for_each_dir + json_path_equals equals:'{path}'), already in the config. (2) validate-package-json also asserts the package NAME matches its directory (@docusaurus/<dir> for packages/<dir>) — this is ALSO expressible: for_each_dir select:'packages/*' + json_path_equals path:'$.name' equals:'@docusaurus/{basename}' (the {basename} token gives the leaf dir name). The config didn't include it, but it's an idiom, not a gap. (3) the 'every published (non-private) package has a README' is monorepo-bundle shape OR for_each_dir + file_exists. (4) coherence between `main`/`types`/`exports` targets and files on disk -> registry_paths_resolve per package. The only things alint genuinely can't do are execution: eslint/stylelint AST rules, tsc, Jest, cspell's actual spell-check (project-words.txt PRESENCE/header is checkable, but running cspell is category-D). So: no genuine gap; only NON-GOALs.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `package-name-matches-directory` parity shorthand (or a documented for_each_dir + {basename} recipe) bundled for monorepos — '@scope/<dir> name == packages/<dir>' is near-universal in lerna/nx/turbo/yarn-workspace repos and currently takes a hand-written validate test everywhere. (2) Extend the monorepo bundle with optional per-package manifest-coherence rules (repository block canonicality, license uniformity, extends-allowlist for tsconfig) gated behind options, so docusaurus-class repos get validate-package-json/validate-tsconfig for free. (3) A `registry_paths_resolve`-backed 'package entry-points resolve' rule wired into the node/monorepo bundle (main/types/exports/bin per package.json) to catch the broken-publish class declaratively. (4) A shared 'license-header' axis: pick {MIT, Apache, ...} once and have file_header patterns + per-language scoping (JS/TS/CSS/MD) generated, instead of one rule per file family.

*Notes.* Config is well-built and correctly uses if_present on the tsconfig rule (so packages without a tsconfig.json don't false-positive) and the {path}/{basename} interpolation for the directory-parity invariant. No ctx-message footgun here (the json_path_equals/for_each_dir messages are static). One easy add the config omits: the @docusaurus/{basename} name<->directory parity rule, which validate-package-json does enforce and which is fully expressible. The ignore: block (node_modules/lib/build/.docusaurus/__fixtures__/__mocks__/examples/versioned_docs) is exactly right for keeping the header + hygiene rules off generated/vendored trees.

### `fastapi-fastapi` — Python web framework, single package fastapi/ (pdm-backend build, uv lockfile), docs-translation monorepo, community governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~10-15% addressable; the repo is value-add-mostly. Of the genuinely-addressable handful (2 codegen-freshness, 1 structure invariant, 2 text-hygiene, manifest assertions) alint covers ~95% — the only true miss is the ADD-delta byte cap. The rest (ruff/mypy/ty/pytest/typos/zizmor/codspeed) is correctly category-D.

**Enforcement surface.** fastapi's enforced surface is DOMINATED by execution/semantic gates (category D); the addressable repo-STATE subset is genuinely small. (hygiene, addressable) .pre-commit-config.yaml (mirrored in CI via pre-commit.yml): trailing-whitespace, end-of-file-fixer, check-added-large-files --maxkb=750 (excludes uv.lock) which caps the size of NEWLY-ADDED files. (codegen-freshness, addressable) scripts/docs.py generate-readme regenerates README.md from docs/en/docs/index.md + docs/en/data/sponsors.yml and exits 1 on drift (pre-commit `generate-readme`); scripts/docs.py update-languages rewrites docs/en/mkdocs.yml extra.alternate from on-disk language dirs + docs/language_names.yml, exits 1 on drift (`update-languages`). (structure invariant, addressable) scripts/docs.py ensure-non-translated forbids EN-only sections (reference/, release-notes.md, management*.md, contributing.md, translations.md, fastapi-people.md, external-links.md, newsletter.md) from appearing under any non-`en` docs/<lang>/docs/ tree (pre-commit `ensure-non-translated`). (manifest, value-add) pyproject [project].license==MIT, version dynamic from fastapi/__init__.py (pdm), .python-version bare 3.x pin read by workflows. (execution-only, D) scripts/lint.sh = mypy + ty + ruff check + ruff format --check; pytest at 100% coverage; typos; zizmor workflow-security lint; codspeed benchmarks; guard-dependencies workflow. These are the bulk of the gate and are all D non-goals.

**What alint replaces.** README codegen freshness -> command_idempotent (or, better, generated_file_fresh outputs-mutating mode — the generator writes in place and exits nonzero on drift, which is exactly generated_file_fresh's mutating contract); mkdocs.yml languages freshness -> command_idempotent / generated_file_fresh; ensure-non-translated 'no EN-only page under a non-en tree' -> file_absent; trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace (+ file_trim_trailing_whitespace fix); end-of-file-fixer -> final_newline (+ file_append_final_newline fix); pyproject license/version/python-version manifest assertions -> toml_path_equals / toml_path_matches / file_content_matches; language_names.yml present -> file_exists; lint.sh + zizmor orchestration -> command

**What alint improves.** alint is the single entrypoint that runs ruff/mypy/ty/pytest/zizmor (currently each a separate pre-commit hook + CI job) AND adds schema-validated manifest assertions the repo does not gate today (license==MIT, dynamic version, python-version pin shape). The two codegen-freshness checks become declarative and tool-version-pinned via timeout. The structure invariant (ensure-non-translated), currently a 40-line python function (scripts/docs.py), collapses to ONE file_absent rule (see gaps note — the config's 9-pattern enumeration is over-verbose and should be a single include+exclude).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE that are true gaps. Two items the config mislabels deserve correction under the reproduce-first lens: (A) The config asserts 'alint has no file_max_size' — FALSE as of the current build: file_max_size with max_bytes EXISTS. The only genuinely-unexpressible part is check-added-large-files's DELTA-ON-ADD semantics (it caps only files the changeset ADDS, not the existing tree). I tried changeset_requires_path (asserts a path was ADDED, status A, but carries no size predicate) and file_max_size (static over the whole tree, no add-delta) — combining them can't gate 'an ADDED file's size' because no kind intersects 'git-added set' with 'byte size.' That intersection is the one real (very narrow) gap; the practical substitute (file_max_size over the tracked tree) is fully expressible and arguably better (catches oversized files that predate the cap). (B) ensure-non-translated is NOT a gap and NOT a limitation: the config enumerates 11 language dirs via brace expansion claiming 'a bare docs/*/docs/reference/** over-matches the legitimate EN source' — but Scope supports include+exclude with `!`-prefix excludes and literal_separator(true) `*` (one path component), so a SINGLE file_absent with include `docs/*/docs/reference/**` + exclude `docs/en/docs/reference/**` (and one line per EN-only page) is the correct, maintainable form. The enumeration is verbosity, not a missing capability — it should be simplified (IMPROVABLE). lint.sh/pytest/zizmor are category-D, not gaps.

**Abstraction ideas.** 1) A `file_max_size` add-delta mode (`since:` + max_bytes, status-A only) would natively reproduce check-added-large-files and is the single concrete capability this repo exposes — small, reuses the commit_range diff machinery + file_max_size's stat path. 2) A docs-translation bundled ruleset (`docs/i18n-mirror`) capturing 'EN-only sections must not appear under non-EN trees' + 'mkdocs.yml alternate matches on-disk langs' — fastapi, and many mkdocs-material i18n repos, share this exact shape; the on-disk-langs<->mkdocs parity is a cross_file resolves/set_equals candidate worth a first-class option. Nothing else; the repo is ~all execution.

*Notes.* Two stale claims to fix: file_max_size now EXISTS (the config predates it), and ensure-non-translated should be one include+exclude file_absent not a brace enumeration. A1/A2 could move from command_idempotent to generated_file_fresh outputs-mutating mode (the canonical codegen-freshness kind) now that mutating mode shipped in v0.12 — both work; generated_file_fresh is the more on-the-nose idiom and keeps the tree pure via snapshot/restore.

---

### `fastlane-fastlane` — Ruby mobile-CI CLI; single-gem monorepo of ~19 tool subdirs (cert/deliver/gym/match/...), bundler + RSpec + RuboCop

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable surface (the four debug greps, both layering greps, bin/ invariant, branding forbidden-forms, release-file existence are all native). The unaddressable remainder is the branding case-sensitivity-with-exceptions arm (markdown-context = non-goal) plus the all-execution RuboCop/rspec/shellcheck/Danger gate (correctly category-D).

**Enforcement surface.** fastlane's REAL gate is .circleci/config.yml + .github/workflows. (content firewall, addressable) fastlane/Fastfile lint_source lane runs ensure_no_debug_code, which is literally `grep -RE '<text>' <path> --include=*.rb --exclude-dir .bundle --exclude-dir vendor` for each of: binding.(irb|pry) (debug breakpoints), '# TODO', 'now: ' (RSpec focus marker that would silently narrow the suite), '<<<<<<<' (merge-conflict marker). (module-boundary firewall, addressable) ensure_no_debug_code(text:' UI\.', path:'spaceship/lib') and the same for credentials_manager/lib — both sit BELOW fastlane_core in the dependency stack and must not call its UI. facility; this is a layering/import firewall expressed as a token-presence grep scoped to a module dir. (structure, addressable) module_test.yml asserts the built gem's spec.executables == [bin-proxy, fastlane, match_file], derived from bin/* minus console (fastlane.gemspec) — so bin/ must hold exactly {bin-proxy, fastlane, match_file, console}. (branding, mostly addressable) CONTRIBUTING.md 'Branding' + fastlane validate_docs -> ensure_tool_name_formatting (helper/tool_name_formatting_helper.rb) over **/*.md: _fastlane_ lowercase+italic in prose, never `fastlane`/**fastlane**/<code>fastlane</code>/[fastlane]/<ins>fastlane</ins>; the helper ALSO does a nuanced case-sensitivity scan WITH env-var/module-name exceptions. (release, value-add) CHANGELOG.latest.md read by the release pipeline; Gemfile.lock committed (application gem). (execution-only, D) internal/rubocop/*.rb = 3 custom syntax-tree RuboCop cops; rubocop itself; rspec via rake test_all (execute_tests); shellcheck of tracked *.sh/*.bash; Dangerfile PR-meta warnings (size/WIP/changelog) operate on the PR diff; the Ruby-3.0 deprecation grep runs on a RUNTIME test artifact (test-reports/ruby_warnings.txt), not the tree; validate_fastlane_swift_generation builds Actions.swift (NOT git-diff freshness).

**What alint replaces.** the four ensure_no_debug_code greps -> file_content_forbidden over **/*.rb (vendor/.bundle/playground.rb excluded) — these are LITERAL grep-to-native one-to-one mappings (binding.(irb|pry), # TODO, now: , <<<<<<<; the merge-conflict one could also be no_merge_conflict_markers); the two UI. layering firewalls -> file_content_forbidden scoped to spaceship/lib + credentials_manager/lib (also legitimate file_graph edge sources); bin/ executables invariant -> dir_only_contains allow:[bin-proxy,fastlane,match_file,console] (basename match); branding forbidden-forms -> file_content_forbidden over **/*.md (CONTRIBUTING.md + CHANGELOG.latest.md excluded); CHANGELOG.latest.md / Gemfile.lock present -> file_exists; rubocop/rspec/shellcheck orchestration -> command

**What alint improves.** Consolidates the lint_source lane (a bespoke Ruby Fastfile method shelling out to N greps) and the module_test.yml executables assertion into one reviewable declarative file, runnable WITHOUT a Ruby/bundler toolchain (the greps and the bin/ check need no Ruby at all today they ride inside `fastlane lint_source`). The UI. layering firewall is real architectural enforcement that today hides inside an ensure_no_debug_code text-grep; expressing it as a named import/layering rule (file_content_forbidden today, or import_gate/file_graph forbidden_edges for the require-graph form) makes the intent legible. dir_only_contains makes the gem's CLI surface a first-class assertion rather than an implicit consequence of a gemspec glob + a CI grep.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE blocking, ONE sharp-edge correctly flagged. The branding helper's CASE-SENSITIVITY arm — it accepts the literal token `fastlane` in specific contexts (environment-variable names, Ruby module/constant identifiers like Fastlane::, code spans documenting the bad forms) while forbidding it in prose. I tried file_content_forbidden with a negative-lookbehind/lookahead to carve out ENV_FASTLANE_/Fastlane::/code-fence contexts, but the exception set is open-ended and context-sensitive (is this `fastlane` inside a fenced code block, an env var, a module path, or prose?), which is genuinely a tokenizer/markdown-AST judgement = category-D, not a regex the rule can carry robustly. The config wisely enforces only the unambiguous forbidden-FORMS arm (`fastlane`/**fastlane**/<code>/<ins>) and leaves the case-sensitivity-with-exceptions arm to the Ruby helper. That is a correct NON-GOAL boundary, not an addressable gap. RuboCop cops / rspec / shellcheck / Dangerfile / ruby_warnings.txt grep / swift-generation build = all category-D execution.

**Abstraction ideas.** 1) The UI. firewall begs for the require-GRAPH form: a bundled-or-config import_gate language:ruby preset (forbid `require 'fastlane_core'` / a UI. token from a lower layer) or file_graph forbidden_edges modeling the spaceship -> fastlane_core layering — today it is a brittle ` UI\.` text grep that false-negatives on `UI .` and false-positives on the substring; a named layering rule is materially better and reusable across the 19 tool subdirs. 2) A `prose_forbidden` / markdown-context-aware content rule (forbid a token in prose but not in fenced code / inline code / link targets) would dissolve the branding case-sensitivity sharp-edge for this and many docs-style guides — a real, recurring need, though it edges toward a light markdown parse. 3) A bundled ruby/bundler ruleset (Gemfile.lock committed for application gems, bin/ executables fixed, no debug breakpoints) capturing exactly this repo's ruby-hygiene floor.

*Notes.* Strong, honest config — the grep-to-file_content_forbidden mappings are exact, and the sharp-edge note on the branding helper is accurate. fl-no-merge-conflict-markers could use the dedicated no_merge_conflict_markers kind (scans more conflict-marker variants than the single '<<<<<<<' literal). The two UI. rules are correctly identified as file-graph edge sources for the broader study.

---

### `flutter-flutter` — Dart UI SDK megamonorepo (~6400 Dart files under packages/*); LUCI .ci.yaml test matrix + dev/bots/analyze.dart custom 'verify*' suite

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of addressable surface (BSD header, three import firewalls' forward arm, two content conventions, gen_defaults existence, governance-file + pubspec structure are native). The unaddressable remainder is the import-allowlist reverse/burndown arm (1 genuine gap) plus the token-template regeneration + dart analyze + custom_rules dep-graph (all category-D execution).

**Enforcement surface.** flutter's dominant gate is execution (the .ci.yaml LUCI matrix, `dart analyze`, the custom analyzer lints under dev/bots/custom_rules, the dep-graph allowlist) — but dev/bots/analyze.dart's verify* family has a sizable repo-STATE subset. (license-headers, addressable) verifyNoMissingLicense/_generateLicense requires the fixed BSD block 'Copyright 2014 The Flutter Authors. All rights reserved.' + 'Use of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license...' on every source file, per-extension (the Dart cohort is ~6400 files); excludes .tmpl, generated_plugin_registrant.dart, *.g.dart, analyzer test-input fixtures, test_fixes/ data. (import firewalls, addressable) check_tests_cross_imports.dart: tests outside test/material + test/cupertino must not import package:flutter/material.dart (carries a knownXxxCrossImports allowlist of pre-existing violations being burned down, issue 177028); verifyNoBadImportsInFlutterTools: packages/flutter_tools/lib/** must not import itself by its own package:flutter_tools/ URI; _findFlutterDependencies checkForMeta: the foundation library must not import package:meta/meta.dart directly (re-export via foundation.dart), framework-internal. (content, addressable) verifyNoCheckedMode: forbid 'checked mode' (use 'debug mode'); verifyIssueLinks: no bare https://github.com/flutter/flutter/issues/new link without /choose or ?template= (skips analyze.dart itself + *_test.dart). (codegen-freshness, partly) verifyTokenTemplatesUpdateCorrectFiles: every materialLib target named in gen_defaults.dart must exist AND be regenerated (the regeneration arm is execution). (structure, value-add) every workspace package has a pubspec.yaml; root PATENT_GRANT / CODEOWNERS / TESTOWNERS governance files. (execution-only, D) the LUCI .ci.yaml matrix; dart analyze; dev/bots/custom_rules/* analyzer-API lints; the dependency-graph allowlist resolution; the gen_defaults RE-generation + token-template-content arm.

**What alint replaces.** BSD license header -> file_header (lines:3, ^Copyright 2014 The Flutter Authors anchored; excludes generated/.tmpl/fixtures); the three import firewalls -> import_gate language:dart (anchored ^\s*(?:import|export) ['\"]...; forbid regex on the EXTRACTED target; allow:[...] for the cross-import burndown list, the flutter_tools self-import, the meta carve-out via paths exclude of foundation/); checked-mode + bare-new-issue-link -> file_content_forbidden (with analyze.dart + *_test.dart excluded); gen_defaults generator-exists -> file_exists; every package has pubspec.yaml -> every_matching_has select packages/*/ + nested file_exists; PATENT_GRANT/CODEOWNERS/TESTOWNERS -> file_exists; dart analyze + custom_rules orchestration -> command

**What alint improves.** The three import firewalls become declarative, language-aware import_gate rules (regex on the EXTRACTED import target, not a brittle raw-line grep) consolidating logic that today lives across three separate hand-written Dart functions in analyze.dart, runnable WITHOUT the Dart SDK. The BSD header over ~6400 files is a single file_header rule vs analyze.dart's bespoke per-extension generator/matcher. The cross-import burndown allowlist (knownXxxCrossImports) maps cleanly onto import_gate's allow: so the firewall stays clean against the pin and only catches NEW violations — exactly flutter's intent. checked-mode/issue-link conventions become reviewable one-line rules.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE real, correctly flagged: the import-firewall BURNDOWN REVERSE arm. flutter's check_tests_cross_imports.dart not only forbids NEW cross-imports but also asserts that every entry in the knownXxxCrossImports allowlist is STILL a real violation — i.e. once a file is fixed it must be REMOVED from the allowlist (the list may not contain stale/now-clean entries), forcing the burndown to monotonically shrink. I tried import_gate (its `allow` is permissive — a stale allow-entry for a now-clean file is silently fine, never flagged), and I could find no kind that asserts 'this allow/exception entry is now UNUSED and must be deleted.' That 'exceptions must not go stale' / unused-suppression-detection is a genuine gap (a cross-cutting need: every burndown allowlist, every // ignore: lint suppression, every nolint has it). The token-template CONTENT/regeneration arm of verifyTokenTemplates and dart analyze / custom_rules are execution = NON-GOAL, not gaps; gen_defaults is only structurally (file_exists) checkable here, which the config honestly marks.

**Abstraction ideas.** 1) An `assert_no_stale_exceptions` capability — a shared option on import_gate (and file_content_forbidden / any allow-bearing rule): `allow_must_be_used: true` flags any allow/exception glob that matched ZERO would-be violations (the entry is now dead and should be deleted). This single primitive reproduces flutter's burndown-reverse arm AND generalizes to unused-lint-suppression detection across the whole tool — high-value, reuses the matcher's hit-tracking. 2) A first-class burndown/baseline concept (a tracked allowlist file the rule both consults AND prunes-checks) would make monotonic-shrink violation budgets a native pattern. 3) A bundled dart/flutter ruleset (BSD header + the foundation/meta + flutter_tools self-import firewalls + pubspec-per-package) — flutter-shaped Dart monorepos share this floor. All grounded in flutter's actual checks; none needs the Dart analyzer.

*Notes.* Config is accurate and the gap note on A2 ('the reverse fixed-import-must-be-removed arm is not expressible') is exactly right and is the single most interesting cross-repo gap in this group — it recurs as 'detect stale suppressions' everywhere. import_gate's dart preset + allow: is a clean fit for the forward arm. A4's meta carve-out via a paths exclude of foundation/ is the right idiom.

### `gin-gonic-gin` — Go web-framework library (single-language, single Go module; community-governed; golangci-lint v2 + goreleaser release flow)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% addressable — value-add only. gin's entire enforced surface is execution/AST/test/scan (category D), correctly excluded from the denominator; A+B+C = 0, so coverage_today is vacuous. alint contributes a value-add coherence/structure layer the repo does not itself gate on.

**Enforcement surface.** gin's CI-ENFORCED bespoke-validation surface is ~100% deliberate-non-goal (category D) tool execution, and the config's R5 framing of this is correct on re-verification. Concretely: (1) golangci-lint v2 (.golangci.yml) running gofmt/gofumpt + goimports + ~14 linters + misspell — AST/format/NLP execution; (2) `go test` across a Go-version matrix + race detector; (3) `go vet`; (4) GitHub CodeQL (SAST); (5) Trivy (vuln/SCA scan); (6) a Codecov ~99% coverage gate. There are NO hand-rolled file-invariant shell checks, NO codegen-freshness `git diff --exit-code`, NO cross-file value gate, NO import-firewall script, NO per-file license-header gate, NO sorted-block check. So the addressable A/B/C ENFORCED set is genuinely EMPTY — coverage_today is vacuous (the §7 table records this as 'n/a → n/a'). The CI workflows themselves are GitHub Actions YAML (one validation workflow + supporting), and gin deliberately pins third-party actions by floating major tag (@v6, @v9), not by 40-char SHA. Release automation is goreleaser-driven (.goreleaser.yaml at root). Documented (NOT CI-gated) conventions: README states a minimum Go version that should track the go.mod `go` directive; version.go carries `const Version = "vX.Y.Z"`; CONTRIBUTING + the PR template point new-feature docs at docs/doc.md.

**What alint replaces.** Nothing that gin ENFORCES (its enforced surface is all D). Everything alint contributes here is value-add over UN-enforced structure/coherence: README-Go-version <-> go.mod `go`-directive floor coherence -> cross_file (relation:equals, regex extract on both sides; the config's gin-readme-go-version-matches-gomod, now the unified cross_file kind); version.go canonical-semver shape -> file_content_matches; docs/doc.md feature-doc target existence -> file_exists; goreleaser/golangci config presence -> file_exists root_only; action-pinning supply-chain note -> yaml_path_matches if_present (downgraded to info because gin pins by tag, so a SHA-pin assertion deliberately under-fires); plus oss-baseline + go + ci/github-actions bundles for README/LICENSE/SECURITY/gitignore/go.mod-go.sum hygiene.

**What alint improves.** The one genuine coherence invariant gin has (README minimum-Go-version must equal the go.mod `go` floor) is materially better as a declarative cross_file rule than gin's current approach, which is NO approach — the two values drift silently today with nothing watching them. A single reviewable .alint.yml line replaces a check gin simply does not have. More broadly, alint's value here is purely as a cheap, no-toolchain drift-guard layer ATOP the heavy D execution gates (you can run the structure/coherence checks in milliseconds without Go installed), not as a replacement for any enforced check.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. This repo enforces nothing alint cannot express, because it enforces no addressable checks at all — every gin gate is execution (gofmt/golangci/vet/test/CodeQL/Trivy/coverage), correctly category D and orchestrable via `command`/`command_idempotent`. The value-add coherence/structure rules are all natively expressible (cross_file equals, file_content_matches, file_exists, yaml_path_matches). There is no validation gin performs that alint cannot, so no genuine gap exists for this repo.

**Abstraction ideas.** none beyond the existing surface that this repo motivates. gin would be a natural early adopter of a thin 'go-library-baseline' bundled ruleset (compose go@v1 + the README<->go.mod-floor cross_file + version-constant-semver + goreleaser/golangci-presence) — a convenience bundle of EXISTING kinds, not a new primitive. No new rule kind, axis, or DSL concept is demanded.

*Notes.* §7 post-build row 37 confirms 'n/a → n/a; none — the one coherence check (README<->go.mod) is plain cross_file equals.' The config's R5 self-assessment (addressable enforced set is empty) holds up exactly under the reproduce-first lens; no over-reported gap to correct here. The cross_file_value_equals in the config is now the byte-compatible `relation: equals` alias of the unified cross_file kind.

### `git-git` — C/CLI systems codebase with a small Rust arm (libgit-rs/libgit-sys) + dual build system (Makefile + meson); mailing-list/maintainer governance; extensive bespoke perl/shell lint suite under t/ and ci/

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~60% of the addressable (non-execution) enforced surface today (5 of the ~8-9 addressable gates are native A: executable_bit, filename_regex, no_trailing_whitespace, no_bidi_controls, final_newline; the 3 doc-cross-reference gates + the numeric-prefix dup remain B/C). §7 scores the uplift ~55% -> ~75% post-build; the residual is the doc-xref name-set family. The overwhelming majority of git's TOTAL enforcement (clang-format/sparse/coccinelle/test-matrix/chainlint/coverity) is correctly D and excluded.

**Enforcement surface.** git's enforced validation is DOMINATED by execution/semantic checks (category D) but, unlike gin/hugo, it ALSO carries a real set of addressable file-invariant gates — the most for any repo in this group. ADDRESSABLE/ENFORCED (A): (1) t/Makefile `test-lint-executable` fails the build for any non-executable t/tNNNN-*.sh or t/perf/pNNNN-*.sh (test discovery relies on +x) -> executable_bit; (2) t/Makefile `test-lint-filenames` rejects any tracked basename containing the Windows-hostile bytes " * : < > ? \ | -> filename_regex with an anchored allowlist; (3) check-whitespace.yml runs `git log --check` + .gitattributes whitespace=trail,space -> no_trailing_whitespace; (4) ci/check-directional-formatting.bash bans Unicode bidi/directional controls -> no_bidi_controls; (5) .editorconfig insert_final_newline=true -> final_newline. NEAR-ADDRESSABLE (C, the named tuning gap): t/Makefile `test-lint-duplicates` forbids two test files sharing the numeric prefix tNNNN; unique_by keys on a path template ({stem}) with no regex-capture of just the 4-digit number, so it can only catch EXACT-stem dups, a strictly weaker check (config keeps it level:info, honestly documented). B/new-primitive (the documentation-cross-reference family): `lint-docs-meson` (meson.build's manpage block must EQUAL the set of git*.adoc filenames on disk), `lint-manpages` (every command has BOTH a .adoc page AND a row in command-list.txt), `lint-gitlink` (doc `gitlink:git-foo[1]` cross-refs must resolve to a real page). DELIBERATE NON-GOALS (D, the bulk): clang-format, sparse, coccinelle/`make coccicheck`, coverity, header self-containment (hdr-check), the t/ test suite across Windows/macOS/Docker/asan/ubsan/leaks/fuzz matrices, the perl Documentation linters, `chainlint` (shell-AST), `check-non-portable-shell.pl`, cargo fmt/clippy/MSRV on the Rust arm, and the git-po l10n helper.

**What alint replaces.** test-lint-executable -> executable_bit (require:true over t/t[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-*.sh + t/perf/p[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-*.sh); test-lint-filenames -> filename_regex ('^[^"*:<>?\\|]+$' over **/*); check-whitespace `git log --check` -> no_trailing_whitespace (oss-no-trailing-whitespace); ci/check-directional-formatting.bash -> no_bidi_controls (oss-no-bidi-controls); .editorconfig final-newline -> final_newline (oss-final-newline). Value-add (not git-gated): oss-baseline README/LICENSE/SECURITY/code-of-conduct/gitignore existence. The numeric-prefix dup check is PARTIALLY replaced by unique_by {stem} (exact-stem only).

**What alint improves.** git's addressable checks are today spread across a hand-rolled t/Makefile target battery (test-lint-executable / test-lint-filenames / test-lint-duplicates), a separate check-whitespace.yml, and a standalone ci/check-directional-formatting.bash — four different mechanisms in three languages (make, sh, bash). alint consolidates all of them into ONE declarative, schema-validated .alint.yml that runs without make or a build, with consistent violation reporting and per-rule levels. filename_regex is also more portable and auditable than the shell test that constructs the hostile-byte check. The bidi-control gate in particular (a bespoke bash script git wrote post the 2021 Trojan-Source CVE) becomes a one-line bundle rule that every alint user gets for free.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** THREE genuine gaps remain, all in the documentation cross-reference / name-set family, and reproduce-first confirms two of the three: (1) `lint-docs-meson` — meson.build's manpage list must EQUAL the union of git*.adoc filenames on disk. This needs a GLOB -> filename-SET source (one side is 'the set of basenames matching Documentation/git*.adoc'). I tried cross_file set_equals: its source is a SINGLE authoritative file (not a glob-union), and its targets-glob extracts CONTENT from each matched file and compares the source set against EACH target PER-FILE (verified in cross_file.rs each_target/check_set) — it cannot build one set from the FILENAMES of a glob. The `file_set` (glob->path-set union) primitive that would do this was EXPLICITLY DROPPED in v0.12 as corpus-unjustified (cross_file.md 5b). Genuine gap. (2) `lint-manpages` row-in-command-list.txt — every command name must appear as a row in a plaintext list; again a glob-filename-set membership check (each command's existence is fine via for_each_file+{stem}, but the 'is in command-list.txt' name-set side is the same glob->name-set union problem). Genuine gap (the registry_paths_resolve forward direction handles list-entry->file, but not the reverse glob->is-listed). (3) `lint-gitlink` — the §7 row OVERSTATES this as 'closed by file_graph no_dangling'. Reproduce-first refutes it: file_graph from_content resolves the CAPTURED STRING verbatim as a path (FromContentSpec has only extract+resolve, NO `to:`/suffix-rewrite — verified in file_graph.rs), but `gitlink:git-foo[1]` captures the bare token `git-foo`, whereas the target page is `Documentation/git-foo.adoc` — the directory prefix and `.adoc` suffix are conventions absent from the text, so no regex capture can yield the real path. derive_target HAS a `to:` template but is bound to `fresh`, not `no_dangling`. So lint-gitlink needs either a suffix/dir-rewrite on from_content edges OR derive_target usable under no_dangling — a narrow but genuine gap. The numeric-prefix dup (C) is a 4th, weaker item: unique_by has no key-capture/regex-normalize to key on just the tNNNN number.

**Abstraction ideas.** Two grounded primitives this repo motivates: (A) a glob->name/path-SET source on cross_file (the dropped `file_set`, but git is concrete demand the original scan missed — meson-manpage-set-equality + command-list membership are two distinct git gates needing exactly 'build a set from a glob's basenames'; worth revisiting the DROP given git is a flagship). (B) a target-rewrite (`to:`/suffix/prefix template) on file_graph `from_content` edges — would let a captured token like `git-foo` map to `Documentation/git-foo.adoc` and close lint-gitlink with the existing no_dangling mode; this is the file_graph analogue of derive_target's `to:`. Smaller: a `key_regex`/capture option on unique_by to key on a sub-pattern (the tNNNN number) rather than the whole {stem}, which would turn the test-number-uniqueness C into a faithful A.

*Notes.* §7 row 38 confirms ~55%->~75% and names lint-docs-meson (#6/B2 glob-union set-equality), lint-manpages (#6/B2 + B1 plaintext name-set), and the test-lint-duplicates numeric-prefix #7 tuning gap. CORRECTION to the §7 row's optimistic 'file_graph would close lint-gitlink via no_dangling': reproduce-first shows from_content has no target-rewrite, so gitlink's bare-token-to-Documentation/{}.adoc mapping is NOT expressible today — keep lint-gitlink as a genuine (narrow) gap. The config's own C-honesty on the numeric-prefix dup (info-level, documented as weaker) is exemplary.

### `gohugoio-hugo` — Go CLI / static-site-generator (single Go module; mage build; community/governance model; CircleCI release pipeline + GitHub Actions test workflow)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% addressable — value-add only (§7 scores it '~0% -> ~0%'). Hugo's entire enforced surface is tool execution (category D); A+B+C = 0. alint adds orchestration (command/command_idempotent wraps) + structure/coherence value-add (hdebug forbidden-content, file presence), none of which sits in the coverage denominator per R3/R5.

**Enforcement surface.** Hugo's ENFORCED validation surface is ~100% tool execution (category D) — the config's R6 framing is correct on re-verification. The ONLY validation CI is .github/workflows/test.yml: one matrix job (go 1.25/1.26 x ubuntu/windows) running, ALL as execution: gofmt (check_gofmt.sh: `gofmt -d` diff), staticcheck, go vet, go test -race (orchestrated via `mage -v check` -> Fmt+Vet+TestRace), gotmplfmt -d over tpl/tplimpl/embedded/templates (embedded-template formatter diff), a dragonfly cross-build, and a dart-sass download-checksum verify. .circleci/config.yml is RELEASE-only (hugoreleaser on release-* branches) with no validation gates. image.yml is Docker build + SBOM/provenance (D). check.sh / check_gofmt.sh / magefile.go wrap the same gofmt/staticcheck/vet/test tools for local iteration (D). CODEGEN: `mage generate` regenerates *.autogen.go + livereload JS + stringer files, but NO CI step runs `generate && git diff --exit-code`, so codegen freshness is NOT an enforced edge (0 file-graph sources — recorded as absent-category per R6). HEADERS: most .go files carry an Apache-2.0 'The Hugo Authors' header but ~24 legitimately omit it (package-doc files, vendored forks) and NO script gates it, so a strict file_header would over-fire (not enforced, R6). DOCUMENTED (not CI-gated) conventions (CONTRIBUTING.md): commit-message format, `Fixes #1234` refs, no-CGO, `./check.sh` must pass, AI-disclosure. AGENTS.md: a stray `hdebug.Printf` must be removed before commit — but this is enforced at RUNTIME (panic when htesting.IsRealCI()) i.e. by the test suite, not a static gate (D). go.sum IS tracked (binary/CLI convention), so no-tracked-artifacts hygiene is safe here.

**What alint replaces.** Nothing Hugo ENFORCES as an addressable static check (its enforced surface is all D). alint's contribution is (1) ORCHESTRATION value-add — wrapping the D tools through one entrypoint: gofmt -> command_idempotent, gotmplfmt over the embedded-templates subtree -> command_idempotent, `mage -v check` (the master CI gate) -> command; and (2) STRUCTURE/coherence value-add the repo relies on but does not gate: hdebug.Printf/Panicf/AssertNotNil static forbidden-content as a cheaper pre-test guard (the runtime-panic invariant) -> file_content_forbidden with include **/*.go + exclude common/hdebug/** and *_test.go; magefile.go / CONTRIBUTING.md / AGENTS.md presence -> file_exists; plus oss-baseline + go + ci/github-actions + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts bundles.

**What alint improves.** The hdebug.Printf guard is materially better as a static file_content_forbidden than Hugo's actual mechanism (a runtime panic gated behind htesting.IsRealCI() that only trips when the full -race test suite executes under CI): alint catches a committed debug-print in milliseconds, pre-commit, without compiling or running tests, and pinpoints the file/line — a genuine shift-left over a test-execution-discovered failure. The single-entrypoint orchestration (one `alint check` fans out gofmt+gotmplfmt+mage-check) is also a modest improvement over Hugo's three scattered wrappers (check.sh, check_gofmt.sh, magefile.go) for local iteration.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Hugo's enforced surface is entirely tool execution (gofmt/staticcheck/vet/test-race/gotmplfmt/dart-sass-checksum), correctly category D and orchestrated via command/command_idempotent — these are DELIBERATE non-goals, not gaps. The two notable ABSENT categories (codegen freshness, per-file headers) are deliberately NOT enforced by Hugo (R6): there is no `mage generate && git diff` gate to replace, and a file_header would over-fire on ~24 legitimate omitters. The reproduce-first lens finds nothing Hugo validates that alint cannot express — even the CONTRIBUTING commit-message shape (a documented, not CI-gated, convention) is now natively expressible via git_commit_subject_matches if one chose to enforce it. So: no genuine gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** none beyond the existing surface. The most interesting OPPORTUNITY (not a gap, since Hugo doesn't gate it) is that IF Hugo adopted codegen-freshness enforcement for `mage generate`'s *.autogen.go + stringer + livereload-JS outputs, the cleanest expression would be the planned MUTATING generated-file-freshness mode (run the in-place generator, git-diff, restore) — the one dominant residual across the whole 111-repo study (~23 repos). Hugo is a textbook candidate for it. But that's a study-wide #1 backlog item, not a gin/hugo-specific new primitive, and Hugo does not currently enforce it.

*Notes.* §7 row 39 confirms '~0% -> ~0%' and 'none still-open — codegen freshness deliberately NOT gated (0 file-graph sources, R6); headers deliberately not gated (~24 legit omitters would over-fire).' The config's R6 discipline (recording the codegen and header ABSENCES as deliberate findings rather than forcing rules that would over-fire) is exemplary and survives the reproduce-first re-examination unchanged. git_commit_subject_matches now exists and would cover the documented commit-message convention if enforcement were ever desired.
