### `golang-go` — Go toolchain + standard library mega-repo (Gerrit/go.googlesource.com + LUCI presubmit/postsubmit, NOT GitHub Actions); enforced 'tree well-formed' checks live AS Go self-tests under cmd/dist test rather than CI YAML

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~55% of the addressable enforced surface today, ~70% post-build (§7 row 40: '~55% -> ~70%'). The copyright header and fips140 digest-presence are native A (with C-tuning caveats), the deps_test path-based firewall and api/next pairing are now file_graph/git-extract-addressable; the residual is the two narrow C-tuning gaps (generated-file header exceptions; path-first sums-line binding) plus the module-NAME graph which is correctly a D non-goal. The overwhelming bulk of golang's total enforcement (gofmt/vet/full-test/api-compat/codegen-freshness self-tests) is correctly D.

**Enforcement surface.** golang/go is developed on Gerrit; its addressable 'is the tree well-formed' checks are encoded AS Go self-tests (run by cmd/dist test / go test) rather than CI workflow YAML. ADDRESSABLE/ENFORCED (A, partial): (1) src/internal/copyright/copyright_test.go requires the BSD copyright notice in the head of every .go and .s file under src/ — the checker reads the first ~2KB and accepts 'Copyright' anywhere OR one of ~13 permitted generated-file prefixes (`// Code generated by ...`) -> file_header (with a real C-tuning gap: the generated-file allowance has no clean expression). (2) lib/fips140/fips140.sum pins the sha256 of each FIPS snapshot .zip (the security policy sent to the FIPS lab references these digests => 'MUST NOT CHANGE'), enforced by `go test cmd/go/internal/fips140` -> pair_hash, BUT golang writes the manifest PATH-FIRST (`<path> <hex>`), the REVERSE of the coreutils/go-.sum `<hex>  <path>` order that pair_hash `sums-line` parses, so only `format: contains` (digest-substring presence) works here — verifying digest PRESENCE but not the path<->hash binding (a C-tuning gap). IMPORT-FIREWALL / cross-file (B->now-A via file_graph): src/go/build/deps_test.go is a DECLARATIVE import-layering DAG (the package-dependency firewall for the whole std lib); src/crypto/internal/fips140deps/fipsdeps_test.go is the FIPS import firewall. PAIRING (B, now git-extract-addressable): api/next/* <-> doc/next/*stdlib/*minor pairing enforced by `cmd/relnote -check` (adding an api/next line requires a doc/next note); both directories are absent at this pin (release-cycle reset). CODEGEN-FRESHNESS self-tests (D, execution): help_test.go, zosarch_test.go, generate_test.go run generators and compare. DELIBERATE NON-GOALS (D, the bulk): gofmt, go vet, the full go test suite, api compatibility checking (api/go1.txt golden compare via the api tool), and the deps_test.go MODULE-NAME graph RESOLUTION itself (resolving `k8s.io/...`-style import paths is the package-graph non-goal). Value-add (not repo-gated as such): fips140.sum existence + its 'MUST NOT CHANGE' provenance banner; api/README + api/go1.txt frozen-golden existence.

**What alint replaces.** copyright_test.go BSD-header invariant -> file_header (scoped to src/**/*.go and src/**/*.s, pattern 'Copyright \d{4} The Go Authors. All rights reserved.', the config's go-bsd-copyright-header-src/-asm); fips140.sum digest-presence integrity -> pair_hash format:contains (per snapshot zip; go-fips140-sum-pins-*); the deps_test.go / fipsdeps_test.go *path-based relative-import* layering -> file_graph forbidden_edges/acyclic (from_content edges over relative imports — NOTE: only the path-based subset; the k8s.io-style module-NAME resolution stays a non-goal); api/next<->doc/next co-change pairing -> changeset_requires_path / pair_changed_together (the since..HEAD diff must add the paired note when api/next changed); fips140.sum existence + banner -> file_exists + file_content_matches; api/README + api/go1.txt existence -> file_exists; plus oss-baseline + go bundles.

**What alint improves.** The copyright-header check becomes a single declarative file_header rule with explicit scoping/excludes, versus golang's bespoke Go test that hand-rolls the first-2KB scan and the 13-prefix allowlist in code — easier to audit and runs without building Go. The api/next<->doc/next pairing is materially better as changeset_requires_path: golang enforces it via `cmd/relnote -check` (a custom Go tool invocation in the release tooling), whereas alint expresses the 'a contribution that adds an api/next line must also add a doc/next note' invariant declaratively over the git diff, with no relnote binary. For the deps_test.go firewall, alint's file_graph covers the RELATIVE/path-based import edges declaratively — though see gaps re: the module-name portion.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** Mostly NARROW C-tuning gaps after reproduce-first; the headline B items are now closable: (1) file_header has NO content-OR `allow:`/exceptions list, so the copyright checker's ~13 permitted `// Code generated by ...` generated-file prefixes (a file passes if it has the copyright OR is a recognized generated file) cannot be expressed precisely. Reproduce-first: Scope supports exclude (paths.exclude), so you can exclude KNOWN generated dirs, and a regex alternation (Copyright...|// Code generated by ...) within `lines:` partially works — but golang accepts ANY of 13 distinct generator banners anywhere in the first 2KB, and enumerating them as paths-excludes is brittle (generated files are scattered, not dir-localized). So a faithful port is a genuine (narrow) gap: file_header wants an `allow:`/`or_matches:` content-exception list. (2) pair_hash `sums-line` has only the `<hex>  <path>` order; fips140.sum is PATH-FIRST `<path> <hex>`, so the path<->hash BINDING cannot be verified (contains-substring only checks digest presence). Genuine narrow gap: sums-line needs a path-first flavor. (3) The deps_test.go firewall's MODULE-NAME resolution (resolving fully-qualified import paths through the module graph) is a DELIBERATE non-goal (package-graph, needs a resolver) — NOT a gap; only its path-based relative-import subset is file_graph-expressible. Everything else golang enforces (gofmt/vet/test/api-compat) is correctly D.

**Abstraction ideas.** Two grounded primitives this flagship motivates: (A) an `allow:`/`or_matches:` content-exception list on file_header — 'the header matches PATTERN, OR the file matches one of these escape patterns (e.g. a `// Code generated by` banner)'. golang's copyright_test.go is the canonical demand (13 generated-file prefixes); the same shape recurs in kubernetes boilerplate and elasticsearch vendored-header excludes, so this is high-leverage, not golang-only. (B) a path-first `sums-line` flavor on pair_hash (a `path_first: true` / order option), so a `<path> <hex>` digest manifest like fips140.sum verifies the full path<->hash binding rather than mere digest presence. Both are option-extensions to existing kinds (compose-don't-accrete), not new kinds. A bundled go-toolchain ruleset (copyright header + fips140 integrity + deps-firewall + api/next pairing) would be a natural composition once (A)/(B) land.

*Notes.* §7 row 40 confirms '~55% -> ~70%' and names exactly the two C-tuning gaps: '#7 file_header needs a generated-file-prefix allow/exceptions list (~13 prefixes)' and '#7 fips140.sum is PATH-FIRST, reverse of pair_hash sums-line, so only contains works (no path<->hash binding)', plus 'api/next + doc/next absent at this pin' (so the pairing is recorded as a file-graph/changeset edge source rather than executed against this clone). The config's honesty about the contains-vs-sums-line downgrade and the header generated-file C-gap is accurate and survives re-examination. file_graph (unused in the config) would now express the path-based deps_test.go firewall as native A — the config predates the build, so it understates this.

---

### `google-guava` — JVM/Java core library, Maven dual-reactor (jre + android), Google single-vendor

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the addressable (non-execution) surface. But that addressable surface is SMALL relative to what guava enforces overall — the denominator is ~all category-D build/test execution, so whole-repo: ~10-15% of total enforcement is alint-addressable and alint covers essentially all of it; the rest is correct non-goal.

**Enforcement surface.** Guava's enforced/validated surface is overwhelmingly EXECUTION (category D). CI = `mvnw install/verify` across a JDK 8/11/17/25 x {jre,android} x {ubuntu,windows} matrix (one build+test behavior), a downstream Gradle integration build, the maven-enforcer plugin (requireMavenVersion / requireJavaVersion / dependencyConvergence), and an OpenSSF Scorecard supply-chain scan. There is deliberately NO checkstyle/spotless/RAT/license-maven plugin in the pom — Google Java Style and the Apache-2.0 boilerplate are REVIEW conventions documented in CONTRIBUTING.md, not tool gates. The small ADDRESSABLE surface: (1) Apache-2.0 license header on every .java source (CONTRIBUTING-required, no tool enforces it); (2) GitHub Actions `uses:` pinned to a full 40-hex SHA (Scorecard Pinned-Dependencies criterion + Dependabot); (3) root LICENSE present (also declared in pom <licenses>). The config also adds VALUE-ADD assertions the repo does not itself gate: jre-flavor parent-POM version coherence (the literal `999.0.0-HEAD-jre-SNAPSHOT` across the 5 jre module poms — Maven's reactor already enforces child<parent><version> at build time, so it's belt-and-suspenders) and maven-wrapper distributionSha256Sum presence. Notably the header rule carries a hand-curated exclude list encoding guava's real exceptions: package-info/module-info (annotation-only), JSR166 public-domain code (AtomicDouble*, Striped64, LongAdder — CC0, not Apache), and a handful of files carrying the older `// Copyright <yr> Google Inc.` banner (Fingerprint2011, InvalidatableSet, etc.).

**What alint replaces.** Apache-2.0 boilerplate header on .java sources -> file_header (with the curated paths.exclude for package-info/JSR166/legacy-banner files); root LICENSE present -> file_exists root_only; GitHub Actions SHA-pinning -> the bundled ci/github-actions `gha-pin-actions-to-sha` rule (already pulled in via extends); maven-wrapper checksum pin present -> file_content_matches with (?m) anchoring; jre parent-POM version coherence -> cross_file_value_equals over a regex extract on the version literal

**What alint improves.** Header enforcement is the standout: guava enforces this ONLY by code review (no RAT/license plugin exists in the build), so alint converts an un-gated CONTRIBUTING convention into a declarative, CI-runnable check WITHOUT adding a Maven plugin to the reactor — and the curated exclude list (JSR166 public-domain, package-info, legacy-banner files) is exactly the kind of cross-cutting exception bookkeeping that a checkstyle RegexpHeader config would also need but that alint expresses in one readable block. SHA-pin enforcement via the bundled ruleset matches Scorecard's intent without running the Scorecard Docker action. The jre POM-version coherence is a relation Maven only validates at build time — alint asserts it on a static tree in milliseconds.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Every addressable item is expressible. The one technically-limited choice — POM <version> coherence is done via a regex extract on the XML version literal rather than a real XPath — is NOT a gap: I confirmed cross_file's Extract enum is {Toml,Json,Yaml,Lines,Regex,WholeFile} with NO Xml variant, so cross_file can only reach into a pom via regex; but this is adequate because each jre pom carries exactly one `999.0.0-HEAD-jre-SNAPSHOT` literal, so the regex extract is unambiguous. (Single-file xml_path_equals DOES exist and could assert a fixed POM coordinate, but cross-file XML-vs-XML value equality is not directly expressible — see abstraction_ideas.) Everything genuinely missing is category-D execution (the build/test matrix, maven-enforcer dependencyConvergence, Scorecard), which is correct non-goal territory.

**Abstraction ideas.** Add an `xml` (and `xpath`) variant to cross_file's Extract enum so POM-to-POM / csproj-to-csproj value coherence is first-class instead of regex-on-markup — guava's jre-reactor coherence is the textbook motivating case, and it would compose with the already-shipped xml_path_equals leaf engine (Format::Xml + xml_to_value already exist in structured_path.rs; only the cross_file extract wiring is missing). A lightweight `maven_reactor_coherent` bundled convenience (parent/child version + module-list consistency) would package this for the large Maven-monorepo audience. Lower value: a `public_domain_exception` shared exclude axis so JSR166-style CC0 carve-outs in license-header rules are named rather than path-listed.

*Notes.* The config is honest that most rules are value-add, not guava-enforced. The exclude-list curation (JSR166 public-domain, legacy Google banner) is the load-bearing intelligence and is correctly expressed. cross_file lacking an Xml extract variant is the single capability seam worth closing, motivated directly here.

---

### `gradle-gradle` — Apache-2.0 JVM build tool, Gradle meta-build (build-logic), TeamCity-primary CI + GitHub Actions for PRs

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — the four checkstyle-derived checks plus all structural assertions are fully covered; only the subprojects.json freshness is downgraded to presence (and is itself expressible via generated_file_fresh, just heavyweight). Whole-repo including execution: alint addresses maybe ~20-25% of Gradle's total enforcement; the large remainder (ArchUnit/japicmp/codenarc/tests) is correct category-D non-goal.

**Enforcement surface.** Gradle has a LARGE bespoke-validation surface, but it is dominated by EXECUTION/semantic checks (category D): checkstyle, codenarc, ArchUnit architecture tests, japicmp binary-compatibility, the dependency-verification PGP/SHA resolution, and the test suite. CI is TeamCity (.teamcity/) with GitHub Actions running `./gradlew sanityCheck` / `compileAll` on contributor PRs. The ADDRESSABLE surface alint expresses: (1) jvmargs coherence — build-logic/build.gradle.kts (the `clean` task) THROWS if `org.gradle.jvmargs` differs between root and build-logic gradle.properties ('may cause two daemons to be spawned on CI and IDEA'); (2) forbidden IDE/editor template residue — checkstyle RegexpSingleline modules (checkstyle.xml ~lines 140-148) ban 'Created with IntelliJ IDEA', 'File | Settings | File Templates', and `@author` Javadoc tags across sources; (3) import firewall — checkstyle IllegalImport (~lines 89-100) bans jdk.internal.*, com.beust.*, org.testng.collections.*, and JetBrains nullability annotations (org.jspecify preferred); (4) Apache-2.0 RegexpHeader (required-header.txt) on every production source. Plus VALUE-ADD structural assertions: dependency.verification=strict policy + verification-metadata.xml presence, .teamcity/subprojects.json codegen-target presence (the diff-vs-generator freshness via CheckSubprojectsInfo stays D), and gradle-wrapper validateDistributionUrl=true.

**What alint replaces.** checkstyle RegexpSingleline (IntelliJ/template/@author bans) -> file_content_forbidden (two rules); checkstyle IllegalImport firewall -> import_gate language:java with a single forbid regex over **/src/main/java/**; checkstyle RegexpHeader (Apache-2.0 license header) -> file_header with a documented paths.exclude for docs/snippets/samples/test-resources/testFixtures; jvmargs cross-properties coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (regex extract on the key=value line, normalize:trim); dependency-verification policy + wrapper validateDistributionUrl + verification-metadata.xml/subprojects.json presence -> file_content_matches + file_exists

**What alint improves.** This is a strong consolidation story: three separate checkstyle XML module configs (RegexpSingleline x2, IllegalImport, RegexpHeader) — each a fragment of a large checkstyle.xml that only runs inside the Gradle/checkstyle plugin invocation — collapse into four readable, schema-validated alint rules that run WITHOUT booting Gradle or checkstyle. The import firewall in particular is materially better expressed: import_gate matches the EXTRACTED import target (not a raw-line regex like checkstyle RegexpSingleline-style hacks), with the java preset handling `import static` and FQN forms. The jvmargs coherence is the highlight — Gradle enforces it via an imperative `throw` buried in a build-logic task that only fires when that task runs; alint expresses the same cross-file invariant declaratively and checks it on a static tree. The header rule's exclude curation (docs snippets, samples, test-fixtures, src/*/resources) mirrors exactly the trees checkstyle is configured to skip, but is visible in one place.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none in the addressable surface. The .teamcity/subprojects.json freshness (CheckSubprojectsInfo regenerates and diffs) is reduced here to mere file_exists, which understates it — but that is NOT a gap: it is expressible via generated_file_fresh (run the Gradle generator, diff stdout/outputs) — the config chose presence-only because running the Gradle generator is heavyweight and the freshness is genuinely category-D execution; if the generator has a stable CLI it would be a clean generated_file_fresh outputs:-mode candidate. The dependency-verification PGP/SHA RESOLUTION is correctly D (alint asserts the policy + manifest, not the cryptographic verification). ArchUnit architecture tests, codenarc, japicmp are all execution non-goals. The header regex is multi-line `(?s)` with an `(A|B)` alternation for the two copyright forms ('the original author or authors' | 'Gradle and contributors') — fully expressible, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `forbid_imports` PRESET-EXTENSION so the bundled java ruleset could ship gradle/idiomatic-JVM IllegalImport defaults (jdk.internal.*, jetbrains-nullability) as an opt-in axis rather than each repo hand-writing the forbid regex. A `properties` extract format for cross_file (java .properties key=value) so the jvmargs coherence doesn't need a regex extract — minor sugar over the working regex form. A bundled `gradle-build` ruleset (wrapper validate-distribution-url + dependency-verification-strict + verification-metadata presence + subprojects-info presence) would package gradle's repeating structural assertions, paralleling the existing java/dotnet bundles.

*Notes.* The import_gate forbid regex bundles four checkstyle IllegalImport entries into one alternation — exactly the cross-file-coherent, single-reviewable-file value-add alint is meant to deliver. No genuine gaps.

---

### `grafana-grafana` — Polyglot monorepo (Go backend + TS/JS frontend), AGPL-3.0-only, Grafana Labs single-vendor, make + go + yarn/nx

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE surface (all doc-presence, SPDX, go-version coherence, and config-presence checks fully covered; only the reject-gh-secrets value-exclusion degrades to an approximation). Whole-repo including execution: alint addresses ~15-20% of grafana's total enforcement; the large remainder (golangci-lint/eslint/tsc/madge/govulncheck + the CUE/swagger/k8s codegen-freshness gates + test suites) is correct category-D non-goal.

**Enforcement surface.** Grafana's enforced surface is heavily TOOL EXECUTION (category D): golangci-lint, eslint, prettier, tsc, knip, madge (dangling/cycle JS deps), govulncheck, vale (prose), shellcheck, actionlint, zizmor (workflow security), plus a battery of codegen-freshness gates (CUE/swagger/k8s/jsonnet/openapi generators diffed against committed output) and the Go + frontend test suites. Governance is gated socially via CODEOWNERS + a policybot. The ADDRESSABLE surface alint expresses: (1) required governance docs present and CODEOWNERS-owned — NOTICE.md (Kibana-attribution + AGPL notice), LICENSING.md, GOVERNANCE.md, MAINTAINERS.md; (2) the AGPL SPDX boilerplate header used by Go codegen (hack/boilerplate.go.txt must carry `SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-only`); (3) cross-file Go toolchain-version coherence — `make update-workspace` / go-workspace-check keep go.mod and go.work's `go X.Y.Z` directive in sync; (4) a reject-gh-secrets policy approximation (workflows may only reference `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN`; Vault is the sanctioned secret source); (5) low-stakes config-policy assertions — .editorconfig trim_trailing_whitespace, .github/zizmor.yml presence, lefthook.yml presence, .github/renovate.json5 presence. GitHub Actions SHA-pinning comes from the bundled ci/github-actions ruleset.

**What alint replaces.** governance/license/maintainers doc presence -> file_exists root_only (4 rules); AGPL SPDX line in the Go codegen boilerplate -> file_content_matches; go.mod<->go.work `go X.Y.Z` coherence (the make update-workspace invariant) -> cross_file_value_equals with regex extract; editorconfig/zizmor/lefthook/renovate presence -> file_content_matches + file_exists; GHA SHA-pinning -> bundled gha-pin-actions-to-sha

**What alint improves.** The go.mod<->go.work version coherence is the clear win: grafana enforces it by running `make update-workspace` and checking nothing changed (a generator-diff, category-D) — alint asserts the same `go X.Y.Z`-must-match invariant declaratively on a static tree, no toolchain boot. Converting the CODEOWNERS-social expectation that NOTICE/LICENSING/GOVERNANCE/MAINTAINERS exist into hard file_exists gates closes a real 'someone deleted the attribution file' hole that policybot/CODEOWNERS alone won't catch. The config is also commendably honest: it labels the secrets rule a deliberate APPROXIMATION rather than overclaiming.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap, correctly self-identified in the config and confirmed under the reproduce-first lens: the reject-gh-secrets policy needs 'forbid `${{ secrets.X }}` for any X != GITHUB_TOKEN' — an allow-list-MINUS-ONE over matched values. I verified this is not expressible: (a) file_content_forbidden has ONLY a `pattern` field — no `allow:`/exception list — and the regex crate is 1.x with NO fancy-regex/lookahead, so a negative-lookahead `secrets\.(?!GITHUB_TOKEN)` cannot compile; (b) import_gate has an `allow:` but it is a glob over FILE PATHS, not a value-allowlist over matched tokens, so it cannot exempt `GITHUB_TOKEN` while forbidding other secret names; (c) structured-query/cross_file subset cannot help because `secrets.X` references live inside `${{ }}` string expressions scattered across `run:`/`with:`/`env:` positions in workflow YAML — they are NOT addressable as a discrete enumerable set of YAML nodes, so 'the set of referenced secret names ⊆ {GITHUB_TOKEN}' has no single JSONPath to enumerate. The config falls back to forbidding only credential-SHAPED names (_PAT/_API_KEY/_PASSWORD/_CREDENTIAL) that never collide with GITHUB_TOKEN — a real coverage loss (a `secrets.NPM_PUBLISH` would slip through). Everything else missing is category-D execution (golangci-lint, eslint, madge, the codegen-freshness diffs) — correct non-goals.

**Abstraction ideas.** Add an `allow:` (or `except:`) value-exception list to file_content_forbidden: 'pattern matches but the captured group ∈ allow ⇒ skip'. This single addition dissolves the only genuine gap here AND generalizes to the broad 'forbid X except a sanctioned set' family (forbid all `secrets.*` except GITHUB_TOKEN; forbid all `http://` except localhost; forbid TODO except TODO(owner)). It is the highest-leverage abstraction this group surfaces — implementable as a capture-group membership test, no new regex engine needed. Secondarily: a `workflow_secret_allowlist` convenience kind (parse `${{ secrets.NAME }}` tokens out of .github/workflows/* and assert membership) would serve the large GitHub-Actions-security audience that zizmor targets.

*Notes.* This repo is the canonical evidence for the file_content_forbidden `allow:` abstraction — the gap is narrow, real, and the config already flags it as a new_kind_candidate. The madge JS dangling/cycle check is interesting: file_graph acyclic/no_dangling could express the SHAPE for non-bundler import graphs, but JS module resolution (aliases, barrel files, ts-paths) is what madge actually resolves, so it stays D.

---

### `guzzle-guzzle` — PHP HTTP client library, MIT, Composer single-package, GitHub Actions CI

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% replaceable in the strict sense (the repo enforces nothing addressable to replace — its whole gate is category-D execution), but ~100% of the reasonable structural/coherence VALUE-ADD surface is expressible and already encoded. Net: value-add only, no replacement story, no gaps. A PHP/Composer bundled ruleset is the clear product opportunity.

**Enforcement surface.** Per the study's R2 ruling, guzzle's ENFORCED validation surface is ENTIRELY tool-execution (category D): PHPUnit, PHPStan (level 9), php-cs-fixer (@PSR12:risky + @Symfony rulesets), and composer-normalize. There are NO bespoke shell gates, NO committed git hooks, NO codegen-freshness diffs, NO import/layering firewall, NO cross-file value gate — the repo's enforced A/B/C surface is effectively ZERO. Therefore EVERYTHING in the config is alint VALUE-ADD: structure/coherence the repo does not gate on today but alint can express declaratively. Those value-add items: (1) required docs present — CHANGELOG.md (hand-maintained), UPGRADING.md (breaking-changes, referenced from CHANGELOG); (2) composer.json policy assertions — `$.name == guzzlehttp/guzzle`, `$.license == MIT` (must match LICENSE), `$.config['sort-packages'] == true` (the cheap declarative subset of what composer-normalize enforces); (3) cross-file COMPOSER_ROOT_VERSION coherence — the env var is duplicated across .github/workflows/ci.yml and static.yml and must stay equal when the dev-branch version bumps; (4) LICENSE content invariant ('The MIT License (MIT)' text); (5) wide-net hygiene (final_newline + no_trailing_whitespace across php/md/json/neon/dist — php-cs-fixer only covers src/+tests/ .php, so alint widens to docs/config).

**What alint replaces.** required-docs presence -> file_exists root_only (CHANGELOG, UPGRADING); composer.json name/license/sort-packages -> json_path_equals (note `$.config['sort-packages']` correctly uses bracket notation for the dashed key); COMPOSER_ROOT_VERSION cross-workflow coherence -> cross_file_value_equals with a YAML extract `$.env.COMPOSER_ROOT_VERSION` (the JSONPath-over-YAML idiom); LICENSE MIT-text invariant -> file_content_matches; tree-wide final-newline / trailing-whitespace -> final_newline + no_trailing_whitespace with fixers

**What alint improves.** Because guzzle gates NONE of this today, every rule is pure additive coverage rather than a replacement — alint provides a baseline of structural/coherence enforcement the project simply lacks. The standout value-add is COMPOSER_ROOT_VERSION coherence: a duplicated env var across two workflow files that nothing currently keeps in sync (a classic silent-drift footgun on a release bump) — cross_file_value_equals with a YAML JSONPath extract expresses exactly the cross-file invariant the repo can't. The composer.json assertions are a cheap, reviewable SUBSET of composer-normalize that runs without booting PHP/Composer. The license name<->LICENSE-text consistency catches a real (if rare) drift. The wide-net hygiene meaningfully extends php-cs-fixer's src/tests-only scope to docs/config files.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Guzzle's entire ENFORCED surface is category-D tool execution (PHPUnit / PHPStan level-9 / php-cs-fixer / composer-normalize) — all correctly out of scope (alint orchestrates these via command/command_idempotent, it does not reimplement static analysis or test runners). There is no addressable enforcement that alint fails to express; the value-add the config does add is fully expressible. The one thing alint deliberately does NOT do — run composer-normalize for FULL normalization — is reduced to the sort-packages subset on purpose (R3), which is the right altitude, not a gap. composer-normalize's full ordering/canonicalization could in principle be wired as command_idempotent (it has a --dry-run mode), but that is execution-orchestration, not a missing capability.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `composer` bundled ruleset (paralleling node/python/dotnet/go): composer.json name/license present, license matches LICENSE/SPDX, sort-packages true, require-dev present, plus PSR-4 autoload path resolution via registry_paths_resolve over `$.autoload['psr-4']`. Guzzle (and the whole Packagist ecosystem) is currently unserved by a bundled ruleset despite PHP being a first-class import_gate preset — this is the most useful abstraction this repo motivates. Lower value: a `value_equals_across_globbed_files` convenience is already covered by cross_file_value_equals targets.files, so no new primitive needed there.

*Notes.* Cleanest example in this group of a 'value-add only' repo: enforced surface is entirely D, so alint's contribution is establishing structural enforcement the project lacks rather than consolidating existing checks. The `$.config['sort-packages']` bracket notation is correctly applied (the dashed-key JSONPath gotcha from memory).

---

### `hashicorp/terraform` — Go CLI/core monorepo (multi-module: 1 root + 10 internal modules), single-vendor governance (HashiCorp/IBM), BUSL-1.1 licensed, make + go tool (Go 1.25)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The addressable surface = header presence/value, multi-module toolchain coherence, VERSION/changie shape+coherence, and tooling/structure presence — all natively expressed. The unaddressable remainder is pure execution (formatters, static analysis, exhaustive-switch, codegen-freshness diffs, the dep-sync graph computation, test suites) = category-D non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** Terraform's repo/dir/file enforcement lives almost entirely in .github/workflows/checks.yml (the 'Quick Checks' PR gate) driving make targets via scripts/*.sh, plus a few config-file policies. By category: (1) LICENSE-HEADERS — `make copyright` -> scripts/copyright.sh -> `copywrite headers --plan` against .copywrite.hcl enforces an SPDX BUSL-1.1 header on every non-ignored .go file; .copywrite.hcl carries per-dir header_ignore globs (*.pb.go, *_string.go, mock*.go, testdata, plus vendored subtrees internal/ipaddr (Go-Authors BSD), internal/tfplugin*, docs/plugin-protocol with their own .copywrite.hcl, and an MPL-2.0 azure helper). (2) CODEGEN-FRESHNESS — the consistency-checks job runs `go generate`, protoc, stringer, go mod tidy and git-diffs the tree (execution). (3) FORMATTING/STATIC-ANALYSIS — gofmtcheck, goimportscheck, staticcheck (staticcheck.conf), go vet, exhaustive (scripts/exhaustive.sh enforces exhaustive enum switches), defect-detector (all execution). (4) MULTI-MODULE COHERENCE — scripts/syncdeps.sh keeps the 11 go.mod files' shared deps in lock-step; .go-version is the single toolchain source of truth that .github/actions/go-version reads, and every module's go.mod `go` directive must agree. (5) CHANGELOG-DISCIPLINE — .changie.yaml + .changes/ (changie) require a changelog fragment per change, with unreleasedDir (v1.16) tracking version/VERSION's dev minor (1.16.0-dev). (6) RELEASE-METADATA — version/VERSION must be a bare semver (version.go //go:embed-parses it as semver; malformed value panics at startup). (7) TEST SUITES — race/e2e/equivalence (execution). The vast majority of the gate is tool execution; the on-disk invariants those tools enforce are the addressable slice.

**What alint replaces.** SPDX/BUSL header presence on .go (the on-disk invariant of `make copyright`/copywrite) -> file_header (tf-spdx-header-present, with .copywrite.hcl header_ignore globs mirrored as paths.exclude); .go-version vs every module's go.mod `go` directive -> cross_file_value_equals/cross_file equals with regex extractors (tf-go-version-coherence, the canonical multi-module toolchain-coherence use); version/VERSION is bare semver -> file_content_matches (tf-version-is-semver); changie unreleasedDir tracks the dev minor -> cross_file_value_equals with regex extract on both files (tf-changie-unreleased-dir-matches-version); required check-scripts + tooling-config presence (.copywrite.hcl/staticcheck.conf/.changie.yaml/.go-version, scripts/*.sh) -> file_exists (tf-check-scripts-present, tf-tooling-config-present); plus oss-baseline (LICENSE/README/SECURITY/CODEOWNERS/no-merge-markers/final-newline/no-bidi) and go@v1 (go.mod/go.sum presence + shape + Go-source Trojan-Source defense) inherited via extends.

**What alint improves.** Consolidates the .go-version<->11x go.mod coherence (which today is only implicitly maintained by scripts/syncdeps.sh, never a standalone gate) into one declarative, reviewable cross_file rule that fires precisely on the drifting module. Expresses changie-dir<->VERSION coherence and VERSION-is-semver as structured violations the repo only catches as a runtime panic or a stale-folder surprise. Runs the entire header + structural surface with ZERO Go toolchain installed (copywrite, go, staticcheck all unneeded for the on-disk-invariant slice) — valuable for fast pre-commit/local gating and for contributors without the full Go env. file_header is schema-validated and self-documenting vs an opaque shell grep.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The two limitations the original config flagged are PHANTOM under the post-v0.12 build: (a) the comment at lines 142-145 claims 'file_exists matches FILES only, not directories so the folder-exists form is not expressible' — but `dir_exists` exists and checks directory existence (e.g. .changes/v1.16), so the changie folder-exists form IS expressible (the value-coherence cross_file form the config chose instead is arguably still better, but the claimed inexpressibility is wrong). (b) The A1 'value must be exactly BUSL-1.1' tuning gap (bucket C) — the config asserts only header PRESENCE because vendored subtrees carry foreign licenses — is ALSO resolvable: it already enumerates the vendored exclusions in paths.exclude, so a per-tree file_header with pattern '(?m)^// SPDX-License-Identifier: BUSL-1\.1' scoped to the main tree (excluding internal/ipaddr, the azure MPL helper, internal/tfplugin*) asserts the exact license token on the files that must carry it; the multi-license reality is handled by scope, not by weakening to presence-only. Everything else (gofmt/goimports/vet/staticcheck/exhaustive/copywrite-the-tool/go-generate/protoc/syncdeps-graph/test suites) is execution = category-D NON-GOAL, correctly orchestrated via command (tf-make-fmtcheck/staticcheck/copyright).

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `compliance/busl-1.1@v1` ruleset (sibling to compliance/apache-2) would give HashiCorp-family BUSL repos the LICENSE-text + SPDX-BUSL-header pair out of the box. A `manifest_set_coherent`/'shared-key lock-step' helper that, given N go.mod files, asserts a named set of require-block versions is identical across all of them would natively express the scripts/syncdeps.sh invariant (today expressible only pairwise via repeated cross_file equals). A first-class `changelog_fragment_required` rule (changeset_requires_path already does the since..HEAD half; a changie/.changes-aware variant that also validates fragment frontmatter would close the changelog-discipline loop).

*Notes.* Strong canonical showcase for cross_file multi-module coherence. Reproduce-first lens flipped BOTH originally-recorded limitations (dir-exists; BUSL-value-strictness) to phantom — neither is a real gap. The repo's own enforcement leans heavily on tool execution, so alint's value here is the structural/coherence layer plus a unified `command` front-door.

---

### `helm/helm` — Go CLI monorepo (Kubernetes package manager), CNCF Apache-2.0, golangci-lint + make + tests; single root go.mod (module helm.sh/helm/v3)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The addressable surface (Apache+Copyright header substrings, CI-version single-source, governance/release-file presence, Apache LICENSE/NOTICE) is fully native; the import-firewall is additionally expressible declaratively (improvable). The remainder (golangci AST linters, govulncheck/CodeQL/Scorecard, PGP signing, go mod tidy, tests) is execution = non-goal.

**Enforcement surface.** Helm's bespoke repo/file enforcement is small and execution-dominated. By category: (1) LICENSE-HEADERS — scripts/validate-license.sh (run by `make test-source-headers` in build-test.yml) greps every *.go/*.sh (excluding testdata/third_party/.git) for TWO substrings anywhere in the file: 'Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License")' and 'Copyright The Helm Authors.'; .go files carry a /* ... */ block comment, not a line-leading header, so the check is a whole-file grep, not a top-of-file header. (2) IMPORT-FIREWALL — .golangci.yml declares depguard/gomodguard deny-lists; enforcement is golangci-lint (execution). (3) STATIC-ANALYSIS/STYLE — golangci-lint (make test-style), go vet, gofmt (execution). (4) CI-VERSION SINGLE-SOURCE — .github/env declares GOLANG_VERSION and GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION consumed by every workflow + golangci-lint-action. (5) SECURITY/SUPPLY-CHAIN — govulncheck, CodeQL, OSSF Scorecard, dependency review (execution). (6) RELEASE-INTEGRITY — release.yml `make sign` detached-signs artifacts with the PGP keys in KEYS. (7) GOVERNANCE — kubernetes-style OWNERS (maintainer/triage/emeritus), SECURITY.md, code-of-conduct.md. (8) FIXTURE HAZARD — internal/third_party/ vendors golang/dep testdata containing DELIBERATELY-broken symlinks (windows-file-symlink -> C:/Users/..., invalid-symlink -> /non/existing/file) that crash a naive tree walk, so it must be ignored up front. Tests + go mod tidy round out the gate (execution).

**What alint replaces.** Apache-2.0 license header substring (anywhere in *.go/*.sh) -> file_content_matches (helm-source-apache-license-header — correctly file_content_matches not file_header, because validate-license.sh greps anywhere and the header is a block comment); 'Copyright The Helm Authors.' line, same file set -> file_content_matches (helm-source-copyright-header); .github/env declares GOLANG_VERSION / GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION -> file_content_matches (helm-ci-env-declares-go/golangci-version); governance/release files present (OWNERS, SECURITY.md, KEYS, code-of-conduct.md, .golangci.yml) -> file_exists; the broken-symlink fixture tree is handled by top-level `ignore: internal/third_party/**`. Inherited via extends: oss-baseline + go@v1 (go.mod/go.sum/shape + Trojan-Source defense) + compliance/apache-2@v1 (LICENSE Apache text + NOTICE + the bundle's own source-header rule) + ci/github-actions@v1.

**What alint improves.** Replaces a two-pass shell grep (scripts/validate-license.sh) with two declarative, schema-validated file_content_matches rules that report the exact offending file with a message, and runs without bash/grep portability concerns. The .github/env single-source assertions promote an implicit CI convention into an enforced invariant. Surfaces the apache-2 bundle's LICENSE-text + NOTICE + governance presence checks helm only partially gates on. Reproducible cross-platform (no make/go needed for the header + structure slice). Note a redundancy worth pruning: compliance/apache-2@v1 already ships apache-2-source-has-license-header (file_header, 25 lines, accepts the 'Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0' form) which overlaps helm-source-apache-license-header — but helm's version is correctly the substring-anywhere form matching validate-license.sh exactly, so keeping the repo-tuned rule (and optionally setting the bundle rule level: off) is the right call.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Tried the reproduce-first lens on every helm bespoke check: the depguard/gomodguard import firewall is golangci-lint execution but the SAME firewall logic is natively expressible with import_gate (per-file, go preset, regex on the extracted import target + allow/deny globs) for the deny-by-package-path subset — so it's not even a gap, it's an IMPROVABLE (alint can express the firewall declaratively without running golangci). The genuinely unaddressable items — golangci-lint's AST linters, govulncheck, CodeQL, Scorecard, `make sign` (PGP signing), go mod tidy freshness, the test suite — are all category-D execution NON-GOALS, correctly out of scope (orchestrate via command). The broken-symlink fixture is handled, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `depguard`-style declarative import-firewall preset that reads an existing .golangci.yml depguard block and lowers it to import_gate rules would let golangci-config'd repos adopt the firewall slice with zero re-authoring. A `ci_version_single_source` helper/idiom (assert that a named env var declared in one canonical file, e.g. .github/env, is referenced — never hardcoded — across all workflow YAMLs) would generalize the helm-ci-env pattern into a reusable cross-file invariant; today the presence half is file_content_matches but the 'no workflow hardcodes a different version' half would need a cross_file equals over extracted workflow `uses:`/`with:` versions (expressible, but a named bundle rule would be cleaner).

*Notes.* Clean, small bespoke surface that maps almost 1:1 to file_content_matches + the bundles. The notable insight is the import_gate-vs-golangci-depguard overlap: alint can declaratively express the import firewall the repo currently only runs via golangci-lint.

---

### `Homebrew/brew` — Ruby package-manager CLI (the `brew` command); RuboCop + Sorbet + RSpec, docs site (Jekyll), large vendored RBI/sorbet tree

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of addressable (non-execution) validation. brew's TOTAL enforcement is ~execution-dominated, so against the WHOLE surface alint replaces a small fraction; but of the ADDRESSABLE (static, non-execution) slice — version coherence, docs frontmatter convention, config-policy presence, structure/metadata, merge-marker hygiene — coverage is ~complete. Honest framing: brew is a 'value-add over a deep execution gate' repo, not a 'replace the gate' repo.

**Enforcement surface.** Homebrew/brew's enforced surface is overwhelmingly EXECUTION (the alint config explicitly buckets it D): brew style = RuboCop (.rubocop.yml, including Homebrew's custom cops), brew typecheck = Sorbet/Tapioca, brew tests = RSpec, brew audit, brew test-bot, Vale prose-lint, markdownlint, actionlint + zizmor + shellcheck on workflows/shell, `licensed status` dep-license scanning, and codegen-freshness git-diff gates (RBI/manpage/completions). By addressable category: (1) CROSS-FILE VERSION COHERENCE — Library/Homebrew/.ruby-version (4.0.5) must equal the RUBY VERSION pinned in Library/Homebrew/Gemfile.lock (bundler enforces lock<->installed at runtime; the version-file<->lockfile equality is the static invariant); docs/.ruby-version is a symlink to the Library one and docs/Gemfile.lock pins the same Ruby ABI. (2) PER-FILE DOCS CONVENTION — the docs Rakefile :lint task flags any docs/*.md (except the generated Manpage.md) lacking a `last_review_date:` frontmatter key (review-enforced, surfaced via xargs echo). (3) CONFIG POLICY — .github/licensed.yml declares an `allowed:` license allowlist; .github/zizmor.yml carries a Homebrew/actions ref-pin supply-chain policy. (4) STRUCTURE/METADATA — .editorconfig (2-space/LF/final-newline), .shellcheckrc (SC2310/2311/2312 suppressions for bin/brew), CODEOWNERS (gates docs/Support-Tiers.md review), .gitattributes (linguist-generated=true on vendored/RBI/licenses dirs). (5) HYGIENE — the Library/Homebrew Ruby tree must not ship merge-conflict markers (excluding vendor/ and sorbet/rbi/).

**What alint replaces.** .ruby-version vs Gemfile.lock RUBY VERSION (Library + docs, two-line regex extract spanning 'RUBY VERSION\n ruby x.y.z') -> cross_file_value_equals/cross_file equals (brew-ruby-version-matches-lock, brew-docs-ruby-version-matches-lock); docs/*.md (except Manpage.md) carry last_review_date frontmatter -> file_content_matches (brew-docs-have-last-review-date); licensed.yml `allowed:` allowlist declared + zizmor ref-pin policy present -> file_content_matches (brew-licensed-allowlist-declared, brew-zizmor-action-pin-policy); .gitattributes marks linguist-generated -> file_content_matches (brew-gitattributes-marks-generated); .editorconfig/.shellcheckrc/CODEOWNERS presence -> file_exists root_only (brew-editorconfig/shellcheckrc/codeowners-exists); merge markers absent in the Ruby tree -> no_merge_conflict_markers (brew-no-merge-markers-ruby, with vendor/ + sorbet/rbi/ excluded); plus oss-baseline inherited.

**What alint improves.** Turns the docs Rakefile's soft `xargs echo` last_review_date warning into a structured, file-pinned violation that fails CI deterministically. Promotes the .ruby-version<->Gemfile.lock equality (today only implicitly guaranteed by bundler at runtime, never a static gate) into an explicit pre-commit-checkable invariant — catching a stale lockfile BEFORE bundler does. Expresses the licensed/zizmor/gitattributes/editorconfig policies as enforced presence rules rather than relying on reviewers noticing their removal. The whole brew config runs with NO Ruby/RuboCop/Sorbet toolchain — a meaningful speedup for the structural slice in a repo whose full check suite is heavy.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Reproduce-first on the docs-frontmatter check: last_review_date presence is file_content_matches (done); if the repo wanted last_review_date to be a valid ISO date, that's still file_content_matches with a date regex (expressible). The two-line 'RUBY VERSION\n ruby x.y.z' lockfile extract works via the regex extractor with an embedded newline (verified pattern in the config). Everything the config buckets D — RuboCop (incl. Homebrew custom cops), Sorbet typecheck, RSpec, brew audit/test-bot, Vale, markdownlint, actionlint/zizmor/shellcheck-as-linters, `licensed status` graph scan, RBI/manpage/completion codegen-freshness diffs — is execution = NON-GOAL, correctly out of scope. The codegen-freshness gates could be orchestrated via generated_file_fresh (mutating outputs: mode) or command_idempotent if brew exposed a --check entrypoint, but that's an orchestration opportunity, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `lockfile_pins_version` cross-file idiom keyed by package manager (bundler/npm/poetry/cargo) — given a .ruby-version/.nvmrc/.python-version and the corresponding lockfile, assert the recorded interpreter version matches — would turn the brew Ruby-coherence pattern (and its npm/python analogues across the corpus) into a one-line rule instead of a bespoke two-line-regex cross_file. A `frontmatter_required` rule (YAML frontmatter block detection + required-key assertion over a markdown glob, with a generated-file exclude) would express the docs last_review_date convention more precisely than a raw regex and generalize to the many Jekyll/Hugo doc trees in the corpus.

*Notes.* Textbook example of the D-dominated repo where alint's role is the static coherence/structure/hygiene layer plus a unified front-door. The .ruby-version<->Gemfile.lock two-line regex extract is a nice demonstration that cross_file's regex extractor handles multi-line lockfile shapes.

---

### `istio/istio` — CNCF Apache-2.0 Go service-mesh monorepo (single root go.mod, module istio.io/istio) + Helm-chart packaging (9 Chart.yaml under manifests/charts/) + ~1,700 release-note YAML fragments + cross-repo SHA pins (istio.deps); CI runs OUT-OF-TREE in Prow (istio/test-infra), so .github/workflows is empty

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (non-execution) validation. The addressable core — Apache header, cross-chart hub coherence, Chart.yaml templated pins, istio.deps SHA shape, release-note schema, VERSION shape, governance/structure markers, AND the dependabot intra-file set-equality (newly confirmed expressible) — is fully native. The unaddressable remainder is the make-lint/gen-check tool chain (golangci incl. depguard, formatters, codegen-freshness, sample validation) = execution non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** istio's enforcement runs in Prow (out-of-tree), driven by `make lint` (a chain of tool wrappers) + `make gen-check` + CODEOWNERS review + bin/check_samples.sh. By category: (1) LICENSE-HEADERS — common/scripts/lint_copyright_banner.sh enforces an Apache header + Copyright line on every source file, excluding *.gen.go/*.pb.go/*_pb2.py (istio uses the short SPDX-template 'Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0' banner). (2) CODEGEN-FRESHNESS — `make gen-check` runs all generators (protoc, charts, CRDs, docs) and git-diffs (execution). (3) LINT-CHAIN — golangci-lint (incl. a depguard IMPORT FIREWALL run BY golangci), gofmt, goimports, shellcheck, hadolint, yamllint, mdl/markdownlint, autopep8, license-lint (all execution). (4) CONFIG-COHERENCE (the bespoke, addressable core) — .github/dependabot.yml's `ignore:` list MUST mirror its `groups.exclude-patterns:` list (the file's own comment: 'Must mirror the ignore list above'); every chart's _internal_defaults_do_not_set.global.hub must match istio-discovery's (registry.istio.io/testing); all manifests/charts/**/Chart.yaml must keep version/appVersion '1.0.0' (release-builder substitutes at build) + apiVersion 'v2'; istio.deps cross-repo pins ($[*].lastStableSHA) must be a 40-char SHA or semver; release-note fragments (releasenotes/notes/*.yaml) must declare apiVersion 'release-notes/v2' + an enumerated kind (bug-fix/security-fix/feature/...); VERSION is a single semver line; values.yaml under multi-key charts wrap defaults under _internal_defaults_do_not_set; common/ vendored files carry a 'DO NOT EDIT ... PROBABLY A COPY' marker. (5) GOVERNANCE — CODEOWNERS with a `* @owner` fallback. (6) SAMPLES — bin/check_samples.sh validates sample manifests (execution).

**What alint replaces.** Apache header on sources -> file_header (istio-apache-header; though redundant with the apache-2 bundle's apache-2-source-has-license-header which already accepts the same banner and excludes generated/vendored — set the bundle rule off and keep the istio-tuned scope); cross-chart global.hub equality -> cross_file_value_equals with yaml extract (istio-chart-hub-consistent); Chart.yaml version/appVersion/apiVersion pins -> yaml_path_equals (istio-chart-*-templated/-v2); istio.deps SHA/semver shape -> json_path_matches (istio-deps-proxy/ztunnel-sha); release-note apiVersion -> yaml_path_equals + kind enum -> yaml_path_matches with an alternation regex + if_present (istio-releasenotes-apiversion/-kind); VERSION single-semver line -> file_content_matches (istio-version-file-shape); istio.deps presence -> file_exists root_only; CODEOWNERS `* @owner` fallback -> file_content_matches; _internal_defaults_do_not_set wrapper + common/ DO-NOT-EDIT marker -> file_content_matches; plus oss-baseline + go@v1 + compliance/apache-2@v1 + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts@v1 inherited.

**What alint improves.** Two material wins. (1) The cross-chart global.hub coherence and the Chart.yaml templated-value pins are real invariants istio does NOT gate on in CI (the release-builder substitutes versions, so a stray non-1.0.0 chart version slips through review) — alint makes them enforced, schema-validated, and file-pinned. (2) istio-chart-hub-consistent can be made STRICTLY BETTER than its current form: the config explicitly lists only 2 of ~9 charts as targets; switching to a glob target `files: manifests/charts/**/values.yaml` + `extract: {yaml: $._internal_defaults_do_not_set.global.hub}` + `allow_missing_target: true` (verified: a chart lacking the key extracts an empty set, suppressed by allow_missing) covers ALL charts in one rule and auto-includes charts added later, while ztunnel (which intentionally omits global.hub) is silently tolerated. The release-note apiVersion+kind schema and istio.deps SHA shape are enforced declaratively without the external release-notes tool. Runs with no Go/protoc/golangci toolchain.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none — and critically, the ONE 'B GAP' the config records is now a PHANTOM. The config (lines 63-71) claims the dependabot.yml `ignore:` <-> `groups.exclude-patterns:` set-equality across two JSONPaths in ONE file 'is not expressible today' and 'needs a new cross_file_set_equals primitive'. Under the post-v0.12 build it IS expressible: `cross_file` with `relation: set_equals`, where `source: {file: .github/dependabot.yml, extract: {yaml: $.updates[*].ignore[*].dependency-name}}` and `targets: [{file: .github/dependabot.yml, extract: {yaml: $.updates[*].groups...exclude-patterns[*]}}]`. I VERIFIED: (a) set_equals is a registered relation that compares multi-value extracted BTreeSets and reports both missing+extra (cross_file.rs:95,276,420); (b) YAML extract via RFC-9535 JSONPath returns a Vec (multi-node match) into the set (extract.rs:154); (c) there is NO guard requiring source.file != target.file — same-file two-query comparison is allowed. So this is REPLACEABLE today, not a gap. (The exact JSONPaths depend on dependabot's nested groups schema, but the single-file multi-query set-equality MECHANISM is fully supported.) Everything else (golangci AST + depguard execution, gofmt/goimports, shellcheck/hadolint/yamllint/mdl/autopep8, make gen-check codegen-freshness diffs, license-lint, bin/check_samples.sh, istioctl validate) is category-D execution NON-GOAL.

**Abstraction ideas.** A first-class `same_file_set_equals` / `intra_file_lists_agree` SUGAR over cross_file set_equals (two JSONPaths in one file must hold a set relation) would make the dependabot ignore<->exclude-patterns mirror a one-stanza rule and self-document the 'these two lists must stay in sync' intent the file's own comment expresses — the underlying engine already does it, but the ergonomics are non-obvious (the study agent missed it entirely). A `chart_values_pin@v1` bundled helper for Helm-packaged repos (assert Chart.yaml version/appVersion/apiVersion across a charts glob + cross-chart shared-default coherence + the _internal_defaults_do_not_set wrapper) would capture the istio/helm-chart pattern reusably. A `vendored_copy_marker` idiom (every file under a vendored-from-upstream tree must carry a DO-NOT-EDIT marker) generalizes the common/ check and the linguist-generated patterns seen across the corpus.

*Notes.* The headline result of this batch: istio's single recorded B-gap (cross_file_set_equals for two JSONPaths in one file) is RETIRED by the post-v0.12 cross_file unification — verified against cross_file.rs (set_equals relation, multi-value YAML JSONPath, no same-file guard). Plus a concrete IMPROVABLE: istio-chart-hub-consistent should use a glob target + allow_missing_target to cover all 9 charts instead of an explicit 2-chart list. Confirms the brief's warning that PRE-build study configs systematically over-report gaps.

---

### `jekyll/jekyll` — Ruby site-generator CLI gem (bundler + rake + release-please; community-maintained, RuboCop incl. custom cops, check-spelling, CodeQL)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable surface (release coherence + frozen-string + dependabot + gemspec-license + config-presence + conventional-commit-title all A). The remaining enforcement (RuboCop custom cops, Minitest/Cucumber, check-spelling, CodeQL) is ~all category-D execution — value-add-only there.

**Enforcement surface.** jekyll's repo-level enforcement is dominated by EXECUTION (category D): Minitest + Cucumber suites (script/test, script/cucumber); `jekyll new` + build sanity (script/default-site, script/profile-docs); RuboCop with custom in-repo cops under rubocop/jekyll/*.rb (NoPutsAllowed / NoPAllowed / AssertEqualLiteralActual) AND Style/FrozenStringLiteralComment:always; GitHub check-spelling action (.github/actions/spelling/* with allow/expect/reject lists, sorted+unique); CodeQL (ruby + javascript). CONFIG/STATE checks (alint-addressable): conventional-commit PR-TITLE lint (.github/workflows/conventional-commits.yml); release-please version coherence — lib/jekyll/version.rb VERSION must equal the '.' package version in .github/release-please-manifest.json (release-please reads both; mismatch mints wrong tag/gem); dependabot covers github-actions + bundler ecosystems; the gem must stay MIT (CODEOWNERS pins LICENSE as 'should never change'). Hygiene: editorconfig present; no tracked build artifacts; Gemfile.lock intentionally gitignored (application-vs-library convention). The two vendored MIME files (lib/jekyll/mime.types) carry a 'generated by script/vendor-mimes' do-not-edit banner but are NOT gated by a `vendor-mimes && git diff --exit-code` freshness job.

**What alint replaces.** release-please version coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (regex-extract VERSION from version.rb, json $['.'] from the manifest; the canonical A1 enforced check, already in the config); frozen_string_literal magic comment on every lib/ and test/ .rb -> file_content_matches \A# frozen_string_literal: true (declarative pre-RuboCop guard for the Style/FrozenStringLiteralComment cop); dependabot bundler+actions ecosystem presence -> file_content_matches; gemspec MIT license -> file_content_matches; .rubocop.yml / spelling expect.txt / release-please-config.json existence -> file_exists; mime.types do-not-edit banner -> file_content_matches; editorconfig + no-tracked-artifacts -> the extended bundles tooling/editorconfig@v1 + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts@v1 + oss-baseline@v1 + ci/github-actions@v1 (all four verified present in rulesets/v1/).

**What alint improves.** ONE declarative reviewable cross_file rule for release-please coherence vs a release-please tool whose mismatch is only discovered when the release PR mints a bad tag/gem — alint catches it on every push, pre-release, before any tag exists. frozen_string_literal as a fast file_content_matches sweep fails in milliseconds on a new file before the full RuboCop run spins up Ruby. The dependabot-ecosystem and gemspec-license assertions are coherence facts RuboCop/CI do not check at all (pure value-add the repo lacks today).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The original config parked the conventional-commit PR-title lint as a D non-goal, but that is an OVER-REPORT: git_commit_message (pattern: + since: PR-range mode, verified in git_commit_message.rs) and git_commit_subject_matches NATIVELY express conventional-commit subject linting against the since..HEAD range — so even that check is A, not D. The custom RuboCop cops (NoPutsAllowed etc.) and the spelling/CodeQL/test runs are genuine EXECUTION non-goals (category D, AST/NLP/semantic), correctly out of scope — orchestrate via command, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `ruby`/`bundler` ecosystem ruleset (corpus finding: alint ships no Ruby bundle) gated on facts.has_gemspec: frozen_string_literal sweep on lib/**+test/**, gemspec license-present, Gemfile.lock-tracked-policy as an OPT-IN toggle (jekyll wants it OFF = app convention, a library wants it ON), dependabot-covers-bundler. A `release-please` composite rule kind that knows the manifest<->version-file pairing convention out of the box (version-file path + manifest package key as two options) would turn the 1 hand-written cross_file rule into a one-liner reusable across the many release-please repos in the corpus.

*Notes.* Config is valid against current alint; the symlink ignore (test/source/_includes/tmp + symlink-test/**) is a legitimate walk-abort guard. Single root MIT LICENSE, no per-file headers — apache-header/SPDX bundles correctly NOT extended (R6). The mime.types rule is honestly framed as a banner-invariant, not a freshness check (jekyll runs no vendor-mimes diff gate); if jekyll later added one, generated_file_fresh STDOUT-mode would cover it.

---

### `JetBrains/kotlin` — Go-... no — Kotlin/JVM compiler+stdlib MONOREPO (~5M LOC; TeamCity CI via .space/safe-merge.json; Gradle; in-repo JUnit CodeConformanceTest/LicensesTests conformance suite run from rootDir; gradle dependency-verification)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the ADDRESSABLE (conformance/state) surface — six of the seven CodeConformanceTest invariants + LicensesTests are fully A; only the lookbehind SSoT check is a partial (over-firing) fidelity loss. Of the TOTAL repo enforcement, the overwhelming majority is category-D Gradle/bytecode/testData execution, so absolute coverage is low — but the slice alint targets it covers almost completely.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforcement is split between (1) Gradle/TeamCity EXECUTION (category D): compile + the full conformance JUnit suite in repo/codebase-tests/**, gradle dependency-verification (verification-metadata.xml hashes enforced at build), bytecode/API-dump checks, testData-driven compiler tests; and (2) repo/codebase-tests CodeConformanceTest.kt + LicensesTests.kt which assert STATE-level invariants that read like declarative lints: testNotUsingCanonicalFileApi (KT-69613: ban .canonicalPath/.canonicalFile in build-tool source trees), testNoDirectPathToStringConversion (KT-83715: ban .absolutePathString() in kotlin-build-tools API/impl), testNoBadSubstringsInProjectCode (banned-package import firewall: org.jetbrains.jet, com.beust.jcommander.internal, gnu.trove — excluding testData/dependencies/codebase-tests), testRepositoriesAbuse (no bintray.com in build scripts outside the cache-redirector module), testNoHardcodedPathSeparatorInSSOT (KT-84449: unescaped ${File.pathSeparator} banned in the arguments-DSL SSoT, via NEGATIVE-LOOKBEHIND), LicensesTests.testLicensesAreExistingFiles (every license/third_party/*.txt referenced from license/README.md must exist on disk). Documented conventions in .ai/guidelines.md (a sub-area CLAUDE.md is exactly '@AGENTS.md'). Structural: license/{LICENSE,NOTICE,COPYRIGHT_HEADER}.txt, gradle/verification-metadata.xml, kotlin-js-store/yarn.lock all tracked.

**What alint replaces.** testNotUsingCanonicalFileApi / testNoDirectPathToStringConversion -> file_content_forbidden scoped to the exact source trees (\.canonical(Path|File)\b, \.absolutePathString\(\)); testNoBadSubstringsInProjectCode banned packages -> file_content_forbidden with include **/*.kt+**/*.java and exclude testData/dependencies/codebase-tests (org\.jetbrains\.jet\b, com\.beust\.jcommander\.internal, gnu\.trove); testRepositoriesAbuse -> file_content_forbidden on **/*.gradle.kts+**/*.gradle excluding the cache-redirector module (bintray\.com); LicensesTests.testLicensesAreExistingFiles -> registry_paths_resolve (source license/README.md, regex-extract \[(license/third_party/[^\]]+\.txt)\], base lint_root — base option verified in registry_paths_resolve.rs); .ai sub-area CLAUDE.md stub -> file_content_matches ^@AGENTS\.md$; license/*.txt + verification-metadata.xml + yarn.lock presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** These conformance invariants run TODAY only as part of a multi-million-LOC JUnit suite that requires the full Gradle build to spin up — alint expresses the SAME bans as ~7 declarative file_content_forbidden/registry rules that run on the raw tree in seconds, ideal for a fast pre-merge/local guard before the heavyweight CodeConformanceTest. Consolidating six string/path bans + the license-registry check into one reviewable .alint.yml is materially more maintainable and auditable than scattered assertions inside Kotlin test methods. The import-firewall bans (org.jetbrains.jet etc.) could alternatively use import_gate (scala/java presets exist) but file_content_forbidden is the RIGHT call here since these are substring bans not import-path-anchored matches.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE narrow GAP: testNoHardcodedPathSeparatorInSSOT uses a NEGATIVE-LOOKBEHIND (?<![\\$])\$\{File\.pathSeparator\} — forbid the token UNLESS preceded by backslash or $ (the escaped / raw-$$-string legal forms). alint's regex engine is the Rust `regex` crate (Cargo.toml: regex="1"), which has NO look-behind/look-ahead, so file_content_forbidden with the bare pattern \$\{File\.pathSeparator\} OVER-FIRES on the legitimate escaped forms (small fidelity loss the config already flags). I tried (a) anchoring alternation and (b) import_gate — neither expresses 'X not preceded by Y' in a presence test. This is a real but EXTREMELY narrow gap (one test, one file tree); not worth a kind on its own. Everything else (banned bytecode patterns, API-dump diffs, testData compiler conformance, dependency-verification hash enforcement) is genuine category-D execution — out of scope, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `forbidden_unless_preceded_by` / lookbehind-emulation option on file_content_forbidden (a `negate_if_prefixed: ["\\\\", "$"]` escape-aware guard) would close the KT-84449 gap generically — many 'ban X but allow the escaped form' checks share this shape. (2) A bundled `kotlin`/`jvm-monorepo` ruleset packaging the recurring CodeConformanceTest-style bans (canonical-file-api, absolute-path-string, deprecated-package firewall, bintray-redirector) as a reusable allowlist-driven set. (3) testData-exclusion is a per-rule pain across this 5M-LOC repo; a config-level shared `exclude:` axis (a named path-set referenced by many rules, e.g. exclude_set: kotlin-testdata) would remove the repeated **/testData/** boilerplate — a real ergonomic need this repo demonstrates.

*Notes.* Hygiene bundles (trailing-whitespace/final-newline) deliberately NOT extended repo-wide — would emit tens of thousands of intentional testData-fixture violations; correctly recorded as C tuning (wants paths.exclude of testData), NOT a defect. Config is valid; only oss-baseline@v1 extended. Conventional-commit/commit-discipline is enforced by TeamCity not in-repo, so no git_commit_* rule is needed here.

### `jgm/pandoc` — Haskell CLI/library — 4-package cabal project (pandoc / pandoc-cli / pandoc-lua-engine / pandoc-server) built with cabal AND stack; solo maintainer (jgm); Makefile-driven prerelease gates

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the addressable Makefile `check-*`/state surface (version-sync, manversion, license-coherence, no-tabs, no-git-deps, trailing-ws, changelog-heading all A — the last via cross_file subset). The DTD/XSD/EPUB/hlint/-Werror validation that forms the bulk of pandoc's CI is category-D execution — value-add-only.

**Enforcement surface.** EXECUTION-dominated (category D): build+test across 4 GHC versions + stack + windows (ci.yml, -Werror warning-free); hlint AST linter (lint.yml + CONTRIBUTING pre-commit hook); JATS DTD validation of writer output (format-validation.yml, remote validator); docx XML-schema validation (xmllint + WML XSD, OOXMLValidator); EPUB validation (epubcheck + ace, Makefile validate-epub); cabal check / cabal outdated / stack-lint-extra-deps (resolver execution); commit-message line-length lint over git log (commit-validation-pr.yml). REPO-STATE checks (alint-addressable), mostly in the Makefile `prerelease`/`check-*` targets: check-version-sync ([ VERSION == PANDOC_CLI_VERSION ] AND the `pandoc == X` build-depends pin inside pandoc-cli.cabal); check-changelog (a `## pandoc <VERSION> (YYYY-MM-DD)` heading must exist in changelog.md); check-manversion (all three man pages carry the current version in their .TH line); license coherence (CONTRIBUTING §8: every package GPL-2.0-or-later); checkdocs (no literal tabs in MANUAL.txt/changelog.md/doc/pandoc-server.md/doc/pandoc-lua.md); check-cabal/check-stack (no `git:` source-deps — prerelease-only; note stack.yaml legitimately HAS 3 git deps at this pin); fix_spacing (no trailing whitespace in Haskell SOURCEFILES).

**What alint replaces.** check-version-sync (3-way: pandoc.cabal version == pandoc-cli.cabal version == the `pandoc == X` pin) -> cross_file_value_equals with two regex-extract targets; check-manversion -> cross_file_value_equals (cabal version -> the .TH version in all three man pages); license coherence -> cross_file_value_equals across the 4 .cabal license: fields; checkdocs no-tabs -> file_content_forbidden \t on the four doc files; check-cabal/check-stack no-git-deps -> file_content_forbidden (?m)^\s*git: on cabal.project / stack.yaml; fix_spacing -> no_trailing_whitespace on src/pandoc-cli/pandoc-server/pandoc-lua-engine **/*.hs; check-changelog version-heading -> see below (NOW cross_file subset, not the inlined literal the config used); editorconfig/.hlint.yaml/.stylish-haskell.yaml presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** Replaces the Makefile's grep/sed/[ ]-glued `check-*` targets — which run ONLY when the maintainer manually invokes `make prerelease` — with ONE schema-validated, always-on .alint.yml. The 3-way version-sync (cabal version + cli version + the `pandoc == X` dependency pin) is exactly the cross-file coherence shell grep expresses fragilely; alint makes it declarative and reviewable. Crucially: cross_file relation:subset (source=cabal version, target=the FULL set of `## pandoc X (YYYY-MM-DD)` headings via multi-match regex) tracks the version AUTOMATICALLY from the cabal, where the Makefile check inlines `$(VERSION)` and the original config inlined the literal 3.9.0.2.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config explicitly recorded a 'new-kind candidate' GAP for check-changelog: 'cross_file_value_equals does NOT fit — changelog.md is an append-only ledger of 219 historical headings, so value-equality produces 204 false violations; need a cross-file source-value-appears-in-target primitive.' On REPRODUCTION this is PHANTOM: cross_file `relation: subset` is exactly that primitive. I verified cross_file.rs set_violation: subset fires only on `missing = source.difference(actual)` — i.e. every SOURCE value must be IN the target set; the 204 historical 'extra' headings are ignored. With source = pandoc.cabal version (single value), target = changelog.md regex '(?m)^## pandoc ([0-9][0-9.]*) ' (extract.rs uses captures_iter = ALL matches = the full heading set), subset asserts 'the current cabal version has a changelog heading' AND auto-tracks the cabal — strictly better than the inlined literal. So the recorded new-kind candidate should be RETRACTED. The hlint/JATS/docx/EPUB/-Werror/commit-line-length runs are genuine category-D execution non-goals.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `.cabal` structured extractor (cabal is neither TOML/JSON/XML, so every version/license pull here is a hand-written regex) — a first-class `cabal:` extract path (version:/license:/build-depends pins) would turn 3 brittle regexes into stable queries, valuable across the Haskell corpus. A composite `version_coherence` convenience kind: 'this source version must EQUAL these targets AND APPEAR-IN these ledger targets' in one rule (today = one cross_file equals + one cross_file subset) — the changelog-heading-plus-version-sync pattern recurs in nearly every release-gated repo in the corpus (pandoc, fzf, jekyll, ...).

*Notes.* Honest config detail: pandoc-stack-no-git-deps is set to WARNING because stack.yaml legitimately carries 3 git deps (typst-hs/typst-symbols/texmath) at this pin — it's a prerelease-only gate, not a main-branch invariant; correct severity choice. The changelog rule is the headline reproduce-first win: the config's own prose flags it as a gap and it is not one under post-build alint.

### `junegunn/fzf` — Go CLI/TUI tool (fuzzy finder; solo maintainer junegunn; go + make build; Ruby/minitest integration tests; multi-file release-version coherence gate)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the addressable surface (the single enforced 'version consistency' behavior across 4 files + the CHANGELOG heading, all A — the heading via cross_file subset). The bulk of fzf's CI (gofmt/rubocop/shfmt/typos/CodeQL/fuzz/codegen) is category-D execution — value-add-only there.

**Enforcement surface.** EXECUTION-dominated (category D): gofmt, go vet/build/test, Ruby rubocop + minitest integration tests, shfmt (shell formatting), typos (spell-check), CodeQL, go-fuzz fuzzing, and the common.sh shell codegen (a generated shell-integration blob). ADDRESSABLE (the enforced 'Verify version consistency' gate, Makefile:148-152 + release.yml:41-50): the source-of-truth version lives in `install` (version=0.73.1); install.ps1 ($version="X"), man/man1/fzf.1 and man/man1/fzf-tmux.1 ("fzf X" in .TH) must all carry the identical X.Y.Z, AND CHANGELOG.md must have a matching top-level version heading (Makefile `grep -q ^$(VERSION_REGEX)$$ CHANGELOG.md`). Structural files tracked: RELEASE.md (release process), .editorconfig (shell indent policy), .github/CODEOWNERS, .tool-versions (golang/ruby/shfmt pins).

**What alint replaces.** version consistency across install/install.ps1/fzf.1/fzf-tmux.1 -> cross_file_value_equals (source=install regex version=([0-9.]+), 3 regex-extract targets); CHANGELOG version-heading present -> see below (NOW cross_file subset tracking the install version, not the un-tracked literal-shape match the config used); RELEASE.md / .editorconfig / .github/CODEOWNERS / .tool-versions presence -> file_exists; .editorconfig hygiene + go conventions -> the extended bundles oss-baseline@v1 + go@v1 (both verified present).

**What alint improves.** Replaces the release.yml 'Verify version consistency' shell step + Makefile grep glue — which run only in the release pipeline — with an always-on declarative cross_file rule that catches a half-bumped version on any push, before the release workflow ever fires. The single source-of-truth (install) with 3 derived targets is precisely the cross-file coherence the shell expresses imperatively. And cross_file subset lets the CHANGELOG-heading check TRACK the install version rather than merely asserting 'some X.Y.Z heading exists' (which would pass even if the CURRENT version's heading were missing but an older one present).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's fzf-changelog-has-version-heading is a file_content_matches (?m)^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ that — as the comment concedes — 'cannot be a value-equality target because CHANGELOG lists all past versions', so it only checks 'SOME version heading exists', NOT that the CURRENT one does. On reproduction this is fully closed by cross_file relation:subset: source=install version (single value), target=CHANGELOG.md regex '(?m)^([0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+)$' (captures_iter = the full set of all release headings), subset asserts the current install version IS among them while ignoring the historical extras — verified against cross_file.rs subset semantics. This makes the check version-tracking and exact, strictly better than the config. Also note: any conventional-commit/commit-discipline CI fzf runs would be A via git_commit_message, not D. The remaining gofmt/rubocop/shfmt/typos/CodeQL/fuzz/common.sh-codegen surface is genuine category-D execution.

**Abstraction ideas.** Same composite `version_coherence` kind useful for pandoc/jekyll applies here: 'one source version EQUALS these N targets AND APPEARS-IN this CHANGELOG ledger' — fzf is a textbook instance (4 equality targets + 1 subset/ledger target) and would collapse the 2 rules into 1. A `release-files` convenience axis (install/install.ps1/man-pages as a named target set) would also DRY the per-repo target enumeration. The common.sh shell-codegen blob, IF fzf added a `regenerate && git diff --exit-code` gate, would map onto generated_file_fresh — worth noting as a latent fit but currently fzf does not gate freshness so it stays value-add.

*Notes.* Cross-repo theme confirmed across all four: the 'CHANGELOG version present' pattern that pandoc AND fzf configs both flagged as a NEW-KIND candidate is a phantom gap — cross_file relation:subset (source single version, target multi-match heading set) expresses it natively and BETTER (auto-tracks the manifest). Recommend retracting that backlog candidate. fzf config is valid against current alint; value-add rules correctly excluded from the coverage ratio.
