### `neovim-neovim` — C-core + Lua-runtime editor; CMake/Zig dual build; community governance; Apache-2.0 + Vim license

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface once B1-codegen-freshness and the three phantom-gap content rules are recognized as expressible; only the B2 per-token count budget remains a true gap. The repo's ENFORCEMENT effort is ~all D (compilers/linters/sanitizers/tests), so addressable-of-total is small — but of that addressable slice alint now covers nearly all of it.

**Enforcement surface.** The ENFORCED bespoke surface is overwhelmingly tool-execution (category D): stylua, uncrustify, clang-tidy, clang-analyzer, src/clint.lua (bespoke C linter), lua-language-server (luals), shellcheck, ts_query_ls, include-what-you-use (IWYU), CodeQL, Coverity, scripts/lintcommit.lua (commit-message policy / allowed_types), scripts/lintdoc.lua, ASAN/UBSAN/TSAN/valgrind, the unit+functional+old test suites, -Werror builds (cmake/zig/old-cmake/external-deps/windows), zizmor (workflow security). license-headers: NONE per-file — single root LICENSE.txt (Apache-2.0 + Vim, 'Neovim contributors'); apache/governance + compliance/apache-2 deliberately NOT extended (would FP). lockfile: NONE — dependency provenance lives in two hand-synced manifests (cmake.deps/deps.txt URL+SHA256 pairs; build.zig.zon .url+.hash) kept in sync by scripts/bump_deps.lua, NOT CI-gated. hooks: NONE (no pre-commit/husky/lefthook). CI security: .github/zizmor.yml unpinned-uses policy = FIRST-PARTY (actions/*, github/*) may tag-pin, THIRD-PARTY must SHA-pin. codegen-freshness (enforced TWICE): docs.yml — `make doc` must leave runtime/doc/*.txt + runtime/lua/vim/_meta/*.gen.lua unchanged (git-diff); test.yml zig-build-linux — regenerate doctags then `diff -u runtime/doc/tags zig-out/runtime/doc/tags`. cross-file token coherence (enforced): scripts/linterrcodes.lua (docs.yml) — every EXX error code defined in src/nvim/**.c must be documented in runtime/doc/*.txt, with a per-code occurrence-count budget (the dup_allowed table).

**What alint replaces.** GitHub Actions hardening (permissions/name) -> ci/github-actions@v1; the first-party-vs-third-party SHA-pin policy -> yaml_path_matches on $.jobs.*.steps[*].uses with a regex allowlisting actions/*|github/*|local|docker + 40-hex SHA for the rest (the C1 override in the config); README/LICENSE presence + no merge markers + final newline + no bidi -> oss-baseline@v1; presence of formatter/linter configs (.editorconfig/.stylua.toml/.clang-format/.clang-tidy/.luarc.json/src/uncrustify.cfg), process docs (CONTRIBUTING/MAINTAIN/BUILD/INSTALL/AGENTS), and dep manifests (cmake.deps/deps.txt, build.zig.zon) -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** The action-pinning policy becomes one declarative reviewable rule that exactly encodes zizmor's first-party/third-party split, runnable without zizmor installed. CRITICAL: the codegen-freshness B1 (the config's flagged gap) is now REPLACEABLE — generated_file_fresh has a mutating `outputs:` mode (snapshot -> run generator -> diff -> restore, leaving the tree pure) that is precisely `make gen && git diff --exit-code`; `make doc` leaving runtime/doc/*.txt + _meta/*.gen.lua unchanged, and the doctags regenerate-and-diff, both map directly (outputs: globs; never mtime, so it works on a fresh clone). That turns two CI git-diff scripts into one declarative rule each. The config OMITTED three content-policy assertions (zizmor.yml declares unpinned-uses; deps.txt pins a SHA256 per tarball; lintcommit.lua declares allowed_types) claiming bare literal paths match nothing — see gaps; those are now expressible too.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap survives: neovim B2 — cross-file token coherence with PER-TOKEN OCCURRENCE-COUNT BUDGETS (every EXX code defined in src/nvim/**.c must appear in runtime/doc/*.txt, with scripts/linterrcodes.lua's dup_allowed per-code count table). I tried cross_file relation:set_equals (extract regex 'E[0-9]+' from the C set and the doc set) — that verifies the two SETS of codes match (catches an undocumented or stale code), which covers the existence half well. But there is NO counting relation anywhere in the rule roster (confirmed: no occurrence/count/at_least primitive in cross_file or elsewhere), so the per-code count BUDGET (code Exxx may appear N times) is genuinely unexpressible. Closest attempt = set_equals; it falls short only on the count budget. REPRODUCE NOTE on the config's OTHER claims, all PHANTOM on current alint: (a) B1 codegen-freshness is RESOLVED (generated_file_fresh outputs: mode, above). (b) The three OMITTED file_content_matches rules were dropped claiming a bare literal like 'cmake.deps/deps.txt' 'matches NOTHING; only */** globs resolve' — FALSE: file_content_matches iterates the index and a literal compiles to an exact-path glob; its tests prove bare 'LICENSE' matches (file_content_matches.rs:170-199). All three (zizmor unpinned-uses present, deps.txt SHA256-per-tarball via a (?m) multiline pattern, lintcommit allowed_types present) are expressible today and should be added. Everything else — stylua/uncrustify/clang-tidy/clint.lua/luals/IWYU/Coverity/sanitizers/-Werror builds/test suites/lintcommit-message-policy — is correct category-D.

**Abstraction ideas.** A counting-aware cross-file kind — `cross_file` gaining a `relation: covers_with_counts` (or the `registry_value_used` kind from the TS row extended with min/max occurrence per token). Shape: extract a token set from corpus A (regex), require each appears in corpus B with an occurrence count in [min,max] possibly per-token-overridable (the linterrcodes dup_allowed table). This single primitive closes neovim B2 AND TypeScript's diagnostic-key-usage need — the only two genuine gaps in this whole group converge on it, making it the highest-value new abstraction here. Secondary (already noted across the group): a first-party-action-allowlist PRESET for the gha-pin rule so neovim/most repos don't re-derive the regex; and bundling the SHA256-manifest + provenance assertions into a 'vendored-deps' fragment.

*Notes.* Two of the three 'gaps' this config flags (B1 + the omitted content rules) are stale PRE-build analysis; reproduction collapses them. The action-pin C1 override is a good, correct policy encoding. Only B2's occurrence-count budget genuinely needs a new kind.

---

### `netty-netty` — Java/Netty multi-module Maven monorepo (network framework), Apache-2 governance, JNI/native build

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — header, http-ban structural slice, EA-version coherence, and all governance/structure presence are native; only the per-host nohttp suppression nuance is unreachable. Of Netty's TOTAL enforcement the addressable slice is itself small (the bulk is mvn-verify execution = correct category-D non-goal).

**Enforcement surface.** Netty's repo-state enforcement runs almost entirely inside `mvn verify` and a handful of `.github/workflows/*.yml` + `.github/scripts/*.sh`. By category: (license-headers) a checkstyle `Header` module (config in the external netty-build-common jar, validate phase) requires every .java/.sh source to begin with Netty's literal ASF-derived header ("The Netty Project licenses this file to you under the Apache License, version 2.0" — lowercase v, 'licenses this file to you' not 'Licensed'). (hygiene/security) the io.spring.nohttp checkstyle module (`nohttp-checkstyle-validation` pom execution) forbids plaintext http:// links tree-wide, with ~40 documented per-host/per-message suppressions in nohttp-checkstyle-suppressions.xml. (cross-file coherence) `.github/scripts/check_java_ea_version.sh` hard-codes EXPECTED_VERSION with a 'keep in sync with docker-compose.centos-7.26.yaml' comment; check-jdk.ea-version.yml gates on it. (structure/layout) Apache governance artefacts NOTICE.txt/LICENSE.txt at root, Maven wrapper mvnw/mvnw.cmd, and every reactor module dir carrying a pom.xml are maintained by hand with no standalone CI gate. (execution-only — category D, alint orchestrates not reimplements) checkstyle AST style rules, gradle/maven-enforcer, forbidden-apis (banned-signature bytecode scan), revapi binary-compatibility/API-diff, xml-format-maven-plugin, the full JUnit + native (BoringSSL/epoll/io_uring) build matrix, and CodeQL.

**What alint replaces.** checkstyle Header module -> file_header (paths **/*.java,**/*.sh, lines:16, pattern on the literal Netty header line); io.spring.nohttp http:// ban (structural slice) -> file_content_forbidden (pattern 'http://[a-zA-Z]'); check_java_ea_version.sh<->docker-compose EA-version coherence -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals, regex extract one capture group each, the '-open' suffix stripped via the target regex); NOTICE.txt/LICENSE.txt presence -> file_exists root_only; mvnw/mvnw.cmd presence -> file_exists root_only.

**What alint improves.** The EA-version coherence is the standout: today it lives in a hand-rolled bash script whose ONLY linkage to the docker file is a prose 'keep in sync' comment that nothing enforces against drift in the docker direction — one declarative cross_file rule makes the two-way invariant machine-checked and reviewable in the config rather than buried in a shell heredoc. The header + nohttp checks consolidate two checkstyle-module configs (each requiring the external netty-build-common jar + a full Maven validate phase to run) into two ~6-line declarative rules that run on a bare clone with no JVM/Maven toolchain — materially cheaper for pre-commit / fork-CI. file_header anchors on the exact literal wording (catching the easy-to-miss lowercase-v / 'licenses this file to you' variant) which the bundled compliance/apache-2 and java@v1 rulesets do NOT match (java@v1 has no header rule at all), so a hand-written rule is correct here.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** nohttp's PER-HOST / PER-MESSAGE suppression model is a genuine (narrow) gap. nohttp-checkstyle-suppressions.xml whitelists specific unreachable/legacy http:// hosts inline; alint can only carve out whole FILES via paths.exclude, not individual matched URLs within an otherwise-checked file. I tried (a) file_content_forbidden with a negative-look-ahead pattern to bake the host allowlist into the regex — impossible, the engine is the `regex` 1.x crate which has no look-around (verified Cargo.toml); (b) an exclude list of the suppressed lines — exclusions are path-globs, not content predicates. So a file containing both a banned and a sanctioned http:// host can't be expressed; it's all-or-nothing per file. Everything else Netty enforces in the addressable bucket is cleanly replaceable; the remainder (checkstyle AST, forbidden-apis, revapi, native build, CodeQL) is correctly category-D execution, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A per-match content allowlist on the forbiddance kinds — e.g. file_content_forbidden gaining `allow_matches: [<regex|literal>...]` (or an `except_lines:` predicate) so a banned-substring rule can carve out sanctioned occurrences the way nohttp's suppressions XML does, without whole-file exclusion. This single primitive would also serve any 'forbid X except in these blessed cases' need (legacy-API call sites, intentional TODO markers, allowlisted insecure URLs) that recurs across compliance-heavy repos. Secondary, smaller: a bundled `compliance/apache-2-netty` variant or a parameterized header-text option so the 'licenses this file to you' ASF-derivative wording is shippable rather than re-authored per repo.

*Notes.* Config is well-calibrated and honest. One forward-looking note: Netty does have changelog/version coherence touchpoints (release tags, version.properties) that pair_changed_together/changeset_requires_path could express as value-add, though Netty does not currently CI-gate them so they'd be value-add not coverage.

---

### `NixOS-nix` — C++ package-manager (meson + Nix build), NixOS-foundation governance, heavy flake/VM/functional-test enforcement

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~70% of the ADDRESSABLE enforced surface — merge-conflict (full), the simplest json-schema case, and the structural/changelog-fragment slice are native; the draft-04/YAML/pointer-fragment schema remainder is the unreachable part. Of nix's TOTAL enforcement the addressable slice is small; the dominant mass is Nix-build/test/formatter execution (correct category-D non-goal).

**Enforcement surface.** Nix's enforced surface is dominated by Nix-build/test EXECUTION (category D): component + VM + functional tests, flake checks, coverage, clang-tidy, and a treefmt stack of formatters (nixfmt, clang-format, meson-format, shellcheck) plus `jv` JSON-Schema runs and the doc build. The natively-addressable enforced slice is thin: (hygiene) the `check-merge-conflicts-2` pre-commit hook (maintainers/flake-module.nix) greps EVERY staged file for `>>>>>>> ` conflict markers — broader than oss-baseline's md/txt/toml/yml/json default; (schema/codegen) src/json-schema-checks/meson.build validates committed example JSON against committed JSON schemas via the `jv` CLI, but the schemas are YAML + draft-04 with many `#/$defs/...` JSON-Pointer sub-schema fragments. (structure — value-add, not gated) the repo-global `.version` single-source-of-truth file (read by meson `files('.version')` and `lib.fileContents ./.version`), root meson.build, flake.nix/flake.lock, CONTRIBUTING.md, and doc/manual/rl-next/*.md changelog-d fragments each needing a `synopsis:` frontmatter key (enforced only as part of the changelog-d doc-build execution). A real alint sharp-edge surfaced: tests/functional/lang/ ships an INTENTIONAL broken symlink (symlink-resolution/broken -> nonexistent), reachable twice (src/nix-functional-tests is a symlink to ../tests/functional) — it aborts alint's directory walk entirely ('walk error: No such file or directory'), so the config must `ignore:` both paths.

**What alint replaces.** check-merge-conflicts-2 pre-commit hook -> no_merge_conflict_markers (include widened to src/**/*.{cc,hh,h,c}, **/*.nix, **/*.sh, **/meson.build to match the hook's all-files scope); json-schema-checks (simplest top-level-hash case) -> json_schema_passes (sha256.json/blake3.json against hash-v1.yaml); .version single-source-of-truth presence + semver shape -> file_exists root_only + file_content_matches '^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\s*$'; root meson.build / flake.nix / flake.lock / CONTRIBUTING.md presence -> file_exists root_only; rl-next fragment `synopsis:` frontmatter -> file_content_matches '(?m)^synopsis:' (value-add structural slice of the changelog-d doc-build).

**What alint improves.** no_merge_conflict_markers is materially better than the pre-commit hook for the same invariant: the hook only fires on STAGED files at commit time (bypassable with --no-verify, and silent on already-committed markers), whereas alint scans the committed tree on every CI run and on a bare clone with no pre-commit/nix toolchain installed — it catches markers that slipped in via a force-push or a contributor without the hook. The json_schema_passes rule expresses data->schema conformance declaratively without invoking the `jv` binary or the meson harness, so it runs in fork CI that lacks the Nix sandbox. The rl-next synopsis check turns a failure that today only surfaces deep inside a full doc build into a one-line targeted assertion.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** The json-schema-checks coverage is INCOMPLETE rather than a hard gap, and the config is honest that it only probes the simplest case. The real limitation: nix's schemas are YAML draft-04 with `$ref` to `#/$defs/...` JSON-Pointer SUB-schema fragments (validate a value against a fragment of a schema, not the whole document). json_schema_passes points a data file at a whole `schema_path:`; it does not expose a JSON-Pointer entry-fragment selector, and draft-04 + YAML-encoded schema support is uncertain. I considered splitting each $defs fragment into its own file to feed json_schema_passes — but that requires generating N derived schema files the repo doesn't ship, so it's not an in-place expression of the existing check. This is a fair partial gap. The DELIBERATELY-broken-symlink walk-abort is a separate, real alint robustness sharp-edge (not a validation gap): one dangling symlink halts the entire run; the config works around it with ignore:, but the fix belongs in alint's walker (skip-and-continue on a dangling link). Everything else (the formatter stack, clang-tidy, VM/functional tests, flake checks) is correct category-D execution.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `subschema_pointer:` (JSON-Pointer / `$defs` fragment selector) option on json_schema_passes so 'this data validates against fragment #/$defs/Foo of schema S' is expressible — directly serves nix's draft-04 + pointer-fragment reality and any repo whose schemas are a library of named sub-definitions. (2) Walker resilience as a config/CLI knob (`on_walk_error: skip|abort`, or auto-skip dangling symlinks) — nix's intentional broken-symlink fixture proves real repos legitimately ship dangling links; today the only escape is enumerating ignore: globs, which is brittle. (3) A small `synopsis`/frontmatter-presence convenience could generalize the rl-next pattern into an 'every changelog fragment has required frontmatter keys' bundled idiom.

*Notes.* The broken-symlink walk-abort is the most reusable finding — it is an alint-engine robustness bug (one dangling link halts the run), distinct from any expressiveness gap, and the corpus config documents the reproduction precisely. Should be filed as an engine hardening task, not a new rule kind.

---

### `NixOS-nixpkgs` — Massive Nix package monorepo (~by-name + lib + maintainers), NixOS-foundation governance, nixpkgs-vet/treefmt enforcement

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~55% of the ADDRESSABLE structural/hygiene slice (package.nix structural invariant, editorconfig policy, all presence checks) — but the two distinctive nixpkgs-vet naming invariants (shard-dir name shape + shard-prefix relation) are genuinely unreachable, and the reference firewall is category-D. Of nixpkgs's TOTAL enforcement alint replaces only a thin structural rim; the mass is nix-eval/treefmt/vet execution (correct non-goal).

**Enforcement surface.** nixpkgs's enforced validation is almost entirely DELIBERATE-NON-GOAL (category D): nix eval / nix parse, nixpkgs-vet (structural + shard-name-derivation + reference-firewall), a treefmt stack (nixfmt + biome + yamlfmt + actionlint + keep-sorted + nixf-diagnose + editorconfig-checker + zizmor), codeowners-validator, lib-tests, and commit-message lint. The alint-addressable slices that overlap (but do not replace) these: (structure/layout) every pkgs/by-name/*/* package directory must contain a package.nix (the alint-expressible STRUCTURAL slice of nixpkgs-vet); (naming — the claimed gap) the by-name SHARD directories are 2-char lowercase prefixes (or _<digit>) that must equal the leading 2 chars of the package dir they contain; (config-policy) .editorconfig DECLARES insert_final_newline=true + trim_trailing_whitespace=true (the policy assertion; editorconfig-checker does the enforcement); (presence) flake.nix, default.nix, lib/.version, maintainers/maintainer-list.nix, ci/OWNERS, ci/default.nix, ci/pinned.json. nixpkgs-vet's two hardest checks — deriving the correct shard name from the package name, and a REFERENCE FIREWALL (by-name packages may not reference paths outside their own dir / must use pkgs.* not relative ../) — are the parts alint cannot express.

**What alint replaces.** nixpkgs-vet 'every by-name package dir has a package.nix' (structural slice) -> for_each_dir select 'pkgs/by-name/*/*' + nested file_exists '{path}/package.nix'; .editorconfig policy declaration -> file_content_matches 'insert_final_newline = true' / 'trim_trailing_whitespace = true'; flake.nix/default.nix/lib/.version/maintainer-list.nix/ci OWNERS/ci default.nix/ci pinned.json presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** The for_each_dir structural slice is a faithful, toolchain-free re-expression of nixpkgs-vet's most basic invariant — it runs on a bare clone without a Nix evaluator, so a fork or a pre-vet sanity pass catches a by-name dir that is missing package.nix in milliseconds. The .editorconfig policy-declaration checks add a layer nixpkgs does NOT have: editorconfig-checker enforces the rules but nothing asserts the .editorconfig still DECLARES them, so a well-meaning edit that drops trim_trailing_whitespace=true silently disables a whole class of enforcement — the file_content_matches guard catches that meta-regression. For such a large repo the presence checks are also a cheap structural smoke-test independent of the (slow, sandboxed) nix eval.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** TWO real naming gaps, both verified against source. (1) DIRECTORY-NAME pattern matching: asserting the by-name shard dirs are 2-char-lowercase (or _<digit>) is NOT expressible. I tried the obvious idiom — for_each_dir select 'pkgs/by-name/*' + nested filename_regex paths '{path}' — and confirmed in crates/alint-rules/src/filename_regex.rs that filename_regex iterates ctx.index.files() ONLY and has no as_per_file impl, so the for_each_dir literal-bypass (which only re-dispatches per-FILE rules) never matches a directory entry; same for filename_case and no_case_conflicts (both files()-only). There is no dirname_regex / directory-name predicate kind. So a pure directory-name-shape assertion has no expression. (2) The harder RELATIONAL invariant — the shard dir name must equal the 2-char prefix of its child package dir name (e.g. pkgs/by-name/fo/foobar) — is also a gap: it needs a derived-target/name-template comparison between parent_name and a substring of the child basename. for_each_dir exposes {parent_name} and {basename} but no nested rule compares a SUBSTRING of one template token against another, and file_graph derive_target builds file-reference edges, not a within-path name equality. Separately, nixpkgs-vet's REFERENCE FIREWALL (by-name packages must not reach outside their dir / must use pkgs.* not ../) is partially shaped like import_gate, but import_gate's presets are language-import syntaxes (go/python/rust/js/scala/java/dart/nix) keyed on import statements, not 'no relative path escapes this directory subtree' — and the firewall is fundamentally a nix-eval-semantic check (does this derivation reference an out-of-tree path), so it stays category-D.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `dirname_regex` kind (or extending filename_regex/no_case_conflicts to iterate directory entries, gated by a `targets: dirs|files|both` axis) — directly closes gap (1) and is broadly useful (Java package-dir casing, monorepo team-dir naming, kebab-case enforcement on dirs). LOW-RISK, high-leverage: the for_each_dir literal-bypass machinery already templates {path} to a directory, it just needs a name-matching rule that consults dir entries. (2) A relational name-template predicate — a nested rule shape like `name_matches: { token: '{parent_name}', equals_prefix_of: '{basename}', len: 2 }` or, more generally, letting filename_regex's pattern interpolate a sibling template token captured from the iteration ('{parent_name}') so 'child basename starts with parent name' becomes expressible. That is the abstraction nixpkgs-vet's shard-derivation most wants and would also serve 'file stem matches enclosing dir' patterns elsewhere. (3) An import_gate option for path-subtree containment ('no edge target escapes {path}') would approximate the firewall's structural half, though the semantic half stays D.

*Notes.* The dirname-pattern gap is the single most actionable new-kind signal across these four repos: the config itself flags 'a dirname_regex / directory-name predicate' as the missing primitive, and I confirmed by reading source that NO existing kind reaches directory names. It recurs well beyond nixpkgs.

---

### `nodejs-node` — Node.js runtime — C++ + JS application (NO root package.json), make + GN/gyp build, OpenJS Foundation governance, ~35 GH Actions workflows

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE enforced surface — CODEOWNERS `files`, lib/ ascii, both editorconfig hygiene rules, and vcbuild.bat CRLF are all native; only codeowners `duppatterns` is unreachable, and the release-proposal gate is partially expressible. Of node's TOTAL enforcement the addressable slice is small; the dominant mass is the make lint-* tool/AST/NLP matrix (correct category-D non-goal, orchestrated via command).

**Enforcement surface.** node is the runtime itself (not an npm library — there is deliberately no root package.json/lockfile/engines, so the node@v1 ecosystem bundle is correctly NOT extended; oss-baseline + ci/github-actions fit). All repo-STATE validation is funneled through Makefile `lint-*` targets that shell out to language-aware tools (category D): eslint + ~32 custom tools/eslint-rules/* (JS AST), cpplint.py (C++ style), tools/checkimports.py ('using is used' — C++ semantic), ruff (py), yamllint, shellcheck, remark/lint-md (markdown NLP), clang-format, and core-validate-commit (commit-message NLP). The ADDRESSABLE enforced slice (repo-state checks whose LOGIC a native kind expresses): (cross-file/structure) the `lint-codeowners` CI job runs codeowners-validator with checks `files` (every .github/CODEOWNERS left-column pattern resolves to a real path) AND `duppatterns` (no duplicate pattern entries); (security/byte-content) the custom eslint rule tools/eslint-rules/non-ascii-character.js forbids any non-ASCII byte under lib/ ('bloats the compiled node binary'); (hygiene) .editorconfig trim_trailing_whitespace=true + insert_final_newline=true on maintained trees (deps/** explicitly unset), and eslint no-trailing-spaces on JS; (line-endings) vcbuild.bat is pinned to CRLF by BOTH .gitattributes ('vcbuild.bat text eol=crlf') and .editorconfig ('[vcbuild.bat] end_of_line = crlf'). Governance files (GOVERNANCE.md/CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md/SECURITY.md/CONTRIBUTING.md) exist but are not CI-gated (value-add). There is also a release-proposal commit-diff gate.

**What alint replaces.** codeowners-validator `checks: files` -> registry_paths_resolve (source .github/CODEOWNERS, regex extract of the left column, base lint_root, entries_are_globs); non-ascii-character.js byte-forbiddance under lib/ -> file_is_ascii (paths lib/**/*.js); .editorconfig trim_trailing_whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace (on {lib,src,doc,tools,benchmark}, deps/fixtures excluded mirroring .editorconfig); insert_final_newline -> final_newline (same trees); vcbuild.bat CRLF pin (.gitattributes + .editorconfig) -> line_endings target crlf; the make lint-ci umbrella -> command (orchestration wrapper, value-add); governance file presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** Three clear wins. (1) file_is_ascii vs the custom eslint rule: the rationale ('non-ASCII bloats the binary') is a pure byte-content invariant; file_is_ascii expresses it in one declarative line that runs WITHOUT node/eslint and without loading the ~32-rule custom plugin — ideal for a fast pre-eslint gate, and it can be tightened with allow: codepoints if ever needed. (2) registry_paths_resolve vs codeowners-validator's `files` check: same logic, but it runs on a bare clone with no dockerized validator and (bonus the validator lacks) can ALSO orphan-reverse-check that real paths are covered. (3) The .editorconfig hygiene rules are enforced by alint directly on the committed tree, independent of whether a contributor's editor honored .editorconfig — and crucially line_endings makes the vcbuild.bat CRLF requirement a first-class assertion rather than relying on .gitattributes being respected on checkout (a Windows batch file silently converted to LF breaks cmd.exe, and gitattributes normalization is easy to defeat). Consolidating these into one reviewable config also documents WHICH editorconfig/gitattributes conventions are load-bearing, which today is tribal knowledge spread across the Makefile and tool configs.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE real (narrow) gap: codeowners-validator's `duppatterns` check (no DUPLICATE pattern entries in CODEOWNERS) is not natively expressible. registry_paths_resolve handles the `files` half but does not de-duplicate the extracted entry list; unique_by keys on PATH templates over file/dir entries, not over lines/values extracted from within a single file, so it can't flag two identical CODEOWNERS lines. I confirmed unique_by iterates ctx.index.files() (path-keyed), not extracted in-file tokens — so 'these extracted values must be distinct' has no expression. Note the release-proposal commit-diff gate is NOT a pure gap: it asserts a version-bump commit touches specific files (src/node_version.h, doc/changelogs/), which pair_changed_together (if src/node_version.h changed then doc/changelogs/** must change) and changeset_requires_path partially express — so it's IMPROVABLE/partially-A, not D, though its full semantic (the diff is a well-formed version bump) stays tool-shaped. Everything else node enforces — eslint+custom rules, cpplint, checkimports semantic analysis, ruff, yamllint, lint-md, clang-format, core-validate-commit — is correct category-D execution that the command wrapper orchestrates, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A 'distinct extracted values' / set-uniqueness option on registry_paths_resolve (or a small cross_file relation `distinct`) so 'no two CODEOWNERS lines are identical' (the duppatterns check) and more generally 'the values this manifest enumerates are unique' becomes expressible — closes the one gap and is reusable for any allowlist/registry that must not have dup entries. (2) Generalize unique_by to key on EXTRACTED in-file values (lines/regex/json-path), not only path templates — same outcome, broader reach (duplicate enum variants, duplicate dependency entries, duplicate registry rows). (3) A bundled `runtime-app` (non-library) baseline — node proves a class of repos that are applications with no package.json where node@v1 mis-fires; a small bundled ruleset of the genuinely-app-level conventions (CODEOWNERS resolves, editorconfig hygiene, governance files) would save each such repo re-deriving it.

*Notes.* Config is exemplary on the absent-category reasoning (R6): explicitly NOT extending node@v1 because there is no root package.json is exactly right and worth preserving as a calibration example. The duppatterns gap pairs naturally with a unique_by-over-extracted-values abstraction that would also help several other corpus repos.

---

### `numpy-numpy` — Python + C/Cython scientific-computing library; meson build; PEP 639 SPDX; BSD-3

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the addressable (non-execution) surface — the 3 coherence/manifest items are all natively expressed; the remaining enforced surface is ~all execution (ruff/cython-lint/mypy/stubtest/vulture/C-API-linter/sanitizers/pytest/meson), correct NON-GOALs.

**Enforcement surface.** numpy's enforced repo-state surface is overwhelmingly TOOL EXECUTION orchestrated from .github/workflows/** and tools/: (1) lint/format — ruff (line-length 88, declared in ruff.toml and mirrored in .editorconfig with a literal 'Keep in sync with tools/lint_diff.ini and tools/linter.py' comment), cython-lint, tools/linter.py (diff-scoped ruff on the PR), spin lint; (2) typing — mypy + the stub suite (stubtest), plus pyrefly/basedpyright experimental gates over numpy/ stubs; (3) dead-code — vulture; (4) the bespoke C-API borrowed-reference linter (tools/ check for refcount/borrowed-ref misuse in C sources) and check-c-api / cython coverage scripts; (5) sanitizers (ASAN/UBSAN) + the full pytest/hypothesis suite + meson build/install/wheel checks across the CI matrix; (6) tools/check_openblas_version.py which verifies the scipy-openblas pin matches the actually-built/loaded OpenBLAS at release time (runtime introspection). Repo-state coherences that are NOT tool-execution: ruff line-length==88 anchored vs the .editorconfig sync comment; the scipy-openblas32 pin kept identical between requirements/ci_requirements.txt and requirements/ci32_requirements.txt (both carry 'keep this in sync'); pyproject [project] license-files (PEP 639) enumerating real on-disk license paths; the towncrier news-fragment workflow (doc/release/upcoming_changes/ with its format README); SPDX BSD-3-Clause declaration. No CODEOWNERS-gated structure check; no committed git hooks beyond pre-commit which itself just runs the tools.

**What alint replaces.** ruff.toml line-length==88 -> toml_path_equals (config A1); scipy-openblas32 cross-requirements pin coherence -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals, regex extract on both files); pyproject [project] license-files manifest resolution -> registry_paths_resolve expect:file (A3); SPDX BSD-3-Clause present -> toml_path_matches; LICENSE.txt / CONTRIBUTING.rst / news-fragment README presence -> file_exists

**What alint improves.** The three sync-comment coherences (ruff line-length vs the editorconfig 'keep in sync' note, and the dual ci/ci32 OpenBLAS pins) are TODAY enforced only by a literal human-readable comment plus check_openblas_version.py's release-time runtime check — alint converts the 'keep this in sync' prose into an executable, schema-validated cross_file assertion that fires in plain CI without a build or a loaded OpenBLAS. license-files resolution is likewise checked by nothing structural today (only pip/build would fail late at sdist time); registry_paths_resolve catches a dangling PEP 639 path declaratively. These run with zero toolchain, much earlier than the meson/wheel jobs.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The one tempting candidate — tools/check_openblas_version.py — verifies the pin against the *runtime-loaded* OpenBLAS build string (numpy.show_runtime), which is genuine execution/introspection (category D), not a tree-readable fact, so it is correctly a NON-GOAL rather than a gap. Everything addressable (the manifest/value coherences + license resolution) is already expressed.

**Abstraction ideas.** A first-class 'keep-in-sync anchor' affordance: numpy literally writes 'Keep in sync with X and Y' comments in .editorconfig/requirements as the de-facto spec. A sugar kind (e.g. values_coherent: with a list of {file, extract}) that asserts N>=2 extracted values are all-equal in ONE rule (today cross_file is 1-source-to-N-targets, which works but reads asymmetrically for a symmetric 'these three must match' invariant) would map directly onto this recurring pattern. Lower priority: a bundled 'pep639' micro-ruleset (license-files resolve + SPDX present) since this recurs across modern Python packages.

---

### `openssl-openssl` — Crypto C library; make + Perl Configure codegen; OpenSSL Foundation governance; Apache-2.0 licensed (not an ASF project)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~60% of the ADDRESSABLE surface today (header A1 + manifest/version presence), rising toward ~85% once generated_file_fresh mutating-mode is credited for check_update (build-toolchain-dependent). The repo's overall enforcement is dominated by execution (clang-format/codespell/doc-nits/Coverity/abidiff/C-semantics linters) which is correctly NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** License-headers: CONTRIBUTING.md §2 mandates every source file open with 'Copyright 20xx-20yy The OpenSSL Project Authors. All Rights Reserved.' + 'Licensed under the Apache License 2.0' (940/945 C files carry it) — a documented review convention. CODEGEN-FRESHNESS (the heaviest gate): the check_update CI job runs `make build_generated && make update && git diff --exit-code`, regenerating the exported-symbol ordinal manifests util/libcrypto.num, util/libssl.num, util/providers.num via util/mkdef.pl, plus opensslv.h from VERSION.dat, and asserts the tree is byte-unchanged. FIPS: fips-checksums.yml runs `make fips-checksums` + diff-fips-checksums (a digest over the FIPS module source set) and abidiff. Docs/prose: check_docs (make doc-nits/md-nits — POD + markdown NLP linters). Hygiene: style-checks.yml + .pre-commit-config.yaml = clang-format(WebKit) + codespell. Supply/ABI: windows.yml checkplatformsyms.pl (built-DLL export allowlist vs the .num files), Coverity static analysis. Home-grown util/ validators: check-malloc-errs, find-unused-errs, ck_errf.pl — all grep/run C source semantics. check-news-changes suggests a NEWS/CHANGES entry when a PR diff touches include/openssl, a CVE id, or Configure options (operates on the git diff). No CODEOWNERS, no .editorconfig, no dependabot, no committed git hooks, no NOTICE file (Apache-licensed but not an ASF redistribution).

**What alint replaces.** Per-file OpenSSL copyright/Apache-2.0 header -> file_header (lines:10, alternation pattern, glob include/exclude of generated headers) [A1]; ordinal-manifest presence (libcrypto/libssl/providers.num) -> file_exists; VERSION.dat declares MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH/SHLIB_VERSION -> file_content_matches with (?s) dot-all; .pre-commit-config/.clang-format/ci.yml/CHANGES.md/NEWS.md presence -> file_exists; the (correctly-disabled) bundled apache-2 NOTICE + source-header rules are overridden via level:off

**What alint improves.** file_header is materially better than OpenSSL's status quo for the header check: today it is enforced ONLY by human review per CONTRIBUTING.md §2 with no CI gate at all, so the 5/945 missing-header files slipped through; alint makes it a real CI gate with precise generated-file excludes and a tolerant Copyright/Rights/Reserved casing pattern. Also note the bundled compliance/apache-2 source-header rule would FALSE-POSITIVE on OpenSSL's bare 'Apache License 2.0' short form, and the config correctly turns it off and supplies the project-specific form — a coherence the bundle alone can't express, demonstrating per-repo override.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that survive reproduce-first, but with two important re-classifications of the config's own 'D' labels: (1) check_update ('make update && git diff --exit-code', regenerating the .num ordinal manifests + opensslv.h) was marked a D non-goal, but this is the canonical generated_file_fresh MUTATING-mode (outputs:) use case post-v0.12-build — snapshot/run-generator/diff/restore IS exactly `make gen && git diff --exit-code`; it would be trust-gated (must live in the user's top-level config, which it does), so it is REPLACEABLE-with-build-dependency, not a gap. It does require the Perl/make toolchain present at lint time (alint orchestrates the spawn but doesn't reimplement mkdef.pl) — that build dependency is the honest caveat, not an expressiveness gap. (2) fips-checksums similarly is pair_hash/file_hash territory once the digest file is built. The genuinely-out-of-scope remainder (clang-format, codespell NLP, doc-nits POD linting, Coverity, abidiff/checkplatformsyms compiled-binary inspection, the malloc/errf C-semantics greppers) is correct NON-GOAL.

**Abstraction ideas.** check-news-changes is a strong motivator for tightening the diff-aware family: it fires a NEWS/CHANGES suggestion when the PR diff touches include/openssl OR mentions a CVE OR changes Configure options. changeset_requires_path + pair_changed_together cover 'PR touched X, must also touch Y', but OpenSSL's trigger is a disjunction over heterogeneous signals (a path-glob OR a content-regex-in-the-diff like a CVE id). A 'when_changed' that accepts a content predicate on the diff hunk (not just a path glob) — i.e. pair_changed_together with an optional if_diff_matches: regex — would natively express 'if the diff adds a CVE reference, require a CHANGES.md entry'. Also: the .num export-allowlist (checkplatformsyms vs the manifest) hints at a 'symbol set in built artifact subset-of manifest' relation, but that needs a compiled DLL, so it stays D.

---

### `pallets-flask` — Single-package Python web-framework library; src/flask layout; flit_core build; uv.lock; tox env matrix; pre-commit

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the addressable surface — every tree-readable invariant (hygiene quartet, both coherences, sansio firewall, frozen-SHA posture, metadata) is natively expressed; the remainder is execution (NON-GOAL).

**Enforcement surface.** All bespoke validation is TOOL EXECUTION run via pre-commit + tox + GHA: .pre-commit-config.yaml = ruff-check + ruff-format + uv-lock (--locked freshness) + codespell + check-merge-conflict + debug-statements + fix-byte-order-marker + trailing-whitespace + end-of-file-fixer; .github/workflows = tests.yaml (tox matrix + typing via mypy & pyright), pre-commit.yaml, zizmor.yaml (GHA security scan), publish.yaml (build + PyPI Trusted Publishing), lock.yaml (issue lock); pyproject [tool.tox] style/typing/docs/tests envs, with a tests-min env that `uv pip install`s EXACT pins (blinker==1.9.0 etc.) to prove the declared [project.dependencies] >= lower bounds actually work; .readthedocs.yaml sphinx -W. Repo-STATE facts (tree-readable): the cosmetic hygiene the hooks enforce (trailing-ws / final-newline / no-BOM / no-merge-markers); tests-min exact pins == [project.dependencies] floors (MSRV-coherence analogue); requires-python '>=3.10' floor agreeing with [tool.mypy] python_version and [tool.pyright] pythonVersion '3.10'; the src/flask/sansio architectural firewall (sansio/README.md: 'cannot do any IO ... cannot use the Flask globals'); the supply-chain posture that every .pre-commit-config rev: is a 40-hex frozen SHA (via `pre-commit autoupdate --freeze`); py.typed marker (PEP 561); committed uv.lock; CHANGES.rst.

**What alint replaces.** trailing-whitespace / end-of-file-fixer / fix-byte-order-marker hooks -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline + no_bom (with fixers); check-merge-conflict -> no_merge_conflict_markers; tests-min exact-pin == floor coherence -> 6x cross_file_value_equals (same-file source+target, per-dep regex extract); requires-python floor == mypy/pyright targets -> cross_file_value_equals (1 source, 2 targets, bare-version regex); sansio globals firewall -> import_gate language:python forbid:'globals$'; every pre-commit rev: is a 40-hex SHA -> yaml_path_matches on $.repos[*].rev; license SPDX / py.typed / uv.lock / CHANGES.rst -> toml_path_equals + file_exists

**What alint improves.** Two coherences alint expresses that Flask's CI cannot cheaply: (1) the tests-min-pin==floor invariant is enforced TODAY only IMPLICITLY (CI installs the pins and runs tests; a drift where the floor is bumped but the pin isn't would silently keep testing the OLD pin and pass green) — alint makes the equality an explicit declarative assertion that fails loudly the moment they diverge, no test run needed. (2) the requires-python/mypy/pyright interpreter-floor agreement is enforced by NOTHING today (raising the floor without bumping the checkers leaves mypy/pyright validating a dropped interpreter, silently) — alint catches it. import_gate also gives the sansio firewall a real CI gate where today it is README-prose + reviewer-vigilance only. And the frozen-SHA pre-commit posture is asserted structurally (the ci/github-actions bundle pins only GH `uses:`; this extends the same supply-chain check to the hook repos).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The honestly-recorded sharp edge — no_merge_conflict_markers false-positives on .rst/.md because reST setext title underlines ('=======' / '-------') are indistinguishable from VCS conflict markers, so the config scopes the rule to .py/.toml/.yaml only — is a real C-tuning limitation but NOT a genuine gap: pre-commit's own check-merge-conflict has the identical blind spot (it keys on '<<<<<<< '/'======='/'>>>>>>> ' and would also trip on a 7-equals setext underline), so alint is at parity, not behind. I tried tightening the pattern to require the 7-char marker be followed by a conflict ref/branch line; the '=======' middle marker genuinely has no such disambiguator, so excluding prose files is the correct resolution, matching upstream. Everything else (ruff/codespell/mypy/pyright/pytest/sphinx/zizmor/uv-lock --locked) is execution = NON-GOAL.

**Abstraction ideas.** The six near-identical flask-min-pin-<dep> rules (one per dependency because cross_file requires the source to resolve to exactly one value) is the clearest abstraction motivator in this group: a 'paired-extract' relation that zips an ordered list of source captures against an ordered list of target captures by a join key (the package name) and asserts element-wise equality would collapse all six into ONE rule — e.g. cross_file with extract:{regex_all: 'name==(ver)'} on the tests-min block vs extract:{regex_all: 'name>=(ver)'} on [project.dependencies], joined on name. This 'MSRV/floor-coherence across a dependency table' pattern recurs in many Python/Rust repos. Secondary: a bundled 'pre-commit-hygiene' ruleset (the trailing-ws/eof/bom/merge-marker quartet + the frozen-rev SHA check) mirroring what fix-byte-order-marker-style hook bundles ship.

### `pandas-dev-pandas` — Python + C/Cython data/analytics library; meson build; NumFOCUS / BSD-3

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the addressable surface — all pygrep forbidden-text hooks + the freshness pair + structure facts are native; the one residual is the look-around-precision shortfall on the type-comment hook (a sharp-edge, ~partial coverage of that single rule). The AST/tokenize scripts are correct NON-GOAL execution.

**Enforcement surface.** The PRIMARY enforcement gate is .pre-commit-config.yaml (run in CI as the ci-typing manual-stage + the pre-commit.ci app). It splits cleanly: (a) `language: pygrep` LOCAL hooks = pure forbidden-text regexes on file content — unwanted-patterns (\.__class__ -> use type(); `from numpy import random`; outdated `# type:` comments except `# type: ignore`), unwanted-patterns-in-tests (force `import pandas._testing as tm`; ban pd.testing./pd.api.types./np.array_equal/unseeded default_rng()), no-return-exception (`return SomeError` -> must raise), incorrect-backticks (rst backticks need surrounding spaces), pg8000-not-installed-CI (ban pg8000 in ci/deps/*.yaml); (b) `language: python` LOCAL hooks in scripts/*.py that are AST/tokenize-based — validate_docstrings, validate_unwanted_patterns (the AST arm), check for inconsistent namespace usage, use-pd_array-in-core, no-bare-pytest-raises, etc. — genuine Python AST analysis; (c) the codegen pair pip-to-conda: scripts/generate_pip_deps_from_conda.py --compare keeps requirements-dev.txt in sync with environment.yml (the generated file carries a fixed provenance header). .github/workflows/** = tests / wheels (cibuildwheel) / docs (sphinx) / codeql — all tool runs. Structure facts: .gitattributes '* text=auto' normalization; .github/CODEOWNERS; pyproject [project].dynamic version (versioneer).

**What alint replaces.** All pygrep forbidden-text hooks -> file_content_forbidden (unwanted-patterns .__class__/numpy-random/type-comment; no-return-exception; unwanted-patterns-in-tests; incorrect-backticks; pg8000-not-installed-CI); the requirements-dev.txt provenance header -> file_content_matches with \A anchor; pip-to-conda freshness (the --compare check-mode tool) -> command_idempotent (correct dispatch — the generator signals via exit code, does NOT print fresh content to stdout, so generated_file_fresh stdout-mode would be wrong); .gitattributes '* text=auto' -> file_content_matches; CODEOWNERS -> file_exists; versioneer dynamic version -> toml_path_equals on $.project.dynamic[0]

**What alint improves.** file_content_forbidden consolidates seven separately-declared pygrep hook entries into one reviewable, schema-validated alint config that runs identically without the pre-commit toolchain bootstrapped — useful as a fast pre-pre-commit structural floor in environments where the full pre-commit/conda env isn't provisioned. command_idempotent is also a cleaner expression of the freshness intent than the raw hook (it pins the exact --compare invocation with a timeout and a precise stale-file message). pandas itself already enforces all of these, so the win here is consolidation/portability + schema validation rather than catching something pandas misses.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** One real but minor SHARP-EDGE, not a true gap: the outdated-`# type:` hook must exclude `# type: ignore`, which upstream does with the look-around `(?! ignore)`; the Rust `regex` crate (1.12.3, confirmed in-tree) has no look-around, so the config approximates it as `(?m)# type: (?:[^i]|i[^g])`. I reproduced this: the approximation is correct for the common cases but is subtly LOOSER/DIFFERENT at the edges — it flags `# type:` at EOL (no following char) differently and would mis-handle `# type: int  # ignore`-style trailers; it cannot exactly replicate the negative-lookahead semantics. This is a known regex-engine limitation (the broader 'no look-around' constraint), honestly a C sharp-edge, and the only item in this group where alint is materially less precise than the upstream check. NOT a gap: the AST/tokenize hooks (validate_docstrings, the namespace-consistency checker, use-pd_array-in-core, no-bare-pytest-raises, the AST arm of validate_unwanted_patterns) are genuine Python AST/semantic analysis = correct NON-GOAL (category D) — alint should orchestrate scripts/*.py via command, not reimplement them.

**Abstraction ideas.** The strongest abstraction signal here is look-around support, or a targeted substitute for the single most-common need it blocks: 'forbid pattern X EXCEPT when the match also matches allowed sub-pattern Y'. A file_content_forbidden option like `except: ' ignore'` (an allow-list of literal/regex exemptions tested against the matched span) would let pandas express `# type:` minus `# type: ignore` EXACTLY, dissolving this entire sharp-edge class without bringing a full PCRE engine (which the regex crate deliberately avoids for ReDoS safety). This 'forbid-with-exception' affordance would also help OpenSSL/many repos. Secondary: a 'pygrep-import' convenience — pandas-style repos keep their forbidden-text rules in pre-commit; a documented mapping (or even an importer) from pygrep local-hook YAML to file_content_forbidden rules would smooth adoption.

### `phoenixframework/phoenix` — Elixir web-framework monorepo (mix) with a co-located Node.js JS client (assets/js, published to npm), a nested installer/ mix project (phx_new), and an integration_test/ mix project; community governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (non-execution) surface. The addressable surface is small but alint covers essentially all of it (3 version-coherence equalities + lockfile/formatter/license existence + whitespace hygiene + CI SHA-pinning); remainder is D execution (the bulk of Phoenix's actual CI minutes, correctly out of scope).

**Enforcement surface.** License-headers: none enforced as a gate (no per-file copyright header convention; LICENSE.md is a single MIT file). Hygiene: `mix format` (.formatter.exs is the canonical config and is itself a published package file), trailing-whitespace/final-newline are only handled implicitly by the formatter for .ex/.exs and by eslint+prettier for assets/js; .editorconfig present. Structure/layout: the repo relies on a tri-project layout (root mix.exs, installer/mix.exs, integration_test/mix.exs) plus assets/ (npm pkg); mix.lock IS committed (application-style repo) — but nothing GATES its presence. Cross-file coherence (the headline): the release version `1.8.x` is hand-duplicated across mix.exs `@version`, installer/mix.exs `@version`, package.json `version` (and assets/package.json + CHANGELOG); RELEASE.md literally lists a 'Files with version' bump checklist and installer/mix.exs documents the @elixir_requirement hand-sync set — NO tool gates these equalities (pure human review convention). Codegen-freshness: assets are rebuilt and auto-committed via a CI git-auto-commit step (priv/static/*) — that's a CD mutation, not a check. Import-firewalls: none formal (Elixir has no enforced layering tool here). Naming: none enforced. Security: GitHub Actions workflows already pin actions (the config extends ci/github-actions to assert SHA-pinning). Commit-discipline: none enforced (no commitlint/husky). Execution-only (D, correctly out of scope): `mix test` at three roots, `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`, `mix dialyzer` where present, ExUnit, Jest/`npm test` for assets/js, ESLint AST, `mix format --check-formatted`, the installer/integration test.sh DB-backed integration suite, and the npm-trusted-publish / `mix hex.publish` release jobs.

**What alint replaces.** RELEASE.md/installer 'bump these together' version checklist -> cross_file_value_equals x3 (phx-version-coherent-installer, phx-version-coherent-package-json, phx-elixir-requirement-coherent-installer) — exactly the tokio-A1 analog, replacing a human review convention with an enforced gate; mix.lock-committed convention -> file_exists (root_only); .formatter.exs presence -> file_exists; MIT-declared-in-manifest -> file_content_matches; GitHub-Actions SHA-pinning -> ci/github-actions bundle; per-file Elixir trailing-whitespace/final-newline (the slice the formatter handles, made standalone + auto-fixable) -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline.

**What alint improves.** The three cross_file_value_equals rules are a categorical improvement over Phoenix's status quo: today the version coherence is enforced ONLY by RELEASE.md prose and an installer code comment ('when you bump this, also update these files') — a reviewer's eyeball. alint turns that into one declarative, schema-validated, CI-runnable file that fails the build the instant mix.exs/installer/mix.exs/package.json drift, with a templated message that names the exact files. It also expresses cross-PROJECT coherence (root vs nested mix project vs npm manifest) that no single tool in the Elixir or Node toolchain spans — `mix` doesn't see package.json, npm doesn't see mix.exs. Lower false-positive than a grep-based pre-commit because it extracts the specific values (@version regex / $.version JSONPath) rather than scanning text.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's only non-replaceables are correctly D (execution: ExUnit/Jest/ESLint/dialyzer/the DB integration suite/CD publish) — these are deliberate NON-GOALS, not gaps. I checked whether the 'bump set also includes assets/package.json + CHANGELOG' could be enforced: assets/package.json version coherence is just a 4th cross_file_value_equals (source mix.exs @version, target assets/package.json $.version) and 'CHANGELOG has a heading for the current @version' is expressible via cross_file extract regex from mix.exs into a file_content_matches-style check OR cross_file with the CHANGELOG heading as target — so even those are addressable, the config simply under-uses the idiom. No claimed gap survives reproduction.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `elixir@v1` ecosystem ruleset (gated on facts.has_mix / presence of mix.exs) is the obvious missing peer to the rust/go/java/node/dotnet bundles: it would package mix.lock-tracked, .formatter.exs-exists, mix.exs declares license/version/elixir requirement, and a 'releases in lockstep with a co-located npm client' multi-manifest version-coherence template. Second idea: a reusable 'version fan-out' composition primitive (declare ONE source-of-truth version literal + a list of {file, extractor} targets, expand to N cross_file_value_equals) — Phoenix, postgres, and rust all hand-write the same fan-out; a `version_consistency` sugar kind or ruleset macro would dissolve the repetition. Otherwise the existing surface covers Phoenix fully.

*Notes.* Config is well-calibrated and honest. The headline cross_file_value_equals use is exactly right and is the strongest single demonstration in this group. Minor under-utilization: assets/package.json version + CHANGELOG-heading coherence could be added (both expressible). cross_file_value_equals is the now-aliased name of the unified `cross_file` (relation: equals) — config is forward-compatible.

### `phpstan/phpstan` — PHP distribution/shim repo (intentionally ships a prebuilt phpstan.phar binary + detached GPG sig); the real source lives in phpstan/phpstan-src. Single-maintainer (ondrejmirtes) project

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~85% of addressable (non-execution) surface — but the addressable surface itself is tiny because this is a dist shim. alint covers all of the structural contract (require.php, bin, sig pairing, shim) + hygiene; remainder is D (PHPUnit matrices, typos, the website toolchain, gpg crypto verification). Honest framing: high coverage of a small surface; most of the repo's real CI is execution.

**Enforcement surface.** This is the DIST/shim repo, so its enforced surface is dominated by D non-goals and the genuinely-addressable surface is small. License-headers: none in-repo (the phar is opaque). Hygiene: .editorconfig declares lf + final-newline project-wide; the only text files are composer.json, the `phpstan` shell shim, README, conf/*.neon, e2e/*.neon. Structure/layout: the dist CONTRACT is load-bearing — composer.json `require.php` MUST stay `^7.4|^8.0` (the phar is deliberately downgraded so the published package accepts PHP 7.4+; documented in CLAUDE.md 'PHP Version Support'), composer.json `bin` must expose BOTH the `phpstan` shim and `phpstan.phar` (consumed by `composer global require`), and the committed `phpstan.phar` must ship alongside `phpstan.phar.asc` (release.yml uploads both). Codegen-freshness: the phar itself is regenerated by a release bot from phpstan-src — a CD artifact, not a check. Import-firewalls/naming: none. Security: GitHub Actions workflows (the config extends ci/github-actions for SHA-pinning); actionlint/zizmor/poutine/octoscan run in CI. Commit-discipline: none enforced. Execution-only (D, correctly out of scope): the PHPUnit e2e/integration/extension test matrices, `typos`, and the entire website/ TypeScript+ESLint+Playwright+CDK toolchain.

**What alint replaces.** composer require.php support-range invariant -> json_path_equals ($.require.php == '^7.4|^8.0'); composer bin contract -> json_path_matches ($.bin[*] matches ^phpstan(\.phar)?$); phar-has-detached-sig existence pair -> file_exists (phpstan.phar.asc); shim-exists -> file_exists; .editorconfig declares lf / final-newline -> file_content_matches x2; NEON final-newline + trailing-whitespace hygiene -> final_newline + no_trailing_whitespace (auto-fixable); GitHub-Actions SHA-pinning -> ci/github-actions bundle.

**What alint improves.** The json_path_equals on require.php is materially better than the status quo: today the '^7.4|^8.0 must not drift' rule lives ONLY in a CLAUDE.md prose note ('the PHAR is downgraded specifically so the published package accepts PHP 7.4+') — there is no executable gate, so a careless composer.json edit changing the support range would ship silently and change who can `composer require phpstan`. alint makes it a hard error. Similarly the bin-contract (json_path_matches $.bin[*]) is otherwise unguarded. The config also correctly REFUSES to extend hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts and node/lockfiles bundles — a naive linter would false-positive on the intentionally-tracked 26MB .phar and the (by-design) gitignored composer.lock; this is exactly the cross-file-aware, repo-specific judgment a pile of generic scripts gets wrong.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. I checked the one plausible candidate: 'the committed phpstan.phar must actually verify against phpstan.phar.asc with the project's GPG key.' That is genuine cryptographic verification (gpg --verify) — but it is EXECUTION (category D, the command escape hatch: command/command_idempotent could orchestrate `gpg --verify phpstan.phar.asc phpstan.phar`), not a structural property alint should reimplement, so it is a NON-GOAL not a gap. Existence-of-the-sig-file is already covered by file_exists. A second candidate — 'the phar's embedded version matches the released tag' — requires parsing the phar's internal stub/manifest (opaque binary), again D (a command running `phpstan.phar --version`). No structural gap survives.

**Abstraction ideas.** A small 'release-artifact pairing' composition idiom would fit: 'for every committed <X>.phar (or .tar.gz / .whl / .jar) there must be a sibling <X>.asc/.sig' generalizes phar-has-signature into a reusable detached-signature pairing — expressible TODAY via pair/{stem} or for_each_file + {stem} + nested file_exists, so it is really a documentation/bundled-ruleset opportunity (a `signed-release-artifacts@v1` bundle) rather than a new kind. Nothing else is needed; this repo is near the floor of addressable surface.

*Notes.* Exemplary 'know what NOT to extend' config — the deliberate omission of no-tracked-artifacts and node bundles is the sharp judgment. All four `rules:` A/value-add items reproduce cleanly under the current kinds. composer.json is JSON so json_path_* applies directly (no JSONC needed here, but the engine would tolerate it).

### `pnpm/pnpm` — TypeScript monorepo (pnpm-managed, ~300 workspace packages under a custom @pnpm/meta-updater canonicalizer) plus a vendored Rust sub-project (pacquet/ + pnpr/). Single-vendor/community governance (zkochan)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~70% of addressable (non-execution) surface, with a large execution remainder. alint natively covers the commit grammar, all editorconfig hygiene, root-manifest invariants, and the permalink convention; it could ADDITIONALLY assert much of the meta-updater's per-package output declaratively (currently logged as value-add). The dominant real surface (tsc/eslint/cspell/jest + the full Rust toolchain + the meta-updater's bidirectional rewrite) is correctly D.

**Enforcement surface.** License-headers: none per-file. Hygiene: root .editorconfig (indent_style=space indent_size=2 for ts/js/cjs/json; lf; final-newline) PLUS .gitattributes `* text eol=lf` git-enforces LF — but the actual gate is eslint/prettier + the editor, not a standalone whitespace check. Structure/layout + cross-file coherence (the heavy machinery): the @pnpm/meta-updater (`pn lint:meta`) is a bespoke tool that CANONICALIZES every workspace package.json (license, repository.url, engines, deps dedupe, tsconfig references, etc.) and DIFFS — i.e. pnpm's structural coherence is enforced by a code-generator-and-compare, not declarative rules. The root package.json must stay `private: true` and pin `packageManager: pnpm@x.y.z` (Corepack contract). Lockfile: `pnpm-lock.yaml` is committed and frozen-install-checked in CI. Codegen-freshness: meta-updater output + several generated files. Import-firewalls: ESLint plugin rules (no cross-package deep imports) + on the Rust side dylint(perfectionist). Naming: none formal. Security: zizmor, CodeQL, `npm audit`, GitHub Actions. Commit-discipline (A): husky `commit-msg` -> commitlint with @commitlint/config-conventional (also documented in AGENTS.md) — conventional-commit subjects are a real enforced gate. Permalink convention (C): AGENTS.md asks contributors to cite a commit-SHA permalink, not a branch URL. Execution-only (D, the bulk): TS compile (tsc), ESLint, cspell, the meta-updater canonicalize-and-diff, jest, plus the Rust side clippy/rustfmt/taplo/cargo-doc/cargo-deny/dylint/typos/nextest.

**What alint replaces.** conventional-commit gate (husky+commitlint) -> git_commit_message (expresses the grammar natively, NOT by wrapping commitlint — so it is A, runs without husky/node); editorconfig whitespace/indent/eol hygiene -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline + line_endings(lf) + indent_style(space,2); root-private invariant -> json_path_equals ($.private == true); packageManager pin -> json_path_matches ($.packageManager ~ ^pnpm@\d+\.\d+\.\d+$); permalink-not-branch convention (scoped to CONTRIBUTING.md to avoid over-fire) -> file_content_forbidden.

**What alint improves.** alint expresses the conventional-commit grammar declaratively and toolchain-free: pnpm needs husky+commitlint+node installed for the commit-msg hook; alint's git_commit_message runs in CI on the range with one regex, no node bootstrap, and (with git_commit_subject_matches + since:) can validate an entire PR range oldest-first rather than only the last commit. The biggest structural win is latent: many of the per-package invariants the meta-updater bakes in (every public package.json has license/repository.url/engines, root README mirrors per-crate, etc.) are expressible as a for_each_dir over packages/* with nested json_path_equals using {basename}/{path} templates — i.e. alint could declaratively ASSERT a large chunk of what the 1000-line bespoke meta-updater currently enforces imperatively, in a reviewable file, without the meta-updater's run-and-diff round trip. The config under-claims this (records it as value-add only).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none survives reproduction, but two candidates deserve the skeptical treatment. (1) 'The @pnpm/meta-updater canonicalizes ~300 manifests bidirectionally (it can also WRITE the fix).' alint can ASSERT each invariant (for_each_dir + nested json_path_equals / cross_file value-predicate parity, e.g. each package.json $.repository.url == derived-from-{path}) and many are auto-fixable, but it cannot REPLICATE the full bespoke transform that derives, sorts, and rewrites dependency blocks across the whole graph — that derive-and-rewrite is a code generator. HOWEVER this is correctly D (it's `pn lint:meta` execution / the command escape hatch), not a structural gap alint 'should' do — alint orchestrates or asserts-the-result, it doesn't reimplement a project's bespoke canonicalizer. (2) The broken-symlink test fixture (store/cafs/.../broken-symlink/doesnt-exist) that aborts the directory walk: the config handles it via `ignore:`, so it's a config-ergonomics footnote, not a capability gap (and no_symlinks/the walker tolerate it once excluded). Net: no GENUINE GAP — the meta-updater is execution (D), and its declarative ASSERTIONS are all expressible.

**Abstraction ideas.** The strongest idea this repo motivates: a 'manifest-canonicalization assertion' bundle / composition primitive for large JS monorepos — declare once {license, repository.url template, engines floor, packageManager pin, private-on-root} and fan it out across a workspace glob (for_each_dir packages/* + nested json_path_equals with {path}-derived expected values). This is the declarative shadow of every meta-updater-style tool (pnpm, Babel, Jest all ship one). It's expressible today via for_each_dir + templates, so it's a bundled-ruleset (`js-workspace-manifests@v1`) + docs opportunity, not a new kind. Also: pnpm SHOULD extend the existing monorepo/pnpm-workspace.yml bundle (it doesn't) — a pure config improvement. No new core kind is warranted.

*Notes.* Config is honest and well-scoped; the conventional-commit A-classification (logic native, not a tool-wrap) is the right call. Two concrete improvements the config leaves on the table: (a) extend monorepo/pnpm-workspace.yml; (b) promote the meta-updater's per-package invariants from value-add prose to actual for_each_dir assertions. The permalink C-rule's tuning (scope to CONTRIBUTING.md only) is exactly the kind of false-positive control the brief rewards.

---

### `postgres/postgres` — Large C systems/RDBMS monorepo with DUAL build systems (autoconf/Make + meson), heavy Perl tooling (src/tools/), PGDG/community governance, no GitHub-CI dependence (Cirrus CI)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~80% of addressable (non-execution) surface — and HIGHER than the original config implies, because the post-v0.12 mutating generated_file_fresh closes the one B item the config logged. alint natively covers the dual-build version equality, ALL the codegen-freshness reminders (now executable, not prose), and the structure/COPYRIGHT/CI/perltidy-pin existence assertions. The dominant real surface (make/meson test, sanitizers, TAP/isolation, pgindent) is correctly D and is the bulk of PG's actual CI minutes.

**Enforcement surface.** License-headers: every C source carries the standard PostgreSQL copyright block, kept current by src/tools/copyright.pl (a Perl regenerator), and the top-level COPYRIGHT names the PostgreSQL Global Development Group — but the GATE is the Perl tool + reviewer, not a declared per-file header check in CI. Hygiene: pgindent (a custom Perl/C indenter, pinned to perltidy version 20230309 'for consistency' per the pgindent README + perltidyrc) reformats C; pg_bsd_indent; whitespace is governed by .gitattributes + the GENERATED .editorconfig. Structure/layout + cross-file coherence (the A core): (A1) the PG version literal is hand-synced by src/tools/version_stamp.pl into EXACTLY configure.ac (AC_INIT([PostgreSQL],[NN])) and meson.build (project version:) — one enforced cross-build-system value equality; both build systems must stay co-present. Codegen-freshness (A2/A3): .editorconfig is GENERATED by src/tools/generate_editorconfig.py FROM .gitattributes, and .gitattributes carries a line-1 banner 'after updating this file, run generate_editorconfig.py'; many other files are generated (gen_node_support.pl -> node copy/equal funcs, kwlist, errcodes, fmgrtab, catalog/*). Security: no GitHub Actions (Cirrus); the CI tasks all reference src/tools/ci/pg_ci_base.conf as TEMP_CONFIG. Commit-discipline: PG uses an email/patch (commitfest) workflow, not GitHub PRs — no commit-message gate in-repo. Execution-only (D, the overwhelming bulk): `make check`/`make installcheck`, the meson test suite, TAP tests, isolation tests, the sanitizer (ASan/UBSan/valgrind) build matrices, pgindent/perltidy reformat, headerscheck/cpluspluscheck, and the catalog/bootstrap generators.

**What alint replaces.** version_stamp.pl cross-build-system version equality -> cross_file_value_equals (configure.ac AC_INIT regex == meson.build version: regex) — the tokio-A1 twin, the single most valuable gate here; .gitattributes regen-banner present -> file_content_matches; .editorconfig is-generated-shape (root=true / indent=tab) -> file_content_matches; both-build-systems-present + Cirrus files + pg_ci_base.conf + COPYRIGHT present/names-PGDG + perltidy-version-pinned -> file_exists / file_content_matches (value-add).

**What alint improves.** The cross_file_value_equals version gate is a clear upgrade over version_stamp.pl's status quo: today nothing FAILS if a human edits configure.ac's AC_INIT but forgets meson.build (or vice versa) — version_stamp.pl is a WRITER you must remember to run, not a CHECK; the two build systems can silently disagree until someone notices. alint makes the disagreement a hard CI error with a message that points at version_stamp.pl. No existing PG tool spans autoconf AND meson to compare the literals. Crucially, this is ALSO where the current capability brief beats the original config: the config (authored PRE-build) records the .editorconfig codegen-freshness as a B/gap because 'generate_editorconfig.py writes the file in place, and generated_file_fresh only diffs stdout' — but generated_file_fresh now has a MUTATING (`outputs:`) mode that snapshots .editorconfig, runs `python src/tools/generate_editorconfig.py`, diffs, reports drift, and restores the tree (exactly `regen && git diff --exit-code`). So the freshness check is now NATIVELY EXPRESSIBLE, not a gap — a materially better, declarative replacement for the prose banner reminder. Same applies to gen_node_support.pl / kwlist / errcodes generators (generated_file_fresh outputs: mode) and to a file_graph `fresh` check for any generated file that embeds a source content-hash.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none survives the reproduce-first lens — and one previously-claimed gap is now CLOSED. The config's only B item was the .editorconfig codegen-freshness ('generated_file_fresh diffs stdout but this generator writes in place'); under the post-v0.12 build, generated_file_fresh's MUTATING `outputs:` mode does exactly snapshot/run/diff/restore for an in-place generator, so this is REPLACEABLE now, not a gap. I also pressure-tested: (a) 'every C file carries the PG copyright header for the CURRENT year' — file_header with a (?m)^ regex over **/*.c handles the header-shape; the year-rolls-over-annually aspect is a copyright.pl WRITE concern (D), and even 'header matches a pattern containing the current year' is expressible by injecting the year via a var, so not a gap. (b) 'the generated catalog/node-support files are in sync' — generated_file_fresh outputs: mode (D-tier trust, but native). (c) pgindent/perltidy formatting itself is genuine AST/whitespace reformatting = correctly D (command/command_idempotent orchestrates pgindent --check). Nothing the repo does that is STRUCTURAL remains inexpressible.

**Abstraction ideas.** Postgres motivates a 'dual-build-system version coherence' template (one literal -> {configure.ac AC_INIT, meson.build version:, optional .pgversion}) which generalizes the same fan-out Phoenix and rust need — argues for a reusable `version_consistency` sugar/bundled-macro (declare source-of-truth + N {file,extractor} targets). Second: a bundled 'in-place codegen freshness' convenience — PG has a whole family of generate-X.pl/py scripts whose 'run it then git diff' contract is now generated_file_fresh outputs: mode; a small ruleset or doc pattern that maps each generator+output to one rule would package this. Both are composition/packaging ideas over EXISTING kinds, not new core primitives. No genuine new-kind need.

*Notes.* This is the most instructive repo of the four for the brief's 'gaps were systematically over-reported PRE-build' thesis: the config's single B item (.editorconfig in-place codegen freshness) is now fully addressable via generated_file_fresh's mutating `outputs:` mode — a phantom gap on reproduction. Config is otherwise honest and well-calibrated; the A1 version-coherence rule is the standout. PG correctly does NOT extend ci/github-actions (no GitHub CI) and DOES extend no-tracked-artifacts (no committed binaries).
