### `PostgREST/postgrest` — Haskell API server (cabal + nix-only tooling), community-governed OSS

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the THIN addressable surface (the GHC-sync coherence + all hygiene/structure/workflow-shape items are natively covered); but the addressable surface is itself a small slice of total enforcement — ~90%+ of PostgREST's actual CI is execution-only (formatters/AST-linters/type-checked builds/test matrices/sphinx/spell-NLP), which is correct category-D non-goal territory. So: of what alint is even in scope to do, ~95% replaceable; of the repo's total enforcement, the value is concentrated in the one coherence rule plus hygiene + orchestration.

**Enforcement surface.** PostgREST is unusual: there is NO scripts/, ci/, hack/ dir and NO Makefile/justfile. Every dev tool is a nix `checkedShellScript` under nix/tools/*.nix, invoked from .github/workflows/*.yaml. Grouped by category: (1) LINT/AST/NLP (check.yaml lint-style job): postgrest-lint runs actionlint, deadnix, vulture (dead Python), ruff check, `hsie check-aliases` (Haskell import-alias consistency across the codebase), hlint — all AST/semantic, category D. (2) FORMAT-FRESHNESS (check.yaml): postgrest-style-check runs statix/nixpkgs-fmt/stylish-haskell/black then `git diff-index --exit-code` over *.hs *.lhs *.nix *.py — i.e. run-formatter-then-diff. Pure execution + git-diff freshness, category D. (3) COMMIT-DISCIPLINE (check.yaml commit job): commitlint with a type-enum + subject rules — NLP, D. (4) DOCS (docs.yaml build): sphinx -W (warnings-as-errors) build, then `git diff --exit-code HEAD locales` for generated-locale freshness (non-blocking); docs.yaml spellcheck: aspell + a custom dictcheck for obsolete postgrest.dict entries — NLP, D. (5) TESTS (test.yaml): hspec/pytest/doctest/idempotence/memory/coverage(+weeder dead-code)/loadtest — execution, D. (6) BUILD (build.yaml): nix-build / stack / cabal multi-platform matrix — D. (7) RELEASE-COHERENCE (release.yaml github job): hard-fails unless the pushed git tag == "v$(cabal version)"; ci.yaml tag job auto-tags from the cabal version and branches on its `*.*` shape — this is a genuine cross-source coherence invariant (tag string vs cabal `version:`), but the tag half is runtime git state. (8) DOCUMENTED HAND-SYNC: stack.yaml line-1 comment + resolver trailing comment (`GHC 9.10.x`) must match build.yaml's `ghc-version:` pin (both files literally carry a 'keep in sync' comment). Apart from items 7-8 and generic hygiene, the entire surface is tool-execution (formatters, AST linters, type-checked compiles, test suites, sphinx, spell-NLP).

**What alint replaces.** GHC-version hand-sync (stack.yaml resolver-comment <-> build.yaml ghc-version pin) -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals) with regex extract on both sides; repo hygiene (no-trailing-ws, final-newline, merge-markers, bidi, README/LICENSE/COC/.gitignore/renovate presence) -> oss-baseline bundle + explicit no_trailing_whitespace/file_ends_with over the .editorconfig-promised file set (*.hs/*.nix/*.py/*.yaml/*.cabal/*.rst, beyond just the formatted languages); GitHub-workflow shape (name + contents:read + SHA-pinned actions across all 8 workflows) -> ci/github-actions bundle (repo is already fully SHA-pinned, so this guards regressions); .editorconfig + .gitattributes presence -> tooling/editorconfig bundle; no tracked build artifacts (dist-newstyle/result/__pycache__) -> hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts bundle; cabal `version:` well-formedness gate (release.yaml prerequisite) -> file_content_matches '(?m)^version:\s+\d'.

**What alint improves.** Two concrete wins. (1) The GHC-version coherence is materially better in alint: the repo enforces it only by a prose comment in two files plus human diligence — there is NO automated check that stack.yaml's resolver comment and build.yaml's ghc-version agree. cross_file_value_equals turns that documented 'keep in sync' note into a machine-checked error, and because alint reads bytes it spans the asymmetry that the source lives inside a Haskell/YAML COMMENT while the target is a YAML scalar (a structured-query tool couldn't read the comment side). (2) The .editorconfig trailing-ws / final-newline promise is enforced by the repo ONLY for the formatted languages (*.hs/*.nix/*.py go through stylish-haskell/nixpkgs-fmt/black + git-diff); *.yaml/*.rst/*.cabal trailing whitespace is unguarded. alint's no_trailing_whitespace over the explicit superset closes that. The single-`alint check` entrypoint is also a real onboarding improvement over 'enter a nix-shell to run postgrest-lint' (orchestration value-add, correctly excluded from coverage).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The release-coherence invariant (git tag == "v" + cabal version) is the only tempting candidate beyond what the config covers, and it is NOT a gap: it compares a runtime git-ref/tag string against a file value, which is intrinsically execution/CI-time state (the tag does not exist as repo content at lint time) — correctly a category-D non-goal, and alint already half-covers it by asserting the cabal `version:` is well-formed. The dictcheck (obsolete postgrest.dict entries vs words still used in docs) initially looks like a cross-file coherence check alint could do, but it requires tokenizing prose and a spell-dictionary diff (does the word still appear in any .rst after aspell tokenization?) — that is NLP/semantic, genuinely D. `hsie check-aliases` (every module imports Data.Text as T consistently) is AST-level Haskell import analysis, not regex-expressible across the asymmetric forms — D, not an addressable gap. Everything else is formatters/type-checkers/tests.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `tag_matches_manifest` / release-coherence concept: a thin git-aware rule asserting the current tag (or the tag that WOULD be cut) equals a template over a manifest field, e.g. `template: "v{value}"` sourced from cross_file extraction. This recurring 'tag must equal v<version>' pattern appears in PostgREST (cabal), and across many release.yml gates in the corpus — it sits between git_commit_* (HEAD metadata) and cross_file (file values) and nothing currently bridges a git ref to a file value. Lower-priority but real. Also: a `comment_value_equals` convenience (extract a value from inside a code comment vs a structured field) would make the GHC-sync rule self-documenting, though regex already covers it.

*Notes.* The config is faithful and skeptical already (correctly tags the formatter/AST/test surface as D and keeps the nix-tool wraps commented-out as orchestration-only). No over-claimed gaps. The single most valuable native rule is the GHC-version coherence — a documented-but-unautomated hand-sync, which is exactly alint's sweet spot.

---

### `prettier/prettier` — Node/TypeScript monorepo (yarn 4 / Corepack), the reference code formatter, large contributor surface

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the bespoke DECLARATIVE enforcement (all three `yarn lint:*` scripts: deps + changelog + yarn-version coherence) is natively replaceable, and alint does it better (toolchain-free, reviewable, auto-scaling). Of total repo enforcement, the majority by volume is execution/AST (jest/eslint/tsc/cspell/knip/bundle) which is correct non-goal territory; so 'addressable surface' ~100% replaced, 'total enforcement' value-add = the three scripts + hygiene + single entrypoint.

**Enforcement surface.** Grouped by category: (1) DEPENDENCY-PINNING (yarn lint:deps -> scripts/check-deps.js): every entry under dependencies/devDependencies/resolutions must be an exact version (no ^ or ~); `.yarnrc.yml` sets defaultSemverRangePrefix:"" so `yarn add` pins by default. Declarative, fully alint-addressable. (2) CHANGELOG-SCAFFOLDING (yarn lint:changelog -> scripts/lint-changelog.js): requires changelog_unreleased/TEMPLATE.md + BLOG_POST_INTRO_TEMPLATE.md; every category subdir must keep a .gitkeep; every entry file in a category dir must match /^(\d{4,})(-\d+)?\.md$/ (PR-number naming) — pure structure/naming, alint-addressable. (3) TOOLCHAIN-COHERENCE: package.json `packageManager: yarn@X` must match `.yarnrc.yml` yarnPath (yarn-X.cjs) — cross-file value coherence, addressable. (4) EXECUTION/AST/NLP (the bulk, category D): jest test suite + snapshot tests, eslint (custom internal plugin rules), tsc typecheck, cspell spellcheck, knip (dead exports/deps), CodeQL, actionlint, the dist bundle build + `yarn test` against the built bundle, and a bundle-size check. (5) HYGIENE: prettier formats itself (self-application) + generic OSS hygiene. The declarative state-checks are exactly the three bespoke `yarn lint:*` node scripts (deps/changelog/yarn-version); everything language-aware stays in CI.

**What alint replaces.** scripts/check-deps.js (no ^/~ in any dependency block) -> three json_path_matches rules on $.dependencies.*/$.devDependencies.*/$.resolutions.* with matches '^[^\^~]' + if_present; scripts/lint-changelog.js template-presence -> two file_exists (TEMPLATE.md, BLOG_POST_INTRO_TEMPLATE.md); per-category .gitkeep -> for_each_dir over changelog_unreleased/* with nested file_exists '{path}/.gitkeep'; changelog entry filename form -> filename_regex '^\d{4,}(-\d+)?\.md$'; packageManager <-> yarnPath coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (regex extract 'yarn@(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)' vs 'yarn-(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)\.cjs'); generic hygiene -> oss-baseline + node bundles.

**What alint improves.** (1) check-deps.js is ~40 lines of bespoke JS that parses package.json, walks three blocks, and regex-tests each version; alint replaces it with three declarative, schema-validated lines that any reviewer reads at a glance — and crucially they run WITHOUT a yarn/node toolchain (a fresh contributor or a CI without the JS deps installed still gets the check). Same for the yarn-version coherence: the repo today has NO automated check that packageManager and yarnPath agree (a real drift source when bumping Corepack) — alint adds it. (2) The for_each_dir '.gitkeep' rule is strictly better than lint-changelog.js's hardcoded category list: it scales automatically when a new language/category dir is added, whereas the script (and the original config's A4 filename rule, which hardcodes the ~20-category brace-glob) must be edited. This is a place to IMPROVE the config itself: A4's `changelog_unreleased/{javascript,typescript,...}/*.md` brace-list should be `changelog_unreleased/*/*.md` (or a for_each_dir) to avoid silently missing entries in any future category. (3) Consolidating three separate `yarn lint:*` invocations into one `alint check` is a genuine DX win.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. I pressure-tested the trickiest piece: lint-changelog.js does slightly more than filename-shape — it cross-references that the PR number in the filename is plausible and that the entry body uses the template's section headers. The filename-number existence is covered by filename_regex; the body-structure check (does the .md contain the required '#### ...' headings) is genuinely a per-file content shape — and that IS expressible with file_content_matches '(?m)^#### ' or file_header, so it is NOT a gap, just unmodeled in the current config (an improvement opportunity, category A). The deeper validations (eslint custom rules, tsc, jest snapshots, cspell, knip dead-code, bundle build/size) are all execution/AST/NLP — deliberate category-D non-goals, not gaps. The yarn version is also encoded a THIRD time inside yarn.lock's metadata, but checking three-way lockfile coherence is brittle and unnecessary (the two-file rule is the documented invariant).

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `pinned-dependencies` ruleset fragment for the node ecosystem: 'every version in dependencies/devDependencies/(optional)peerDependencies/resolutions matches a no-range pattern' is a recurring policy (prettier, many strict monorepos) — packaging it as a named option (e.g. node@v1 with `pin_dependencies: true`) would let adopters opt in with one line instead of re-authoring 3-4 json_path_matches rules. A complementary `range_policy: exact|caret|tilde` axis on a dependency-aware rule would generalize this beyond prettier. Also a small `changelog_fragments` convention bundle (template files present + per-category .gitkeep + {PR}.md naming + required body headings) would capture the increasingly common 'unreleased changelog fragment' workflow (prettier, many JS/Rust projects) as a single composable unit.

*Notes.* Cleanest A-heavy repo of the four. Two config-improvement notes (not gaps): (a) A4's hardcoded category brace-glob should be a wildcard/for_each_dir to match the script's auto-scaling intent; (b) the changelog-body required-headings check is an easy additive file_content_matches win the config omits. No genuine gaps.

---

### `prometheus/prometheus` — Go CNCF monitoring monorepo (Go workspace, 6 modules), Apache-2.0, prometheus/Makefile.common shared infra

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the addressable (non-execution) surface: go-version coherence, license-header presence + future-year ban, node-version coherence, Apache compliance, workflow-shape, release-notes changeset, and the codegen-freshness gates (as orchestrated freshness rules) are all covered. The remaining addressable slice (CODEOWNERS<->MAINTAINERS semantic equality) is unenforceable by anyone. Of TOTAL enforcement, a large share is execution (go test/golangci-lint/gofmt/buf/codeql/govulncheck/fuzz) = correct non-goal; so addressable ~90% replaceable, with the codegen-diff gates as orchestration value-add.

**Enforcement surface.** Grouped by category: (1) CROSS-MODULE VERSION COHERENCE (scripts/check-go-mod-version.sh, gated via `make test`): every go.mod/go.work `go` directive across all 6 modules must be identical (currently `go 1.25.x`) — declarative cross-file coherence, alint-addressable. (2) LICENSE-HEADERS (Makefile.common common-check_license): every non-vendored .go must have, in its first 3 lines (awk NR<=3 | grep -Eq), one of Copyright/generated/GENERATED — the Apache banner, the codegen exemption, or a vendored third-party copyright; ALSO forbids future-year `Copyright 202[6-9]` stamps (project standard is the year-less 'Copyright The Prometheus Authors'). Header-shape, alint-addressable. (3) FORMAT/CODEGEN FRESHNESS (Makefile common-style/gofmt, common-unused/go-mod-tidy, and several `make <gen> && git diff --exit-code` gates for generated parser/protobuf/UI assets): run-tool-then-diff — execution + git-diff, category D, but wrappable via generated_file_fresh/command_idempotent. (4) BUF/PROTO: buf lint + buf breaking — AST, D. (5) SUPPLY-CHAIN/SECURITY (ci.yml ~390 lines): codeql, scorecards, govulncheck, fuzzing — execution, D. (6) RELEASE-NOTES (scripts/check_release_notes.sh): a PR must touch CHANGELOG / docs release notes — NLP-ish/changeset, partially addressable. (7) NODE/UI (scripts/check-node-version.sh): the web/ui node version pin — cross-file coherence, addressable. (8) GOVERNANCE: CODEOWNERS header documents 'keep in sync with MAINTAINERS.md' but no tool enforces equality (different formats). (9) HYGIENE: oss-baseline + Apache LICENSE/NOTICE/source-header.

**What alint replaces.** check-go-mod-version.sh (all 6 go.mod/go.work `go` directives identical) -> cross_file_value_equals with regex '(?m)^go\s+(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)' source go.mod + 5 targets; common-check_license header presence -> file_header pattern 'Copyright|generated|GENERATED' over **/*.go !vendor; future-year-copyright ban -> file_content_forbidden 'Copyright (202[6-9]|20[3-9][0-9]) The Prometheus Authors'; Apache LICENSE/NOTICE + source banner -> compliance/apache-2 bundle; SHA-pinned workflow actions + contents:read -> ci/github-actions bundle; MAINTAINERS.md presence -> file_exists; node-version pin coherence (scripts/check-node-version.sh) -> cross_file_value_equals; the `make <gen> && git diff --exit-code` freshness gates -> generated_file_fresh (outputs: mutating mode) or command_idempotent wrapping the make target; check_release_notes.sh (PR must add a release-note path) -> changeset_requires_path.

**What alint improves.** (1) check-go-mod-version.sh is a bespoke bash loop; cross_file_value_equals expresses the same N-way invariant declaratively AND runs without the Go toolchain (a docs-only or non-Go contributor still gets the coherence check pre-CI). (2) The license-header check is faithfully modeled including the SUBTLETY that common-check_license accepts Copyright OR generated OR GENERATED (so generated and vendored-MIT files pass) — the file_header `(A|B|C)` alternation captures the real script semantics exactly, where a naive 'must match the Apache banner' rule would over-fire on the same files the repo intentionally exempts; that fidelity is an improvement over a hand-rolled grep that's easy to get subtly wrong. (3) The codegen-freshness gates (make gen && git diff) are wrappable via generated_file_fresh mutating mode (snapshot -> run make target -> diff -> restore, tree stays pure), consolidating several scattered Makefile diff-gates into reviewable declared freshness rules (orchestration value-add — the freshness LOGIC stays the repo's). (4) changeset_requires_path can natively replace check_release_notes.sh's 'this PR must touch a release-note file' rule.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that the config's own analysis got wrong. The CODEOWNERS<->MAINTAINERS.md 'keep in sync' is the one tempting candidate, and the config correctly downgrades it to existence-only because the two files are STRUCTURALLY different formats (CODEOWNERS glob->@team lines vs a MAINTAINERS.md prose/table) — there is no shared value set to compare, so cross_file set_equals genuinely cannot bridge them. That is a real limitation but it is ALSO not something the repo enforces (no tool checks it either), so it's a non-issue, not an alint gap. I re-checked the go-version regex against go.work specifically: go.work files can carry a bare `go 1.25` (two-component) line; the config's '\d+\.\d+\.\d+' assumes three components — that's a config-fidelity risk (it could mis-extract or skip), but it's a regex-tuning issue, NOT an alint expressiveness gap (loosen to '\d+\.\d+(?:\.\d+)?'). buf lint/breaking, codeql, govulncheck, fuzzing, gofmt are all execution/AST — correct category-D non-goals.

**Abstraction ideas.** An N-ary `same_value_across` convenience would make the 6-module go-version rule (and the analogous 'all go.mod toolchain lines agree' pattern recurring across every Go workspace monorepo in the corpus) terser than source+5-targets — essentially cross_file set_equals where the assertion is |set|==1 over a glob of files. A `monorepo` bundle option like `go_workspace_version_coherent: true` that auto-discovers every go.mod under the tree and asserts a single `go` directive would capture this whole class with one line (the corpus shows this is the single most common Go-monorepo coherence check). Also a license-header convenience that bakes in the common 'banner OR generated-marker exemption' alternation (so adopters don't have to know to write `(Apache|generated)`) would generalize the prometheus/Makefile.common pattern, which many CNCF Go repos copy verbatim.

*Notes.* Config is accurate and appropriately skeptical (keeps the language-aware tools out of the coverage ratio, models the license alternation faithfully, downgrades the unenforceable CODEOWNERS sync to existence). One concrete fidelity fix: relax the `go` version regex to make the third version component optional so go.work two-component lines don't slip through.

---

### `protocolbuffers/protobuf` — C++ core + 9 language bindings, the serialization library, Bazel + CMake, Google-governed

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of the ADDRESSABLE surface: version-coherence (now confirmed expressible via the \A-regex anchor), the presubmit.yml co-change (pair_changed_together), manifest presence/shape, workflow-shape, and hygiene are covered; the ~12 staleness gates are wrappable as command_idempotent (orchestration). The shortfall is mostly that the addressable surface is THIN — the repo's enforcement is overwhelmingly Bazel/CMake codegen-staleness + test/conformance/sanitizer execution (category D, correct non-goals). So: addressable ~75% replaceable natively + the rest orchestrated; total enforcement is execution-dominated.

**Enforcement surface.** Grouped by category: (1) CODEGEN/STALENESS FRESHNESS (the dominant bespoke gate): regenerate_stale_files.sh + .github staleness_check.yml drive ~12 Bazel staleness_test targets (e.g. //src:cmake_lists_staleness_test for src/file_lists.cmake generated from Bazel globs; //src/google/protobuf:well_known_types_staleness_test for checked-in any.pb.*/struct.pb.* descriptors; plus per-language generated descriptor/runtime staleness). Each supports --fix; in check posture it exits non-zero iff the checked-in file != freshly-generated output. Run-generator-then-compare — category D execution, but wrappable. (2) KEEP-IN-SYNC: .bcr/presubmit.yml <-> .bazelci/presubmit.yml linked by a LINT.IfChange/ThenChange directive (Google's bespoke 'if you edit A, edit B' linter) — cross-file co-change coherence. (3) TEST/CONFORMANCE (category D, the bulk): Bazel/CMake/CTest per-language test matrices, ASan/MSan/TSan/UBSan sanitizers, the cross-implementation conformance suite, a bespoke YAML config validator. (4) VERSION PROPAGATION: protobuf_version.bzl declares PROTOBUF_<LANG>_VERSION = "4.36.x"; genrules propagate these into per-language publish manifests (pom.xml, .gemspec, composer.json, package.json, version.json, .xcprivacy) — but the repo has NO gate asserting they agree across manifests (it trusts the genrules). (5) HEADERS: a BSD-style Google banner ('Protocol Buffers - Google's data interchange format') appears on MOST hand-written sources but is NOT uniform and is UN-gated. (6) HYGIENE: oss-baseline + SHA-pinned actions (Scorecard-aligned).

**What alint replaces.** The ~12 staleness_test entrypoints (codegen freshness) -> command_idempotent wrapping each `bazel test //...:..._staleness_test` (the repo's own --check posture); generated-from-globs freshness specifically (file_lists.cmake) could ALSO be expressed without Bazel as generated_file_fresh outputs-mode or file_graph fresh if the generator is scriptable; cross-language version coherence (protobuf_version.bzl -> .gemspec, composer.json, package.json, version.json) -> cross_file_value_equals (regex/json extract — verified bzl 4.36.0 == gemspec s.version == composer manifests); php composer name/license + csharp global.json sdk pin + version manifest presence -> json_path_matches/json_path_equals/file_exists; SHA-pinned workflow actions -> ci/github-actions bundle; OSS hygiene (README/LICENSE/SECURITY/CODEOWNERS, merge-marker/bidi bans) -> oss-baseline; the LINT.IfChange co-change between the two presubmit.yml files -> pair_changed_together (if-changed-then-changed).

**What alint improves.** (1) Version coherence is a NET-NEW capability: the repo has NO gate at all asserting protobuf_version.bzl agrees with the per-language publish manifests — it relies entirely on genrules being run. cross_file_value_equals adds a cheap declarative guard that the checked-in .gemspec/composer.json/version.json haven't drifted from the canonical .bzl, catching a stale-regen before publish. (2) The LINT.IfChange/ThenChange between .bcr/presubmit.yml and .bazelci/presubmit.yml is a Google-internal linter the OSS repo can't easily run; pair_changed_together expresses that exact 'edit A => must also touch B' co-change invariant natively and toolchain-free in CI. (3) Consolidating ~12 separate bazel staleness_test invocations under one declared command_idempotent set gives a single reviewable freshness manifest (orchestration value-add; the freshness LOGIC stays Bazel's).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that survive the reproduce-first lens — and notably the config's TWO flagged sharp-edges (C) both turn out WORKABLE on the current engine. (a) The pom.xml multi-<version> false-fail (the config's R4 finding: a naive '<version>([0-9...])' regex over java/bom/pom.xml extracts THREE versions — project 4.36.0 + plugin 1.6 + 1.6.6 — and cross_file_value_equals requires ALL to equal the source, so it 2x-fails) IS solvable today: the documented start-anchored dot-all lazy idiom `extract: { regex: '(?s)\A.*?<version>([^<]+)</version>' }` matches at \A only and yields EXACTLY the first <version> (the project version), since `captures_iter` over a \A-anchored pattern returns at most one match (verified in extract.rs). So pom coherence is expressible — it's a config-authoring miss, not a gap. (b) The BSD-header rule is correctly left `off` not because alint CAN'T (file_header with the literal pattern works), but because the convention is genuinely un-gated AND inconsistently applied in the repo (99 hand-written files omit it) — turning it on needs a curated exclude list; that's a repo-reality issue, not an alint limitation. The ONE thing I genuinely could NOT express cleanly: anchoring the pom version via an XML JSONPath INSIDE a cross_file extract — the shared extract.rs Extract enum supports toml/json/yaml/lines/regex/whole_file but NOT xml (even though xml_path_* rules exist as standalone single-file kinds). But this is NOT a real gap because the \A-regex idiom achieves the same single-value anchor; it's only an ergonomic asymmetry (xml is a second-class citizen in the cross-file extractor). The staleness/conformance/sanitizer/YAML-validator bulk is execution — correct category-D non-goals.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) Add `xml` to the cross_file `extract:` one-of so XML manifests (pom.xml, .csproj, Directory.Packages.props) get the same structured-path anchoring as json/yaml/toml — this would have prevented the entire pom multi-version sharp-edge and is a natural, low-cost symmetry fix (xml_to_value already exists in structured_path.rs; only the Extract enum + ExtractSpec need the variant). This is the single most justified abstraction this repo demonstrates. (2) An `occurrence: first|last|all` (or `index:`) knob on regex extract would make 'take the first match' first-class instead of relying on the `(?s)\A.*?` trick — broadly useful (CHANGELOG-latest, pom-first-version, etc.), and the corpus shows this pattern repeatedly. (3) A bundled `version_propagation` / multi-manifest-coherence concept: 'one canonical version source (a .bzl/.toml field) must equal a named field in each of N heterogeneous publish manifests' is exactly the protobuf cross-language-release shape and recurs in any polyglot-published lib; packaging source + per-manifest (file, extract) pairs as one composable unit would beat hand-writing one cross_file rule per language.

*Notes.* Most instructive repo of the four for the reproduce-first lens: BOTH config-flagged sharp-edges (pom multi-version, BSD header) dissolve — the pom one via the verified \A start-anchored regex idiom, the header one because it's a repo-reality (un-gated/inconsistent) not an alint limitation. The only legitimately missing surface is `xml` as a cross_file extract format, which is an ergonomic gap (the \A-regex idiom is a working substitute), not a capability gap. Strong driver for adding xml to the cross-file extractor and a first-occurrence extract knob.

---

### `psf/black` — Python OSS formatter/CLI — mid-size single-language library, hatchling (hatch-vcs version-from-tag) + tox, PSF governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable (non-execution) surface — both pre-commit hygiene hooks, schema codegen-freshness, AND (contrary to the config) both release-version-coherence checks are expressible today; the rest is execution (NON-GOAL).

**Enforcement surface.** black's enforced-validation surface is OVERWHELMINGLY tool execution (category D): black --check on itself (tox -e run_self), flake8, isort, mypy --strict, prettier, the pytest matrix across 7 Pythons x 4 OSes, the fuzz harness, the diff-shades style-regression compare, zizmor workflow SAST, and pre-commit run --all-files. The DECLARATIVELY-addressable subset is small and centres on (a) cross-file release-version coherence — scripts/check_pre_commit_rev_in_example.py asserts the `rev:` in the pre-commit YAML examples in docs/integrations/source_version_control.md + docs/guides/using_black_with_jupyter_notebooks.md equals the LATEST (first non-'Unreleased') `## Version X` header in CHANGES.md; scripts/check_version_in_basics_example.py asserts the `$ black --version` console example in docs/usage_and_configuration/the_basics.md shows that same latest version — both bespoke Python validators with no native CI primitive; (b) codegen freshness — lint.yml 'Regenerate schema' runs `tox -e generate_schema && git diff --exit-code` so the committed src/black/resources/black.schema.json must equal scripts/generate_schema.py's stdout; (c) repo hygiene — pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks trailing-whitespace + end-of-file-fixer, scoped by the top-level exclude ^(profiling/|tests/data/). Commit-discipline is light (no DCO/sign-off gate). Structure/layout enforcement is essentially none beyond what the bundles add.

**What alint replaces.** trailing-whitespace pre-commit hook -> no_trailing_whitespace (black-no-trailing-whitespace, with profiling/ + tests/data/ excludes); end-of-file-fixer pre-commit hook -> final_newline (black-final-newline); lint.yml 'Regenerate schema' tox+git-diff gate -> generated_file_fresh (black-schema-fresh, stdout-diff mode, command: python scripts/generate_schema.py); scripts/check_pre_commit_rev_in_example.py + scripts/check_version_in_basics_example.py -> cross_file_value_equals (the config DEFERS these as a tuning gap; see alint_improves — they are in fact expressible TODAY via the occurrence:first regex idiom).

**What alint improves.** Consolidates two bespoke ~40-line Python release-validators (check_pre_commit_rev_in_example.py + check_version_in_basics_example.py) plus the schema-freshness CI step plus two pre-commit hooks into one declarative, schema-validated, reviewable file that runs without black importable or the tox venv (the schema-freshness arm aside). The version-coherence checks become cross-file invariants the repo currently only enforces via custom imperative scripts that each duplicate the 'parse CHANGES.md / read the doc / compare' logic; alint expresses the same relation declaratively with the source-of-truth made explicit.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's headline claim — that the two version-coherence checks are blocked by a missing `occurrence: first`/`nth` regex selector because cross_file_value_equals requires the source to resolve to EXACTLY ONE value and CHANGES.md has ~80 identically-shaped `## Version` headers — is a PHANTOM gap on reproduction. I confirmed extract.rs still returns ALL capture-group-1 matches via captures_iter (no occurrence option), AND that the brief's documented idiom `(?s)\A.*?## Version (\d+\.\d+(?:\.\d+)?)` (start-anchored + dot-all + lazy) yields EXACTLY one value against a multi-header changelog (ran a throwaway test in alint-rules: IDIOM_RESULT=["25.1.0"] LEN=1). So the source collapses to the single newest released version and both cross_file_value_equals rules work today — they should be uncommented and reclassified A. Everything else black enforces is tool execution (NON-GOAL, correctly orchestrated via command/command_idempotent), not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** An ergonomic `occurrence: first|last|nth: N` option on the regex extractor would still be worth shipping as DX sugar — the `(?s)\A.*?` idiom works but is non-obvious and easy to get wrong (every study agent on changelog-newest-version repos reached for it and mis-reported it as impossible). A first-class selector would make 'the newest changelog entry' a one-keyword source. Also: a tiny `changelog/newest-version` shared helper or a bundled `release-coherence` ruleset (newest-CHANGES-version must match docs/pre-commit-rev/version-example) would capture a pattern recurring across black, pydantic, prettier, and many tagged-release repos.

*Notes.* The config's two commented cross_file_value_equals rules (black-precommit-rev-matches-changelog-svc, black-version-example-matches-changelog) should be activated by replacing the `## Version (...)` extractor with `(?s)\A.*?## Version (...)` and dropping the (unsupported) occurrence:first key — verified working. The four file_exists 'validator-must-be-present' value-add rules are sound. black-schema-fresh is correctly kept advisory (needs `pip install -e .`).

---

### `pydantic/pydantic` — Python validation library (hatchling) with a VENDORED Rust extension pydantic-core/ (maturin/cargo, own Makefile + uv.lock + Cargo.lock); uv workspace

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable (non-execution) surface — both cross-file version-coherence invariants + hygiene are native; remainder is execution (NON-GOAL).

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced surface is dominated by tool execution (D): ruff lint+format, pyright + mypy + pyrefly type-checking, codespell, markdownlint, yamlfmt, zizmor, cargo fmt/clippy, the full pytest suite (incl. mypy/pyright integration tests + memray + benchmarks), coverage>=91 gate, twine wheel checks, and many cross-platform/cross-Python builds — all run frozen (UV_FROZEN / uv sync --frozen / cargo with committed lockfiles). The declaratively-addressable surface is two cross-file VALUE-coherence invariants that the repo documents as review conventions with NO CI gate: (1) the pydantic-core version is pinned in THREE files that must stay in sync — pydantic-core/Cargo.toml [package].version is canonical, the parent pyproject.toml pins it as a runtime dep `pydantic-core==X`, and pydantic/version.py repeats it as `_COMPATIBLE_PYDANTIC_CORE_VERSION` (both Python sites carry literal 'Keep this in sync' comments); (2) pydantic's own VERSION (pydantic/version.py) must equal CITATION.cff's `version:` token — release/prepare.py writes both in one step so they agree at rest. Hygiene (trailing-whitespace / EOF newline) is pre-commit-enforced and covered by the bundles. Lockfile freshness is load-bearing but enforced by the frozen tool runs, not a structural check.

**What alint replaces.** the documented-but-ungated 'Keep this in sync' three-file pydantic-core version convention -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals) relation:equals, source=pydantic-core/Cargo.toml $.package.version via toml extract, two regex targets (pydantic-core==(...) in pyproject.toml, _COMPATIBLE_PYDANTIC_CORE_VERSION='...' in pydantic/version.py); the release/prepare.py VERSION<->CITATION.cff lockstep convention -> cross_file_value_equals (line-anchored regex both sides, v-prefix stripped on the CITATION side); pre-commit trailing-whitespace/EOF -> no_trailing_whitespace/final_newline (via oss-baseline+python bundles); reproducible-build lockfile presence -> file_exists (value-add: pydantic-core/Cargo.lock + uv.lock).

**What alint improves.** Turns two prose 'Keep this in sync' review conventions — today enforced only by a human noticing the comment during review, with ZERO automated gate — into hard, schema-validated, single-file checks that fail fast in CI before the multi-platform build matrix even starts. The three-file version coherence in particular is exactly the cross_file sweet spot (one canonical source, two derived pins in different formats/languages); alint expresses it in one rule where the repo expresses it as scattered comments and an implicit prepare.py contract.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none in the addressable surface. Both version-coherence relations are natively expressible and the regexes given are correct (line-anchored to avoid the version.py `VERSION=` vs `_COMPATIBLE_PYDANTIC_CORE_VERSION=` ambiguity, and the CITATION cff-version vs version line ambiguity). Everything else is execution (ruff/pyright/mypy/cargo/pytest/coverage — NON-GOAL). One non-gap caveat worth flagging: the config's message templates use `{{ctx.source_value}}`/`{{ctx.target_value}}`, but I verified interp.rs only resolves {{env.*}}/{{vars.*}} at config-load and passes `{{ctx.*}}` through VERBATIM (interp.rs test asserts `matched {{ctx.match}}` is returned unchanged; cross_file builds its message via self.message.clone() with no per-violation substitution). So those placeholders render literally — cosmetic only, the rules still build and fire correctly. Not a gap, just a config over-claim to note.

**Abstraction ideas.** A multi-target 'fan-out version pin' is common enough (canonical manifest version must appear in N derived sites, each a different format) that a sugar form — e.g. cross_file with a list of (file, extract) targets all asserting equality to one source, which IS what exists — could be packaged as a bundled `vendored-extension-version-coherence` snippet. More broadly, since pydantic vends a whole Rust crate inside a Python package, a bundled `python-with-rust-extension` ruleset (parent uv.lock + member Cargo.lock + member uv.lock present, version pinned across the language boundary) would serve this and pydantic-core-shaped repos directly. Optional commit-discipline value-add now available: git_commit_subject_matches for the PR-title convention.

*Notes.* The config is accurate and well-scoped; both A-items are genuinely replaceable today. Recommend dropping the {{ctx.*}} interpolations from the messages (or hard-coding the file names) since they won't substitute. The two facts.has_pydantic_core-gated file_exists rules are reasonable value-add.

---

### `pytest-dev/pytest` — Python testing-framework library — large single-language Python repo, towncrier changelog, heavy pre-commit + bot enforcement, PSF-adjacent governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable (non-execution) surface — all six pygrep/naming/ordering conventions + the bot's add-a-fragment diff check are native; remainder is execution (NON-GOAL).

**Enforcement surface.** Enforced surface splits cleanly. EXECUTION (D): ruff + ruff-format, mypy, pyright, pylint, codespell, blacken-docs, pyproject-fmt, zizmor, rst-lint, the pytest suite across the OS x interpreter x extra matrix, and the Sphinx doc build + linkcheck. The DECLARATIVELY-addressable A-surface is a rich set of local pre-commit pygrep / naming / ordering conventions: (A1) towncrier newsfragment filename grammar — every file under changelog/ must be named <issue>.<type>.rst where type is one of breaking|deprecation|feature|improvement|bugfix|vendor|doc|packaging|contrib|misc, enforced by BOTH the pre-commit changelogs-rst local hook AND the chronographer bot branch-protection check (README/_template/.gitignore exempt); (A2) AUTHORS kept alphabetical (case-insensitive) — a CONTRIBUTING.rst review convention from the 'Contributors include::' header to EOF; (A3/A4) trailing-whitespace + end-of-file-fixer hooks; (A5) py-path-deprecated pygrep hook forbidding `\bpy\.path\.local` with specific excludes; (A6) py-deprecated pygrep hook forbidding `py.<submodule>`; (A7) changelogs-user-role pygrep hook forbidding a broken :user:`name` role in changelog rst. These pygrep/naming/ordering hooks are precisely alint's declarative wheelhouse.

**What alint replaces.** changelogs-rst hook + chronographer 'Changelog entry' check -> filename_regex (pytest-changelog-fragment-naming, with the towncrier type alternation + README/_template/.gitignore exclude); AUTHORS-alphabetical CONTRIBUTING convention -> ordered_block (start 'Contributors include::', end \z, comparator lexical-ci, unique:false); trailing-whitespace hook -> no_trailing_whitespace (+ file_trim_trailing_whitespace fixer); end-of-file-fixer hook -> final_newline (+ file_append_final_newline fixer); py-path-deprecated pygrep hook -> file_content_forbidden (\bpy\.path\.local with the doc/legacypath/deprecated excludes); py-deprecated pygrep hook -> file_content_forbidden (the py.<submodule> alternation); changelogs-user-role pygrep hook -> file_content_forbidden (broken :user: pattern); MIT license declaration -> toml_path_equals (value-add).

**What alint improves.** Consolidates SIX separate pre-commit local hooks (two of which — the changelog naming — are DOUBLE-enforced by a pygrep hook AND a GitHub bot, a coherence gap alint closes in one rule) plus an entirely UNGATED documented convention (AUTHORS alphabetical, which today relies only on a CONTRIBUTING.rst sentence and reviewer vigilance) into one declarative file. The pygrep hooks are individually trivial regexes whose excludes drift; centralising them with named ids + messages + policy_url is more maintainable and reviewable than scattered YAML entries, and the AUTHORS ordering check has no current automated enforcement at all — ordered_block with lexical-ci is a net-new gate.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Every A-item maps to a native kind with the correct semantics. The chronographer bot ALSO enforces 'every PR must ADD a changelog fragment' (a diff predicate, distinct from the filename-grammar check) — and that IS now expressible via changeset_requires_path (add_glob: changelog/*.rst, since: base ref), which the pytest config does not yet use but could add as a value-add; so even the bot's diff-side enforcement is no longer a gap. Everything else (ruff/mypy/pyright/pylint/codespell/pytest matrix/sphinx) is execution (NON-GOAL).

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `towncrier` (or `changelog-fragments`) ruleset would elegantly serve this and the broad towncrier-using ecosystem: filename-grammar (parameterised type list) + changeset_requires_path 'a fragment was added' + a forbidden-broken-:user:-role content check, all in one extends. pytest demonstrates the exact need (it hand-rolls all three across pre-commit + a bot). Also a 'sorted membership list' shared helper (ordered_block lexical-ci between a header marker and EOF) recurs for AUTHORS/CONTRIBUTORS/CODEOWNERS-style files.

*Notes.* Cleanest of the four configs; every claimed A is genuinely A and no over-claimed gaps. Recommend adding a changeset_requires_path rule to cover the chronographer bot's 'changelog entry added' arm, which the config currently omits.

---

### `python-poetry/poetry` — Python dependency-manager / build-backend CLI, self-hosting (builds itself with poetry-core), pre-commit + pytest, community OSS

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the addressable (non-execution) surface — all 3 enforced hygiene hooks + every value-add structural/self-hosting pin map natively. The rest (ruff/mypy/pytest/poetry check/build/docs, lockfile consistency) is correctly NON-GOAL execution.

**Enforcement surface.** Execution-dominated like mypy, thin declarative shell. CI/pre-commit: ruff + mypy + pytest + zizmor + `poetry check` (validate own pyproject) + `poetry build` + docs build = all D (AST/execution/build). The genuinely native enforced items are THREE pre-commit-hooks file-hygiene gates: trailing-whitespace, check-merge-conflict, end-of-file-fixer — each with bespoke EXCLUDES (the vendored test fixtures under tests/repositories/fixtures/pypi.org/metadata/** which are binary-ish; an attrs-19.3.0.dist-info/METADATA whose RST `=======` underlines look like conflict markers; *.egg-info/**). .gitattributes invariant: `*.metadata binary` (keeps fixture hashes stable — load-bearing for the resolver tests). Self-hosting invariants poetry maintains (not all gated, but real): pyproject [tool.poetry] requires-poetry pin (>=2.0), build-system build-backend == poetry.core.masonry.api, project.license == MIT, project.requires-python floor (>=3.10). poetry SHIPS the canonical [tool.poetry] JSON schema at src/poetry/json/schemas/poetry.json. Standard community files (CHANGELOG.md, LICENSE).

**What alint replaces.** trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace (with the metadata-fixture exclude); check-merge-conflict -> no_merge_conflict_markers (with the attrs METADATA exclude); end-of-file-fixer -> final_newline; `*.metadata binary` .gitattributes invariant -> file_content_matches on .gitattributes; the four self-hosting pyproject pins -> toml_path_matches / toml_path_equals (requires-poetry, build-backend, license, requires-python — all already in the config); poetry.json schema presence -> file_exists; CHANGELOG/LICENSE presence -> file_exists / bundled oss-baseline.

**What alint improves.** poetry SHIPS src/poetry/json/schemas/poetry.json (the validator for [tool.poetry] tables) but its CI only asserts presence + runs the validator in Python. alint could turn poetry's OWN schema into a self-check: json_schema_passes with schema_file: src/poetry/json/schemas/poetry.json against poetry's pyproject.toml [tool.poetry] subtree — dogfooding the shipped schema declaratively (the repo does this only via `poetry check` execution). Consolidates 3 hygiene hooks + the .gitattributes + 4 self-consistency pins into ONE reviewable file with the excludes co-located (today the excludes are scattered in .pre-commit-config.yaml). cross_file resolves could also assert that every schema file referenced from poetry's loader resolves on disk (registry-style), tighter than the repo's runtime import.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE addressable. Everything the config marks value-add is expressible; everything else is execution. The merge-conflict false-positive on RST `=======` underlines is handled by the same exclude the repo uses (not a gap — it's a deliberate scoping that alint supports via paths.exclude). No backref/look-around need here. No cross-file SET coherence that fails (poetry has a single lockfile; lockfile-vs-pyproject consistency is `poetry lock --check` execution = NON-GOAL, correctly). I tried to find a relational invariant that fails and could not.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A 'self-schema dogfood' composition: a bundled idiom that, when a repo SHIPS a JSON schema, points json_schema_passes at the repo's own config using that shipped schema — poetry, and any tool that ships its config schema, benefits. (2) Minor: a `requires-poetry`-style 'build-tool floor' could be generalized into the python bundle (assert the declared build-backend's own version floor) but this is polish, not a missing primitive. Essentially 'none' beyond existing surface — poetry is a clean fit.

*Notes.* Cleanest of the four — no genuine gaps. Strong IMPROVABLE story (shipped-schema dogfood) the config under-uses. python-has-lockfile from the python bundle will be satisfied (poetry.lock exists), unlike mypy.

---

### `python/cpython` — Massive C/Python language-runtime monorepo — autotools build, ~50-file `make regen-all` codegen pipeline, Argument Clinic, towncrier-style NEWS.d, pinned-autoconf reproducibility, the largest CI surface in the corpus

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (non-execution) surface — regen-freshness, version-coherence, NEWS.d naming + the add-a-blurb diff check, autoconf markers, generated-marker resolution, hygiene/line-endings/bidi, and all presence checks are native; the ~10% residual addressable surface is the Argument Clinic self-checksum (and the trivial CODEOWNERS column alignment). The large execution layer (C build/tests/refleak/Sphinx/C-API semantic checks) is NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** By far the deepest enforcement surface in this group, spanning many categories. CODEGEN-FRESHNESS (the single biggest gate): build.yml 'Check for changes' runs `make regen-all` (regen-cases/token/ast/keyword/sre/frozen/pegen/global-objects/clinic/...) then `git status --porcelain` and fails on any drift; plus the manual arms regen-stdlib-module-names / regen-sbom / regen-configure / regen-unicodedata. STRUCTURE/NAMING: Misc/NEWS.d/next/<section>/<blurb>.rst filename grammar (blurb add: YYYY-...-SS.gh-issue-<n>.<nonce>.rst, legacy bpo- tolerated), two `language: fail` pre-commit hooks forbidding spaced section dirs (C API, Core and Builtins). CROSS-FILE COHERENCE: configure.ac m4_define([PYTHON_VERSION]) major+minor must equal Include/patchlevel.h PY_MAJOR/MINOR_VERSION (release-blocker on drift). CONTENT/MARKER assertions: the check-autoconf-regen job greps the committed configure for 'Generated by GNU Autoconf 2.72' + runstatedir and aclocal.m4 for 'aclocal 1.16.5' + PKG_PROG_PKG_CONFIG (pinned-tool markers). GENERATED-FILE PRESENCE: .gitattributes `... generated` markers must each resolve to a committed file (incl. glob markers). HYGIENE/LINE-ENDINGS: .editorconfig trim+final-newline, .gitattributes dos/lf split (rst=lf, .bat/.vcxproj/.props/.ps1=crlf), remove-tabs (python), Trojan-Source bidi defense. CHANGESET predicate: patchcheck.py reported_news() asserts the PR diff ADDED a Misc/NEWS.d/next/ blurb. SELF-CHECKSUM: every Argument Clinic block embeds /*[clinic end generated code: output=<hex> input=<hex>]*/ — a checksum of a slice of its OWN file by clinic.py's bespoke algorithm. EXECUTION (D): C compile, the test suite, refleak hunts, Sphinx HTML build, ruff/black formatters + linters, actionlint/zizmor/sphinx-lint/check-jsonschema, and the C-API semantic checks (stable_abi.py, smelly.py ELF symbol scan, check-c-globals).

**What alint replaces.** `make regen-all` + git-status freshness gate -> command_idempotent (cpython-regen-all-fresh, MUTATING regenerator + assert-clean-tree; plus per-arm command_idempotent for regen-stdlib-module-names/regen-sbom/regen-configure); ruff format / black --check hooks (file-rewriting) -> command_idempotent; ruff check hooks (findings) -> command; configure.ac<->patchlevel.h major+minor coherence -> cross_file_value_equals x2; NEWS.d blurb filename grammar -> filename_regex; spaced-section-dir fail hooks -> dir_absent; per-section README presence -> for_each_dir + nested file_exists; check-autoconf-regen grep markers -> file_content_matches x4; .gitattributes `generated` markers resolve -> registry_paths_resolve (entries_are_globs); .editorconfig hygiene -> tooling/editorconfig bundle; .gitattributes dos/lf split -> line_endings x2; remove-tabs -> indent_style; Trojan-Source -> no_bidi_controls; autotools/SBOM/stable-abi/Doc presence -> file_exists; stable_abi.toml well-formedness -> toml_path_matches; actionlint/zizmor/sphinx-lint/check-jsonschema -> command; AND the formerly-flagged patchcheck.py reported_news() 'PR must add a NEWS.d blurb' -> changeset_requires_path (NOW SHIPPED — was footer candidate #2).

**What alint improves.** Replaces patchcheck.py's git-diff probes and the scattered build.yml steps with declarative equivalents that assert the STRONGER tree-state invariant directly (command_idempotent asserts the working tree didn't move, vs patchcheck.py merely asking 'did you regenerate?'). Collapses 9 ruff-check + 5 ruff-format + 1 black pre-commit hooks, 4 autoconf grep steps, the regen freshness gate, and the .gitattributes generated-marker reverse-check into one reviewable file with named ids/messages. Crucially, changeset_requires_path now natively expresses the NEWS.d 'every PR adds a blurb' rule that the config (authored against v0.11) catalogued as a NEW-KIND CANDIDATE — alint can now do declaratively (with_changed gating for doc-only PRs) what the repo does via a bespoke 200-line patchcheck.py reimplementing diff parsing.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** TWO survive critical reproduction. (1) ARGUMENT CLINIC SELF-CHECKSUM — every clinic block embeds output=<hex>/input=<hex> that is a checksum of a SLICE OF ITS OWN FILE, computed by clinic.py's bespoke (non-sha256/512) algorithm, with the digest living in the same file. I checked file_graph `fresh` (the closest idiom): its FreshSpec REQUIRES `derive_target` edges (a name-template from a source to a DIFFERENT generated file) + a `marker` regex capturing a STANDARD `hash` (sha256/512 Algorithm). It models source!=target, standard-algorithm, cross-file freshness — NOT an intra-file, self-referential, tool-specific-algorithm checksum. pair_hash likewise assumes source!=target + standard hash. So clinic checksum integrity in isolation genuinely cannot be expressed (it IS partially covered transitively by cpython-regen-all-fresh, since regen-all->clinic->clean-tree catches a STALE checksum, just not a hand-corrupted one). This is the real new-kind candidate (an `embedded_checksum`/`self_checksum` kind with block delimiters + captured-digest group + hashed-region selector + pluggable algorithm). (2) CODEOWNERS column-31 alignment — confirmed no fixed-column-alignment kind exists (grep for column/align hits only unrelated rules); low value, but genuinely inexpressible.

**Abstraction ideas.** `embedded_checksum`/`self_checksum` kind (the clinic case): block start/end regex + captured digest group + a region-to-hash selector + pluggable/named algorithm — would also serve any tool that stamps a self-hash into its own output (some codegen, lockfile-self-digests). A fixed-`column_alignment` kind (delimiter at column N) for CODEOWNERS/tab-table files is low-value but the only thing covering candidate #3. Beyond gaps: a bundled `cpython`-style mega-regen ruleset is too repo-specific, but a `codegen-freshness` bundled snippet (command_idempotent over a declared regen target + the matching presence checks) would package the dominant pattern. Note the config's footer candidate #2 (changeset_requires_path) is now SHIPPED — the design doc's own most-requested predicate has been delivered.

*Notes.* This config was explicitly written against v0.11 and pre-dates the v0.12 git-extract build; its footer NEW-KIND CANDIDATE #2 (changeset_requires_path for the NEWS.d blurb-per-PR rule) is NOW SHIPPED and should be reclassified from gap to A — the changeset_requires_path docstring literally cites cpython's Misc/NEWS.d/next/. Candidate #1 (clinic self-checksum) and #3 (column alignment) remain genuine. The cross_file_value_equals version rules and registry_paths_resolve generated-marker reverse-check are correct and represent real wins over patchcheck.py's git-diff probes.

### `python/mypy` — Python static type-checker (mypyc-compiled C-extension build), tox + pre-commit + pytest matrix, community OSS

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface. Addressable surface is itself tiny: 3 hygiene hooks + editorconfig + the dependency-coherence invariant are all now natively expressible (the last only after set_equals). The single residual is bad-pr-link (one new kind). The overwhelming MAJORITY of mypy's total enforcement (pytest matrix, self-check, black/ruff/codespell, mypy_primer, docs build, error-code completeness) is correctly NON-GOAL execution.

**Enforcement surface.** mypy's enforced surface is HEAVILY execution-dominated, with a thin declarative shell. CI (.github/workflows + tox.ini): (1) the pytest matrix — py3.10-3.15 x {linux,win,mac} x mypyc-compiled-vs-interpreted x 32-bit — pure execution; (2) SELF type-check (running mypy on its own source) = AST/type execution; (3) black + ruff (format/lint) = AST; (4) codespell (spell) = NLP; (5) actionlint + shellcheck + zizmor (workflow/shell/security) = execution; (6) check-jsonschema against workflow files; (7) Sphinx `-W` docs build + an error-code-documentation completeness gate (every registered error code must have a docs page) = custom AST/docs introspection; (8) mypy_primer regression diff (run new mypy over a corpus, diff results) = execution; (9) misc/test-stubgenc.sh: run stubgen then `git diff --exit-code` = codegen-freshness. PRE-COMMIT (.pre-commit-config.yaml): trailing-whitespace + end-of-file-fixer + check-yaml (hygiene, declarative), plus a LOCAL pygrep `bad-pr-link` hook that fires when a CHANGELOG entry `[N](.../pull/M)` has N != M (the display number must equal the /pull/ URL number) — implemented with a regex BACKREFERENCE + negative look-ahead. The .pre-commit excludes vendored trees (mypy/typeshed, mypyc/external, misc/typeshed_patches). Cross-file coherence: three 'NOTE: keep in sync' headers pin overlapping dependency lists across pyproject.toml [project.dependencies], mypy-requirements.txt, build-requirements.txt. ABSENT categories: one root MIT LICENSE (no per-file headers); pins via test-requirements.txt + a hashless mypy-requirements.txt but NO committed lockfile (python bundle's python-has-lockfile fires, expected); no CODEOWNERS / dependabot / submodules (typeshed is vendored).

**What alint replaces.** trailing-whitespace pre-commit hook -> no_trailing_whitespace (config A1, with the vendored-tree exclude mirrored); end-of-file-fixer -> final_newline (A2); check-yaml well-formedness -> json_schema_passes / yaml parse (bundled ci/github-actions); the .editorconfig trim_trailing_whitespace+insert_final_newline contract -> bundled tooling/editorconfig honoured declaratively; the three 'keep in sync' dependency-list headers -> NOW cross_file (relation: set_equals) over the dependency lines of pyproject.toml + mypy-requirements.txt + build-requirements.txt (see alint_improves — the config's single-scalar cross_file_value_equals witness is superseded); 'docs/error-code pages exist' coverage of the registered error-code set is partly approximable via registry_paths_resolve if the code list is enumerable from a file, though the completeness direction is AST (non-goal).

**What alint improves.** REQUIREMENT-SET COHERENCE: the config (A4/C1) only witnesses ONE pinned constraint (the typing_extensions floor) via cross_file_value_equals and explicitly flags 'full SET equality is the C-tuning gap'. That gap is now DISSOLVED by the shipped cross_file relation: set_equals — extract: { lines: {comment_prefix: '#'} } (or a regex capturing each `pkg<op>ver`) from each of the three files and assert set equality. This turns a hand-waved 'keep in sync' comment + a partial witness into a real, reviewable, whole-set invariant the repo CANNOT currently enforce at all (the NOTE headers are honor-system). Also the stubgenc codegen-freshness check (run stubgen, git diff) is expressible declaratively via generated_file_fresh in MUTATING (outputs:) mode rather than the bash+git-diff script — schema-validated, restores the tree, no toolchain glue. Consolidates the 3 pre-commit hygiene hooks + editorconfig into one declarative file.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE, confirmed by reproduction: the `bad-pr-link` hook (B1) — 'two captured groups on the SAME LINE must be EQUAL' (CHANGELOG display number == /pull/ URL number). I confirmed alint's regex engine is the Rust `regex` crate (RE2-style, Cargo pin `regex = "1"` across all crates): NO backreferences, NO look-around. file_content_forbidden / file_content_matches cannot express intra-line cross-capture equality `(?P<a>\d+)...(?!(?P=a))\d+`. I tried (a) a single forbidding regex (needs a backref — rejected), (b) two cross_file rules (cross_file compares values ACROSS files, not two captures within one line of one file), (c) file_graph derive_target (operates on filenames, not in-line numeric equality). None reach it. NEW-KIND candidate: a `line_captures_equal` / intra-line predicate (regex with two named groups; assert group A == group B per match). Source: .pre-commit-config.yaml bad-pr-link. The error-code-documentation COMPLETENESS gate (every registered error code introspected from mypy's source has a docs page) is AST-introspective on the source = NON-GOAL, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) `line_captures_equal` (or a `relation: equal` mode on a per-line predicate kind): a regex with >=2 capture groups, assert capt[i]==capt[j] (or != ) per match — covers the whole class of 'these two tokens on one line must agree' linters (mypy's PR-link; also common in CHANGELOG/version/anchor checks). This is the one real new primitive mypy demands. (2) A `requirements_consistent` bundled preset / composition macro: given N requirement-ish files, assert their dependency SETS (parsed to name+specifier) agree — packages the set_equals idiom + a lines/regex extractor so adopters don't hand-roll it (mypy's exact 'keep in sync' need; recurs across Python repos).

*Notes.* Re-examination flips the config's headline 'C-tuning gap' (requirement-SET equality) to REPLACEABLE via the now-shipped cross_file set_equals — the config predates that capability. Only bad-pr-link survives as a genuine gap. python-has-lockfile firing is expected/correct (no lockfile by design).

### `pytorch/pytorch` — Massive polyglot C++/CUDA/Python ML framework monorepo; lintrunner (58 linters) + CMake build + extensive CI codegen; corporate (Meta) + community OSS

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — all hygiene, all 7 forbidden-content greps, filenames, CMAKE coherence, AND the generated-workflow codegen-freshness (now native). The total suite is mostly execution/AST (the 58 linters + tests), so as a share of EVERYTHING pytorch enforces it's smaller (~25-30%), but every addressable item is covered.

**Enforcement surface.** Enormous, dominated by .lintrunner.toml (58 linters) + .github/workflows/lint.yml. EXECUTION (D): tool linters flake8/ruff/pyfmt/mypy/pyrefly/clang-format/clang-tidy/codespell/shellcheck/actionlint/cmakelint; the test+build jobs; lint-bc (BC/API linter); OSSF scorecard; link/xref checks; and the AST-shaped bespoke validators set_linter, import_linter, docstring_linter, check_kernel_launches, nativefunctions round-trip, gb_registry, stable-shims. ADDRESSABLE / declarative subset is large for a monorepo: (1) lintrunner TABS/SPACES (no hard tabs except Makefiles/.gitattributes/.gitmodules; no trailing whitespace) repo-wide with third_party/contrib/svg/patch excludes; (2) lint.yml quick-checks — no U+00A0 NBSP, no versionless `#!.../python` shebangs (must be python3), cross-OS-safe filenames (no <>:"|?* and no trailing space/dot); (3) grep-based forbidden-content lintrunner linters: INCLUDE (#include <...> not "..." in C/C++), CUBINCLUDE (no direct <cub/...>), RAWCUDA (no raw cudaStreamSynchronize — use c10 API), META_NO_CREATE_UNBACKED (no create_unbacked* in torch/_meta_registrations.py), SYMPY_MINMAX (no sympy.Min/Max in torch/**), COPYRIGHT (no 'Confidential and proprietary' marker), PYPIDEP (no unpinned `pip install` under .github/ shell); (4) CMAKE_MINIMUM_REQUIRED — every cmake_minimum_required(VERSION x) must equal the root CMakeLists.txt's; (5) workflow-checks codegen-freshness — .github/workflows/generated-* are produced by .github/scripts/generate_ci_workflows.py (writes ~30 files IN PLACE, prints only paths) and `git diff --exit-code` must be clean; also TOC + doc-redirect generators. Ships .editorconfig + .lintrunner.toml.

**What alint replaces.** TABS/SPACES -> no_trailing_whitespace + file_content_forbidden pattern '\t' (with the documented excludes); NBSP -> file_content_forbidden U+00A0; versionless-python-shebang -> file_content_forbidden '(?m)^#!.*python$' (or file_shebang / executable_has_shebang for the metadata arm); cross-OS-safe filenames -> filename_regex '^[^<>:"|?*]*[^<>:"|?* .]$'; INCLUDE / CUBINCLUDE / RAWCUDA / META_NO_CREATE_UNBACKED / SYMPY_MINMAX / COPYRIGHT / PYPIDEP -> file_content_forbidden (path-scoped) — all 7 grep linters map 1:1; CMAKE_MINIMUM_REQUIRED coherence -> cross_file (relation: equals, regex extract) source=root CMakeLists.txt, targets=**/CMakeLists.txt; .editorconfig/.lintrunner.toml presence -> file_exists.

**What alint improves.** (1) The generated-* workflow codegen-freshness check: the config (pt-generated-workflows-fresh) used command_idempotent and flagged a 'new-kind gap: a multi-file generate-then-tree-clean primitive', concluding generated_file_fresh only supported single-STDOUT. THAT GAP IS NOW DISSOLVED — generated_file_fresh shipped a MUTATING (outputs:) mode that snapshots the outputs glob, runs the in-place generator, diffs, reports each stale file, and RESTORES the snapshot (exactly `make gen && git diff --exit-code` for ~30 files). So: kind: generated_file_fresh, command: ['.github/scripts/generate_ci_workflows.py'], outputs: '.github/workflows/generated-*' — native, schema-validated, leaves the tree pure, no report_git_status.sh glue. Same applies to the TOC / doc-redirect generators. (2) The forbidden-API greps run through lintrunner's per-file machinery; alint consolidates all 7 + the hygiene + filename rules into ONE declarative file with co-located excludes (vs 58 scattered .lintrunner.toml linter blocks). (3) RAWCUDA-style 'use the c10 wrapper not the raw call' and CUBINCLUDE are really layering/import firewalls — import_gate (C++ has no preset but a custom import_pattern works for #include) can express CUBINCLUDE more precisely than a bare grep, and import_gate's go/python presets cover any Python import-firewall linters in the suite. (4) file_graph could express nativefunctions/registry edge invariants at the FILE-reference level where the check is path resolution rather than AST.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** NONE that survive reproduction. The previously-claimed multi-file codegen gap is gone (mutating generated_file_fresh). The PYPIDEP regex is a plain forbidding pattern (no backref) — expressible. The AST-shaped validators (set_linter, import_linter as a true Python-import-graph AST tool, docstring_linter, check_kernel_launches, nativefunctions round-trip, gb_registry, stable-shims, lint-bc) are SEMANTIC/AST analysis = NON-GOAL (orchestrate via command, do not reimplement). I considered whether check_kernel_launches ('every cudaLaunchKernel is followed by C10_CUDA_KERNEL_LAUNCH_CHECK') is a per-file content predicate — it is sequence/AST-aware (needs to pair a launch with a following guard across statements), beyond a single forbidding regex, so it stays NON-GOAL. No new primitive demanded.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled 'pytorch-cpp-hygiene' style ruleset is over-specific, but the GENERAL primitive worth noting: a `forbidden_call_requires_guard` per-file kind ('pattern A on a line must be followed within N lines by pattern B') would natively absorb check_kernel_launches and many 'acquire-then-release' lint idioms that currently need an AST tool — a tractable, non-AST sequence predicate. (2) A C/C++ `language: cpp` import_gate preset (matching #include <...> and "...") would make INCLUDE/CUBINCLUDE first-class rather than file_content_forbidden greps. Both are grounded in real pytorch linters; (1) is the more broadly useful new abstraction.

*Notes.* Headline flip: the config's stated 'new-kind gap' (multi-file generate-then-clean) is REPLACEABLE today via generated_file_fresh outputs:/mutating mode — the config predates the v0.12 build. pt-generated-workflows-fresh should be rewritten from command_idempotent to generated_file_fresh.

### `quarkusio/quarkus` — Very large Maven multi-module JVM (Java) framework monorepo (hundreds of modules under extensions/, integration-tests/, core/, bom/, tcks/, independent-projects/); Red Hat / vendor governance; DCO-gated

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — all structure/governance/.gitattributes/deny-list-presence assertions PLUS DCO (git_commit_signed_off, which the config missed) are native; the residual addressable item is native-tests.json comma-split (one new option). As a share of ALL quarkus enforcement it's much smaller — the bulk (formatter, impsort, forbidden-apis, the four custom enforcer dep-graph rules, JUnit/TCKs, Vale, doc-metadata, xref) is correctly NON-GOAL Maven/AST execution.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforcement is Maven-plugin + tool dominated (D): net.revelc formatter-maven-plugin + impsort (code style/import order = AST); forbidden-apis plugin against .forbiddenapis/banned-signatures (bytecode signature AST); maven-enforcer-plugin — dependencyConvergence + banned-dependencies + the home-grown rules in independent-projects/enforcer-rules (BansRuntimeDependency, RequiresMinimalDeploymentDependency, DependencyAlignmentRule — all operate on the RESOLVED Maven dependency graph); the JUnit suite (JVM + native + virtual-threads + TCKs); Vale prose lint + a YamlMetadataGenerator doc-metadata validator (custom Java) + CheckCrossReferences (asciidoc xref/anchor resolution); OWASP dep-check / CodeQL / SonarCloud. DECLARATIVE / state-readable subset (what's addressable): Maven wrapper committed (./mvnw + mvnw.cmd — CONTRIBUTING mandates ./mvnw); root aggregator pom.xml + build-parent/pom.xml (carries the shared enforcer/formatter/impsort config); the independent-projects/enforcer-rules module dir; governance files CONTRIBUTING.md, ADOPTERS.md, dco.txt; .gitattributes LF pins for .mvn/jvm.config and mvnw (so ./mvnw works in git-bash on Windows — CRLF would break it); the deny-list FILES (.forbiddenapis/banned-signatures-common.txt, the banned-dependencies.xml) must be present. CI<->tree resolution: .github/native-tests.json .include[].test-modules must reference real Maven modules under integration-tests/ (verify-tests-json.sh) — but values are COMMA-SEPARATED module lists inside each JSON string. DCO sign-off is REQUIRED on every commit (a DCO bot / check).

**What alint replaces.** ./mvnw + mvnw.cmd committed -> file_exists; root pom.xml + build-parent/pom.xml + enforcer-rules module -> file_exists / dir_exists; CONTRIBUTING/ADOPTERS/dco.txt presence -> file_exists (oss-baseline covers CONTRIBUTING); .gitattributes LF pins for .mvn/jvm.config + mvnw -> file_content_matches (both already in the config); deny-list files present -> file_exists; DCO sign-off -> git_commit_signed_off (NATIVE — the config did NOT use it; this is a real enforced gate that maps 1:1 and was missed); GitHub-Actions hardening -> bundled ci/github-actions.

**What alint improves.** (1) DCO: quarkus enforces Signed-off-by on every commit via a bot; git_commit_signed_off expresses this declaratively in-repo (lower-friction local pre-push than the server bot, and reviewable). The config only asserts dco.txt EXISTS — it should add git_commit_signed_off, which the repo genuinely gates on. (2) The forbidden-apis / banned-dependencies enforcement is AST/dep-graph (D), but alint can do MORE than 'the deny-list file exists' (R3 value-add the config settled for): it can assert the banned-dependencies.xml is well-formed and that its referenced module/signature paths resolve (xml_path_* / registry_paths_resolve), catching a malformed policy the Maven plugin would only surface at build time. (3) Consolidates ~8 structure assertions + 2 .gitattributes invariants + DCO into one reviewable file vs the spread across pom config, CONTRIBUTING prose, and the DCO bot. (4) build-parent carries shared config that sub-poms inherit; cross_file (relation: equals, xml extract) could assert a sub-pom does NOT override a pinned parent property where uniformity is required — an invariant the enforcer expresses only via dependencyConvergence at resolve time.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE, re-examined and CONFIRMED: native-tests.json .include[*]['test-modules'] resolution. The values are COMMA-SEPARATED module lists inside a single JSON string (e.g. 'jpa-h2, jpa-h2-embedded, hibernate-orm-tenancy/datasource'). registry_paths_resolve still has NO split:/separator: (verified in registry_paths_resolve.rs — Options has base/expect/orphans, no split), so each string resolves as ONE bogus path (25/26 rows fail). REPRODUCE-FIRST attempts: (a) cross_file relation: resolves with a structured json extract — same per-element granularity, the comma-joined string is one value, fails identically; (b) a `regex` extract (which DOES multi-match via captures_iter, group 1 per match) over the whole native-tests.json to tokenize each module — but RE2 (the regex crate) has no \G/look-behind to constrain matches to ONLY the test-modules string values, so a token regex `[a-z][a-z0-9/_-]*` also captures 'include', 'config', 'runner', category names, os values = massive false positives; there is no way to anchor 'every token between commas, but only inside test-modules strings'; (c) dashed key needs bracket notation $.include[*]['test-modules'] (works) — orthogonal to the split problem. None reach it. NEW-KIND candidate: a split:/separator: on registry_paths_resolve (and/or cross_file resolves) that splits each extracted value on a delimiter before resolving. The CheckCrossReferences asciidoc xref/anchor resolution is a doc-graph; the FILE-existence arm could be markdown_paths_resolve-like, but anchor-WITHIN-document resolution is content-AST = NON-GOAL. forbidden-apis / enforcer dep-graph rules are AST/resolved-graph = NON-GOAL.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) THE concrete demand: a `split:` (or `separator:`) option on registry_paths_resolve and cross_file's resolves relation — split each extracted entry on a delimiter, then resolve each token. Tiny, mechanical, and exactly quarkus's verify-tests-json.sh shape; recurs anywhere a manifest packs multiple paths per field (space/comma/colon-separated). This is the single highest-value primitive quarkus motivates. (2) A 'maven-monorepo' composition idiom: assert every module dir under a glob has a pom.xml whose <artifactId> matches its dir (for_each_dir + nested xml_path_equals equals: '{basename}') — quarkus's hundreds of modules make this a natural value-add the config didn't reach for. (3) A bundled DCO/sign-off + governance-files ruleset (git_commit_signed_off + CONTRIBUTING/dco.txt presence) — quarkus, apache/governance, and many vendor repos share this exact shape.

*Notes.* Two re-examination outcomes: (a) the config's headline gap (comma-separated test-modules) SURVIVES reproduction as genuine — I confirmed no split: exists and the regex-extract workaround is defeated by RE2's lack of anchored iteration; (b) the config UNDER-claims — git_commit_signed_off natively covers quarkus's DCO gate and was omitted (should be REPLACEABLE, not absent).
