### `rails/rails` — Ruby framework monorepo — 12 gems (11 framework + the `rails` meta-gem) on bundler+rake, plus a 3-package npm sub-workspace (actioncable/actiontext/activestorage)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — the version invariant, per-gem structure, frozen_string_literal, hygiene, CHANGELOG shape, and config presence are all natively expressed. The dominant ENFORCED surface (test matrix, ~100 rubocop cops, eslint, shellcheck, rail_inspector AST) is correctly execution/D and out of scope. Of the repo's TOTAL enforcement, the addressable slice is small (Buildkite-execution-dominated); within it, near-total.

**Enforcement surface.** EXECUTION (category D, off-repo): the headline test + rubocop gate runs on Buildkite (pipeline defined OUTSIDE the tree), so the full enforced execution surface is largely invisible to in-repo CI files. (1) Tests: `rake test` across activerecord's 5 DB adapters (sqlite3/mysql2/trilogy/postgresql + others), Minitest, isolated + parallel. (2) RuboCop ~100 cops across Style/Layout/Performance/Minitest/Packaging departments (.rubocop.yml + per-gem overrides) — AST. (3) eslint (semi/quotes/import-order) on the JS packages via eslint.config.mjs. (4) shellcheck on .devcontainer/*.sh. (5) rail_inspector: a Prism-AST tool with three checks — `changelogs` (CHANGELOG entry grammar: '*'+3-spaces header, 4-space body indent, author-presence), `configuration` (a doc<->code coherence check that every `config.x` default in configuring.md matches the framework default), and `requires` (an import-firewall: which files may `require` which). GitHub Actions in-tree are only the bot/devcontainer/releaser/tool jobs. NON-EXECUTION addressable surface (what alint targets): (A) the single-source-of-truth VERSION invariant — root `RAILS_VERSION` is canonical; every gem's `*/lib/*/gem_version.rb` MAJOR/MINOR/TINY/PRE constants and every JS `package.json` `version` must agree (the releaser's `update_versions` rake task writes them; alint asserts the result). (B) per-gem structure (each ships CHANGELOG.md + MIT-LICENSE + <name>.gemspec). (C) the `# frozen_string_literal: true` magic-comment convention (AGENTS.md + RuboCop Style/FrozenStringLiteralComment). (D) Ruby-source hygiene (final newline, no trailing whitespace — RuboCop Layout/TrailingEmptyLines + Layout/TrailingWhitespace). (E) CHANGELOG entry-header shape. (F) policy-config presence (.rubocop.yml, eslint.config.mjs, CONTRIBUTING.md, AGENTS.md).

**What alint replaces.** VERSION single-source-of-truth (RAILS_VERSION anchor + gem_version.rb MAJOR/MINOR/TINY/PRE parity + npm package.json core parity) -> cross_file / cross_file_value_equals (one rule per version component, regex-extract); per-gem CHANGELOG/MIT-LICENSE/gemspec presence -> for_each_dir + nested file_exists ({path}/CHANGELOG.md, {path}/{basename}.gemspec); frozen_string_literal magic comment -> file_header (pattern within lines:3); Ruby-source no-trailing-whitespace + final-newline -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline (with fixers); CHANGELOG header shape -> file_content_matches (\A(\*   \S|## Rails)); .rubocop.yml/eslint.config.mjs/CONTRIBUTING.md/AGENTS.md presence -> file_exists root_only

**What alint improves.** One declarative file replaces what is otherwise a releaser rake task (`update_versions`) plus the implicit trust that nobody hand-edits a gem_version.rb out of sync — alint makes the cross-file VERSION invariant a CONTINUOUSLY-checkable gate (gem<->npm<->root) that runs without the Ruby toolchain or Buildkite, on every PR, in milliseconds. The npm-vs-gem-vs-root coherence is genuinely cross-workspace (Ruby gems AND a node sub-workspace from ONE source of truth) — a single rail_inspector/rake task could express the Ruby half but not the JS half coherently; alint does both in the same model. The per-gem structure sweep (for_each_dir over the 12 named gems) is more robust than a hardcoded list in a rake task because the path template ({basename}.gemspec) ties the gemspec name to the directory name. Improvement available not yet in the config: the npm package.json version is checked by REGEX extract ('"version":\s*"...'); switching the npm targets to cross_file json extract: {json: "$.version"} (or json_path_equals) would be parse-correct rather than line-regex-fragile.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none in the bespoke addressable surface. The one candidate — 'compose MAJOR.MINOR.TINY.PRE from the four gem_version.rb constants into a single 8.2.0.alpha string and compare the whole thing in ONE rule' — is NOT a genuine gap on reproduction: crate::extract's Extract::Regex returns capture-group-1 only (no \1.\2 template to compose), so a single composing rule is unsupported, BUT the capability brief explicitly sanctions the per-component idiom ('multi-component version compose -> one cross_file rule per component + a var'), which is EXACTLY what the config already does (rails-gem-version-major/minor/tiny/pre-matches-root). One logical invariant expressed as four rules is the intended decomposition, not a workaround for a missing feature — the prior 'multi-capture-compose' gap cluster was already adjudicated workable. rail_inspector's `configuration` (configuring.md default == framework code default) and `requires` (Ruby import-firewall) are real file<->code coherence intents but are enforced via Prism AST over Ruby source, so they are execution non-goals (D), not alint gaps — alint's import_gate is a per-file textual import firewall and could approximate `requires` on the *textual* `require` lines, but the rail_inspector check resolves Ruby constant/autoload semantics, which is AST.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A 'version_components' convenience on cross_file: declare source + targets once with a list of named component extracts ([major, minor, tiny, pre]) and have alint fan out to N parity checks under one id — pure sugar over the existing per-component idiom this repo demonstrates 4x, would collapse the 4 rails-gem-version-* rules into one block and dedupe the message boilerplate. (2) A bundled `ruby@v1` ecosystem ruleset (gated on a Gemfile/*.gemspec fact) mirroring go/rust/java/dotnet: gemspec-present + Gemfile.lock-tracked + frozen_string_literal header preset + MIT-LICENSE — rails, rubocop, and ruby/ruby in this very batch would all extend it. (3) A 'monorepo member manifest' axis: 'every directory in this set must each ship {CHANGELOG.md, LICENSE, <name>.gemspec}' as one rule rather than three parallel for_each_dir blocks differing only in the nested file_exists path.

*Notes.* Config is well-calibrated and skeptical-already: it pre-flags the version-compose limitation but lands on the correct per-component idiom. The only forward motion is cosmetic (json extract for npm; a version_components sugar). The 'mid-path glob' shape `*/lib/*/gem_version.rb` is the proven-firing structured glob form (the brief notes bare-string mid-path globs were a phantom no-op that turned out workable).

---

### `redis/redis` — C systems/in-memory datastore — make-based build, single-vendor (Redis Ltd.), tri-licensed (RSALv2/SSPLv1/AGPLv3)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE surface — which is essentially ONE check (commands.def freshness), now fully native after the mutating-mode ship, plus the value-add structural assertions. But redis's overall enforcement is ~all execution (compile/test/sanitize/static-analysis/NLP): so from the repo's TOTAL gate surface this is '~0% beyond the one codegen gate is addressable — the rest is correctly NON-GOAL', and the headline is that the single addressable gate flipped from a shell-wrap to native.

**Enforcement surface.** Almost the entire enforced surface is COMPILE + TEST + SANITIZER execution (category D): ci.yml builds `make -Werror` across gcc/clang/32bit/libc-malloc/debian-old/centos/macos/old-gcc-4.8 + `runtest` + ASan; daily.yml repeats across arm/jemalloc/fortify/io-threads/tls + valgrind x4 + asan/msan/ubsan/tsan + freebsd/alpine/centos + force-defrag + vectorset; coverity.yml (Coverity); codeql-analysis (CodeQL cpp); codecov.yml (lcov upload); spell-check.yml (codespell — NLP); reply-schemas-linter.yml (ajv compile of each src/commands/*.json reply_schema for JSON-Schema meta-validity); daily reply-schemas-validator (req-res-log-validator.py runtime check). The SINGLE non-execution enforced check is CODEGEN FRESHNESS: ci.yml runs `touch src/commands/ping.json; make commands.def; git diff` — the tracked, generated `src/commands.def` must match what utils/generate-command-code.py produces over all src/commands/*.json. The generator MUTATES the file in place (Python f.write), then CI greps `git diff` for emptiness. Supporting invariants the repo relies on but does not all gate: .gitattributes pins `src/commands.c merge=binary` (the documented rebuild-on-merge convention); src/commands.def carries a 'do not edit' generator banner; .codespell/.codespellrc + wordlist feed the spell job; src/commands/ must be populated for the reply-schema linter + codegen to have input. redis has NO enforced code-format / trailing-whitespace / EOF / SPDX-header / lockfile / cross-file-version gate (recorded absent).

**What alint replaces.** src/commands.def codegen freshness (the one non-execution enforced gate) -> generated_file_fresh in MUTATING/in-place mode (outputs: src/commands.def + command: [make,-s,commands.def]) — NOW NATIVE (the kind's own doc example IS redis's commands.def); the .gitattributes `src/commands.c merge=binary` invariant -> file_content_matches; the generator 'do not edit' banner -> file_content_matches; .codespell config-file presence -> file_exists; src/commands/ populated-dir guard -> file_exists (or dir_contains); governance docs (CONTRIBUTING/CODE_OF_CONDUCT/SECURITY/MANIFESTO/REDISCONTRIBUTIONS) -> file_exists; tri-license declaration -> file_content_matches

**What alint improves.** The codegen-freshness check is the whole story and alint now does it MATERIALLY better than the repo's `command` shell-wrap that the config currently uses: generated_file_fresh mutating mode snapshots src/commands.def, runs the generator, diffs, reports every file it would change, then RESTORES the snapshot — so `alint check` leaves the working tree pure (the raw `make commands.def && git diff` mutates the checkout and depends on a clean git state + a working make + the python toolchain being installed exactly as CI has it). It is the alint-native, tree-safe form of `make gen && git diff --exit-code`, schema-validated and declared in one reviewable line instead of an embedded `bash -c 'touch ... && make ... && test -z "$(git diff ...)"'` string. It also composes the supporting invariants (merge-driver pin, banner persistence, schema-dir population) that redis's CI does NOT separately gate — alint catches an emptied src/commands/ (which would SILENTLY pass redis's reply-schema linter) and a dropped merge=binary line, both real footguns.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's CENTRAL claimed gap — 'the generator mutates in place, so generated_file_fresh (stdout-only, v0.10) does NOT fit; this is the mutating-generator-freshness gap (B)' — is NOW CLOSED: generated_file_fresh shipped its `outputs:` mutating/in-place mode (@57aa0244), and redis's commands.def is the literal worked example in the rule's module doc. So redis-commands-def-fresh should be REWRITTEN from `kind: command` (execution escape hatch) to `kind: generated_file_fresh, outputs: src/commands.def, command: [make,-s,commands.def], normalize: final-newline, level: error`. Everything else redis enforces (compile-Werror, runtest, the four+ sanitizers, Coverity, CodeQL, codespell, ajv reply-schema compile, the python runtime validator) is genuine execution / static-analysis / NLP and a deliberate alint NON-GOAL (D) — alint can orchestrate them via command but does not and should not reimplement a C compiler, sanitizers, or a JSON-Schema meta-validator.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `ci/github-actions@v1`-adjacent or `make-codegen@v1` micro-ruleset packaging the recurring 'generated artefact is fresh + carries its do-not-edit banner + its merge-driver is pinned + its source dir is non-empty' quartet — redis demonstrates all four around ONE generated file; protobuf/grpc/k8s-style codegen repos repeat this shape. (2) generated_file_fresh could grow an optional `banner:` assertion (the regenerated/committed file must contain a marker) so the freshness check and the banner-persistence check (currently a separate file_content_matches) fold into one. (3) A 'reply-schema dir is non-empty AND every entry parses as JSON' helper — bridges file_exists (population) and json_schema_passes (validity) so an emptied or malformed src/commands/ can't silently pass.

*Notes.* This repo is the canonical demonstration that the v0.12 mutating-generator-freshness work paid off: the original study recorded it as the corpus's flagship B-gap and even named the future kind; that kind shipped and its example is THIS file. The config text is now stale on its central claim and the redis-commands-def-fresh rule should be migrated off `command` to `generated_file_fresh`.

---

### `rubocop/rubocop` — Ruby CLI/linter gem — the canonical Ruby static analyzer; bundler+rake, community governance, ships config/default.yml as data

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of the ADDRESSABLE (project_spec.rb structural + repo-convention) surface. The directory-shape, filename, presence, final-newline, version-coherence, and release-pairing invariants are native; the residue is the two genuine gaps (intra-file link resolution + per-line changelog grammar) and the narrow markerless-sort gap. The RSpec/AST/registry/network half of the enforcement is correctly NON-GOAL.

**Enforcement surface.** Three enforcement loci. (1) .github/workflows/*.yml CI: rubocop.yml runs `rake spec`/`ascii_spec`/`prism_spec` (RSpec across Ruby 2.7..head x ubuntu/windows/jruby — ascii_spec forces ASCII external encoding, prism_spec swaps in the Prism parser) + `documentation_syntax_check` (parses every cop's YARD @example as Ruby across Ruby25/26/27/Prism parser generations); linting.yml runs `internal_investigation` (rubocop lints its OWN source) + yamllint --strict on all YAML; spell_checking.yml runs codespell with a custom ignore-list; github_release.yml on a v* tag publishes relnotes/<tag>.md. (2) spec/project_spec.rb — a HAND-ROLLED structural validator suite (the richest alint-shaped surface): config/default.yml top-level cop keys sorted alphabetically; CHANGELOG.md has a final newline + link-definitions for all implicit [name][] links + the per-entry Markdown grammar (each '* ' entry has a leading [#N](url): link, body starts uppercase + ends in punctuation, NO [Fix #x] directive, trailing ([@user][]) signature with single-space-after-comma); changelog/ pending-entry inbox has NO subdirectories + every entry named {new,fix,change}_<desc>.md; config-key SET equals the registered-cop set; every SupportedStyles matches EnforcedStyle; a @safety YARD tag implies Safe:false; 'require all of lib with -w -W2 emits no warnings'. (3) tasks/*.rake (cut_release hand-syncs the gem version into installation.adoc + integration_*.adoc) + CONTRIBUTING.md/AGENTS.md conventions; tasks/references.rake HTTP-GETs every cop Reference URL.

**What alint replaces.** CHANGELOG.md final newline -> final_newline (with fixer); '## master (unreleased)' anchor header -> file_content_matches; changelog/ has no subdirectories -> dir_only_contains (allow: *.md/.gitkeep); changelog entry filename {new,fix,change}_*.md -> filename_regex; changelog/.gitkeep sentinel + spec/project_spec.rb + config/default.yml + CONTRIBUTING.md/.codespellrc/.editorconfig presence -> file_exists; relnotes/v<version>.md release-pairing for the current version -> file_exists; docs version coherence (installation.adoc ~> X.Y == integration_*.adoc rev: vX.Y.Z) -> cross_file_value_equals (regex extract); contributor-link-block presence -> file_content_matches ((?m)^\[@\S+\]: https?://); YAML/MD config trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace; .rubocop.yml self-lint plugin + gemspec MFA -> file_content_matches

**What alint improves.** alint consolidates the STATE-checkable half of a hand-rolled ~400-line RSpec validator (project_spec.rb) into a dozen declarative, schema-validated rules that run WITHOUT booting Ruby/RSpec/the gem's own load path — the changelog/ directory-shape rules (dir_only_contains, filename_regex) and the relnotes<->version release-pairing are exactly the kind of structural invariant that is brittle and slow as an RSpec example but trivial and fast declaratively. The docs version-coherence (cross_file_value_equals) is genuinely cross-file and the repo expresses it only as a release-time rake MUTATION (cut_release writes the docs), never as a continuously-enforced gate — alint turns the convention into a check, and correctly leaves README.md OUT (it is allowed to lag the cut, a real finding). The release-pairing (a v1.87.0 tag without relnotes/v1.87.0.md => empty GitHub Release body) is caught BEFORE the tag, not after.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** TWO survive reproduce-first, plus ONE narrowed-but-still-real. (1) GENUINE: implicit_link_resolves — every [name][] reference in a Markdown file must have a matching [name]: http... link-definition in the SAME file (project_spec 'has link definitions for all implicit links'). I tried file_graph no_dangling: its edges resolve to PATHS on disk (from_content extract -> resolve as a relative/repo-root path), not to other LINES within the same file; the 'target' here is a link-definition line, not a filesystem path, so file_graph cannot model it. No native kind expresses an INTRA-FILE reference-graph / orphan check. Config currently approximates with file_content_matches (the block merely EXISTS), which is strictly weaker. (2) GENUINE: per-entry changelog-line grammar ('changelog_entry_format' — ~15 sub-assertions over ONE line: leading [#N](url): link, uppercase-start, punctuation-end, no [Fix #x], trailing ([@user][]) with single-space-after-comma). file_content_matches can assert ONE regex per file but cannot express 'EVERY line matching P (an entry line) must ALSO match Q1..Qn'; there is no per-matching-line conjunction-of-predicates kind. High corpus value (Keep-a-Changelog/changelog-d/towncrier shops all hand-roll it). (3) NARROWED BUT STILL REAL: config/default.yml top-level cop keys sorted alphabetically. The config's note that ordered_block 'wants a pattern:/select: option' is now PARTLY addressed — ordered_block DID gain a `select:` regex (@442425d2) that skips non-matching lines, so the non-contiguous-header objection (cop headers at col 0 interleaved with indented option lines) is now expressible via select: '^[A-Za-z].*:$'. BUT the deeper objection holds: ordered_block REQUIRES non-empty start/end marker lines (build() rejects empty markers), and config/default.yml has NO keep-sorted markers — the WHOLE FILE's selected lines must be sorted. ordered_block has no markerless/whole-file mode. So this remains a real, now-narrow gap: 'sort all selected lines in the file, no start/end markers'. NON-GOALS (D), correctly excluded: the RSpec/ascii/prism matrix, internal_investigation (rubocop AST on itself), documentation_syntax_check (YARD-example-as-Ruby parse), yamllint --strict, codespell, the -w -W2 warnings run, references:verify (network HTTP), and the cop-REGISTRY invariants (config-key set == registered cops; SupportedStyles==EnforcedStyle; @safety=>Safe:false) — those resolve a CODE-DERIVED set (the loaded cop registry) against config, which needs the Ruby runtime/AST.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) implicit_link_resolves / intra_file_reference_graph — a new kind: within each file, every reference matching one regex (the [name][] use) must have a corresponding definition matching another regex with the same captured key ([name]: ) IN THE SAME FILE; an orphan/dangling check whose nodes are lines not paths. Generalizes file_graph's no_dangling/no_orphans from file->file to line->line, and serves any reference-style Markdown, BibTeX-like, or footnote format. (2) changelog_entry_format / lines_all_match — 'every line matching SELECT must also satisfy ALL of [pattern1..patternN]' (a per-matching-line conjunction), the missing dual of ordered_block's select:; closes the changelog-grammar gap generically (towncrier/changelog-d/Keep-a-Changelog). (3) ordered_block markerless/whole-file mode — make start/end OPTIONAL; when absent, the sortable scope is the whole file's selected lines. With the existing select: this would finally express rubocop's config/default.yml alphabetical-cop-key sort, plus any 'this entire data file must be sorted' convention. (4) A bundled `keep-a-changelog@v1` micro-ruleset (unreleased-header anchor + final-newline + entry-filename-prefix + no-subdirs inbox) — rubocop and many changelog-d shops would adopt it wholesale.

*Notes.* Config is the most carefully reasoned of the batch and explicitly carries a B-backlog (changelog_entry_format, implicit_link_resolves) plus a C-note on the cop-key sort. Two of those three are STILL valid after reproduce-first; the cop-key-sort C-note is now PARTIALLY stale (select: landed) but the markerless-whole-file objection keeps it alive. The version-coherence rule's deliberate exclusion of README.md (allowed to lag) is a genuine, well-documented finding, not an oversight.

---

### `ruby/ruby` — C language-runtime monorepo — MRI/CRuby; autoconf+make build; vendored subtrees (ccan, enc/onigmo); embeds Rust JITs (yjit + zjit) with their own Cargo workspaces

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the bespoke ADDRESSABLE surface (the auto-style hygiene trio + the configure.ac forbidden-substitution grep) plus the MSRV value-add. ruby/ruby's enforcement is otherwise overwhelmingly build+test execution (D), so from the repo's TOTAL gate surface the addressable slice is small; within it, complete.

**Enforcement surface.** EXECUTION-dominated (D): the build itself (autoconf ./configure + make across a large platform matrix) and the full test suite (make test/test-all/btest + bootstraptest + spec/ruby) are the primary gates, plus MJIT/YJIT/ZJIT-specific CI. The BESPOKE non-execution enforcement that ruby/ruby actually gates on, and that alint targets, is narrow and grep/hygiene-shaped: (A) tool/auto-style.rb — a hygiene pass gated on PRs via check_misc.yml's 'Check for code styles' and AUTO-FIXED on push via post_push.yml: it strips trailing whitespace, appends a final newline, and expands hard tabs to spaces on a CURATED C/H/Y/Ruby source set, EXCLUDING vendored + default-gem subtrees (the IGNORED_FILES / DIFFERENT_STYLE_FILES lists — e.g. io_buffer.c, scheduler.c keep their own style). (B) check_misc.yml grep gates, notably 'Check for bash specific substitution in configure.ac': `git grep -n '\${[A-Za-z_0-9]*/' -- configure.ac` must find NOTHING (configure.ac runs under /bin/sh, not bash, so bash-only ${var/...} parameter expansion is forbidden); check_misc also greps for other portability/format violations (e.g. NEWS file shape, no trailing spaces in specific files). Cross-cutting value-add (NOT a ruby gate): the Rust MSRV is shared — root Cargo.toml and zjit/Cargo.toml both pin rust-version 1.85.0 (and zjit-{ubuntu,macos}.yml pin the same); Cargo.lock is tracked because yjit/zjit are statically linked for reproducible builds. ruby/ruby does NOT gate cross-crate MSRV equality or most cross-file version coherence.

**What alint replaces.** tool/auto-style.rb trailing-whitespace strip on the curated C/H set -> no_trailing_whitespace (include internal/**/*.c|h, exclude ccan/**|enc/**; with fixer); auto-style final-newline append -> final_newline (with fixer); auto-style hard-tab->spaces expansion (net invariant: space indentation on the non-excluded set) -> indent_style (style: spaces, width: 4, exclude io_buffer.c/scheduler.c); check_misc.yml 'no bash ${var/...} in configure.ac' -> file_content_forbidden (pattern \$\{[A-Za-z_0-9]*/); Rust MSRV lockstep root<->zjit (value-add) -> cross_file_value_equals (toml extract $.package['rust-version']); Cargo.lock tracked -> already covered by bundled rust@v1 rust-cargo-lock-exists

**What alint improves.** alint replaces a Ruby script (tool/auto-style.rb) plus a couple of shell `git grep` gates buried in check_misc.yml with a handful of declarative, schema-validated rules that need no Ruby interpreter and run identically on a fresh clone or in CI. The configure.ac portability grep is exactly file_content_forbidden's wheelhouse: a single reviewable rule with a clear message ('configure.ac runs under /bin/sh; bash-only ${var/...} substitution is forbidden') versus an inline `git grep -n` in a YAML step whose intent is opaque. The MSRV-lockstep check is a genuine cross-file invariant (root Cargo.toml == zjit/Cargo.toml rust-version) that ruby/ruby does NOT gate at all — pure alint value-add via a structured toml extract that is parse-correct (handles the dashed key 'rust-version' via JSONPath bracket notation), not a brittle line regex. The curated include/exclude sets (mirroring IGNORED_FILES/DIFFERENT_STYLE_FILES) are expressed once in the config rather than maintained inside a Ruby constant.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none in ruby/ruby's BESPOKE addressable surface — the three auto-style hygiene behaviors (trailing-ws, final-newline, tab-expansion) and the configure.ac forbidden-substitution grep are all natively and faithfully expressed; the MSRV value-add is native. The only nuance is fidelity, not capability: tool/auto-style.rb's tab handling is CONDITIONAL ('only expand when the file has no existing tab indentation'), whereas indent_style enforces the net invariant (spaces on the non-excluded set) unconditionally — but that is a deliberate, documented modeling choice (the net invariant matches the post-auto-fix tree), not a missing feature. check_misc.yml's OTHER grep gates (NEWS-file shape, doc-format checks) are equally file_content_matches/forbidden-shaped where they are pure-text. The build + the entire test/spec/bootstraptest matrix and the YJIT/ZJIT compile/run are genuine execution NON-GOALS (D) — alint orchestrates via command but does not reimplement a C runtime test harness.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A reusable 'auto-style hygiene preset' bundling no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline + indent_style(spaces) over a shared include/exclude pair — ruby/ruby applies all three to the SAME curated C/H set with the SAME vendored exclusions; today that is three rules repeating the include/exclude block. A composition primitive that shares one `paths:` scope across a small ruleset of hygiene kinds would dedupe this exactly (and recurs in many C codebases that auto-format a curated subtree while excluding vendored deps). (2) A conditional indent_style mode ('expand tabs only when no existing tab indentation') to model tool/auto-style.rb's actual semantics precisely, for shops that want the auto-fixer's exact behavior rather than the net invariant. (3) A bundled `ruby@v1` ecosystem ruleset (shared with rails/rubocop in this batch): frozen_string_literal preset + gemspec/Gemfile.lock presence + the auto-style hygiene set — ruby/ruby would extend it for the hygiene half even though it is a C project that merely embeds Ruby tooling.

*Notes.* Config is tight and already reflects two v0.12 reproduce-first findings: (a) a bare-string mid-path glob (internal/**/*.c) was a phantom no-op for per-file rules, so it uses the proven structured two-entry include: form (53 hits on space-indent); (b) the dashed TOML key rust-version needs JSONPath bracket notation ($.package['rust-version']), which the MEMORY notes as a recurring gotcha. No stale gap claims — this config did not over-report.

---

### `rust-lang/rust` — Massive Rust compiler/stdlib monorepo (Cargo workspace, bespoke `src/tools/tidy` in-tree linter + dense CI shell/python gates)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) tidy surface — registry/sorted-block/edition/placement/filenames/known-bug/forbidden-content all native; remaining ~10% = the extdeps inline allowlist (genuine gap) + tidy-alphabetical multi-line-join. The repo's TOTAL enforcement is ~80% execution (D: rustfmt/clippy/test/miri/reuse/typos/compiletest), so against the whole surface alint addresses the structural slice only.

**Enforcement surface.** rust-lang/rust's enforcement is dominated by its bespoke in-tree `src/tools/tidy` linter (run via `./x test tidy` and CI) plus a wall of tool-execution gates. EXECUTION-ONLY (category D, non-goal): rustfmt, clippy (-Dwarnings on many crates), the full test/UI/compiletest suite + compiletest directive *semantics*, miri, rustdoc (-Dwarnings), `reuse`/SPDX license compliance, `typos` spellcheck, ruff (python tools), shellcheck, clang-format (LLVM), cargo-deny-style license+dependency allowlists, and error-code/feature-gate derivation computed from compiler source. ADDRESSABLE tidy checks (what the config encodes): (license-headers) tidy `style` requires license headers, but rust delegates that to `reuse`/REUSE.toml (D). (hygiene) tidy `style` bans committed `dbg!(`, bans `TODO` in the cranelift/gcc codegen backend trees (FIXME instead), bans the `borrowck_graphviz_postflow` debug attr in tests, `debug_artifacts`. (structure/layout) tidy `tests_placement` forbids `src/test` (tests must live under `tests/`); tidy `filenames` bans `:`/control-char filenames for Windows compat; tidy `edition` requires each crate Cargo.toml pin a known edition; tidy `deps`/`extdeps` walk every `[workspace].members` dir (must exist + contain Cargo.toml). (cross-file coherence) tidy `extdeps` restricts every `source = "..."` line in Cargo.lock to a 2-element hardcoded allowlist (crates.io-index + the pinned rust-lang/team git); tidy `triagebot` checks triagebot.toml `[autolabel.*].trigger_files` (and `[mentions.*]`/`[assign.owners]` table-key paths) resolve on disk. (sorted-blocks) tidy `alphabetical` enforces `# tidy-alphabetical-start/-end` fenced blocks stay sorted (numeric-aware version-sort + multi-line-join) in Cargo.toml, typos.toml and ~hundreds of source files. (known-bug) tidy `known_bug` requires every `tests/crashes/**/*.rs` carry a `//@ known-bug:` directive. Commit-discipline is light (no enforced commit-message format on the main repo). The config correctly scopes to the A-bucket and marks the rest D.

**What alint replaces.** tidy `deps`/`extdeps` workspace-member walk -> registry_paths_resolve (source: Cargo.toml, extract toml $.workspace.members[*], expect dir, must_contain Cargo.toml, exclude_query $.workspace.exclude[*]); tidy `alphabetical` sorted fences -> ordered_block (start/end markers, and NOW comparator: numeric for the version-sort case — see below); tidy `edition` -> toml_path_matches on **/Cargo.toml $.package.edition ^(2021|2024)$ if_present; tidy `tests_placement` -> dir_absent src/test; tidy `filenames` Windows ban -> no_illegal_windows_names; tidy `known_bug` -> file_content_matches on tests/crashes/**/*.rs '//@ known-bug: '; tidy `debug_artifacts` (borrowck_graphviz_postflow) + `style` dbg ban + `codegen` TODO ban -> file_content_forbidden with include/exclude scopes; tidy `triagebot` trigger_files -> registry_paths_resolve (toml $.autolabel[*].trigger_files[*]).

**What alint improves.** Consolidation + cross-clone honesty: tidy is ~10k lines of bespoke Rust that only runs inside a built checkout via `./x`; the alint config replaces a meaningful slice of it with one declarative, schema-validated, reviewable file that runs on a bare clone with no Rust toolchain (CI can gate the structural/hygiene invariants in seconds without building the compiler). registry_paths_resolve adds the REVERSE orphan check (orphans:) tidy's member-walk doesn't do natively. ordered_block's per-block start/end markers mirror tidy-alphabetical 1:1 and now degrade-gracefully via comparator: numeric. file_content_forbidden gives uniform include/exclude scoping where tidy hardcodes path skips in Rust.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap survives reproduce-first: tidy `extdeps` ALLOWLIST over Cargo.lock — every `source = "..."` line must be one of exactly 2 literal strings hardcoded in extdeps.rs (ALLOWED_SOURCES). I tried cross_file relation: subset (S=the extracted source-line set ⊆ T=allowlist): it works set-theoretically, BUT cross_file targets are strictly file-backed (TargetsSpec is Glob|List-of-files, each with an extract) — there is NO inline-literal-values target, and the rust repo has no allowlist FILE to point at (the 2 values live in compiler tool source). I tried file_content_forbidden with a negative-lookahead 'any source NOT in {…}': alint's regex engine (the `regex` crate) has no look-ahead, so the inverted-membership pattern can't be written. So 'extracted set ⊆ a small INLINE literal allowlist' is currently inexpressible. This is exactly what the planned value_set_membership kind targets. NOTE: the config's claimed C-gap on typos.toml version-sort (A10) is now STALE — ordered_block gained comparator: numeric (9 before 10), so the per-line numeric case is REPLACEABLE; only tidy's multi-line-join continuation remains beyond reach (minor). And the C1 triagebot table-keys-as-paths is NOT a hard gap — registry_paths_resolve accepts a regex: extract, so the quoted-key form `[mentions."src/..."]` is capturable with `regex: '^\[(?:mentions|assign\.owners)\."([^"]+)"\]'` then resolves; it is brittle for bare/dotted keys (a sharp edge), not inexpressible.

**Abstraction ideas.** 1) value_set_membership (or a cross_file relation that accepts targets: { values: [...] } INLINE literals): assert an extracted set ⊆ / == a small hardcoded allowlist — directly closes the extdeps gap and generalizes to dozens of 'enum of allowed registries/licenses/sources' checks across monorepos. 2) An ordered_block 'join continuation' option (multi-line entries joined before compare) to fully match tidy-alphabetical. 3) An extract mode 'object_keys'/'table_keys' (since RFC 9535 JSONPath only selects values, never property names) so registry_paths_resolve can treat TOML/JSON table KEYS as the path set robustly — retires the triagebot keys-as-paths sharp edge.

*Notes.* Config kinds all verified live (registry_paths_resolve/ordered_block/no_illegal_windows_names/toml_path_matches). Git rules here are working-tree content scans (file_content_forbidden), correctly — there is no `git log -G` history grep in alint, which matches what tidy does (scans the tree, not history). The config's A10 'version-sort C-gap' comment should be downgraded now that comparator: numeric exists.

### `scikit-learn/scikit-learn` — Python ML library (Cython extensions, meson-python build, NumFOCUS) with a pre-commit + bespoke `build_tools/linting.sh` enforcement stack

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE surface (hygiene + per-file headers + content firewalls + cross-file version coherence + metadata assertions all native). Against the TOTAL enforced surface it is lower (~30-40%) because ruff/mypy/cython-lint/pytest/sphinx dominate and are correct non-goals.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforcement runs through pre-commit hooks, `build_tools/linting.sh`, and CI (Azure/CircleCI/GH Actions). EXECUTION-ONLY (D, non-goal): ruff (lint + format, the dominant gate), mypy, cython-lint, black-historical/now-ruff-format, the pytest + doctest suite, sphinx doc build, towncrier changelog fragments, and meson build checks. ADDRESSABLE (what the config encodes): (hygiene) pre-commit trailing-whitespace + end-of-file-fixer repo-wide (excluding sklearn/externals + asv env) -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline. (license-headers) ruff CPY001 / flake8-copyright `notice-rgx` requires every non-excluded sklearn/**.py begin with the two-line `# Authors: The scikit-learn developers` + `# SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause` header. (import-firewalls) build_tools/linting.sh greps that forbid: the default doctest directives `# doctest: +ELLIPSIS|+NORMALIZE_WHITESPACE` (enabled by default in conftest, so redundant in source); and direct `from joblib import delayed|Parallel` (must use sklearn.utils.parallel) everywhere except parallel.py. (cross-file coherence, documented-but-script-light) the ruff version must agree across .pre-commit-config.yaml, sklearn/_min_dependencies.py and the dev-setup .rst docs (an in-file 'remember to update' comment is the only 'enforcement'); the scipy + cython floors must agree between _min_dependencies.py and pyproject.toml (in-file sync comments). (structure/metadata) pyproject declares license BSD-3-Clause and ruff flake8-tidy-imports ban-relative-imports=all. Commit-discipline: none enforced.

**What alint replaces.** pre-commit trailing-whitespace -> no_trailing_whitespace; end-of-file-fixer -> final_newline; ruff CPY001 copyright/SPDX header -> file_content_matches x2 (Authors line + SPDX line) on sklearn/**/*.py with the externals/tests/_build_utils excludes; linting.sh doctest-directive ban + joblib import ban -> file_content_forbidden (or import_gate for the joblib case — cleaner); ruff/scipy/cython version coherence -> cross_file_value_equals with regex extracts (one source -> N targets); license/ban-relative-imports assertions -> toml_path_equals on pyproject.toml.

**What alint improves.** Two clear wins. (1) The cross-file VERSION COHERENCE that scikit-learn only 'enforces' via a hopeful `# remember to also update _min_dependencies.py and the docs` COMMENT becomes a real, failing gate: cross_file_value_equals turns a soft convention into CI-blocking parity across _min_dependencies.py / .pre-commit-config.yaml / pyproject.toml — this is genuinely new coverage the repo lacks today. (2) The joblib import firewall is currently a brittle grep in linting.sh (`joblib import.+delayed`); import_gate expresses it as a proper per-file Python import firewall with an allow-list exception for parallel.py — lower false-positive rate (won't trip on the string in a comment/docstring the way a raw grep can) and self-documenting. Both run on a bare clone with no ruff/python toolchain.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None addressable-but-missing. Reproduce-first on the borderline items: the CPY001 header is a per-file 'must match' regex (file_content_matches) — fully native; the ruff `notice-rgx` is a single regex over the file head, which file_header could anchor even more precisely with (?m)^. The version-coherence checks are textbook cross_file_value_equals (single scalar, regex extract). The doctest-directive and joblib bans are content-forbidden/import_gate. Everything genuinely missing (ruff's AST lint rules, cython-lint, mypy, doctest execution, meson build correctness) is EXECUTION (category D) — a deliberate non-goal alint orchestrates via command/command_idempotent, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled 'python-scientific'/'numfocus' ruleset variant that pre-packs: the SPDX+Authors per-file header pair, the trailing-ws/final-newline hygiene with the conventional sklearn/externals + asv-env excludes, and a parameterized cross-file 'dependency-floor coherence' template (one declared min-version mirrored into pyproject build-requires + pre-commit rev). The repeated `extract: { regex: '...VERSION = "([^"]+)"' }` -> pyproject `>=` pattern is common enough across the scientific-Python ecosystem to deserve a named composition primitive (a 'pin mirrors floor' shorthand).

*Notes.* All config kinds verified live. The cross-file rules use the cross_file_value_equals alias (-> cross_file kind) with regex extract — the canonical single-source/single-target value-parity case, fully supported. Suggest upgrading skl-no-joblib-* from file_content_forbidden to import_gate (Python preset) for fewer false positives.

### `serde-rs/serde` — Rust serialization library — small multi-crate workspace (serde / serde_core / serde_derive / serde_derive_internals); validation is almost entirely toolchain-execution CI

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE surface — but that surface is TINY. Honestly this repo is ~95% execution (D); alint is VALUE-ADD-dominant here (the MSRV/version-lockstep coherence is real new coverage the repo lacks, but it's a thin sliver of serde's total CI). Net: ~5-10% of total enforcement is addressable, ~100% of that is replaceable.

**Enforcement surface.** serde's ENFORCED surface is overwhelmingly tool execution (category D, non-goal, per the study's ruling R2). ci.yml runs: cargo build/test across a toolchain matrix + feature powersets, clippy with -Dclippy::pedantic, miri, rustdoc with -Dwarnings, cargo-outdated, and a minimal-versions/`-Z minimal-versions` check; the derive crate gets its own pinned-toolchain (dtolnay/rust-toolchain@1.71.0) job and serde/serde_core a 1.56.0 job. None of that is alint-addressable — alint orchestrates but does not reimplement compilers/clippy/miri/rustdoc. The THIN ADDRESSABLE surface is cross-file VALUE coherence that the repo relies on convention/cargo to catch rather than a dedicated check: (cross-file coherence) the three published crates ship in LOCKSTEP at one version (serde/serde_core/serde_derive [package].version equal); the exact-pin deps (`serde_core = { version = "=1.0.228" }` in serde, and serde_core's cfg(any()) `serde_derive = "=…"`) must equal the depended crate's own version (cargo enforces at build; asserting it localizes the failure); the MSRV tiers must match their verifying CI job (serde/serde_core rust-version == the 1.56 build job; serde_derive rust-version == the 1.71 derive job toolchain). (license) dual MIT/Apache; both root LICENSE files present; license + edition coherent across crates. Commit-discipline: none enforced. There is NO custom in-repo linter/script tier here (unlike rust-lang/rust) — serde leans entirely on cargo + dtolnay's CI actions.

**What alint replaces.** published-crate version lockstep -> cross_file_value_equals (source serde/Cargo.toml toml $.package.version -> targets {serde_core,serde_derive}/Cargo.toml); exact-pin dep coherence -> cross_file_value_equals with a regex extract of the `=x.y.z` pin; MSRV<->CI coherence -> cross_file_value_equals (serde_derive rust-version vs a regex extract of dtolnay/rust-toolchain@(\d+\.\d+) from ci.yml); edition/license coherence across crates -> cross_file_value_equals (info); dual-license presence -> file_exists LICENSE-APACHE/LICENSE-MIT.

**What alint improves.** The headline win is making implicit, build-time-only invariants into explicit, fast, localized gates. The 'three crates share one version' rule is a maintainer CONVENTION that only blows up at publish time (or silently ships a skewed version if a bump is partial); cross_file_value_equals turns it into a reviewable rule that fails in <1s on a clone with no Rust toolchain. The MSRV<->CI coherence rule is genuinely novel coverage: serde verifies 1.56/1.71 by RUNNING those toolchains in CI, but nothing asserts the Cargo.toml rust-version pin and the CI job's pinned toolchain string actually AGREE — alint reads both files and diffs them, catching the classic 'bumped the job but forgot rust-version' (or vice-versa) drift the execution gate cannot see. cargo-outdated/minimal-versions remain D.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None. Reproduce-first: every addressable item here is a 1:1 or 1:N scalar value-parity check, which is exactly cross_file_value_equals' core competency; the regex extracts (the `=x.y.z` pin, the toolchain@x.y string) are within the regex-extract idiom; the version lockstep across {serde_core,serde_derive} uses the brace-glob target form, supported. Everything truly out of reach (the feature-powerset build matrix, clippy::pedantic, miri UB detection, rustdoc warnings, minimal-versions resolution) is EXECUTION — a correct, deliberate non-goal (alint shells out to them via command, it does not reimplement them).

**Abstraction ideas.** A small composition primitive for 'declared-MSRV must equal the CI job that verifies it' would be broadly reusable across Rust libs: a template pairing a manifest rust-version with the toolchain string in a named CI job/action (dtolnay/rust-toolchain@, actions-rs, rust-toolchain.toml). Also a 'workspace crates ship in lockstep' shorthand (one rule = all sibling [package].version equal, with an opt-in for synchronized non-version fields like edition/license) would collapse the 4 near-identical cross_file rules this config writes by hand into one declarative line — a recurring monorepo pattern.

*Notes.* Config kinds verified live (cross_file_value_equals alias -> cross_file). Good calibration example of an R2/R5 repo where the right alint posture is 'orchestrate the execution gates, add the coherence checks cargo/CI imply but never explicitly assert'. The config correctly excludes value-add from coverage_today's denominator.

### `signalapp/Signal-Android` — Large Kotlin/Java Android app — Gradle (Kotlin DSL) with a `build-logic` composite build, ~40 modules, single-vendor (Signal Messenger, LLC), AGPL-3.0-only; enforcement is one `./gradlew qa` orchestration

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% addressable beyond a single check — VALUE-ADD-dominant. Of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface, alint covers it (checkStopship is the only enforced addressable check, and it's native); but the repo's TOTAL enforcement is ~99% execution/AST/build (ktlint + Android lint + custom UAST detectors + tests + Gradle verify + reproducible builds), all correct non-goals. alint's role here is orchestrator + the NDK coherence gate the repo lacks.

**Enforcement surface.** Signal-Android's enforced surface is almost entirely tool/AST/build execution funneled through a single Gradle entrypoint `./gradlew qa` (run by Android CI). EXECUTION-ONLY (category D, non-goal): ktlintCheck across every module + build-logic (D, formatter/style); buildQa (build-logic :tools/:plugins unit tests + ktlint); app unit tests (testPlayProdReleaseUnitTest); Android lint (lintPlayProdRelease) = built-in lint + lint.xml severities + the in-tree CUSTOM UAST detectors in lintchecks/ (LogNotSignal, SystemOutPrintLn, VersionCode, RecipientIdDatabase, ThreadIdDatabase, StartForegroundService, AlertDialogBuilder, CardView, BlockingGet — all AST-level, category D); instrumentation compile + per-subproject test/lint. Outside qa: the lefthook pre-push `format` hook runs ./gradlew format then asserts a clean `git status --porcelain` (D, wraps ktlint); Gradle wrapper-validation action (checksum of gradle-wrapper.jar) (D); Gradle dependency verification via gradle/verification-metadata.xml (D, Gradle's own verify); docker.yml daily reproducible-build image builds (D); diffuse.yml APK size-diff PR comment (informational, not a gate). The ONE enforced check that is natively alint-addressable: checkStopship — a bespoke Gradle task wired into qa that walks .kt/.kts/.java/.xml (skipping build/ dirs without a `src` segment) and fails if any contains the literal `STOPSHIP` (Android lint also flags it via lint.xml StopShip=fatal — same behavior, one unit). Commit-discipline: none enforced beyond the format pre-push hook. Single-vendor, so no license-header-per-file gate; a root NOTICE + AGPL LICENSE + an aggregated third_party_licenses resource exist but are regenerated at release, not CI-gated.

**What alint replaces.** checkStopship (+ lint.xml StopShip=fatal) -> file_content_forbidden 'STOPSHIP' on **/*.{kt,kts,java,xml} with the build.gradle.kts/lint.xml/.idea/**/build/** excludes (the single enforced addressable check); root NOTICE / third_party_licenses / reproducible-build asset presence -> file_exists (value-add only — the repo does not gate these).

**What alint improves.** Marginal but real on the addressable sliver: checkStopship is ~20 lines of bespoke Gradle that only runs inside a configured Gradle build; file_content_forbidden expresses the identical scan declaratively and runs on a bare clone in milliseconds with no JDK/Gradle/Android SDK — useful as a fast pre-CI gate. The genuine value-add is the NDK-version coherence (below) which the repo does NOT enforce anywhere. alint cannot improve the ktlint/Android-lint/custom-detector tier — that is correctly orchestrated, not replaced.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** None addressable-but-missing. The honest story is that ~all of Signal's enforcement is category-D execution (ktlint formatting, Android lint's built-in + 9 custom UAST detectors that do real semantic AST analysis like 'use Log.tag not android.util.Log' / 'no raw RecipientId in DB queries', unit/instrumentation tests, Gradle wrapper+dependency verification, reproducible-build image builds). These are DELIBERATE non-goals — alint shells out to `./gradlew qa` via command, it does not reimplement a UAST detector or ktlint. Reproduce-first on the value-add NDK check: the NDK revision duplicated across gradle/libs.versions.toml, diffuse.yml NDK_VERSION, and the reproducible Dockerfile is a clean single-source -> 2-target value-parity case -> cross_file_value_equals with regex extracts; fully expressible (and currently UNGATED in the repo, so it's net-new coverage). No inexpressible-but-should item found.

**Abstraction ideas.** An 'android-gradle' bundled ruleset could pre-pack the few declarative invariants Android repos share: a STOPSHIP/NOSUBMIT forbidden-marker scan over .kt/.kts/.java/.xml with the conventional build/ + .idea/ excludes; presence of gradle-wrapper.jar + gradle-wrapper.properties + a pinned distributionSha256Sum (a structured-query check on gradle-wrapper.properties, which today the wrapper-validation ACTION does via checksum — partially alint-expressible as 'distributionSha256Sum key present'); and a 'version-catalog value mirrored into CI env/Dockerfile' coherence template (the NDK case generalizes to compileSdk/AGP/Kotlin versions duplicated across libs.versions.toml + workflow env). None of these are new KINDS — they're a bundled composition.

*Notes.* Config kind (file_content_forbidden, cross_file_value_equals, file_exists) all verified live. Good example of a 'mostly-D, AST-heavy' repo: the right framing is NOT a coverage gap but a correct non-goal boundary — alint runs `./gradlew qa` as a command step and adds the cross-file NDK coherence that Gradle/lint cannot see. e2e/YAML caution noted in the brief applies if any fixture embeds real APK/ZIP magic; not relevant to this config's text-only scans.
