### `spf13/cobra` — Go CLI-framework library (single-module, community/Apache governance)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable. cobra's only non-execution enforced check (the Apache header) is natively replaced; everything else is correctly category-D execution. Addressable surface is tiny but fully covered.

**Enforcement surface.** cobra's enforced surface is deliberately thin and almost entirely tool-execution. CI (.github/workflows) runs four gates: (1) license-headers — a `lic-headers` job runs `addlicense -c 'The Cobra Authors' -y '2013-2023' -l apache -ignore '.github/**' -check .`, i.e. every *.go file (under `.` and `doc/`) must open with the exact Cobra copyright line + the Apache-2.0 boilerplate; this is the one bespoke text/structure invariant. (2) golangci-lint (policy in committed `.golangci.yml`) — pure AST/lint execution. (3) gofmt — formatter execution. (4) `go test ./...` — the test suite. There are NO pre-commit hooks, NO codegen-freshness checks, NO cross-file coherence gates, NO commit-message discipline beyond GitHub defaults. Structure (MAINTAINERS roster, CONTRIBUTING, LICENSE.txt with NO NOTICE by design) exists but is not CI-gated. license-headers is the ONLY category cobra enforces that isn't a wrapped third-party binary.

**What alint replaces.** `addlicense -check` (the exact Cobra Apache header on every *.go) -> file_header (rule `cobra-go-apache-header`, pattern pins `Copyright 2013-2023 The Cobra Authors` + Apache boilerplate, include `**/*.go` minus `.github/**` and future-proofed generated globs); LICENSE presence + Apache compliance scaffold -> compliance/apache-2 bundle (with NOTICE recommendation correctly silenced via `apache-2-notice-file-exists: off`, R6 absent-category); generic Go-repo structure -> go bundle; baseline OSS hygiene (README/trailing-ws/final-newline/etc.) -> oss-baseline.

**What alint improves.** The license header is the whole story and alint expresses it as ONE declarative, schema-validated, reviewable rule that runs on a bare clone with no Go toolchain, no `addlicense` install, no module download — versus a CI job that must `go install github.com/google/addlicense`. alint also pins the exact copyright owner+year string in-config (addlicense's invariant is otherwise buried in the CI yaml flags), and the apache-2 bundle adds free, correct LICENSE/compliance assertions cobra happens to satisfy but never gates. Consolidation here is modest (cobra only has one bespoke check) but the cross-toolchain portability is a real win.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The non-license checks (golangci-lint, gofmt, go test) are category-D execution NON-GOALS — alint orchestrates them via `command`/`command_idempotent` but correctly does not reimplement AST linting, formatting, or the test suite. There is no cross-file/structure/codegen invariant cobra enforces that alint cannot express.

**Abstraction ideas.** Marginal. A first-class `apache-header` *parameterized* bundled rule (owner + year-range + ignore-globs as inputs, emitting the addlicense-equivalent file_header) would let cobra collapse its bespoke rule into a one-line bundle invocation — addlicense-style headers are ubiquitous across Go OSS, so a shared `compliance/addlicense@v1(owner, years)` ruleset would amortize across the corpus. Otherwise nothing beyond the existing surface is needed.

*Notes.* Textbook 'thin enforced surface, fat value-add' repo. The config honestly keeps value-add (MAINTAINERS, .golangci.yml presence) at level:info and OUT of the coverage numerator — methodologically clean. No stale claims; the config's analysis holds under the current capability brief.

---

### `spring-projects/spring-boot` — Large JVM/Gradle multi-module web framework (128 modules, 173 starters; ASF-style governance, buildSrc custom-plugin enforcement)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~30-40% of the BESPOKE (non-execution) enforced surface today: license header + AutoConfig sorting + import firewall + the regexp bans + DCO + SHA-pin signal are all native; layering/acyclicity is file_graph-expressible (push toward ~50%). The remaining majority (spring-javaformat, NullAway, full ArchUnit bytecode rules, BOM/classpath resolution, the test suite) is correctly category-D execution.

**Enforcement surface.** Spring Boot has one of the richest enforced surfaces in the corpus, almost all in buildSrc custom Gradle plugins + checkstyle + ArchUnit. By category: (license-headers) checkstyle `SpringHeaderCheck` + `config/checkstyle/checkstyle-header.txt` require the ASF Apache-2.0 header on every .java; CONTRIBUTING.adoc mandates it. (formatting) spring-javaformat (a custom formatter, gate `checkFormatMain/Test`) — execution. (import-firewall) checkstyle `IllegalImportCheck` (illegalPkgs: sun.*, com.google.common.*, jetbrains annotations, shaded micrometer/testcontainers, etc.) AND `import-control.xml` package-layering rules — partially structural. (source-pattern bans) a large `RegexpSinglelineJava` family: ban `org.junit.Assert` (must use AssertJ), ban `assertThatThrownBy(`, `Mockito.when`, `List.of()`, etc. (codegen/registry coherence) buildSrc `CheckAutoConfigurationImports` requires the ~99 `META-INF/spring/...AutoConfiguration.imports` files to be alphabetically sorted; `CheckSpringFactories`; AutoConfiguration registry consistency. (architecture) ArchUnit rules (package dependencies, no-cycles) — execution/AST. (null-safety) NullAway — execution. (security/http) nohttp (allowlist at `config/nohttp/allowlist.lines`) bans plaintext http:// URLs. (commit-discipline) DCO sign-off enforced via `.github/dco.yml` + CONTRIBUTING. (dependency-graph) BOM resolution + prohibited-classpath-deps checks — execution. (CI) GitHub Actions workflows (some SHA-pinned, some floating-tag). (tests) the giant JVM test suite.

**What alint replaces.** ASF Apache header on every .java -> file_header (`sb-java-license-header`, pattern `Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0`, excludes build/generated); AutoConfiguration.imports alphabetical sort across all ~99 registry files -> ordered_block (`sb-autoconfig-imports-sorted`, whole-file region `^\S`..`\z`, comparator:lexical) — a direct, exact replacement of `CheckAutoConfigurationImports`; checkstyle IllegalImportCheck illegalPkgs subset -> import_gate (`sb-import-firewall`, language:java, forbid alternation on sun./guava/jetbrains/shaded prefixes); RegexpSinglelineJava `import org.junit.Assert;` ban -> file_content_forbidden (`sb-no-junit-assert-import`); nohttp plaintext-URL ban -> file_content_forbidden (expressible, not in config); SHA-pinning OpenSSF signal -> yaml_path_matches (`gha-pin-actions-to-sha`, if_present, level:info — correctly non-blocking since SB mixes pinned+floating); DCO sign-off -> git_commit_signed_off (the actual commit-discipline check, stronger than the config's `file_exists .github/dco.yml` value-add).

**What alint improves.** Three places alint is materially better. (1) AutoConfiguration.imports sorting: `CheckAutoConfigurationImports` is bespoke Gradle/buildSrc code (compile + run Gradle); alint's ordered_block expresses the SAME invariant declaratively across all 99 files, reviewable in 8 lines, running on a bare clone with no JDK/Gradle. (2) The RegexpSinglelineJava family: checkstyle scatters these across an XML config; each maps to a one-line file_content_forbidden, and the FULL family (assertThatThrownBy, Mockito.when, List.of, …) could be consolidated into one reviewable alint config block instead of buried checkstyle regexps. (3) The import firewall subset is cleaner as import_gate (matches the EXTRACTED import target, language-aware) than checkstyle's package-string matching. Cross-toolchain: none of these need the Gradle build to run.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that are addressable. ArchUnit's full package-dependency/no-cycle architecture rules and import-control.xml's package-LAYERING (not just forbidden-prefix) are richer than import_gate's per-file regex firewall — BUT this is genuinely expressible via file_graph: `forbidden_edges` encodes layering (from-glob -> to-glob) and `require: acyclic` encodes the no-package-cycle rule across the whole-repo reference graph (I checked file_graph.rs: from_content edges + acyclic/forbidden_edges modes exist). The remaining ArchUnit checks that inspect bytecode/annotations/method-call graphs (not file-to-file imports) are category-D AST execution, correctly out of scope. spring-javaformat, NullAway, BOM-resolution, prohibited-classpath-deps, and the test suite are all category-D execution NON-GOALS. So: no genuine gap — what looks like an architecture-rule gap is mostly file_graph-expressible, and the rest is correct execution-orchestration territory.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `checkstyle-regexp@v1` translator concept: many JVM repos carry a RegexpSinglelineJava battery; a shared ruleset that bundles the common bans (no-junit-assert, no-System.out, no-printStackTrace, no plaintext-http) as named file_content_forbidden rules would let SB drop a dozen lines. (2) file_graph forbidden_edges is the natural home for import-control.xml — a worked 'package-layering firewall' example/preset for Java (analogous to the go/python import_gate presets) would make the architecture-rule coverage discoverable. (3) The 99-file ordered_block over a glob is a strong validator for `file_graph fresh`-adjacent 'registry must be sorted' patterns — no new kind needed, but a `registry_sorted` documentation idiom would help.

*Notes.* Config is high-quality and methodologically clean (value-add kept at info, out of the ratio). One under-claim: it expresses DCO as `file_exists .github/dco.yml` (value-add) rather than the available git_commit_signed_off, which is the ACTUAL enforced invariant and a stronger native replacement. The architecture-rules surface (ArchUnit/import-control) is the config's biggest under-explored seam — file_graph forbidden_edges + acyclic cover a real chunk of it.

---

### `sqlalchemy/sqlalchemy` — Python DB/ORM library (Gerrit-reviewed, GitHub mirror; tox->nox migration, Cython-accelerated)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~15-20% of the enforced 13-command lint session natively (2 structural checks: headers + cython block). With orchestration (command_idempotent / generated_file_fresh-mutating) wrapping the remaining --check tools, alint can GATE the full session, but the LOGIC of ~11 commands is correctly category-D execution (flake8p, black, slotscheck, the runtime-introspecting generators, sync_test_files' transform). Addressable-declarative coverage is low by design — this repo is execution-heavy.

**Enforcement surface.** SQLAlchemy's enforced bespoke surface lives entirely in the `pep8`/`lint` nox session (noxfile.py / tox.ini), run by .github/workflows/run-on-pr.yaml via `nox -e pep8`. That session runs ~13 commands. By category: (formatting/style) flake8p, black --check, format_docs_code --check (black on rst code blocks), slotscheck (__slots__ correctness) — all category-D tool execution. (codegen-freshness, runtime-introspecting) generate_tuple_map_overloads --check, generate_proxy_methods --check, generate_sql_functions --check — these IMPORT the built package and introspect at runtime, then diff; and walk_packages imports every subpackage — category-D (cannot run without building the C extensions). (license-headers, structural) tools/normalize_file_headers.py --check requires every lib/sqlalchemy/**/*.{py,pyx,pxd} to begin with a path-comment line + a `Copyright (C) <yr>-<yr> the SQLAlchemy authors and contributors` line. (generated-block, structural) tools/cython_imports.py --check requires every `*_cy.py` to contain a fixed literal `# START/END GENERATED CYTHON IMPORT` block. (file-derivation) tools/sync_test_files.py --check derives one test file from another via a pure text transform + black, then diffs. No license header on non-lib files; AUTHORS file referenced by every header. Notably NO version-coherence gate (lib __version__ 2.1.0b3 vs doc release 2.1.0b2 deliberately differ).

**What alint replaces.** normalize_file_headers.py --check (the per-file MIT/path/copyright header on lib/sqlalchemy sources) -> file_header (`sqla-source-license-header`, pattern `^# <path>\n# Copyright (C) \d{4}-\d{4} the SQLAlchemy authors and contributors`, lines:2 — correctly matching the LOOSER enforced invariant, not the full block, to avoid the 6 known false-fires); cython_imports.py --check (the fixed generated block in every *_cy.py) -> file_content_matches (`sqla-cython-import-block-present`, whole-file pattern on the START/GENERATED-marker prefix); AUTHORS presence -> file_exists; README/test-guide presence -> oss-baseline + file_exists.

**What alint improves.** Both structural checks become declarative, toolchain-free rules. normalize_file_headers.py and cython_imports.py are bespoke Python scripts that must run under the repo's Python env; alint expresses the same two invariants as two reviewable rules that run on a bare clone with no nox/no built C-extensions. The config's calibration insight is sharp: it matches the script's ACTUAL loosened invariant (path-comment + copyright line, tolerating extra attribution lines and wrapped <see AUTHORS file>) rather than a naive full-block regex that false-fires on log.py/processors.py/async drivers — that's a genuine false-positive-reduction win over a hand-rolled regex.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** sync_test_files.py --check is the one real seam, but it must be split. The DECLARATIVE expression — 'file B's bytes == pure_text_transform(file A) + black' computed by alint itself — is a GENUINE GAP: I checked cross_file.rs (`identical` relation does whole-file BYTE-equality with optional skip_header_lines, but `normalize:` only offers trim/lower/semver-major/semver-minor — no arbitrary text transform), and file_graph `fresh` embeds a content-HASH (not a transform-equality), and `pair` only checks sibling EXISTENCE not content. So no alint kind computes a project-specific source-to-source text transform as data. HOWEVER the ENFORCEMENT is fully covered: sync_test_files.py HAS a --check mode CI runs, so command_idempotent natively orchestrates it (run --check, exit 0 = clean, with files_from/files_pattern to attribute per-file offenders), AND since it rewrites files in place, generated_file_fresh MUTATING mode (`outputs:` glob -> snapshot/run/diff/restore, keeping the tree pure — I verified this mode exists in generated_file_fresh.rs) fits exactly the `python sync_test_files.py && git diff --exit-code` pattern. So this is a gap ONLY in declarative-transform expression, NOT in enforceability — the config's B1 label ('no alint kind expresses it') is correct about declarative expression but understates that orchestration closes the enforcement gap. The runtime-introspecting generators (tuple_map_overloads/proxy_methods/sql_functions) ARE genuine category-D (they import the built package), correctly orchestrated not reimplemented.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `content_transform` / `derived_file` rule kind: 'file(s) matching B must equal a declared transform of file(s) matching A', where the transform is a small composable pipeline (regex-substitute, line-map, header-swap) — the generalization of sync_test_files-style mirror-with-edits. This is the principled fill for the one true gap and would also subsume okhttp/curl-style 'mirrored fixture' patterns seen elsewhere in the corpus. Short of a new kind, documenting the generated_file_fresh-mutating idiom AS the canonical answer for 'pure-transform sibling files with a --check/regen tool' would dissolve the apparent gap for almost all real repos (which always ship the regen tool).

*Notes.* Config is excellent and unusually self-aware (the R4 calibration note on header looseness, the explicit non-addition of a version-coherence cross_file because docs deliberately lag). The single genuine gap (declarative source-to-source transform) is real but narrow and fully enforceable today via orchestration — worth re-stating in the log as 'declarative-only gap, enforcement covered' rather than a coverage hole.

---

### `square/okhttp` — Kotlin/Java HTTP client library (Gradle, Block/Square governance; multi-target JVM/Android/GraalVM)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95-100% of addressable. okhttp's only on-disk structural/content invariants (de-facto header, golden-.api presence, wrapper URL, editorconfig contract, renovate schema, publish group) are all natively expressible; everything genuinely enforced (Spotless/ktlint, Checkstyle, AnimalSniffer, apiCheck ABI-diff, the test/consumption matrix, mkdocs) is correctly category-D execution. Addressable surface is thin (typical of an execution-heavy library) but fully covered.

**Enforcement surface.** okhttp's enforced surface is overwhelmingly tool/test execution. By category: (formatting/style) Spotless running `ktlint()` ONLY — crucially, with NO licenseHeaderFile step (build-logic okhttp.quality-conventions.gradle.kts), so the Apache header is a de-facto reviewer convention, NOT a gated check; Checkstyle; Android Lint — all category-D. (binary compatibility) binary-compatibility-validator: every published module ships a golden `.api` file under api/ and the `apiCheck` task diffs the built ABI against it — execution, but the golden artefacts are on-disk structure. (api-compat/animal-sniffer) AnimalSniffer checks Android API-level signatures — execution. (build/consumption) Maven consumption tests, JPMS module tests, GraalVM native-image tests — execution. (docs) mkdocs build. (hygiene contract) root `.editorconfig` declares 2-space indent / trim_trailing_whitespace / final-newline, enforced at build by ktlint — the contract is declared on disk, the enforcement is D. (deps) Renovate (config `.github/renovate.json`, validated by renovate-config-validator TOOL). (wrapper integrity) gradle/actions/wrapper-validation checksums the wrapper jar — D; the .properties declares the distribution URL. (publish coordinates) group `com.squareup.okhttp3` set in build-logic. NO enforced license-header tool; NO commit-message discipline gate; NO codegen-freshness; NO cross-file coherence gate.

**What alint replaces.** the de-facto Apache header convention on source (NOT tool-enforced by okhttp) -> file_header (`okhttp-source-apache-header`, level:warning, pattern accepting all 3 header forms via invariant `the Apache License` | SPDX, excludes kotlinTemplates/build/hpack-test-case/module-info/package-info — 578/580 comply); golden binary-compat `.api` presence -> file_exists (`okhttp-core-api-golden-present`); Gradle wrapper distribution-URL declaration -> file_content_matches; .editorconfig hygiene-contract declaration -> file_content_matches (could also be the real indent_style/no_trailing_whitespace/final_newline rules applied to the tree); renovate $schema declaration -> json_path_matches (see improves); publish group coordinate -> file_content_matches; Apache LICENSE/compliance scaffold -> compliance/apache-2 bundle; baseline hygiene -> oss-baseline + java bundle + ci/github-actions bundle.

**What alint improves.** Two notable wins. (1) STALE config rationale on renovate: the config uses file_content_matches for `.github/renovate.json` BECAUSE it claims a trailing comma at line 49 makes it JSON5 that `json_path_matches` ERRORS on. Under the CURRENT brief I verified structured_path.rs `strip_jsonc` tolerates `//` + `/* */` comments AND trailing commas (Pass 2 drops `,` before `}`/`]`), so json_path_matches NOW PARSES renovate.json fine — the robust native expression is `json_path_matches $.['$schema']` (note bracket notation for the `$`-prefixed dashed key), not the regex workaround. The JSON5-quirk corpus note is obsolete. (2) The .editorconfig 'hygiene contract' is better enforced by alint applying the REAL invariants (indent_style space width 2, no_trailing_whitespace, final_newline) directly to the source tree — alint can check the actual files, not merely that .editorconfig DECLARES the intent, which is a strictly stronger guarantee than okhttp's 'declare + trust ktlint'. All of these run with no Gradle/Spotless/ktlint install on a bare clone.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable. binary-compatibility-validator's `apiCheck` (diff the BUILT ABI signature against the golden .api) is genuine category-D — it requires compiling the Kotlin/Java to extract the ABI; alint can only assert the golden file is present/non-empty (which it does). AnimalSniffer (Android API-level signature checking against bytecode), Spotless/ktlint formatting, Android Lint, the Maven/JPMS/GraalVM consumption+native test matrix, and mkdocs are all execution NON-GOALS, correctly orchestrated (command) not reimplemented. There is no cross-file/structure invariant okhttp enforces that alint cannot express; the apparent 'gaps' are all execution.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `editorconfig-coherence@v1` ruleset: read `.editorconfig` and assert the TREE actually obeys the declared indent/trim/final-newline (bridging the 'declare vs enforce' gap generically) — okhttp, and most editorconfig-shipping repos, would get real enforcement for one bundle line instead of a declarative file_content_matches. (2) A `golden-artifact-present@v1` idiom/preset for binary-compat .api / .api.txt / snapshot files (okhttp, AndroidX, kotlinx all ship these) — for_each over published-module dirs + nested file_exists of the api/ golden, the value-predicate-parity idiom. (3) Nothing requires a NEW kind; the renovate finding argues for surfacing 'JSONC is tolerated' more loudly so study agents stop reaching for file_content_matches workarounds.

*Notes.* Config is solid but carries ONE stale rationale worth correcting in the log: the renovate.json JSON5/trailing-comma claim is obsolete now that JSONC parsing is tolerant — json_path_matches works (with bracket notation for the `$schema` dashed/$-prefixed key), so V4 should migrate off file_content_matches. The config is honest that the Apache header is a CONVENTION not a tool-gated check (level:warning, 578/580) — a good R5 value-add classification. The editorconfig 'declare vs actually-enforce' seam is the most interesting product opportunity surfaced here.

---

### `sveltejs/svelte` — JS/TS compiler+runtime, pnpm single-real-package 'monorepo' (packages/svelte), changesets release, GitHub Actions CI, community-governed

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable (the import firewall + both codegen-freshness gates are now all natively expressible; bulk of the repo is D execution, excluded from the denominator)

**Enforcement surface.** svelte's gated surface is overwhelmingly EXECUTION/SEMANTIC (category D): vitest test suite (node 18/20/22/24 x 3 OS, + a SVELTE_NO_ASYNC variant), `pnpm check`/`check:tsgo` (tsc/tsgo type-checking), `pnpm lint` = eslint + `prettier --check` (incl. the custom `lube/svelte-naming-convention` snake/camel AST rule and type-aware @typescript-eslint rules), `pnpm bench` (perf), `check-treeshakeability.js` (rollup-bundles each export and asserts treeshakeable/empty output — bundler-semantic), and `--frozen-lockfile` (pnpm dep-graph resolution). There is NO pre-commit/husky/lefthook, NO committed git hooks, NO CODEOWNERS/renovate/dependabot. STRUCTURE/CROSS-FILE coherence (the addressable slice): (1) an eslint custom rule `no_compiler_imports` (eslint.config.js:4-34) — an IMPORT FIREWALL forbidding any `../compiler/` or `#compiler` reference from packages/svelte/src/** except src/compiler/** (it deliberately string-searches raw text so it also catches type-only `import('../compiler/...')` in JSDoc that AST misses); (2) two CODEGEN-FRESHNESS gates in ci.yml — `pnpm build && [ "$(git status --porcelain=v1)" = "" ]` (regenerate checked-in types/version.js/messages, assert whole tree clean) and `git status --porcelain documentation/docs/07-misc/.generated/` after `pnpm generate:browser-support` (path-scoped tree-freshness). HYGIENE is declared in .editorconfig but enforced only by `prettier --check` (final-newline, no-trailing-ws). Package-metadata invariants (root private:true, packageManager pin, MIT license, .d.ts types entry) are NOT gated — pure value-add.

**What alint replaces.** no_compiler_imports import firewall -> import_gate (language: js, forbid regex on extracted target + path include/exclude scope) NATIVELY reimplements the rule logic (config rule svelte-no-compiler-imports-in-runtime); final-newline/no-trailing-ws on src/** -> final_newline + no_trailing_whitespace (with fixers); package metadata -> json_path_equals/json_path_matches (private:true, license:MIT, packageManager pin, .d.ts types) + file_exists (README/CHANGELOG)

**What alint improves.** import_gate is materially better than the eslint custom rule on TWO axes: (a) it is one declarative reviewable rule rather than ~30 lines of bespoke eslint plugin JS, and it runs WITHOUT the node/eslint toolchain (a fresh clone with just the alint binary enforces the firewall); (b) the `language: js` preset is now COMMENT-AWARE (strip_js_comments blanks // and /* */ incl. JSDoc /** */, preserving line numbers) so it does NOT false-positive on commented-out `import(...)` lines — a precision win over raw string-search. NOTE the directional flip vs the config's framing: with the default js preset, comment-stripping means a type-only JSDoc `import('../compiler/Foo')` is NOT flagged (the comment is blanked) — which svelte's rule INTENTIONALLY catches. This is recoverable: supplying a custom `import_pattern:` disables comment-stripping (strip_comments is auto-true only for the bare js preset), giving exact raw-text parity with svelte's rule. So the firewall is fully replaceable either way (comment-aware default, or raw-text via import_pattern).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's stated B gap ('generated_file_fresh is a non-mutating single-file STDOUT diff, whereas svelte runs a MUTATING multi-output generator and asserts the working tree is git-clean') is STALE — it was authored pre-v0.12-build. generated_file_fresh now has a MUTATING `outputs:` mode (generated_file_fresh.rs Mode::Mutating): it snapshots the `outputs:` scope, runs the in-place generator, diffs (changed + removed + NEW in-scope files the generator created), reports staleness, and restores the tree byte-for-byte — exactly the `pnpm build && git diff --exit-code` shape. `outputs:` accepts an arbitrary glob or list, so `outputs: ["packages/svelte/src/**", "documentation/docs/07-misc/.generated/**"]` with `command: ["pnpm","build"]` (and a second rule for `pnpm generate:browser-support` scoped to .generated/) expresses both ci.yml freshness gates. I tried this against the kind's contract and it holds: the only residual vs `git status --porcelain` on the LITERAL whole tree is that you declare the output scope explicitly (which is arguably better — it documents intent and avoids flagging unrelated dirty files). Not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A whole-tree convenience form of generated_file_fresh — `outputs: tree` (or omitting outputs in mutating mode) meaning 'snapshot the entire working tree, run, then `git diff --exit-code` everything' — would let `pnpm build && git status --porcelain` be transcribed verbatim without enumerating output globs. Lower priority since explicit `outputs:` globs already cover it and read better. Otherwise the existing surface fully serves this repo.

*Notes.* Strongest evidence in this group that the original corpus systematically OVER-reported gaps: svelte's sole claimed B (mutating-generator git-clean freshness) is fully covered by generated_file_fresh's outputs: mode, and the import_gate JSDoc 'residual' is resolvable via a custom import_pattern. Both the svelte note AND the batch-1 eslint sharp-edge ('import_gate js over-matches JSDoc @typedef') were addressed by the comment-aware js preset.

---

### `swiftlang/swift` — Swift + C/C++ language/compiler monorepo, CMake build, Apple/swift.org governance, EXTERNAL Jenkins CI (no in-repo workflows)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% addressable beyond the single header — i.e. of the TINY addressable surface (essentially just the license header), alint replaces ~100%; the repo's enforcement is otherwise ~all D execution/external-CI, so this is a value-add-dominant repo

**Enforcement surface.** Distinctive shape: swiftlang/swift has essentially NO in-repo enforcement infrastructure — no .github/workflows, no GitLab/Circle/Azure/Jenkins config in-tree, no pre-commit/husky/lefthook, no committed git hooks, no Makefile/justfile/Taskfile, no package.json. CI is EXTERNAL and comment-triggered: docs/ContinuousIntegration.md documents `@swift-ci Please <test>` comments driving an off-repo Jenkins fleet. The ONLY CI-documented lint mapping to an in-repo script is `@swift-ci Please Python lint` -> ./utils/python_lint.py (a flake8 wrapper, category D). Everything else gated is build/test EXECUTION (the C++/Swift compile, the lit/XCTest test suites, sanitizers, the freestanding-symbol allowlist, ABI/symbol-graph diffing, clang-format) — all D. The single ADDRESSABLE, de-facto-enforced invariant is the SOURCE-FILE LICENSE HEADER: CONTRIBUTING.md 'Code Templates' requires every new hand-written source file to begin with the canonical Swift.org header whose invariant line is 'This source file is part of the Swift.org open source project' (Apache-2.0-with-Runtime-Library-Exception); 966/970 lib/*.cpp already carry it, gyb-generated/.def/.inc/vendored/test-input trees stamp their own or none. Convention/metadata anchors the project KEEPS but does not gate on: CONTRIBUTING.md, LICENSE.txt (Apache-2 text), .github/CODEOWNERS, .editorconfig (2-space + final-newline), .clang-format (BasedOnStyle: LLVM), .gitattributes (*.swift.gyb -> linguist-language=Swift). Two broken symlinks in a depth-1 clone (a deliberate test fixture + a dangling update-checkout sister-repo link) must be `ignore:`d so the tree walk doesn't crash.

**What alint replaces.** source-file Swift.org license header on lib/**/*.{cpp,h} + include/swift/**/*.h (excluding .def/.gyb/.inc) -> file_header (pattern on first N=12 lines) NATIVELY replaces the CONTRIBUTING.md 'Code Templates' convention, which today is enforced only by human code review (no script gates it); LICENSE.txt Apache-2 text -> file_content_matches; CONTRIBUTING.md/.github/CODEOWNERS existence -> file_exists; .editorconfig/.clang-format/.gitattributes policy declarations -> file_content_matches

**What alint improves.** alint MATERIALLY upgrades the header check from a documented review convention (a human eyeballs every new file's preamble) to a mechanically-enforced, fix-suggesting gate that runs with zero toolchain on every commit — for a 970-file compiler core this is the difference between drift-prone manual review and a hard invariant. file_header is more precise than a naive grep: it anchors to the first 12 lines (so a stray copy of the sentence deeper in a file doesn't falsely satisfy it) and can use (?m)^ alternation to tolerate the leading `//===` rule-comment preamble. The bundled oss-baseline also adds structural backstops (no-merge-conflict-markers, final-newline, etc.) the repo gets entirely for free.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable. This repo is the clearest 'value-add only' case in the group: its real enforcement is ~entirely D (external Jenkins, the C++/Swift compile, lit tests, sanitizers, ABI symbol diffing, flake8 via python_lint.py) — DELIBERATE non-goals alint orchestrates via command but never reimplements. There is no in-repo cross-file/structure/codegen check that alint cannot express; the header is the lone addressable item and it is fully covered. I considered whether the freestanding-symbol allowlist or ABI symbol-graph diff could be a structured-query gap, but both are compiler-output (symbol graph / nm) semantic checks = D, not file invariants. No GENUINE GAP.

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `swift` (or generic `apache-2-with-rle`) ruleset capturing the Swift.org/LLVM header convention + the editorconfig/clang-format policy assertions would let swift-family repos adopt this in one `extends:` line. Beyond that, nothing: the repo demonstrates no need the existing surface can't meet. (The LLVM-family header is close enough to the existing compliance/apache-2 source-header pattern that a small variant ruleset, not a new kind, is the right unit.)

*Notes.* Archetypal 'enforcement lives off-repo' case: the most useful framing is that alint moves swift's review-CONVENTION header from human-eyeball to mechanical gate, and provides structure/policy backstops the repo never bothered to script. No gaps; no over-claim either — the config was honest that the addressable slice is tiny.

---

### `symfony/symfony` — PHP web-framework MONOREPO — 58 Components + 6 Bundles + 5 Bridges + 13 Contracts under src/Symfony/, each git-split to a read-only standalone composer package; GitHub Actions CI; composer.lock gitignored (library convention)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (header, all per-package meta files across the full set, the registry, intl/emoji data form, hygiene, and the translations-freshness gate via outputs: mode are all native; residual is packaging convenience — no php bundle yet — and a couple of value-predicate rules the original config under-built rather than alint being unable)

**Enforcement surface.** symfony has a RICH bespoke structural surface (unusual depth for a study repo), split across category boundaries. A-EXPRESSIBLE: (1) per-file LICENSE HEADER — .php-cs-fixer.dist.php `header_comment` stamps the canonical 'This file is part of the Symfony package. (c) Fabien Potencier...' on every src/ PHP file (~5,865 non-test files), gated by the fabbot CS workflow; (2) PER-PACKAGE META-FILE invariants (package-tests.yml 'Verify meta files are correct') — every modified package must ship README.md, CHANGELOG.md, LICENSE, .gitignore, .gitattributes, phpunit.xml.dist AND must NOT commit phpunit.xml (the gitignored generated copy); Contracts/* are special-cased (no .gitattributes / no phpunit.xml.dist); (3) ROOT-REPLACE REGISTRY — package-tests.yml requires every subpackage name to appear in root composer.json `replace` mapped to 'self.version'; (4) COMMITTED INTL/EMOJI DATA must be the plain uncompressed .php form, never .php.gz (intl-data-tests.yml pre-checks `[ -f .../en.php ]` && `[ ! -f .../en.php.gz ]`); (5) HYGIENE — .editorconfig LF + final-newline + trim-trailing-ws (php-cs-fixer @Symfony also enforces on src/*.php). CODEGEN-FRESHNESS (one gate): integration-tests.yml:249-251 runs `php .github/sync-translations.php` then `git diff --exit-code src/` (XLIFF translations must be in sync). D-EXECUTION (the bulk): patch-types.php return-type AST check (unit-tests.yml), psalm + phpstan (static-analysis.yml, baselined), php-cs-fixer @Symfony AST/format rules, twig-cs-fixer (twig-cs-fixer.yml), the PHPUnit suites. alint has NO php/composer bundle (ecosystem gap).

**What alint replaces.** per-file Symfony header on src/**/*.php (with the fixer's Finder excludes) -> file_header (pattern on first 8 lines); per-package meta files -> for_each_dir (select src/Symfony/{Component,Bundle,Bridge,Contracts}/*, when_iter iter.has_file(composer.json), nested file_exists {path}/README.md|CHANGELOG.md|LICENSE|.gitignore, + Component/Bundle/Bridge-scoped phpunit.xml.dist/.gitattributes); no committed phpunit.xml -> file_absent (src/Symfony/**/phpunit.xml); intl/emoji data present+uncompressed -> file_exists + file_absent (**/*.php.gz); the translations git-clean freshness gate -> generated_file_fresh MUTATING outputs: mode (NOT the `command` shell-out the config used); hygiene -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline; CS/phpstan config existence -> file_exists

**What alint improves.** Two strong improvements over symfony's current approach. (a) The per-package meta-file gate: symfony's package-tests.yml only checks the MODIFIED subset of packages (a shell loop over changed paths); for_each_dir asserts the invariant across ALL ~82 packages in one declarative rule — strictly stronger coverage, and it runs without the PHP/CI harness. (b) The translations-freshness gate: the config wrapped it as `command: [bash,-c,'php sync-translations.php && git diff --exit-code src/']` and called generated_file_fresh inadequate — but that is STALE (pre-build). generated_file_fresh's mutating `outputs:` mode (outputs: 'src/**', command: ['php','.github/sync-translations.php']) snapshots the XLIFF tree, runs the mutator, diffs, reports each stale file by PATH, and restores byte-for-byte — strictly better than the opaque command (per-file violations + tree-purity + a 120s default timeout) and NATIVELY expressed rather than shelled out. Also the root-replace registry: registry_paths_resolve (source: composer.json, extract: { json: "$.replace[*]"-style keys }, orphans: reverse-check) would assert every replace KEY resolves to an on-disk package AND every package is registered — far stronger than the config's single hardcoded json_path_matches on one key ($.replace['symfony/console']).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that alint should own. Candidate gaps, each tried: (1) 'replace value is ALWAYS exactly self.version for EVERY key' (a value-predicate over a dynamic key SET, not one key) — the config only did json_path_matches on a single literal key. Tried: registry_paths_resolve checks key-RESOLUTION but not the per-key VALUE; a json_path over `$.replace[*]` validates the value-SET. With value_set_membership (already a planned/validated v0.12 kind per the case-study backlog) or a cross_file set check this is expressible; even today, 'every value == self.version' is coverable by asserting the value set equals {self.version} via cross_file set_equals on `$.replace[*]` vs a literal — so NOT a true gap, just under-built in the config. (2) 'every package's composer.json $.name == symfony/{basename}' (per-package value parity) — for_each_dir + nested json_path_equals equals matching the dir basename covers this (the value-predicate parity idiom); the config simply didn't write it. (3) The PHP/composer ECOSYSTEM bundle absence is a packaging convenience gap, not an expressiveness gap — every individual symfony check above is expressible with existing kinds; there's just no one-line `extends: php@v1`. So: no GENUINE GAP in expressiveness.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A bundled `php`/`composer` ruleset — the clearest abstraction this repo demands — composing file_header (Symfony/PSR header), the per-package meta-file for_each_dir pattern, registry_paths_resolve over composer `replace`/`require`, and json_path checks on composer.json (license, type, autoload PSR-4 namespace<->dir parity). symfony, laravel, and every PHP monorepo would adopt it in one extends. (2) A `value_set_membership` kind (already on the validated v0.12 backlog) makes '$.replace[*] all == self.version' a one-liner rather than a cross_file set_equals against a singleton — the symfony replace-map is a textbook motivating case. (3) A composition primitive pairing for_each_dir's {basename} with a nested json_path_equals (PSR-4 'package dir name == declared namespace tail') would make the monorepo dir<->manifest coherence idiom first-class.

*Notes.* Richest addressable surface in the group and the case where the config most under-used existing kinds: it shelled out the translations freshness (now native via outputs:), used a single hardcoded key for the replace registry (registry_paths_resolve + orphans covers it whole), and didn't attempt per-package name<->dir value parity (for_each_dir + nested json_path_equals). Primary real takeaway: demand for a bundled PHP/composer ruleset.

### `systemd/systemd` — C / meson systems project (init+service manager), Red Hat + community governance, meson test-driven CI

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~90% of addressable (SPDX headers, both .gitattributes halves, final-newline, the Python tabs ban, all structure invariants, and the ruff line-length declaration are native; the residual is the N-agnostic 4-way line-length coherence across heterogeneous unstructured configs, which is expressible as 4 literal-pinned rules and only a narrow gap in its fully-relational form)

**Enforcement surface.** systemd's gated surface is overwhelmingly D-EXECUTION via the meson `test`/`dist` suites: clang-tidy, coccinelle semantic patches, ruff (format+lint) + mypy on the Python tooling, shellcheck, the large C unit-test corpus, sanitizers (ASan/UBSan/etc.), fuzzers, Coverity, and OSSF Scorecards. The A-EXPRESSIBLE slice is the hygiene/structure/cross-config-coherence layer declared by .gitattributes, .editorconfig, and the tools/check-*.sh scripts: (1) per-file SPDX LICENSE headers — every C/H source carries `/* SPDX-License-Identifier: ... */` and meson.build/python carry the `#` form (de-facto repo-wide; required by the contribution docs for each new file); (2) .gitattributes `*.[ch] whitespace=tab-in-indent,trailing-space` — no trailing whitespace AND C indentation must use spaces not a leading tab; same trailing-space marker on man/*.xml; (3) .editorconfig `insert_final_newline=true` ([*]) + `[*.{c,h}] indent_style=space`; (4) a Python TABS BAN — linter.yml 'Check that tabs are not used in Python code' = `! git grep -P '\t'` over named .py files; (5) STRUCTURE invariants — the REUSE-style LICENSES/ dir (per-file SPDX tags resolve against it), the dual LICENSE.GPL2/LICENSE.LGPL2.1 texts, meson.version + meson_options.txt build entrypoints; (6) a documented EDITOR-CONFIG TRIPLE-SYNC convention — CODING_STYLE.md: 'If you update .editorconfig make sure to update .dir-locals.el and .vimrc too', and the 109-char line limit is declared in .editorconfig + .clang-format (ColumnLimit) + .vimrc + ruff.toml in lockstep. A self-referential test/testdata symlink ('testdata -> .') must be `ignore:`d so the tree walk doesn't loop.

**What alint replaces.** per-file SPDX header on C/H + meson.build -> file_content_matches (or more precisely file_header anchored to the head) on 'SPDX-License-Identifier:'; .gitattributes trailing-space half -> no_trailing_whitespace (with fixer) on src/**/*.{c,h} + man/**/*.xml; the tab-in-indent half + editorconfig indent_style=space -> indent_style (style: spaces) on src C; final-newline -> final_newline on the editorconfig [*] surface; Python tabs ban (`! git grep -P '\t'`) -> file_content_forbidden (pattern: tab) on the python surface; LICENSES/ dir + dual license texts + meson entrypoints -> dir_exists + file_exists; editor-config triple presence -> file_exists; ruff line-length=109 declaration -> toml_path_equals ($['line-length'] == 109)

**What alint improves.** Materially better on the Python tabs ban and the trailing-ws/indent policy: systemd enforces these via ad-hoc `git grep -P` one-liners scattered across linter.yml and via .gitattributes attributes that git itself only checks on checkout/diff (not as a hard CI gate on existing content) — alint turns them into uniform, fixer-equipped, reviewable rules that run repo-wide with no meson/ruff/git-grep dependency (file_content_forbidden/indent_style/no_trailing_whitespace, all with auto-fix). The SPDX header check likewise becomes a single declarative invariant rather than relying on reviewer vigilance + REUSE tooling. The ruff line-length=109 toml_path_equals is a genuine cross-config COHERENCE assertion the repo only documents in prose ('keep these four in sync') — alint can mechanically assert the ruff.toml value, closing one corner of the manual triple-sync.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none addressable, after reproducing the hardest candidate. The interesting one is the CODING_STYLE.md TRIPLE-SYNC: '.editorconfig, .dir-locals.el, .vimrc must all be updated together' and 'the 109 line-limit must match across .editorconfig + .clang-format + .vimrc + ruff.toml'. I tried to express the cross-config NUMERIC coherence (all four declare 109): the four files use four DIFFERENT syntaxes (editorconfig `max_line_length=109`, clang-format YAML `ColumnLimit: 109`, vimrc `set textwidth=109`/`colorcolumn`, ruff.toml `line-length=109`). cross_file with relation equals needs an extractable value per file: ruff.toml -> toml_path; clang-format -> yaml_path ($.ColumnLimit); but .editorconfig (INI-ish, no toml/yaml parser) and .vimrc (vimscript) have NO structured extractor — only a regex extract on cross_file/file_content_matches against a literal `109`. So you CAN assert each of the four 'contains 109 in the right place' independently (4 rules: toml_path_equals + yaml_path_equals + 2x file_content_matches '(?m)^.*(max_line_length|textwidth)\s*=?\s*109'), and that gives you the practical guarantee. What you CANNOT do is bind them RELATIONALLY ('whatever N clang-format uses, ruff must use the SAME N') without pinning the literal — because cross_file's regex/lines extract can pull a value from the unstructured files, but editorconfig/vimrc have no key-path addressing, so a true N-agnostic 4-way equals across mixed syntaxes is not cleanly expressible. This is a NARROW genuine gap (cross-config value coherence across heterogeneous unstructured config syntaxes), but in practice the literal-pinned 4-rule form is what a maintainer wants anyway (109 is a deliberate house constant, not an arbitrary value), so I rate it borderline/low-value rather than a must-fix. Everything else (SPDX, hygiene, structure) is fully covered.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) The cleanest abstraction systemd motivates: a generalized cross_file `regex` extractor usable as the relation source/target so a value can be pulled from ANY text file by a capture group and compared relationally — this would turn the editorconfig/.vimrc/clang-format/ruff '109' quad into a true N-agnostic coherence rule (source = ruff.toml toml_path, targets = the other three via `extract: { regex: '...(\d+)' }`, relation equals). cross_file already has a regex extract for some arms; making it first-class on every arm closes the heterogeneous-config-coherence case broadly (Cargo MSRV vs rust-toolchain vs CI matrix is the same shape). (2) An INI/editorconfig structured extractor (editorconfig_path / ini_path) so .editorconfig values get key-path addressing like toml/yaml — editorconfig is ubiquitous and currently only regex-addressable. (3) A small bundled `c-meson` or `spdx` ruleset (SPDX header + LICENSES/ dir existence + REUSE-style structure) would package systemd/kernel/mesa-style compliance in one extends.

*Notes.* The one place in this group with a defensible (if narrow, low-value) genuine gap: relational value-coherence across heterogeneous UNSTRUCTURED config syntaxes (.editorconfig + .vimrc have no path-addressable extractor), where the practical workaround (pin the literal 109 in 4 independent rules) is what a maintainer wants anyway. Everything else is fully covered; the editor-config TRIPLE existence (not value-sync) is trivially file_exists.

### `tensorflow-tensorflow` — C++/Python data/ML hyperscale Bazel monorepo (Google single-vendor)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable (which is a thin slice: the case-collision check + the review-convention header). The repo's ENFORCED surface is ~95% execution (Bazel queries, golden API test, formatters, OSV) = correctly out of scope; alint covers essentially all the non-execution remainder.

**Enforcement surface.** TF's machine-ENFORCED validation surface is almost entirely execution/semantic (category D), orchestrated through Bazel + a sprawl of CI workflows and bats scripts. By category: (1) BUILD/codegen/dep-graph invariants — the pip-package license, dep-closure, and CUDA-build invariants are enforced via Bazel query (`bazel query`/`cquery` over the action graph in tensorflow/tools/pip_package + ci scripts); the public-API surface is gated by the api-compatibility golden test (tensorflow/tools/api/golden + api_compatibility_test) that diffs the generated API signature against checked-in golden .pbtxt files — pure semantic/codegen, D. (2) FORMATTERS/AST linters — buildifier (BUILD/.bzl), clang-format (Google C++ style, .clang-format at root), pylint/pyformat for Python, all run as `git diff`-after-format gates = D. (3) HYGIENE via bats — ci/official/utilities/code_check_full.bats encodes a battery of repo-state asserts: the 'no duplicate files on Windows' check (line ~290: no two tracked paths may collide when lowercased, the case-insensitive-FS hazard), plus pip-smoke / do-not-submit / BUILD-file freshness greps. (4) LICENSE-HEADERS — Apache-2.0 header on new source files is a *documented review convention* (CONTRIBUTING.md §License with C/C++/Python/Java/Go/Bash examples; .gemini/styleguide.md echoes it) with NO CI gate, so it's review-enforced not machine-enforced. (5) SUPPLY-CHAIN — OSSF scorecards + OSV scanning workflows = D. (6) META — CITATION.cff schema-validated by cffconvert in CI (D); workflows declare least-privilege `permissions:` blocks; .bazelversion pins the toolchain. Commit discipline is light (no DCO sign-off enforced).

**What alint replaces.** code_check_full.bats:290 'no duplicate files on Windows' (case-insensitive path collision) -> no_case_conflicts (whole-path case-only collision; EXACT semantic match) OR unique_by + case_insensitive: true; CONTRIBUTING §License Apache-2.0 C/C++ header review convention -> file_header (pattern on tensorflow/core|c|cc/**/*.{cc,h}); .bazelversion / .clang-format / CITATION.cff / SECURITY.md / CODEOWNERS presence -> file_exists; workflows declare top-level permissions: -> file_content_matches '(?m)^permissions:'

**What alint improves.** Consolidates the scattered bats greps + the documented-but-ungated header convention into one declarative, reviewable .alint.yml that runs on a fresh clone with zero Bazel/Python toolchain — TF's actual header rule is *prose in CONTRIBUTING.md* that nothing enforces, so alint converts an aspirational convention into an executable info-level gate. no_case_conflicts is a portable one-liner versus a hand-rolled bats lowercase-collision loop, and runs cross-platform in CI rather than only where bats is wired up.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config flagged exactly one C-gap (A2: 'unique_by has no case-fold' — recorded as level: off because key={name} would over-fire on legitimate same-basename files in different dirs). That gap is now CLOSED two ways: (a) no_case_conflicts is a precise native match for the bats:290 Windows-collision check, and (b) unique_by gained case_insensitive: true (verified in crates/alint-rules/src/unique_by.rs — Options.case_insensitive field with doc 'the case-insensitive-filesystem hazard (Windows/macOS)'). Everything else TF enforces (Bazel dep/license/CUDA queries, API golden test, buildifier/clang-format/pylint, OSV/scorecards) is execution = correct NON-GOAL, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** none needed beyond the shipped surface. (The post-build re-analysis row 101 confirms ~60%->~100% of addressable; the config's normalize:lower ask is superseded by no_case_conflicts.) If anything, a tiny convenience: a bundled 'bazel-monorepo' ruleset asserting .bazelversion + .bazelrc + buildifier-config presence — but that is packaging, not a new capability.

*Notes.* The config's A2 rule is the one substantive finding and it is now fully resolved — re-author A2 from 'C/off, partial' to a live no_case_conflicts rule scoped to tensorflow/**. The Apache-header A1 (file_header, level:info) is correct and remains the cleanest A. Verified live: unique_by.case_insensitive and no_case_conflicts both present in current build.

### `Textualize-rich` — Python TUI library (poetry build, Textualize single-governance, mid-size single-language)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~0% of ENFORCED surface is addressable because the enforced surface is ~100% execution (black/mypy/pytest/codespell/CodeQL = correct NON-GOAL). Of the small VALUE-ADD/non-execution structure layer (py.typed, poetry.lock, FAQ.md presence, pyproject value asserts), alint covers ~100%. Post-build row 102 records 0%->0% addressable-enforced, consistent.

**Enforcement surface.** rich's enforced surface is ~100% tool execution (category D), wired through a Makefile + GitHub Actions: (1) FORMATTERS/TYPE/TEST — black (format check), mypy (strict typing — rich ships PEP 561 and advertises `Typing :: Typed`), pytest + coverage, all run as CI gates = D. (2) SPELL — codespell over the tree = D. (3) SECURITY — CodeQL workflow = D. (4) BUILD/PACKAGING — poetry build + the pyproject [tool.poetry] table (name/version/license/classifiers); poetry.lock committed for reproducible dev installs (CONTRIBUTING: `poetry install`). (5) CODEGEN (NOT freshness-gated) — FAQ.md is generated by FAQtory from questions/*.question.md (faq.yml declares the source->output relationship) but rich does NOT enforce freshness; the generated FAQ.md is simply committed. (6) PROVENANCE — rich/_unicode_data/*.py carry a generated-from-Unicode-DB provenance banner on the data modules (but not on __init__.py/_versions.py). No license-header gate, no commit-discipline gate. Net: there is essentially no bespoke repo-STATE gate; the addressable surface is hygiene the python+oss-baseline+ci bundles already cover, plus a few structure-existence value-add asserts.

**What alint replaces.** PEP 561 rich/py.typed marker must ship (advertised Typing::Typed) -> file_exists; poetry.lock committed at root -> file_exists root_only; FAQ.md generated artifact committed -> file_exists; pyproject [tool.poetry] name/license/version assertions -> toml_path_equals/toml_path_matches (VERIFIED working — see gaps); trailing-whitespace/final-newline/merge-marker hygiene -> bundled python/oss-baseline; the ci/github-actions bundle covers workflow hygiene

**What alint improves.** alint asserts the *cross-file* PEP 561 invariant cheaply (Typing::Typed classifier in pyproject implies a py.typed marker on disk) — composable as for_each: classifier-present-then-marker-exists — which the repo never checks. It also gives a portable, toolchain-free presence gate for poetry.lock + the FAQtory-generated FAQ.md that today rely only on convention. And it can express FAQ FRESHNESS (which rich does NOT enforce) via generated_file_fresh outputs:/file_graph fresh if Textualize ever wanted it.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none — and one recorded 'C sharp-edge' is EMPIRICALLY REFUTED. The config (lines 47-49) claims toml_path on $.tool.poetry.license / $.tool.poetry.name 'produced no result — the [tool.poetry] nested table is not surfaced.' I reproduced this against the CURRENT build with a [tool.poetry] fixture: toml_path_equals $.tool.poetry.name correctly resolved to 'rich' (fired on a deliberate mismatch) and toml_path_matches $.tool.poetry.license matched ^MIT$. The claim was a v0.11.1-era artifact (the study ran against the published 0.11.1 binary); structured_path.rs parses via toml::from_str which handles nested tables natively. So this is an A, not a C. The second recorded item (mid-basename glob `unicode*.py` matched 0 files) was an AUTHORING mistake (wrong glob: should be `**/_unicode_data/*.py`), not a tool gap — and even the provenance-banner check is expressible via file_content_matches with per-tree paths excluding __init__/_versions. Everything else (black/mypy/pytest/codespell/CodeQL) is execution = NON-GOAL.

**Abstraction ideas.** A small composition idiom worth blessing as a bundled rule: 'PEP 561 coherence' — if pyproject classifiers include `Typing :: Typed` then `<package>/py.typed` must exist (cross_file/for_each from the classifier list to a marker path). Generally useful across the entire typed-Python ecosystem (not rich-specific). Otherwise nothing beyond the existing surface.

*Notes.* ACTION: the config's toml-nested-table 'C tuning finding' should be re-authored as live toml_path_equals/matches rules on $.tool.poetry.* — I verified it fires today. This is the single most important correction for this repo: the prior study systematically over-reported it as a gap because it tested 0.11.1.

### `tokio-rs-axum` — Rust cargo-workspace library cluster (axum, axum-core, axum-extra, axum-macros; community-governed)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — the three version-coherence checks (the only real bespoke gate) plus all the declarative deny.toml/workspace-lints/CHANGELOG/pin value-add. The enforced surface is otherwise ~execution (clippy/fmt/test/cargo-* = NON-GOAL). Post-build row 103 records ~75%->~100%.

**Enforcement surface.** axum's enforced surface splits cleanly. EXECUTION (D, orchestrated not reimplemented): clippy, rustfmt, the test matrix incl. test-msrv (MSRV 1.80) + test-nightly (axum-macros pins its own nightly toolchain for trybuild/UI tests), cargo-deny (license allowlist MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD-3-Clause/Unicode-3.0 + bans.multiple-versions=deny resolved over the graph), cargo-hack (feature powerset), cargo-public-api / external-types (public-API surface), typos, cargo-sort (Cargo.toml key ordering) — all = D. The DISTINCTIVE BESPOKE gate is a pure REVIEW CONVENTION with NO tool behind it: multiple Cargo.toml files carry the literal comment '# remember to bump the version that axum-extra depends on' — each sibling crate's [package].version is RESTATED in the path+version dependency declarations of the other crates (axum-core's version pinned in axum/ and axum-extra/ deps; axum-macros likewise; axum's version pinned in axum-extra's optional dep). cargo path-resolves the workspace build and IGNORES the version field, so nothing machine-enforces this equality — it is exactly the review-checklist denominator. Plus declarative config: deny.toml license allowlist + bans; workspace Cargo.toml [workspace.lints.rust] unsafe_code = 'forbid' (a load-bearing invariant); per-published-crate CHANGELOG.md (Keep-a-Changelog); CI.yml declares the MSRV env.

**What alint replaces.** the 'remember to bump the version axum-extra depends on' version-coherence battery (3 rules: axum-core/axum-macros/axum own [package].version == the path+version dep pins across axum/axum-extra) -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals) relation equals, extract toml $.package.version on source + regex on the dep-line targets — EXACTLY the right kind; deny.toml $.licenses.allow[*] allowlist -> toml_path_matches; deny.toml $.bans['multiple-versions']=='deny' -> toml_path_equals (note bracket notation for the dashed key); [workspace.lints.rust].unsafe_code=='forbid' -> toml_path_equals; per-crate CHANGELOG.md -> for_each_dir select:'axum*' + nested file_exists; CI.yml MSRV env -> file_content_matches; axum-macros/rust-toolchain pin -> file_exists

**What alint improves.** This is alint at its strongest: the version-coherence invariant is a documented HUMAN review convention ('remember to bump...') that NO tool enforces because cargo path-resolves — alint's cross_file equals turns three fragile reviewer-memory checklist items into a machine gate that fails CI on drift. The deny.toml/workspace-lints assertions let alint gate the DECLARATIONS (allowlist present, unsafe forbidden) on a fresh clone without invoking cargo-deny over the dep graph. for_each_dir select:'axum*' + {path} expresses 'every published crate keeps a CHANGELOG' declaratively versus an ad-hoc shell loop.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The config's three A-items are textbook cross_file equals and are correctly classified A; I confirmed the unified cross_file kind ships all needed relations (equals/subset/superset/set_equals/identical/resolves in crates/alint-rules/src/cross_file.rs) and for_each_dir accepts a glob select: ('axum*'). The remainder is value-add (deny.toml allowlist, per-crate CHANGELOG, MSRV/nightly pins) and the rest of axum's gates (clippy/fmt/test-matrix/cargo-deny/cargo-hack/external-types/typos/cargo-sort) are execution = correct NON-GOAL. No idiom falls short here.

**Abstraction ideas.** The version-restatement-across-crates pattern (own [package].version == every '<dep> = { path=..., version="X" }' pin) is a recurring Rust-workspace need (axum, tokio, many cargo-workspace clusters) that today takes one cross_file rule per dependent. A bundled 'cargo-workspace-version-coherence' rule (or a cross_file convenience that, given a crate, auto-derives the [package].version vs all sibling path+version pins) would collapse N hand-written rules into one declaration — a clean, grounded composition primitive. Also: bundled/rust could optionally assert [workspace.lints.rust].unsafe_code policy.

*Notes.* Re-author note: migrate the three rules from kind: cross_file_value_equals to the unified kind: cross_file (alias still accepted) for forward-consistency; behavior identical. The dashed-key $.bans['multiple-versions'] correctly uses bracket notation (the documented JSONPath idiom). This config needs essentially no correction — it was authored well and is fully current.

### `tokio-rs-tokio` — Rust 10-member Cargo workspace with members at the repo ROOT (no crates/ prefix); 5 published crates (tokio, tokio-macros, tokio-test, tokio-stream, tokio-util) + 5 internal publish=false crates; MIT/no-DCO; all structural validation lives in .github/workflows (no ci/ dir, no hack/verify scripts).

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~95% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface. Of tokio's repo-state checks, only the single spellcheck.dic header-count invariant is unexpressible; the README mirror, version-in-README, full MSRV coherence, dep-pin coherence, sorted-dic, trailing-whitespace, and ALL workspace/manifest invariants are replaceable (two of the config's three self-listed gaps are now closed by cross_file identical + set_equals). The huge COMPILE/EXECUTE matrix (miri/asan/valgrind/cross/wasm/clippy/semver/spellcheck-engine) is ~all execution = correctly NON-GOAL; alint orchestrates those via command/command_idempotent but does not reimplement them.

**Enforcement surface.** ci.yml is ~1390 lines of matrix cargo invocations. EXECUTION gates (D, correctly out of scope): rustfmt --check (fmt job over `git ls-files '*.rs'`), cargo clippy --workspace (2 feature passes incl. RUSTFLAGS=--cfg tokio_unstable), cargo doc -Dwarnings, cargo-semver-checks per published crate, cargo-deny check (deny.toml, via audit.yml/pr-audit.yml), cargo-spellcheck --code 1, and a large COMPILE+EXECUTE matrix (test-tokio-full, test-workspace-*, cross-test/check, miri-{lib,test,doc}, asan, valgrind, wasm32-{unknown,wasip1,wasip2}, sgx/redox/freebsd/illumos, no-atomic-u64, test-hyper/test-quinn, check-external-types, check-fuzzing, cargo-hack feature-powerset/minimal-versions). REPO-STATE checks (the addressable core, mostly in shell within CI jobs): (1) check-readme job: `diff README.md tokio/README.md` (byte-mirror) + grep the tokio Cargo.toml version line appears in README.md; (2) check-spelling job: spellcheck.dic header-is-int + header-count==remaining-line-count + `sed 1d | sort -uc` (sorted+unique) + a repo-wide `grep -rne '\\s$'` trailing-whitespace gate; (3) ci.yml env `rust_min: '1.71'` is hand-synced (an explicit lines-23-31 comment lists the 5 files: tokio/tokio-util/tokio-test/tokio-stream Cargo.toml + README/CONTRIBUTING prose) — the classic MSRV drift surface; (4) workspace/manifest invariants the repo relies on: per-crate license=MIT, edition 2021/2024, internal crates publish=false, every member [lints] workspace=true, the [patch.crates-io] tokio->local-path block, deny.toml license allowlist non-empty + bans.wildcards=deny; (5) presence of ci.yml/audit.yml/pr-audit.yml/deny.toml/spellcheck.toml/spellcheck.dic + per-published-crate CHANGELOG/README + tests-integration [[bin]] src files.

**What alint replaces.** README byte-mirror (diff README.md tokio/README.md) -> cross_file relation:identical (skip_header_lines:0) — REPLACES the config's pair_hash workaround AND the config's own listed candidate #2; tokio version current in README -> cross_file (equals, toml extract $.package.version source, regex extract in README target); MSRV rust_min coherence across 4 published Cargo.toml + ci.yml rust_min regex -> cross_file (equals, one source + 5 targets); tokio-util dep major matches tokio major -> cross_file (equals, regex extracts); spellcheck.dic sorted+unique after the int header -> ordered_block (start:^\d+$, end:\z, unique:true); trailing whitespace repo-wide -> no_trailing_whitespace (+ file_trim_trailing_whitespace fixer); deny.toml license allowlist non-empty -> toml_path_matches; deny.toml bans.wildcards=deny -> toml_path_equals; per-crate license=MIT / edition / publish=false / [lints] workspace=true / [patch.crates-io] tokio.path -> toml_path_equals + toml_path_matches; per-crate CHANGELOG/README -> for_each_dir + file_exists; tests-integration bin src files + workflow/config presence -> file_exists; shell-outs cargo fmt --check / clippy / doc / deny / semver-checks / spellcheck -> command + command_idempotent.

**What alint improves.** BIG correction vs the config's own NEW-RULE-KIND candidate list (#2 and #3 are now SHIPPED, so two of its three claimed gaps are closed): (a) candidate #2 cross_file_value_equals(lines) full-file-equality-with-diff EXISTS as cross_file relation:identical — so the README mirror should move OFF pair_hash (which only says 'digests differ') ONTO cross_file identical, which reports `<file> is not byte-identical to <file>` directly (real diff-on-mismatch). (b) candidate #3 toml_member_set_equals — assert [workspace] members set == [patch.crates-io] keys — is now expressible as cross_file relation:set_equals between two TOML JSONPaths in Cargo.toml (set_equals reports BOTH missing and extra; verified set_equals_reports_both_missing_and_extra test), upgrading the config's single-key toml_path_equals patch check into a full set-coherence check. More broadly: tokio's MSRV/version/mirror coherence is enforced today only by bespoke shell in a 1390-line ci.yml with a hand-maintained 'also update these 5 files' COMMENT — alint consolidates all of it into declarative, schema-validated rules that run WITHOUT cargo/rustup, on a fresh clone, in milliseconds, and catch the exact drift the comment warns about.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE genuine gap remains: spellcheck.dic's header-count == remaining-line-count invariant (the config's candidate #1 `line_count_header`: first line is an integer equal to wc -l of the rest). I tried (a) file_min/max_lines — they take a FIXED literal N, not a value read from the file's own first line, so they cannot express a self-referential count; (b) ordered_block — sorts/dedups but does not count; (c) cross_file — can extract values from two files but cannot compare a numeric line-1 token against the file's own line-count (there's no 'count of remaining lines' extractor, and no arithmetic/identity between a field and a derived line count). Only the 'header is an int' half is expressible (file_content_matches `\A\d+\n`). This is a narrow but real primitive (generic to any `N\n<N lines>` manifest: spellcheck dictionaries, some hosts files, GNU gettext .po-ish headers). Everything else the config listed as a gap is now closed.

**Abstraction ideas.** NEW KIND `line_count_header` (or a more general `self_referential_count`): assert a captured numeric field (file's first line, or a regex-captured `count: N`) equals the count of matching lines/records in the same file — directly closes tokio's last gap and generalizes to any N-prefixed manifest. Second (composition, no new kind): document the cross_file set_equals idiom for 'two key-sets in one TOML must agree' as the canonical replacement for ad-hoc workspace<->patch coherence — many Cargo/pnpm/Bazel monorepos need it. Third: a `cargo-workspace@v1` enhancement adding an MSRV-coherence helper (one cross_file equals over [package].rust-version across all published members + an optional ci.yml rust_min target) since hand-synced MSRV across N crates + CI is a near-universal Rust-workspace footgun that tokio documents with a maintenance comment.

*Notes.* This is the post-v0.10/v0.11 re-analysis config (examples/tokio-rs-tokio/.alint.yml), and it ALREADY uses cross_file_value_equals + ordered_block + pair_hash — but it predates cross_file relation:identical and set_equals, so its own candidate list (#2, #3) is now stale and should be trimmed; only #1 line_count_header survives as a real gap. The pair_hash README-mirror rule is functional but strictly inferior to cross_file identical now (no diff on mismatch) — a worthwhile config upgrade. Matches the memory's tokio calibration (coverage_today ~71%); the residual non-coverage is dominated by the execution matrix (correct NON-GOAL), not by alint gaps.

### `valkey-io-valkey` — C systems-DB server (C99, make+cmake dual build, Linux Foundation / TSC governance; redis fork with provenance tracking)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface: the one repo-state gate (commands.def freshness, now mutating-mode), DCO sign-off, and all the governance/SPDX/tooling-config value-add. The repo's ENFORCED surface is ~95%+ execution (make -Werror matrix, Tcl/gtest, ASan/UBSan/Valgrind, clang-format/yamlfmt, typos, ajv/jsonschema, CodeQL/Coverity, cross-repo provenance) = correct NON-GOAL. Post-build row 104 records 0%->~0% addressable-enforced; that 0% reflects how little valkey gates declaratively, not an alint limitation.

**Enforcement surface.** valkey's enforced surface is overwhelmingly execution (D), but it has ONE repo-state gate that is the canonical motivating case for v0.12's headline feature. By category: (1) BUILD/TEST matrix — ci.yml/daily.yml run a `make -Werror` build matrix (ubuntu/macos/debian/alma/alpine/freebsd/s390x/arm/32bit; libc & jemalloc; TLS builtin+module; RDMA) + the Tcl integration suite (runtest/-cluster/-sentinel/-moduleapi) + gtest units + ASan/UBSan/Valgrind = D (the matrix collapses to a few distinct behaviors). (2) FORMATTERS — clang-format-18 -i + git diff (clang-format.yml; src/.clang-format); yamlfmt -lint (ci.yml format-yaml; .config/format.yml) = D. (3) SPELL — typos (.config/typos.toml) = D. (4) SEMANTIC schema linters — node reply_schema_linter.js (ajv compile) + jsonschema reply-response validator = D. (5) PROVENANCE — valkey-io/verify-provenance (cross-repo blame/graph against redis history) = D, genuinely cross-repo. (6) SAST/coverage — CodeQL/Coverity/scorecard/codecov = D. (7) COMMIT-DISCIPLINE — DCO Signed-off-by required (CONTRIBUTING + copilot-instructions). (8) THE ONE REPO-STATE GATE — ci.yml:47-52 codegen freshness: `touch src/commands/ping.json && make commands.def && git diff` fails if dirty. src/commands.def is a CHECKED-IN generated file produced from ~425 src/commands/*.json descriptors via utils/generate-command-code.py (src/Makefile:765-766), written IN-PLACE (f.write to src/commands.def, must run from src/ with the build toolchain — NOT stdout). Also committed generated sources commands.c/fmtargs.h. Governance: GOVERNANCE/MAINTAINERS/CODE_OF_CONDUCT/SECURITY/CONTRIBUTING docs; COPYING declares BSD-3-Clause SPDX; only 42/211 src/*.{c,h} carry an SPDX header (6 non-canonical 'BSD 3-Clause' form) with NO header gate.

**What alint replaces.** the commands.def codegen-freshness gate (ci.yml:47-52: generator mutates src/commands.def in place then git diff) -> generated_file_fresh MUTATING mode (outputs: 'src/commands.def', command: ['sh','-c','cd src && make -s commands.def']) — the snapshot/run/diff/restore mode that SHIPPED 2026-06-03 and whose design doc names valkey+redis as THE motivating case; DCO Signed-off-by -> git_commit_signed_off (reads commit-range metadata; CONFIG WRONGLY classed this D 'alint sees only the working tree'); governance docs presence -> file_exists root_only; COPYING BSD-3-Clause SPDX -> file_content_matches; tooling configs (.config/typos.toml, .config/format.yml, src/.clang-format, dependabot.yml) presence -> file_exists; committed generated sources + src/commands/*.json non-empty -> file_exists; the no-tracked-artifacts + ci/github-actions + oss-baseline bundles

**What alint improves.** Two material wins over the repo's status quo. (1) The codegen-freshness gate: valkey's CI recipe needs make + the C/Python build toolchain wired into CI; alint's generated_file_fresh outputs: mode expresses the SAME 'run generator -> git diff --exit-code -> restore tree' contract as a single reviewable declaration, panic-safe (Drop-guard restore so `alint check` stays pure), with the generator command pinned in config — and crucially it is the ONLY non-stdout codegen shape, which the prior config could not express at all (it had to be commented out). (2) git_commit_signed_off gives DCO enforcement that the config incorrectly believed was impossible. alint also makes the 'a CI gate silently no-ops if its config file vanishes' failure mode explicit via file_exists on the tooling configs.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that alint should close. The config's #1 C-gap — 'generated_file_fresh writes to stdout only; valkey's generator mutates in place + needs the build env, so the clean native expression is unavailable' — is now CLOSED by the mutating outputs: mode (verified present in crates/alint-rules/src/generated_file_fresh.rs: Mode::Mutating{outputs,globs} + OutputRestorer snapshot/restore; the design doc generated_file_fresh_mutating.md example IS outputs:'src/commands.def'). The build-env coupling (needs make+toolchain in CI) is a runtime prerequisite, not an expressibility gap — alint orchestrates the declared command. The DCO claim was a stale D-classification, now A via git_commit_signed_off. The ONLY thing alint genuinely cannot do is the cross-repo verify-provenance (blame/graph against the upstream redis history) — but that is a bespoke cross-REPOSITORY supply-chain tool, a correct NON-GOAL, not a within-repo gap. The SPDX header rule is intentionally not enabled (would fail ~169 ungated files) = a policy choice, not a tool gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** none beyond shipped. valkey is essentially the poster child that the mutating generated_file_fresh mode was BUILT for (named alongside redis in the design doc), so the abstraction it needed already landed. A marginal nicety: a bundled 'linux-foundation-governance' ruleset (GOVERNANCE/MAINTAINERS/CODE_OF_CONDUCT/SECURITY/CONTRIBUTING + SPDX-in-COPYING) mirroring apache/governance — packaging of existing kinds, not a new capability.

*Notes.* ACTION: uncomment + re-author the valkey-commands-def-fresh rule as generated_file_fresh with outputs: 'src/commands.def' (mutating mode) instead of the commented stdout-mode draft — the mode shipped 2026-06-03 and this repo is its canonical example. Add git_commit_signed_off (with a since: for PR-CI) and drop the 'DCO is D' note. These two are the substantive corrections; the rest of the config (governance/SPDX/tooling presence value-add) is correct and current.
