### `vapor/vapor` — Swift web framework (SwiftPM package), community governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of addressable surface — but the addressable surface is tiny because Vapor enforces almost nothing in-repo. Of Vapor's actual gates, ~all are execution (swift test / CodeQL = NON-GOAL); everything alint does here is value-add structure on top of a repo with essentially zero declarative state-checks. So: '~0% of an enforced denominator (it's all execution), but 100% of the structural surface a reviewer would want — pure value-add.'

**Enforcement surface.** Vapor's machine-enforced validation is almost entirely DELEGATED out of the repo. (1) CI/test execution: GitHub workflows call EXTERNAL reusable workflows from `vapor/ci` — `run-unit-tests.yml` (swift test across Swift toolchain matrix + platforms) and `submit-deps.yml` (dependency-graph submission). These are pure tool-execution (swift build/test). (2) Security scanning: CodeQL (code-scanning workflow) — SAST execution. (3) Sponsors automation: `sponsors.yml` (workflow_dispatch) regenerates the README block between `<!-- backers -->`/`<!-- sponsors -->` markers — a generator, not a PR/push gate. (4) Structure/layout: standard SwiftPM tree (root `Package.swift` with `// swift-tools-version:` first line; `Sources/`, `Tests/`; a C shim target `Sources/CVaporBcrypt` with paired .c/.h). (5) Compliance: `NOTICES.txt` for third-party attribution; `AGENTS.md` AI-contribution policy; contribution guidelines at the non-standard `.github/contributing.md`. CRITICALLY ABSENT (per the study): NO in-repo formatter/linter config (no swift-format/SwiftLint config committed), NO lockfile (`Package.resolved` is gitignored), NO codegen-freshness git-diff gate, and effectively NO per-file license headers (only 2 of ~293 sources carry SPDX). So the repo gates on essentially nothing alint-addressable beyond 'tests pass' + 'CodeQL' — everything in the config is alint VALUE-ADD, not coverage of an existing enforced check. Commit-discipline: none machine-enforced (no commitlint/hooks).

**What alint replaces.** Root manifest present -> file_exists (vapor-package-manifest-exists); swift-tools-version on line 1 -> file_header with (?m)^ anchor (vapor-swift-tools-version-declared); NOTICES.txt/AGENTS.md/CONTRIBUTING discoverability -> file_exists (vapor-notices-exists, vapor-agents-policy-exists, vapor-contributing-exists with the .github/contributing.md fallback path); README backers markers + docs link -> file_content_matches (vapor-readme-backers-markers, vapor-readme-mentions-docs); workflow has a name -> yaml_path_matches $.name (vapor-test-workflow-named); C-shim .c/.h pairing -> pair primary/partner {stem} (vapor-cbcrypt-c-has-header). Plus the inherited oss-baseline bundle (README/LICENSE/SECURITY/.gitignore existence + hygiene).

**What alint improves.** alint is the ONLY repo-state safety net here, since Vapor itself outsources all enforcement to vapor/ci and CodeQL. (1) The README `<!-- backers -->` marker assertion is materially better than the status quo: the sponsors generator silently no-ops if the markers are ever deleted, and nothing in Vapor catches that — a one-line file_content_matches turns a silent generator failure into a CI/pre-commit error. (2) The .c/.h pairing for CVaporBcrypt is a real cross-file invariant Vapor relies on implicitly but has zero gate for; `pair` with {stem} expresses it declaratively without any toolchain. (3) Asserting swift-tools-version is the literal first line catches a class of Package.swift corruption that `swift package` only surfaces at build time. All of this runs WITHOUT a Swift toolchain installed (the config's whole point: structural review without compiling).

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. This repo's enforced surface is split cleanly between execution (swift test / CodeQL — correct NON-GOALs, orchestratable via command if desired) and structure (fully covered above). The config makes no gap (B) claims, and re-examination confirms there is nothing Vapor validates that alint cannot express. The one thing one might wish for — asserting Package.resolved coherence — is moot because Vapor deliberately gitignores it.

**Abstraction ideas.** A first-class `swift@v1` / SwiftPM bundled ruleset is the obvious win and the config explicitly notes its absence ('There is no swift bundle yet, so we extend oss-baseline only'). It would bundle: root Package.swift exists + swift-tools-version header, Sources/Tests layout, NOTICES/SPDX-header policy, and the C-shim pairing idiom — making configs like this a 3-line `extends`. This is purely composing EXISTING kinds (no new rule kind), exactly like the shipped go/rust/dotnet bundles. Lower-value: a `marker_block_intact` convenience (assert a generator's `<!-- begin -->`/`<!-- end -->` delimiters both exist and are correctly ordered) generalizing the sponsors-marker pattern seen across many README-generator repos — but file_content_matches already covers the 90% case.

*Notes.* Cleanest 'value-add only' case of the four. The config is honest that nothing here is coverage of an enforced check. Strongest argument for a swift@v1 bundle. No reproduce-first work needed — no gaps were claimed and none exist.

### `vercel/next.js` — Very large JS/TS + Rust web-framework monorepo (pnpm workspace ~19 packages lerna-locked + 64-member cargo workspace)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~70% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface. The repo-state gates are: 3 cross-file coherence (fully A, and previously UNENFORCED — pure upgrade), 3 codegen-freshness gates check_error_codes/check-examples/check-precompiled (all A via command_idempotent or generated_file_fresh mutating mode — 2 wired, 1 under-claimed), structure/naming/supply-chain (all A). The remaining ~30% addressable is validate-externals-doc (IMPROVABLE, under-claimed). The big-headcount jobs (tsc/eslint/clippy/prettier/ast-grep) are execution = correctly out of scope, so they're not in the addressable denominator.

**Enforcement surface.** Enforcement concentrates in `.github/workflows/build_and_test.yml` (~32 jobs behind a tests-pass aggregator) + `build_reusable.yml`. The repo-STATE (read-the-tree, not compile) gates: (1) lint job runs `pnpm lint-no-typescript` = prettier --check / eslint / alex (prose) / ast-grep (19 structural rules) — all TOOL-EXEC; PLUS `pnpm check-examples` (normalize examples/*/package.json + next-env.d.ts + .gitignore, then `git status` clean = codegen/normalize freshness) and `pnpm validate-externals-doc` (a jsonc list <-> mdx list sync check). (2) check-types-precompiled runs tsc (exec) + `pnpm check-precompiled` (ncc re-bundle + git-diff = codegen freshness). (3) build/taskr runs `check_error_codes` (scan source for new Error()s -> consolidate into packages/next/errors.json -> git-diff = codegen freshness). (4) rust-check/rustdoc-check = clippy/cargo-doc (exec); ast-grep job = structural AST scan (exec). Hooks: `.husky/pre-commit` = lint-staged (prettier+eslint+rustfmt = exec); `.husky/pre-push` guards against pushing to `canary` (workflow policy, not content). CROSS-FILE coherence invariants (the alint sweet spot, several only as in-file 'if you change this also update X' comments): rust-toolchain.toml `channel` == .devcontainer/rust feature `version`; all packages/* version agree (lerna version.exact:true, source of truth lerna.json); pnpm-workspace.yaml security settings (blockExoticSubdeps/minimumReleaseAge) mirrored into scripts/install-native.mjs. Supply-chain: .npmrc lockfile=true + packageManager pnpm pin + .gitignore bans package-lock.json. Conventions (AGENTS.md/contributing): read READMEs up the tree, signed commits — social or exec.

**What alint replaces.** rust-toolchain<->devcontainer drift -> cross_file (alias cross_file_value_equals) toml extract vs anchored regex extract (next-rust-toolchain-devcontainer-coherence; correctly anchors the NESTED version not the bare "0.0.0" feature version); lerna lockstep package versions -> cross_file source=lerna.json $.version vs 5 package.json $.version (next-package-version-coherence); pnpm security mirror -> cross_file regex-vs-regex on minimumReleaseAge (next-pnpm-security-minrelease-coherence); error-codes freshness gate -> command_idempotent wrapping check-error-codes (next-error-codes-fresh); examples normalize-then-diff gate -> command_idempotent wrapping check-examples.sh (next-examples-normalized); docs NN-slug ordered-prefix naming -> filename_regex with include globs (next-docs-numbered-prefix); forbid npm/yarn lockfiles in a pnpm repo -> file_absent (next-no-foreign-lockfiles); packageManager pins pnpm -> json_path_matches $.packageManager (next-packagemanager-is-pnpm); blockExoticSubdeps + eol=lf gitattributes + sgconfig.yml present -> file_content_matches / file_exists. Plus the 5 inherited bundles (oss-baseline + node + monorepo + ci/github-actions + hygiene/no-tracked-artifacts).

**What alint improves.** (1) The three cross-file coherence invariants are the headline win: today they survive only as ENGLISH COMMENTS ('if you update this, also update...') at the top of rust-toolchain.toml and pnpm-workspace.yaml, with NO machine gate at all — a botched manual sync ships silently. cross_file turns each into an error-level rule. The lerna lockstep check is especially valuable: lerna only enforces the bump AS IT RUNS, so a hand-edited package.json that drifts post-bump has no guard; one declarative rule covers all 19 packages. (2) check_error_codes and check-examples are bespoke per-repo scripts hidden inside taskr/lint jobs; command_idempotent gives them a single uniform `alint check` entrypoint with attributed messages, runnable locally without the full CI harness. (3) The devcontainer regex correctly dodges the documented R4 false-positive (a naive '"version":...' grabs the feature's "0.0.0") — schema-validated config beats a hand-rolled grep.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none that survive the reproduce-first lens. The one candidate is `pnpm validate-externals-doc` (a jsonc allow-list must equal an mdx-rendered list). I tried to express it: if both sides are extractable as token sets it is a textbook `cross_file set_equals` (extract: lines or regex on each, BTreeSet compare) — and the config author chose not to add it only because the mdx side needs format-specific token extraction, not because alint can't compare sets. If the two lists are plain enough to regex (very likely — it's a doc of external package names), it IS expressible. So I do NOT count it a gap; at worst it's an IMPROVABLE the config under-claimed. Everything else is either covered (above) or a deliberate NON-GOAL: tsc/eslint/clippy/prettier/alex/ast-grep are all execution/AST linting (category D) that alint orchestrates via command but does not reimplement. check-precompiled (ncc re-bundle + git-diff) is generated_file_fresh-shaped (mutating `outputs:` mode) and could be added as an A item — another under-claim, not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) The 'lockstep version across N workspace packages' pattern recurs in EVERY lerna/changesets monorepo; a `workspace_versions_agree` convenience (or a parameter on the monorepo bundle: 'all packages/* package.json $.version == <source>') would collapse the 5-target boilerplate here into one line — composes cross_file, no new kind. (2) A `nextjs@v1` or broader 'lerna-locked pnpm monorepo' bundle could ship the packageManager-pin + no-foreign-lockfile + blockExoticSubdeps + eol=lf set as defaults. (3) A composition primitive for 'set extracted from file A (format X) equals set extracted from file B (format Y, e.g. mdx)' with per-side extractors would make jsonc<->mdx list-sync (validate-externals-doc) trivially first-class — but this is really just documenting that cross_file set_equals already takes a regex extractor per side.

*Notes.* The cross-file coherence trio is the single most compelling alint demo in this whole group — three real drift bugs that today have ONLY a code comment guarding them. The config is solid; if anything it UNDER-claims (validate-externals-doc and check-precompiled are both expressible as A). No B (gap) claims were made and none are warranted.

### `vercel/turborepo` — Dual-stack Rust (54-crate cargo workspace) + TS (pnpm ~30 packages) build-tool monorepo, single-vendor (Vercel)

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE surface. All 4 repo-STATE checks (turbo_types_check, check-openapi, toolchain-sync, fmt-width-sync) are natively expressible and wired. The large remainder (cargo/clippy/deny/nextest/oxlint/tsc/attw/check-examples-sandbox/check-lockfiles/check-links/lint-pr-title) is execution or non-tree (PR title) = correctly out of the addressable denominator. So among checks alint COULD address, coverage is complete.

**Enforcement surface.** All bespoke validation lives in `.github/workflows/**` + `.husky/pre-push` + a handful of `*-check` turbo tasks. Enforced STATE checks (answerable by reading the tree): (1) `turbo_types_check` — regenerate schema.json + config-v2.ts from `turborepo-schema-gen` (a Rust generator), then `git diff --exit-code` = codegen freshness. (2) `check-openapi` — `apps/docs/scripts/generate-openapi-docs.ts --check` diffs generated MDX/JSON vs committed and exits 1 on drift = codegen freshness. (3) rust-toolchain.toml `channel` hand-synced to `packages/turbo-repository/scripts/build.sh` (the file's own 'Needs to be copied to ...' comment). (4) .rustfmt.toml `max_width=100` reciprocally 'Keep in sync with .editorconfig' <-> .editorconfig [*.rs] max_line_length=100. Enforced TOOL-EXEC (D): cargo fmt --check / cargo clippy(`cargo lint`) / cargo check / cargo nextest (3 OS) / cargo-deny check licenses / turbo run quality (oxlint+oxfmt+taplo) / check-types(tsc) / package-checks(attw) / check-examples(sandbox run) / check-lockfiles(parser integration tests) / check-links(doc link graph) / lint-pr-title (conventional-commit on PR TITLE). Declared-but-tool-enforced policy: deny.toml MIT/Apache allowlist, Cargo.toml [lints.clippy] unwrap_used=deny, .taplo.toml reorder_keys=true. ABSENT: NO per-file SPDX (0/488 *.rs), NO dependabot/renovate, NO pre-commit/lefthook (husky pre-push only), NO root Makefile/justfile; Cargo.lock + pnpm-lock.yaml ARE tracked. Versions are NOT statically coherent (crates/turborepo=0.1.0, cli/package.json=1.0.0, version.txt=2.9.17-canary.2 — stamped at release) so NO version-equality rule applies.

**What alint replaces.** rust-toolchain channel <-> build.sh hand-sync -> cross_file toml extract vs regex extract (turbo-rust-toolchain-channel-coherence); rustfmt max_width <-> editorconfig max_line_length reciprocal sync -> cross_file regex-vs-regex (turbo-fmt-width-coherence); turbo_types_check codegen freshness -> generated_file_fresh STDOUT mode (file=schema.json, command=cargo run -p turborepo-schema-gen ... -o -) (turbo-types-schema-fresh); check-openapi --check freshness -> command_idempotent (turbo-openapi-docs-fresh); declared policy mirrors (deny.toml allowlist, clippy unwrap_used=deny, taplo reorder_keys) -> file_content_matches; governance files (CODEOWNERS/CONTRIBUTING/pnpm-workspace.yaml/rust-toolchain.toml present) -> file_exists. PLUS the orchestration wrappers (cargo fmt/clippy/deny/oxlint via command/command_idempotent — value-add single entrypoint). PLUS 8 inherited bundles (oss-baseline + rust + node + cargo-workspace + pnpm-workspace + ci/github-actions + no-tracked-artifacts + editorconfig).

**What alint improves.** (1) The rustfmt<->editorconfig and toolchain<->build.sh syncs are again guarded today only by RECIPROCAL ENGLISH COMMENTS ('Keep in sync', 'Needs to be copied to') — zero machine enforcement; cross_file makes both error-level. This is the same high-value pattern as next.js but doubled (two independent hand-syncs). (2) turbo_types_check and check-openapi are bespoke generators buried in CI; generated_file_fresh STDOUT mode is a perfect fit for the schema-gen case (it emits to stdout via `-o -`, exactly the model) and gives a clean, attributed, locally-runnable check. (3) The orchestration block (cargo fmt/clippy/deny/oxlint behind one `alint check`) consolidates four separate CI invocations into one declarative, reviewable file — the canonical 'N scripts -> one config' win, even though the underlying tools are D. (4) editorconfig bundle + the explicit width-coherence rule together close the loop the repo only documents.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Re-examined every enforced check: the 4 STATE checks are all A (2 codegen-freshness, 2 cross-file — all wired correctly). Everything else is execution (cargo/oxlint/tsc/attw/nextest/check-links/check-examples-sandbox) = deliberate NON-GOAL, correctly left as D and partially orchestrated via command. `lint-pr-title` (conventional-commit on the PR TITLE) is NOT a repo-content check at all — it validates a GitHub PR field, outside any tree-linter's scope (git_commit_subject_matches validates COMMIT subjects, a different object; not a gap, just a different surface). check-lockfiles is a parser INTEGRATION TEST (executes turborepo's lockfile parsers against fixtures) = execution, not 'is the lockfile present/coherent'. No B claim is made and none is warranted.

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) A `reciprocal_sync` / bidirectional flavor of cross_file would be ergonomic for the 'two files each say keep-in-sync with the other' pattern that appears TWICE here (toolchain/build.sh and rustfmt/editorconfig) and in next.js — today you write a one-directional rule and rely on the comment for the other direction; a symmetric form (or just documenting that one cross_file rule suffices since equality is symmetric) is a small DX nicety. (2) A `turbo`/`oxc-stack` bundle could ship the cargo+pnpm dual-workspace defaults + editorconfig/rustfmt width-coherence + taplo/clippy policy mirrors. (3) Nothing requires a new rule KIND — this repo is fully served by the current surface, which is itself evidence the v0.12 build closed the relevant gaps.

*Notes.* Twin of next.js but with TWO documented hand-syncs (strongest cross_file showcase). generated_file_fresh STDOUT mode is an exact fit for schema-gen's `-o -` output. The version-incoherence note is a sharp, honest observation: the config correctly DECLINES to add a version-equality rule because turborepo's three version strings are intentionally divergent (release-time stamped) — a good example of NOT over-applying cross_file.

### `vim/vim` — C (+ Vim Script) editor, autotools + make build, community governance

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~75% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface, materially higher than the original config's self-assessment now that the build landed. Addressable group-C checks: editorconfig whitespace policy (A, +fixers), codestyle line-regex subset (A), no-exec-bit on test inputs (A via executable_bit — was B), Filelist orphans (A/improvable via registry regex-source — was B), proto/vimtags in-place codegen freshness (A via generated_file_fresh outputs mode — was B). REMAINING unaddressable-but-should-be: hlgroups set-minus-ignore (genuine gap, ~1 check) and utf-8 round-trip (soft gap, ~1 check). The huge build/test/ASan/Coverity/CodeQL/codecov/lychee/codestyle-AST surface is execution = correctly NON-GOAL, not in the addressable denominator.

**Enforcement surface.** vim's enforced bespoke validation lives almost entirely in `.github/workflows/**` (reusable ci-linux/macos/windows/asan workflows + two composite actions) plus `ci/*.make` and runtime Makefile targets driven by the `matrix.extra` knob in ci-linux.yml. NO pre-commit/lefthook/husky/committed git hook, NO scripts/ validator beyond ci/, NO commitlint (the strict commit format in AGENTS.md is review-enforced only). Splits into: (A) EXECUTION/SEMANTIC (D): build+test across OS×features(tiny/normal/huge)×compilers(gcc/clang)×arch(native/arm64/i386)+FreeBSD via .cirrus.yml; ASan/UBSan (ci-linux_asan.yml); `make -C src/testdir codestyle` (test_codestyle.vim — a Vim-SCRIPT AST/style TEST that compiles+runs vim over the sources); Coverity; CodeQL (cpp+python); codecov; lychee link-check (ci/lychee.toml). (B) CODEGEN-FRESHNESS git-diff gates via matrix.extra: `proto` (cproto regenerates src/proto/*.pro), `vimtags`/`make html` (runtime doc tags), `preproc_indent` — each runs a GENERATOR then `git diff --exit-code` (in-place tree mutation). (C) STRUCTURE/CROSS-FILE invariants: Filelist completeness (ci/unlisted.make: diff git-ls-files vs Filelist patterns — orphan detection, Filelist is MAKEFILE-VARIABLE syntax with `VAR = \` continuations); hlgroups doc<->highlight.c coherence (ci/hlgroups.make: doc `*hl-X*` set must equal highlight.c `default link X` set MINUS ci/hlgroups.ignore); utf-8 runtime encoding round-trip (ci-linux.yml: `iconv -f utf-8 -t utf-8`); no-executable-bit on syntax-test inputs (runtime/syntax/Makefile: `find testdir/input -type f -perm /a+x`). .editorconfig declares trim_trailing_whitespace+insert_final_newline repo-wide with documented per-glob OPT-OUTS (markdown, runtime/doc/*.txt, test*.vim, *.ok), but vim runs NO editorconfig-checker in CI. The codestyle line-regex forbids (space-before-tab ` \t`, double-semicolon `;;+$`, trailing-ws) are plain per-line regex — alint's wheelhouse.

**What alint replaces.** CI orchestrator + composite actions + Filelist + hlgroups.ignore present -> file_exists / file_min_size (vim-ci-*-exists, vim-filelist-exists, vim-maintainers-registry-*); declared .editorconfig whitespace policy (on non-opted-out file types) -> no_trailing_whitespace + final_newline with **/-prefixed include globs + the documented testdir/libvterm/po/proto excludes (vim-no-trailing-whitespace-c, vim-final-newline-c) WITH fixers; the simple exclusion-free codestyle line forbids -> file_content_forbidden mirroring test_codestyle.vim's own hard-exclude list (vim-codestyle-no-space-before-tab ` \t`, vim-codestyle-no-double-semicolon `;;+$`); CONTRIBUTING->MAINTAINERS doc cross-link + AGENTS.md present -> file_content_matches / file_exists. PLUS inherited oss-baseline. NEW (post-build, NOT yet in config): B4 no-executable-bit on syntax-test inputs -> executable_bit require:false (now a SHIPPED kind); B1 Filelist orphan-detection -> registry_paths_resolve with a REGEX source extract + orphans mode (registry source now accepts extract.regex, so Make-variable token enumeration is reachable); the proto/vimtags in-place codegen-freshness gates -> generated_file_fresh MUTATING `outputs:` mode (snapshot/run/diff/restore — exactly the 'mutate tree then git-diff' shape).

**What alint improves.** (1) vim runs NO editorconfig-checker in CI despite shipping a detailed .editorconfig — alint's no_trailing_whitespace/final_newline with fixers turn the declared-but-unenforced policy into an actually-checked (and auto-fixable) one, respecting vim's own documented opt-outs. (2) The codestyle line-regex subset (trailing-ws/space-before-tab/double-semicolon) currently only runs INSIDE a full vim build (test_codestyle.vim must compile+run vim); file_content_forbidden runs them with zero toolchain — a contributor on any platform gets the feedback pre-build. (3) Consolidation: today Filelist-completeness, hlgroups-coherence, the encoding check, and the exec-bit check are four separate ci/*.make fragments invoked by an opaque matrix.extra knob; alint would express several of them in one reviewable declarative file. (4) The mutating-mode generated_file_fresh gives the proto/vimtags freshness gates a uniform local entrypoint instead of a CI-only `git diff --exit-code`.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** ONE narrow genuine gap survives reproduce-first; THREE prior B-claims are now OBSOLETE (the v0.12 build closed them) and must NOT be re-counted. OBSOLETE: B4 no_executable_bit — `executable_bit require:false` is now a shipped kind, exact fit for `find -perm /a+x`. The vim codegen 'mutates many files in-place then git-diffs' claim (config lines 45-49 say generated_file_fresh 'does not express' this) — FALSE as of the build: generated_file_fresh has a MUTATING `outputs:` mode that snapshots, runs, diffs the tree, and restores, i.e. precisely `make gen && git diff --exit-code`; proto/vimtags are now A (modulo needing the build toolchain at runtime, which is an execution cost, not an expressiveness gap). B1 Filelist orphans — the config says Make-variable syntax is unparseable by extract, but registry_paths_resolve's source now accepts an extract.regex, and captures_iter collects ALL token matches across the (multi-line) manifest text, so 'every git-tracked file is enumerated in Filelist' is reachable via registry orphans mode with a token regex (the `VAR = ... \` continuation is just text the regex scans); I'd call this IMPROVABLE-now / borderline, not a hard gap. The REMAINING genuine gap: B2 hlgroups symbol-set-equality MINUS an ignore-list. I TRIED cross_file set_equals (it extracts a regex/lines SET from each side and BTreeSet-compares) — but the hlgroups check subtracts a THIRD file's set (ci/hlgroups.ignore) from one side before comparing, and cross_file has only source/targets (no exemption/minus-set input, confirmed: only source.difference(actual) internally, no third-set subtraction). So the exact check — set(doc `*hl-X*`) == set(highlight.c `default link X`) \ set(hlgroups.ignore) — is NOT expressible as a single rule today. (Without the ignore-list it would be a clean cross_file set_equals; the gap is specifically the third-file exemption.) B3 file_encoding_valid (round-trip iconv utf-8 validity) is a SOFT gap: file_is_ascii is stricter (rejects all non-ASCII, wrong for UTF-8 runtime files) and no_bom is adjacent but different; there is no 'is valid UTF-8' primitive — but this is a thin, arguably low-value gap (malformed UTF-8 in a committed text file is rare).

**Abstraction ideas.** (1) The clear new-capability win is an EXEMPTION/IGNORE-SET on cross_file set relations: an optional `except:` taking a third file (extracted as a set) subtracted from the comparison, which would make the hlgroups doc<->highlight.c coherence (the one real residual gap) first-class — this is the single most-justified abstraction across this whole group, grounded in a concrete documented check. (2) A `file_encoding`/`valid_utf8` per-file kind (round-trip decode under a named encoding) would close B3 and serve the recurring 'keep runtime files valid UTF-8' need (also seen in i18n-heavy repos). (3) A `c@v1` / autotools bundle: vim's config explicitly notes 'No C/native ecosystem bundle exists,' forcing the codestyle/whitespace/exec-bit rules to be hand-written; a bundle composing executable_bit(require:false) on test inputs + the codestyle line-forbids + editorconfig-policy whitespace would make this reusable across the large C-tree corpus. (4) Document loudly that generated_file_fresh MUTATING mode covers 'generate-in-place then git-diff' so configs stop mis-recording it as a gap (this repo's config is now stale on that point).

*Notes.* By far the most gap-claiming config of the four, and the reproduce-first lens flips THREE of its four B-claims: executable_bit, generated_file_fresh mutating mode, and registry regex-source all postdate the config's authoring. The config's own NOTE (lines 45-49) asserting generated_file_fresh can't do in-place-then-diff is now factually stale. The ONLY hard residual gap is the third-file ignore-set subtraction in the hlgroups coherence check — a precise, well-evidenced motivator for an `except:` option on cross_file set relations. Also note the validated glob gotcha the config documents (a `src/**/*.c` pattern matches ZERO files; must use `**/`-prefixed globs) — a real authoring sharp-edge worth surfacing in docs.

### `vitejs-vite` — JS/TS pnpm-workspace monorepo (build tool / bundler); MIT; community-governed; published packages under packages/* (vite, create-vite, plugin-legacy).

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface — but that addressable surface is small: only the pnpm-only policy is actually ENFORCED-and-addressable. Of vite's enforced gates, ~95% are execution (oxfmt/eslint/tsc/vitest/actionlint/zizmor/LLM-template-check) = correctly NON-GOAL. So: ~100% of addressable-enforced replaced; the bulk of the config is high-value value-add (cross-package coherence vite doesn't gate on at all).

**Enforcement surface.** vite's ENFORCED gate surface is overwhelmingly tool-execution (category D): (1) FORMAT — `pnpm format` runs oxfmt over the whole source tree and CI asserts `git diff --exit-code` (so trailing-whitespace / final-newline / LF that .editorconfig declares are enforced by the formatter, NOT a bespoke grep). (2) LINT — eslint with typescript-eslint + import-x + eslint-plugin-n + eslint-plugin-regexp, AST/type-aware; includes a real import-layering FIREWALL in packages/vite/src/node/** via no-restricted-globals (require/__dirname/__filename) and n/no-restricted-require. (3) TYPECHECK — tsc per-package + a build-types-check (tsconfig.check.json) asserting bundled .d.ts don't leak devDependency types. (4) TEST — vitest unit + serve + build E2E across Node 20/22/24/26 x 3 OS. (5) WORKFLOW HYGIENE — actionlint + shellcheck on workflows, zizmor (GitHub-Actions security dataflow). (6) PR/issue — an LLM-agent template-check (non-deterministic semantic eval) + semantic-pull-request (conventional-commit regex on the PR TITLE, PR metadata not repo state). STRUCTURE/repo-state the repo actually GATES on: only the pnpm-only package-manager policy — package.json `preinstall: only-allow pnpm`, the corepack `packageManager: pnpm@<x.y.z>` pin, a committed pnpm-lock.yaml, and NO foreign lockfile. Everything else in the config (per-package engines.node coherence, license/type/repository.directory shape, per-package CHANGELOG/LICENSE, .gitattributes eol=lf, renovate pinDigests, simple-git-hooks wiring, workflow presence) are invariants vite HOLDS but does not gate on — alint value-add, not in the enforced denominator.

**What alint replaces.** pnpm-lock.yaml present -> file_exists; no foreign lockfile (package-lock.json/yarn.lock/bun.lockb/npm-shrinkwrap.json) -> file_absent; corepack pin `packageManager: pnpm@x.y.z` -> json_path_matches; per-package license:MIT / type:module / repository.directory -> json_path_matches on packages/*/package.json; per-package CHANGELOG.md + LICENSE* -> for_each_dir + nested file_exists; engines.node identical across the 4 manifests -> cross_file (equals); .gitattributes eol=lf + .editorconfig trim_trailing_whitespace + renovate pinDigests -> file_content_matches; simple-git-hooks pre-commit -> json_path_matches; zizmor.yml / commit-convention.md presence -> file_exists; plus the inherited bundles oss-baseline/node/monorepo/ci-github-actions/no-tracked-artifacts/editorconfig.

**What alint improves.** The whole bundle of cross-package coherence invariants (engines.node literal-equal across root + 3 published manifests; license/type/repository.directory uniformity; every published dir keeps CHANGELOG+LICENSE) is something vite has NO single gate for — alint expresses it as one declarative, reviewable file that runs WITHOUT installing Node/pnpm or the toolchain (a fresh-clone or CI-bootstrap reviewer can validate package-manager policy + workspace shape in milliseconds). The pnpm-only policy is today split across three mechanisms (preinstall script, corepack field, lockfile presence) that only fully bite at install time; alint asserts all three as static repo state in one place. Also: alint's no-tracked-artifacts + editorconfig bundles consolidate hygiene vite leaves implicit.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. The one structurally interesting check vite enforces that looks alint-shaped is the src/node import firewall (no require/__dirname/__filename), but (a) it is eslint/type-aware-enforced so it is correctly a NON-GOAL in the enforced denominator, and (b) alint's import_gate (js comment-aware preset) could in fact express the cheap syntactic half — so it is not even a gap, it's an IMPROVABLE value-add alint could offer as a pre-eslint fast check. The conventional-commit grammar lives on PR TITLES (PR metadata, not committed state) so it is genuinely outside alint's repo-state model — but that is a category boundary, not a missing capability (git_commit_subject_matches covers the commit-message analogue on actual commits).

**Abstraction ideas.** A bundled `pnpm-workspace@v1` (or generalize the existing monorepo ruleset with a package-manager-policy axis) that, given `manager: pnpm`, bundles: only-allow-<mgr> preinstall present + packageManager pin regex + the manager's lockfile present + all OTHER managers' lockfiles absent — vite re-derives this policy by hand and so does every pnpm/yarn-berry monorepo. Second idea: a cheap pre-eslint `import_gate` js preset shipped as part of node@v1 for the common `no require()/__dirname in ESM src` firewall, positioned explicitly as a fast syntactic gate that complements (not replaces) eslint's type-aware version — turns vite's eslint-only firewall into a layered check.

*Notes.* Config is exemplary in honesty: it explicitly separates the single enforced A-item from value-add and does NOT inflate coverage. No JSONC issue here (vite's manifests are plain JSON). The src/node firewall is the only place a reproduce-first reviewer might over-claim a gap — confirmed it's a deliberate D (eslint-enforced) with an alint value-add option, not a genuine gap.

---

### `withastro-astro` — JS/TS pnpm-workspace monorepo (web framework); MIT; community-governed; TypeScript project-references solution layout (root tsconfig.json references every package).

**Addressable replaceable today:** ~100% of the ADDRESSABLE (non-execution) surface, and the addressable surface is now genuinely larger than the study recorded because the JSONC blocker is fixed (the references graph moved from C-blocked to fully REPLACEABLE). The enforced denominator is still ~90%+ execution (Biome/ESLint/knip/tsc/publint/playwright/semgrep) = NON-GOAL; alint replaces the references-graph + pnpm-policy + config-presence remainder completely.

**Enforcement surface.** astro's ENFORCED gates are almost entirely tool-execution (D): Biome (primary format+lint, biome.jsonc), ESLint (type-aware rules Biome can't do, eslint.config.js), knip (dead-export/dependency detection), tsc (typecheck across the project-references solution), publint (package-export correctness), vitest + node:test (unit), playwright (E2E/browser), semgrep (security patterns), zizmor (GitHub-Actions security). Releases are changesets-driven (.changeset/config.json + changeset markdown files). Repo-STATE that astro relies on (mostly review-enforced, not all CI-gated): (1) the root tsconfig.json `references[].path` array is the top-level solution file and every entry must resolve to an on-disk tsconfig — maintained MANUALLY per CONTRIBUTING's 'TypeScript project references' section; (2) pnpm-only policy — package.json `preinstall: npx only-allow pnpm` + pnpm-workspace.yaml + a `packageManager: pnpm@` corepack pin; (3) .nvmrc Node pin (contributors + Gitpod/devcontainer); (4) presence of the config files the tools consume (biome.jsonc, eslint.config.js, .changeset/config.json).

**What alint replaces.** root tsconfig.json references[*].path each resolve on disk -> registry_paths_resolve (source: tsconfig.json, extract json $.references[*].path) — NOW WORKS NATIVELY because Format::Json is JSONC-tolerant (see alint_improves); only-allow pnpm preinstall guard present -> file_content_matches `only-allow pnpm`; packageManager pnpm pin -> json_path_matches `^pnpm@`; .nvmrc / .changeset/config.json / biome.jsonc / eslint.config.js presence -> file_exists; plus inherited bundles oss-baseline/node/monorepo-pnpm-workspace/hygiene-lockfiles/editorconfig/ci-github-actions.

**What alint improves.** HEADLINE CORRECTION vs the original study: the config flags astro-root-tsconfig-references-resolve as a C 'sharp-edge' — it asserts the rule's LOGIC is correct (31 refs resolve) but that it FAILS on the clone because root tsconfig.json is JSONC (leading // comments) and the strict JSON extractor errored 'expected value at line 1 column 1'. That sharp-edge is now RESOLVED: structured_path.rs strip_jsonc() (verified by the json_parse_tolerates_jsonc unit test) makes Format::Json tolerate // + block comments + trailing commas, exactly the tsconfig.json/.jsonc/.vscode shape. So registry_paths_resolve on the JSONC solution file works today and should be flipped warning->error. This is materially better than astro's status quo: the references graph is maintained MANUALLY (a contributor adding a package and forgetting the reference, or deleting a tsconfig and orphaning a ref, is caught by NO tool today) — alint's registry_paths_resolve gives a real file->file reference-graph gate (with the orphan reverse-check) the repo currently lacks, runnable without tsc.

**Genuine gaps (post-build, critically assessed).** none. Every astro-addressable repo-state item is now expressible, and the single blocker (JSONC) is gone. All the heavy validation (Biome/ESLint/knip/tsc/publint/playwright/semgrep) is type-aware/execution = correct NON-GOAL. I checked whether 'each package referenced in the root solution must ALSO have its own per-package tsconfig with the right extends' could be a gap — it is expressible by composing for_each_dir over packages/* + nested file_exists / json_path_equals on tsconfig.json, so not a gap.

**Abstraction ideas.** A `format: jsonc` is now redundant for JSON (auto-tolerant), but a small win would be a bundled `ts-project-references@v1` micro-ruleset: registry_paths_resolve on the root solution file's references[*].path PLUS the reverse orphan check (every packages/*/tsconfig.json appears in the root references) — a TS-monorepo idiom astro, vue, and many others re-implement only via review. Could ship inside node@v1 gated on the presence of a root tsconfig with a `references` array. Second: a `changesets@v1` micro-ruleset (.changeset/config.json schema + presence) since changesets is near-universal in this ecosystem.

*Notes.* This repo is the strongest demonstration in the group of a prior 'gap'/'sharp-edge' that reproduce-first dissolves: the study's own note 'blocked today by JSONC parsing' is stale post-build. Worth re-running `alint check` against a fresh astro clone to confirm the 31 references resolve and flip the rule to error — that is the calibration gotcha from the tokio worked-example (re-pin + check vs the clone catches config bugs validate-config misses).
